## §0 Executive summary (final, 7d — replaces the 7a stub)

Notation is fixed in §1. $(\mathcal H,\omega_1,\{\ell_n\}_{n\ge1})$ is a cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra in the physical grading, $S(\Psi)=\sum_{n\ge2}\frac1{n!}V_n(\Psi^n)$, $V_n=\omega_1(\cdot,\ell_{n-1}(\cdots))$, $\Delta=\omega_1^{-1}$, $\tilde\Delta^{ab}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}\Delta^{ab}$ (the BV weight of (1.5), R16's $W$). The $\tilde\Delta$-raised products are $p_{k,\ell}=\tilde\Delta^{\otimes\ell}$-raising of $V_{k+\ell}$ on $\ell$ legs (§1.3, (3.27)); $p_{k,1}=\ell_k$, $p_{2,2}=m_2$, $p_{k,0}=V_k$, $p_{0,\ell}=\tilde\Delta^{\otimes\ell}V_\ell$.

**Statement A (CFL Def. 2.3 form, augmented, $k,\ell\ge1$) — false in general (counterexample at $(2,2)$); at every $(k,\ell)\ge(2,2)$ equivalent to the extra condition $P_{k,\ell}=0$, seen to fail at $(2,2),(2,3),(3,2)$; true for $\min(k,\ell)=1$.** The genus-zero relation (0.1) for the family $\{p_{k,\ell}\}_{k,\ell\ge1}$ is equivalent (CFL Prop. 7.2, (3.20)) to $\{H_0,H_0\}=0$ with $H_0=S(\Psi+\chi)-S(\Psi)-S(\chi)$ (0.2)–(0.3). Given cyclic $L_\infty$, $\{H_0,H_0\}|_{(k,\ell)}=\tfrac12M_{k,\ell}$, the sum of mixed two-vertex configurations (0.5), (3.31); $M_{k,\ell}=0$ identically when $\min(k,\ell)=1$ (no mixed configuration, (3.10)) and $M_{k,\ell}\ne0$, i.e. $P_{k,\ell}\ne0$, seen to fail at $(2,2),(2,3),(3,2)$ for $k,\ell\ge2$ (Prop. 3.2; at $(2,2)$: $M=\tilde\omega(\ell_2(\Psi,\chi),\ell_2(\Psi,\chi))$, nonzero for $\mathfrak{sl}_2$-type data, zero for Heisenberg (3.18)–(3.19)). Toy: $R_{2,2}|_{\rm even}=20.3=\tfrac12|M|$ on a genuine cyclic $L_\infty$ generator (§4). Status: **proven false** (§3.1–§3.2), **verified** (§4, E5, 7c table).

**Theorem A′ (the correct statement) — PROVEN (§3.4), VERIFIED (7c, D0, E1–E7).** The *non-augmented* family $\{p_{k,\ell}\}_{k,\ell\ge0,\,k+\ell\ge2}$ satisfies the genus-zero relations (3.29) at every $(k,\ell)$ $\Longleftrightarrow$ $(\mathcal H,\omega_1,\{\ell_n\})$ is cyclic $L_\infty$. Proof: (3.29) is the bidegree decomposition of $\{S(\Psi+\chi),S(\Psi+\chi)\}=0$, the polarised classical master equation (3.28). It satisfies the genus-zero IBL relations in generating-function form (polarised master equation), of the non-augmented type CFL admit in §8; the degree bookkeeping requires $d=\tfrac12$, outside CFL's integer-$d$ hypotheses, and the slot symmetries need the per-slot dictionary of §1.2 (iii); not in the Def. 2.3 sense; MS's loop algebra is the other packaging (single-output vertices + bivector), augmented in outputs only (3.32)–(3.33). Content beyond $(S,S)=0$: none — the family is the Taylor expansion of $S$ about a second field.

**R16 adjudicated WRONG at $(2,2,0)$ (§3.3, §3.5; confirmed by critic pass A, 2026-09-08, verdict (a)).** 010's final step "$=0$ by Jacobi" was never computed (its script check 7b is a hard-coded PASS) and is false: the tensor $W\Delta\circ([Q,\ell_3]+\ell_2\circ_1\ell_2)$ with plain-$\Delta$ products is not the cyclic $L_\infty$ relation (its $Q$-part is not the symmetric $\delta_QV_4$; check 3c, residual 19.7). 010's identity is non-hollow in its own convention; what IS hollow is 7a's weighted "end-to-end" re-run, whose terms are $\bar\varepsilon$-antisymmetric and project to $0=0$ ((3.23)–(3.24), E6); with the uniform $\tilde\Delta^{\otimes2}$ raising the relation is $R_{2,2}=A_1+A_2+A_3+A_4=0$, which fails by $\tfrac12M_{2,2}$ (3.25). Survives: $(1,2,0)\Leftrightarrow[Q,\ell_2]=0$, Lemma A and B1 as raw identities, byproduct 1 (1.7). Ledger actions in §7 (exact rewording; R6 flag). Sub-family reading (§6, W12): the augmented components of $V_{\le4}$ need $p_{1,3}$ as well ($(1,3,0)$) and still fail $(2,2,0)$; the non-augmented components of $V_{\le4}$ close exactly at $k+\ell\le4$.

**Part B (converse, item 2 of the multi-string question) — Theorem B′ (§5′.3), PROVEN (Lemma W; quantifier corrected B′d), VERIFIED for the identity (B′.1) (W1–W4) and the converse on odd sectors (W5), 99/99 — the iff itself is proven, not machine-checked; the old Theorem B (§5.3) is superseded.** $\hat{\mathfrak p}=\sum\hbar^{k-1}\hat p_{k,\ell}$ is parity-inhomogeneous in every grading of $W$ (§5′.1: $\hat Q$ odd, $m_2$ even), so the classical brace theorem does not apply. What holds: its Koszul braces $\ell^W_n=\Phi^n_{\hat{\mathfrak p}}$ form a curved $L_\infty$ algebra **iff** $F_{\hat{\mathfrak p}^2}(a)=F_{\hat{\mathfrak p}}(a)^2$ for all even $a\in A\otimes\Lambda$, $\Lambda$ any auxiliary Grassmann algebra — equivalently the multilinear relations in all input-parity sectors; over even $a\in A$ alone only the even-input relations follow (W12) — ((B′.4); equivalently the braces of $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2$ are the shuffle square of those of $\hat{\mathfrak p}$). Refuted: 'iff $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0$' (free theory: $L_\infty$ braces with $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2\ne0$, §5′.2) and the symbol form 'iff $(\hat{\mathfrak p}\star\hat{\mathfrak p})_{s\ge1}=0$' (counterexample $\hat p_{2,0}$, W2). On single-string inputs $\ell^W_n=\hbar^{n-1}\sum_\ell p_{n,\ell}$ (Lemma B1) and A′ is the leading-$\hbar$ content of (B′.4); the extension to multi-string inputs (the $\partial\partial a$-type terms of Lemma W, WB2 (b)) is **Verdict (C)** (§5′.6–§5′.7): it has no formulation as a Koszul-brace structure of differential operators on a single graded-commutative polynomial host in the strand variables with even $\hbar$, **for the class $T=(A^{\otimes\ell}\otimes B^{\otimes k})\circ V$ with slot-uniform, parity-homogeneous raising maps on the slots and $\delta$-contraction** — within that class the dichotomy is proved: every such host either projects a slot symmetry (host (i), W7″) or replaces A′'s $\tilde\Delta$-contraction by a bare index sum (host (iii), (5′.15)) — and is untested, not excluded, for hosts with separate input/output generators, a non-polynomial product, the $\star$-calculus (W10), or an odd parameter in a non-graded-commutative host; so '(5.17) is the first obstruction' is withdrawn. Host: the theorem itself is host-independent. For $\ell=1$ the décalage signs are **fixed**: the naive coderivation extension on $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ carries no sign ((5′.12)) and is verified to arity 3 (W6, W6a, W7). For $\ell\ge2$ no single graded-commutative polynomial host in the strand variables, in the class with slot-uniform parity-homogeneous raising maps and $\delta$-contraction, realises the family with A′'s contraction (§5′.7; other hosts untested, not excluded), and the doubled symbol algebra is bookkeeping only — *not* an operator on $S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$ ($S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ kills $m_2(\Psi,\Psi)$ on physical inputs, P3; the all-slots-raised realisation is host (iii), which fails). Survives at the level of the brace *products*: non-cyclicity for degree-diagonal pairings and the cotangent lift with criterion (B′.4) (§5.5); HS (b) is the re-weighted $V_4$-slice of the products and, now proven in general, not the brace structure of any operator ((5′.10), W4); A′ gives (b) the $\min(k,\ell)=1$ relations, hence R11; $(2,2,0)$ never enters. Consequences sub-section by sub-section: §5′.4. Ledger wording: §7.

**Reading order:** §1 (conventions), §3.4 (A′), §3.3 (R16), §5.2–§5.5 (part B), §6 (interpretation, sub-family), §7 (ledger). Numbers: §4, W9. Older §0 formulation (0.1)–(0.5) is kept below because §3 refers to it.

**Equations of the 7a stub kept for reference.** (0.1): $\sum_{k_1+k_2=k+1,\ \ell_1+\ell_2=\ell+1}(\hat p_{k_2,\ell_2}\circ_1\hat p_{k_1,\ell_1})|_{E_k\mathcal H}=0$, $k,\ell\ge1$. (0.2): $H_0(\Psi,\chi)=S(\Psi+\chi)-S(\Psi)-S(\chi)=\sum_{k,\ell\ge1}\frac1{k!\ell!}V_{k+\ell}(\Psi^k,\chi^\ell)$. (0.3): $\{H_0,H_0\}=\partial_{\Psi^a}H_0\,\tilde\Delta^{ab}\,\partial_{\chi^b}H_0=0$. (0.4): with $u=\Psi+\chi$, $\{H_0,H_0\}=B(u,u)-B(u,\chi)-B(\Psi,u)+B(\Psi,\chi)$. (0.5): given cyclic $L_\infty$, $\{H_0,H_0\}|_{(k,\ell)}=\tfrac12[\text{polarised }L_\infty]_{(k,\ell)}+\tfrac12\sum_{\rm mixed}[V_A\circ_\Delta V_B]=\tfrac12M_{k,\ell}$.

## §1 Conventions and the two suspension dictionaries

### §1.1 Graded space, pairing, $\Delta$

**Space and grading.** $\mathcal H=\bigoplus_n\mathcal H^{(n)}$ graded by ghost number $N_{\rm gh}$; basis $e_a$ with $N_{\rm gh}(e_a)=n_a$; physical parity $\varepsilon_a:=n_a\bmod2$. All Koszul signs below are in $\varepsilon$ unless a shifted parity $\bar\varepsilon_a:=\varepsilon_a+1$ is named explicitly. Closed string: $N_{\rm gh}(\Psi)=2$, $N_{\rm gh}(\Sigma_k)=2k$, $N_{\rm gh}(\ell_n)=3-2n$ (odd for all $n$), $N_{\rm gh}(p_{k,\ell,g})=3\ell-2k+g$ (R23).

**Pairing.** $\omega_1(A,B)=\langle A|c_0^-|B\rangle$, normalisation $\langle c_{-1}\bar c_{-1}c_0^-c_0^+c_1\bar c_1\rangle=-\tfrac12$, no factor $i$. $\omega_1(e_a,e_b)=:\omega_{ab}\neq0$ only if $n_a+n_b=5$; hence $\omega_1$ pairs opposite parities and is graded antisymmetric,
$$\omega_1(A,B)=-(-1)^{\varepsilon_A\varepsilon_B}\omega_1(B,A)=-\omega_1(B,A)\ \text{on support},\qquad \omega_{ab}=-\omega_{ba}.\tag{1.1}$$
Parity of the pairing as an operation: odd (one $c_0^-$). $Q=\ell_1$ has $N_{\rm gh}=+1$ and is cyclic: $\omega_1(QA,B)+(-1)^{\varepsilon_A}\omega_1(A,QB)=0$.

**Bivector.** $\Delta:=\omega_1^{-1}$, components $\Delta^{ab}$ with $\omega_{ab}\Delta^{bc}=\delta_a{}^c$; as an element $\Delta=\Delta^{ab}e_a\otimes e_b=\sum_\alpha u_\alpha\otimes v_\alpha$. Properties (toy checks 1a/1e of `verify-220-relation.py`, R16):
$$\Delta^{ab}=-\Delta^{ba},\qquad \Delta^{ab}\neq0\Rightarrow n_a+n_b=5\ (\varepsilon_a+\varepsilon_b=1),\qquad N_{\rm gh}(\Delta)=+5,\tag{1.2}$$
$$\text{odd-pairing }Q\text{-move (010 Lemma A, reused):}\quad Q^a{}_c\,\Delta^{cb}-(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}\Delta^{ac}\,Q^b{}_c=0,\tag{1.3}$$
with $Qe_c=Q^a{}_c e_a$. (1.3) is the invariance of $\Delta$; the naive coderivation form (relative $+$) is false. "Parity flow across $\Delta$": $(-1)^{\varepsilon_b}=-(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}$ whenever $\Delta^{ab}\neq0$.

**Cyclic vertices and products.** $V_{n+1}(A_0,A_1,\dots,A_n):=\omega_1(A_0,\ell_n(A_1,\dots,A_n))$, totally graded symmetric in $\varepsilon$ (closed-string convention: the $\ell_n$ are graded *symmetric* in the physical parity; $\mathcal H_{\rm phys}$ is already the "suspended" space of the Lie-grading picture, cf. §1.2). $V_{n+1}$ saturates at $\sum_i n_{a_i}=2(n+1)$ ($V_3$ at 6, $V_4$ at 8). Inverse (component form used by the script, `l2_from_V3`):
$$\ell_n(A_1,\dots,A_n)=\tilde\Delta^{ba}\,V_{n+1}(e_a,A_1,\dots,A_n)\,e_b,\qquad\text{i.e.}\quad \omega_1(e_c,\ell_n(\cdots))=-(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}\,V_{n+1}(e_c,\cdots)\ \text{(5′.2); the dressing of §1.5 is this sign.}\tag{1.4}$$
*(Corrected 2026-09-14, §8.6: the previous wording asserted both the sign-free identity and the signed one in the same sentence. The sign-free form $\omega_1(e_c,\ell_n(\cdots))=V_{n+1}(e_c,\cdots)$ belongs to the **plain**-$\Delta$ product $\ell^{\rm H}_n=\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$ — Harold's convention, and the one §3.2 (3.11) uses; the signed form above belongs to the $\tilde\Delta$-raised $\ell_n$ defined here. The two differ by $(-1)^{\varepsilon_{\rm out}}$ per raised leg; dictionary in §8.1 (8.6)/(8.9).)*
Classical BV action and antibracket (physical grading, left derivatives $\partial_a:=\partial_L/\partial\Psi^a$, $\Psi=\Psi^ae_a$ Grassmann-even so $\varepsilon(\Psi^a)=\varepsilon_a$):
$$S(\Psi)=\sum_{n\ge2}\frac1{n!}V_n(\Psi^n),\qquad (F,G):=(-1)^{\varepsilon_a(\varepsilon_F+1)}\,\partial_aF\,\Delta^{ab}\,\partial_bG,\qquad B(x,y):=(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}\partial_aS(x)\,\Delta^{ab}\,\partial_bS(y).\tag{1.5}$$
The weight $(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}$ is the right-vs-left derivative conversion for even $S$; without it $(S,S)$ would vanish identically by (1.2) (then $\partial_aS\,\partial_bS$ commute on the support). With it $(S,S)=B(\Psi,\Psi)$ is symmetric and cyclic $L_\infty$ $\Leftrightarrow(S,S)=0\Leftrightarrow B(x,x)=0\ \forall x$ (standard; Zwiebach 1993 §4, Erbin 2023 ch. on BV). **This $(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}$ is the first-strand weight $W$ of R16** (Lemma A: $W=(-1)^{\varepsilon(\text{first output})}$): in bare physical variables every $\Delta$-contraction that closes a bracket carries it. Recorded here once; §1.3 uses it.

### §1.2 Suspension dictionary I: Lie grading ↔ physical ↔ CFL $C[1]$

Three gradings appear in the sources; one table, used everywhere below.

| object | Lie-grading space $L$ | physical $\mathcal H_{\rm phys}$ ($=L[1]$) | CFL $C[1]$ / MS $A_c$ |
|---|---|---|---|
| symmetry of $\ell_n$ inputs | graded antisymmetric | graded symmetric in $\varepsilon$ | symmetric in $\lvert c\rvert$ |
| closed-string field | — | $N_{\rm gh}=2$, even | degree 0 (MS: $A_c=s^{-2}\tilde A_c$, parity-even shift) |
| $\ell_n$ degree | $n-2$ (Lie) | $N_{\rm gh}=3-2n$ | $\lvert\mathfrak p_{n,1,0}\rvert=-2d(n-1)-1$ |
| pairing | even, symmetric | $\omega_1$ odd, antisymmetric, saturates at 5 | $\omega_c$ degree $-1$, antisymmetric |

Rules. (i) Physical parity $\varepsilon=N_{\rm gh}\bmod2$ **is** the symmetric ($L[1]$) parity for single-string inputs: no further shift is needed on input slots; the MS shift $s^{-2}$ is parity-even (R7, plan T1 note). (ii) CFL's integer degree $\lvert c\rvert=\deg c-1$ and $\lvert\hbar\rvert=2d$ are bookkeeping for the *integer* grading; the genus-zero relations (0.1) contain no $\hbar$-degree and their signs depend on **parities only** (Koszul signs $\varepsilon(\rho)$ in CFL (2.2)/(2.8)). So the only content of "CFL grading vs physical" relevant to (0.1) is: which parity governs the symmetry of each slot. (iii) Output slots: by (1.2), raising a vertex leg of parity $\varepsilon_b$ produces a vector $e_a$ with $\varepsilon_a=\varepsilon_b+1$. The vertex symmetry (in $\varepsilon_b$) therefore becomes symmetry of the output vectors in $\bar\varepsilon_a=\varepsilon_a+1$ — R16 byproduct 1 (exact in the toy, both conventions), R7's $\omega_2^{\rm symp}=\omega_1^{\otimes2}\circ(s\otimes s)$. **Inputs of $p_{k,\ell}$ are symmetric in $\varepsilon$, outputs in $\bar\varepsilon$.** This per-slot asymmetry is exactly why no single global shift of $\mathcal H$ turns the bare $p_{k,\ell}$ into a CFL map $E_kC\to E_\ell C$ with one grading on $C$ — R23's "per-slot dictionary", now stated at the level of parities. §1.4 gives the per-slot rule that does it.

### §1.3 The $\Delta$-raised products $p_{k,\ell}$ on inputs of arbitrary parity

**Definition (canonical form: raised legs first, outputs in leg order).** For $k,\ell\ge1$ and inputs $A_1,\dots,A_k\in\mathcal H$ of arbitrary parities,
$$p_{k,\ell}(A_1,\dots,A_k):=\tilde\Delta^{a_1b_1}\cdots\tilde\Delta^{a_\ell b_\ell}\;V_{k+\ell}\bigl(e_{b_1},\dots,e_{b_\ell},A_1,\dots,A_k\bigr)\;e_{a_1}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{a_\ell}\;\in\mathcal H^{\otimes\ell}.\tag{1.6}$$
No sign: the contracted legs are adjacent and leftmost, and the scalar $V(\cdots)$ carries no Koszul sign against the vectors $e_{a_i}$. $p_{k,1}=\ell_k$ by (1.4); $p_{2,2}=m_2$, $p_{1,2}=\tilde\Delta\circ\ell_2$ of R16 (the script's `M2 = einsum("ai,bijk->abjk", Delta, L3)` is (1.6) with the two legs in the order $(b_2,b_1)$, i.e. up to the Koszul sign $(-1)^{\varepsilon_{b_1}\varepsilon_{b_2}}$ per component — irrelevant after output symmetrisation, and immaterial for R16's raw-tensor lemmas, which were fitted with that convention). Ghost number: each raising converts an input slot ($-2$) into an output ($+3$): $N_{\rm gh}(p_{k,\ell})=3-2(k+\ell-1)+5(\ell-1)=3\ell-2k$ ✓ (R23, $g=0$).

**Symmetry of the outputs (byproduct 1 of R16, now a one-line proof).** Swapping $a_i\leftrightarrow a_j$ in (1.6) is the same as swapping the legs $b_i\leftrightarrow b_j$ inside $V_{k+\ell}$, which costs $(-1)^{\varepsilon_{b_i}\varepsilon_{b_j}}$; by (1.2) $\varepsilon_{b}=\varepsilon_a+1=\bar\varepsilon_a$. Hence
$$p_{k,\ell}(\cdots)^{\dots a_i\dots a_j\dots}=(-1)^{\bar\varepsilon_{a_i}\bar\varepsilon_{a_j}}\;p_{k,\ell}(\cdots)^{\dots a_j\dots a_i\dots}:\quad\text{outputs graded symmetric in the shifted parity }\bar\varepsilon.\tag{1.7}$$
Inputs: graded symmetric in $\varepsilon$ (inherited from $V$). So $p_{k,\ell}:\mathrm{Sym}^k_\varepsilon\mathcal H\to\mathrm{Sym}^\ell_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$; the target is R7's twisted symmetric power.

**Raising a non-leftmost slot (the input-parity-dependent dressing; item T1).** If one wants the raised leg in position $j$ of $V$ (as happens when an output of one product is contracted into a non-first slot of another, or when a spectator sits to the left), graded symmetry of $V$ gives
$$\Delta^{ab}\,V(A_1,\dots,A_{j-1},e_b,A_{j+1},\dots)\,e_a=(-1)^{\bar\varepsilon_a\,(\varepsilon_{A_1}+\cdots+\varepsilon_{A_{j-1}})}\;\Delta^{ab}\,V(e_b,A_1,\dots,A_{j-1},A_{j+1},\dots)\,e_a.\tag{1.8}$$
**Rule R1:** *an output strand Koszul-commutes past inputs and other outputs with its shifted parity $\bar\varepsilon_a$; inputs commute among themselves with $\varepsilon$.* (1.8) is the whole content of the "input-parity-dependent Koszul dressings" of 010's residual: the dressing is $(-1)^{\bar\varepsilon_{\rm out}\sum\varepsilon_{\rm in}}$, and it is invisible on the even input sector only when the inputs it passes have $\sum\varepsilon_{\rm in}=0$ — which for two inputs of equal parity is automatic for the strand passing both, but not for a strand passing one of them. This is why the coderivation term $\hat\ell_2\circ_1\hat p_{1,2}$ could not be fitted with a uniform sign in 010 (checks 6b/M1): its two summands (strand passing $x$ only, strand passing neither) carry different dressings.

**Composition $\circ_1$ in bare variables.** Feeding output $a$ of $p_{k_1,\ell_1}$ into input slot $i$ of $p_{k_2,\ell_2}$ yields a $\Delta$-contraction of two vertices,
$$V_{k_2+\ell_2}(\dots,e_a,\dots)\,\Delta^{ab}\,V_{k_1+\ell_1}(e_b,\dots),\tag{1.9}$$
with the dressing of R1 for moving $e_a$ into slot $i$ and for the relative order of the surviving strands. Note (1.9) is symmetric under exchanging the roles of the two vertices up to $\Delta^{ab}=-\Delta^{ba}$ and the Koszul reorderings — the ordered pairs $(1,2)$ and $(2,1)$ of §0 are the same tensor up to a sign that R1 fixes.

**Hat-extension and the spectator problem.** CFL's $\hat p$ (their (2.2)) lets spectators pass through unchanged. In bare variables a spectator is an input (parity $\varepsilon$) that is also an output (must be counted with $\bar\varepsilon$ by R1): the identity strand is *not* a $p_{1,1}$-type map with inputs in $\varepsilon$ and outputs in $\bar\varepsilon$. Consequently (0.1) with CFL's hats cannot be written with uniform signs in bare physical variables on all sectors — this is the precise statement of 010's flagged residual, and it is *not* a defect of the products but of the hat-formalism in unsuspended variables. **Decision (binding for 7b–7d):** prove and verify the genus-zero relations in the generating-function form (0.3) with the weighted BV contraction (1.5), where there are no spectators (untouched variables of the polynomial play that role automatically and commute with the correct signs because $\Psi,\chi$ are Grassmann-even superfields). The equivalence (0.3)$\Leftrightarrow$(0.1) is CFL Prop. 7.2 (leading $\hbar$ order) *in CFL's grading*; the translation of that equivalence to bare variables is exactly R1 and is not needed for any physical statement.

**Bare-variable form of the mixed term at $(2,2)$ (worked, from (0.4); the T1 deliverable).** With $X^{\,a}(\Psi,\chi):=\partial_a$-component of $V_3(\cdot,\Psi,\chi)$, i.e. $X_a:=V_3(e_a,\Psi,\chi)$ of parity $\varepsilon_a$ as a function (both superfields even), the mixed configuration of §0 is
$$M(\Psi,\chi)=(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}\,X_a\,\Delta^{ab}\,X_b\qquad(\text{weight from (1.5)}).\tag{1.10}$$
Without the weight it vanishes identically (relabel $a\leftrightarrow b$, use (1.2) and $X_aX_b=X_bX_a$ on the support); with it, relabelling gives $M=(-1)^{\varepsilon_b}X_b\Delta^{ba}X_a=-(-1)^{1+\varepsilon_a}X_a\Delta^{ab}X_b=+M$: no constraint. In components (inputs $x,y$; outputs $c,d$; the E$_2$ projections of §1.2): $M(x,y)^{cd}=\sum_{a,b}(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}\Delta^{ca'}\Delta^{db'}\,V_3(e_a,x,e_{a'})\,\Delta^{ab}\,V_3(e_b,y,e_{b'})+\text{R1-dressed }(x\leftrightarrow y),(c\leftrightarrow d)$. **Prediction for 7b/7c (to verify, not yet):** after symmetrising $(x,y)$ in $\varepsilon$ and $(c,d)$ in $\bar\varepsilon$, the two orderings cancel iff the net sign collected by R1 in exchanging the roles of the two vertices is $-1$; on the even input sector ($\varepsilon_x=\varepsilon_y$) 010's check 6b says it is. Whether it is $-1$ on all sectors is the open core of Statement A in bare variables; in the suspended variables of CFL the answer is what Prop. 7.2 says it is, and 7b must compute it. **[Prediction falsified by §3.2: the cancellation does not hold in general — $M_{k,\ell}\ne0$, equivalently $P_{k,\ell}\ne0$, at $(2,2),(2,3),(3,2)$; the net R1 sign is not uniformly $-1$.]**

### §1.4 Suspension dictionary II: CFL degree ↔ physical ghost number, per slot

**Claim (convention-level, exact for all $(k,\ell,g)$).** Let $\sigma$ be a uniform per-slot shift ($\deg A:=N_{\rm gh}(A)-\sigma$ on every strand, inputs and outputs alike) and use the cohomological sign ($Q$ of degree $+1$). A map with $k$ inputs and $\ell$ outputs of ghost number $N$ then has degree
$$\deg_\sigma p_{k,\ell,g}=N_{\rm gh}(p_{k,\ell,g})-\sigma\ell+\sigma k=(3-\sigma)\ell+(\sigma-2)k+g\qquad(\text{R23: }N_{\rm gh}=3\ell-2k+g).\tag{1.11}$$
Two bookkeepings of the genus parameter are in use: CFL count *inputs*, $\hat{\mathfrak p}=\sum\hat{\mathfrak p}_{k,\ell,g}\hbar^{k+g-1}$ (from $p_\gamma\to\hbar\partial_{q_\gamma}$, their (7.4)); Münster–Sachs count *outputs*, $\mathfrak D=\sum x^{n+g-1}D^{n,g}$ with $n$ the number of outputs (1109.4101 §IV.B, 1208.5626 App. A.2). Requiring the total operator to be homogeneous of degree $+1$:
$$\text{CFL:}\ (3-\sigma)\ell+(\sigma-2)k+g+(k+g-1)|\hbar|=1\ \forall k,\ell,g\iff \sigma=3,\ |\hbar|=-1;\qquad \text{MS:}\ \dots+(\ell+g-1)|\hbar|=1\iff\sigma=2,\ |\hbar|=-1.\tag{1.12}$$
Check: CFL with $\sigma=3$: $k+g-(k+g-1)=1$ ✓; MS with $\sigma=2$: $\ell+g-(\ell+g-1)=1$ ✓; MS's $\sigma=2$ is their $A_c=s^{-2}\tilde A_c$ (field at degree 0, $|l_n^g|=1$ ✓, $|\omega_c^{-1}|=5-4=1$ ✓ — note $N_{\rm gh}(\Delta)=5$, not the $k\ge1$ formula). Translating CFL's homological convention ($|\hat{\mathfrak p}|=-1$, $|\hbar|=2d$) by a global sign flip: $2d=+1$, i.e. **$d=\tfrac12$: the physical structure satisfies the genus-zero IBL relations in generating-function form (polarised master equation), of the non-augmented type CFL admit in §8; the degree bookkeeping requires $d=\tfrac12$, outside CFL's integer-$d$ hypotheses, and the slot symmetries need the per-slot dictionary of §1.2 (iii)**, $|\mathfrak p_{k,\ell,g}|_{\rm CFL}=-2\cdot\tfrac12(k+g-1)-1=-(k+g)=-\deg_{\sigma=3}p_{k,\ell,g}$ ✓ exactly, for every $(k,\ell,g)$.

**Why this is the right reading, and what R23 saw.** R23 fixed $|\hbar|$ even (integer $d$) and found that no per-map offset works (its critic S2: $k$-slope wants $d=1$, $g$-slope $d=-\tfrac12$); with per-slot shifts and an odd $\hbar$ both slopes are matched at once. Physically: a handle is a $\Delta$ self-contraction of two legs, ghost $+5-2\cdot2=+1$ (R23's $+g$), so the parameter multiplying it must carry $-1$ to keep $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ homogeneous — the same statement as "the BV Laplacian $\hbar\Delta_{\rm BV}$ is odd" and as the ħ-grading mismatch noted in the 1109.4101 review (BV vertices $\hbar^{2g+b+n/2-1}$ vs coderivations $\hbar^{g+b-1}$, absorbed by $c'=\hbar^{1/2}c$, which is what makes MS's $\hbar$ degree-0). CFL require integer $d$ only so that the Weyl variables $|p_\gamma|+|q_\gamma|=2d$ commute *evenly*; for $2d$ odd their Weyl algebra becomes the odd (Clifford–Weyl) algebra of the BV antibracket, which is precisely (1.5). Nothing in the genus-zero relations (0.1) depends on this integer bookkeeping (§1.2 rule (ii)); it matters for the $g\ge1$ relations' $\hbar$-homogeneity only. **Per-slot parity dictionary (the part that carries signs) restated:** inputs $\varepsilon$, outputs $\bar\varepsilon$ (§1.2 (iii), (1.7)); the uniform integer shift $\sigma$ of (1.12) is parity-odd for $\sigma=3$ (CFL) and parity-even for $\sigma=2$ (MS) — CFL's $C[1]$ is the parity-shifted space on *all* strands, MS's $A_c$ is not shifted in parity, and the $\bar\varepsilon$ on outputs is then supplied by the odd $\hbar$ (one odd $\hbar$ per extra output in MS's counting: $x^{n-1}$, $n$ outputs). This is the honest form of 010's "one-unit suspension"; it is a statement about where the parity flip is booked, not about physics.

### §1.5 Toy-generator choice (inherited verbatim by 7b–7d)

**Requirement.** A finite-dimensional cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra with $\ell_1,\dots,\ell_5$ all nonzero, every relation term $O(1)$ (non-hollow), the relations satisfied *exactly*, in the physical grading, with a seeded generic instance.

**Candidates and cost-to-information.**
- (i) *Cyclic homotopy transfer* of a random cyclic dg Lie algebra onto a deformation retract: gives all $\ell_n$, but needs the cyclic HTT formulas with signs (a derivation in itself) and a dg Lie algebra with a compatible odd pairing; highest cost, and the transferred $\ell_n$ are only as trustworthy as the HTT signs. Rejected.
- (ii) *Lemma-by-lemma on unconstrained data* (010's strategy): each identity holds on generic cyclic data, the $L_\infty$ relation is used once at the end. This remains the right strategy for the *proofs* (7b), but it does not provide an actual $L_\infty$ algebra to test the *assembled* $(k,\ell,0)$ relations end to end, which 7c must do. Kept as the second leg (the script runs the R16 lemmas on generic data as in 010).
- (iii) *Hand-chosen nilpotent example*: getting $\ell_5\ne0$ with all lower relations satisfied by hand is error-prone and gives a single non-generic point. Rejected.
- (iv) **Chosen: canonical-transformation twist of the free theory.** With the BV antibracket (1.5) on polynomial functionals of the toy, take $S_0=\tfrac12\omega_1(\Psi,Q\Psi)$ and an odd generator $G=G_3+G_4$ (random graded-symmetric tensors of rank $r$ saturating at ghost number $2r+1$, so that $(G,S_0)$ has the saturation $2n$ of a vertex $V_n$). Then
$$S:=e^{\mathrm{ad}_G}S_0=\sum_{m\ge0}\frac1{m!}(G,(G,\cdots(G,S_0)\cdots)),\qquad (S,S)=0\ \text{exactly},\tag{1.13}$$
because $e^{\mathrm{ad}_G}$ is an automorphism of the antibracket (graded Jacobi). $\mathrm{ad}_G$ raises the polynomial rank by $\ge1$, so truncating at rank 6 is exact for $V_2,\dots,V_6$ (only $m\le4$ contribute) and the rank-$N$ part of $(S,S)$, which involves $V_i,V_j$ with $i+j=N+2\le8$, vanishes for $N\le6$: the relations $(n,1,0)$, $n\le5$, hold exactly with $\ell_n=\tilde\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$ (W3.2). The paper's Zwiebach bracket $\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$ differs from this $\ell_n$ by $(-1)^{\varepsilon_{\rm out}}$, with the relations dressed accordingly; only the $\tilde\Delta$ family satisfies the record's graded Jacobi identity (script 3b PASS / 3c FAIL). Cyclicity is automatic ($V_n$ graded symmetric). Cost: rank-6 tensors and shuffle products (15 shuffles at rank 6); at the default toy (multiplicities $(2,4,4,2)$, $\dim\mathcal H=12$) this is seconds; the 010 size $(3,5,5,3)$, $\dim16$, is available via `--mult 3,5,5,3` and is slower but feasible. The algebra so generated is gauge-equivalent to the free one (contractible $Q$) — which is irrelevant for testing multilinear identities and is in fact what guarantees a solution exists at every rank — and it is *not* hollow: 010's degeneracy ("every $Q$-compatible cyclic $\ell_2$ had $\ell_2\circ\ell_2=0$") is avoided because $\ell_2\circ_1\ell_2=-[Q,\ell_3]\neq0$ here (checked, script 3b).

**What the script establishes and how it can fail.** (a) The generator's $(S,S)=0$ rank by rank (checks 2c) *and*, independently of the antibracket implementation, the $(2,1,0)$ and $(3,1,0)$ relations through 010's commutator/Jacobiator formulas with physical Koszul signs (3a, 3b) — a wrong sign convention in (1.5) would make e$^{\mathrm{ad}_G}$ fail to be an automorphism and 2c/3b would FAIL; the break-on-purpose leg 2d perturbs $V_4$. (b) The output symmetry (1.7) for $(k,\ell)$ up to $k+\ell=6$ (4a), in the shifted parity only. (c) The R16 chain (Lemma A, Lemma B1, 6b inertness, even-sector assembly) on generic data (non-hollow, assembly $\neq0$) and on the generator's $L_\infty$ data (assembly $=0$). (d) New: 6b′ records that the mixed chain term is *not* inert off the even sector — the numerical form of the residual of §1.3.

**Inherited by 7b–7d.** Generator (iv), physical grading, default toy $(2,4,4,2)$, seed 20260908, the script's `Toy`, `build_generator`, `raise_map`, `antibracket` and the generating-function form (0.3)/(1.5) as the object to be verified (7c) and proven (7b). 7c adds the general $(k,\ell,0)$ table by evaluating $\{H_0,H_0\}$ bidegree by bidegree with `antibracket` on the two-variable polynomial $H_0$ (implementable as tensors with a colour label per slot), and the CFL-form (0.1) for $(k,\ell)\le(3,3)$ with the R1 dressings, to pin the equivalence numerically.

## §2 Literature verdict

### §2.A Part A (cyclic $L_\infty$ ⇒ genus-zero IBL of the $\Delta$-raised products)

Sources checked (equation numbers from the caches): CFL 1508.02741 §2 Def. 2.3, Lemma 2.6 / eq. (2.4)–(2.5), remark (5), §7 eqs. (7.1)–(7.6), Prop. 7.2; Münster–Sachs 1109.4101 §IV.A eqs. (14)–(17), §IV.B (the expansion $\mathfrak D=\sum_n x^{n-1}D^{(n)}=\sum_{n,g}x^{n+g-1}D^{n,g}$, the dIBL conditions (18)), App. B eqs. (B1)–(B11); 1208.5626 §II.2 eq. (5) ($\mathfrak L_c=\sum_g\hbar^gL^g+\hbar\,\Omega_c^{-1}$) and App. A.2.

1. **CFL §2 remark (5).** An $L_\infty$ algebra is an IBL$_\infty$ algebra with $\mathfrak p_{k,\ell,g}=0$ for $\ell\ge2$ or $g>0$ — the *trivial* embedding, all multi-output products zero. It says nothing about the $\Delta$-raised $p_{k,\ell}$, and CFL have no $\omega_1$ or $\Delta$ at the level of Def. 2.3 (review, "Gap"). *Not the theorem.*
2. **CFL §7, Prop. 7.2.** One-to-one correspondence IBL$_\infty$ (with (7.5)) ⇔ differential Weyl algebra $(\mathcal W,\mathbb H)$ with $\mathbb H\star\mathbb H=0$ and augmentation (7.2), via $\mathfrak p_{k,\ell,g}=\hbar^{-k}\overrightarrow{H_{k,\ell,g}}$ (7.4). This is the dictionary (0.1)⇔(0.3) *in CFL's grading* (integer $d$; §1.4 gives the physical $d=\tfrac12$ reading). It reduces Statement A to $\{H_0,H_0\}=0$ for $H_0=S(\Psi+\chi)-S(\Psi)-S(\chi)$; it does **not** assert that $(S,S)=0$ implies it, and by (0.4)–(0.5) it does not without the mixed-configuration identity. *Dictionary known; theorem not stated.*
3. **Münster–Sachs 1109.4101 §IV.A–B and App. B; 1208.5626 §II.2.** The closed-string loop homotopy algebra is $\mathfrak L_c=\sum_g\hbar^gL^g+\hbar\,D(\omega_c^{-1})$ — first-order coderivations $L^g$ (single-output $l^g_n$) plus the *strict second-order coderivation with no inputs* $D(\omega_c^{-1})$ (their (25)–(27); 1208.5626 eq. (5)). $\mathfrak L_c^2=0$ ⇔ main identity + cyclicity (their (28)–(29)); App. B shows this is the quantum BV master equation (B8: $\hbar\Delta_cS\sim\omega_c\circ l^g(\omega_c^{-1}\wedge e^{c'})$, i.e. the BV Laplacian *is* the insertion of $\omega_c^{-1}$ into a vertex — a $\Delta$ self-contraction, our genus term $p_{k,\ell,g\ge1}$). **No multi-output product $p_{k,\ell\ge2}$ built from the vertices appears anywhere**: in MS's structure the only multi-output object is the invariant bivector itself, and the genus-zero part of $\mathfrak L_c^2=0$ is literally $L^2=0$ ($L_\infty$) plus $[L,D(\omega_c^{-1})]=0$ (invariance of $\Delta$, our (1.3) and its $\ell_n$ analogues). The $\Delta$-raised structure is a *different* IBL$_\infty$ packaging of the same data (shear $p\to p+q$ of the Weyl variables applied to $S(p)$, minus the augmentation terms — Working notes); MS never consider it. The dIBL conditions (18) (bracket–cobracket compatibility, involutivity) are the strict $(2,2,0)$, $(1,1,1)$ cases, stated for a general dIBL, not derived from a cyclic Lie algebra. *Not in MS.*
4. **1208.5626 App. A.2.** Restates CFL's definitions in coderivation language ($\mathrm{Coder}^n(SA)\cong\mathrm{Hom}(SA,\Sigma^nA)$, the bracket (A1) $[\mathrm{Coder}^i,\mathrm{Coder}^j]\subset\mathrm{Coder}^{i+j-1}$, MC elements, gauge transformations $\frac{d}{dt}\mathfrak U=[\mathfrak L,\hat\lambda]\mathfrak U$). No $\Delta$-raised products. *Not there.*

**Verdict A: new.** Neither CFL nor Münster–Sachs state or prove that the $\Delta$-raised products of a cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra form a genus-zero IBL$_\infty$ algebra; the closest statements are (i) CFL Prop. 7.2, which converts the claim into $\{H_0,H_0\}=0$, and (ii) MS's loop algebra, which is the *other* IBL$_\infty$ packaging (single-output vertices + invariant bivector). The claim is not a corollary of either: by (0.5) it is equivalent to the vanishing of the mixed two-vertex configurations, which is an extra identity (010's 6b at $(2,2)$, verified there numerically on the even sector only, unexplained). **Deliverable of 7b:** an explicit proof in the physical grading of the mixed-configuration cancellation for general $(k,\ell)$ (or its precise failure sector), presented as: Theorem A = [CFL Prop. 7.2 dictionary, cited] + [cyclic $L_\infty$ ⇒ (0.4)] + [mixed-configuration lemma, new]. If the lemma holds only on the even input sector, Theorem A is to be stated on the physical sector (which contains every object of the construction, R16) and the odd sector reported as the convention residual of §1.3.

### §2.B Part B (converse: IBL products of $\Delta$-raised form ⇒ $L_\infty$ on $\bigoplus_n\mathrm{Sym}^n\mathcal H$)

1. **Projection to $\ell=1$, $g=0$.** CFL (2.4) at $(k,1,0)$ contains only $\mathfrak p_{\cdot,1,0}$ (since $\ell_1+\ell_2=2$ forces $\ell_1=\ell_2=1$); hence the single-output, genus-zero slice of *any* IBL$_\infty$ algebra is an $L_\infty$ algebra (CFL §2 (5) and the review's "Relation" paragraph). For $\Delta$-raised products the $\ell_n=p_{n,1}$ are automatically cyclic (they come from symmetric $V_n$). *Known, trivial*; it is not what the multi-string question asks.
2. **$L_\infty$ on the multi-string space.** CFL remark 2.2 and their §1: $\hat{\mathfrak p}_{k,\ell,g}$ is a differential operator of order $\le k$ on the graded-commutative algebra $EC=\bigoplus_nE_nC$ and $(EC,\hat{\mathfrak p})$ is a BV$_\infty$ structure "in the sense of [20]" (review, key observation 4; the identity of CFL's ref. [20] is not in the cache — do not cite it by name). MS 1109.4101 cite Akman q-alg/9506027 and Bering–Damgaard–Alfaro hep-th/9604027 ("algebra of higher antibrackets") as their refs. [26],[27] for higher-order coderivations; 1208.5626 App. A.2 gives $\mathrm{Coder}^n(SA)\cong\mathrm{Hom}(SA,\Sigma^nA)$ and the commutator bracket (A1). The standard mechanism (higher derived brackets / Koszul braces of a square-zero differential operator of finite order on a graded-commutative algebra give an $L_\infty$ structure on that algebra) is exactly what turns $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ on $EC=\bigoplus_n\mathrm{Sym}^n\mathcal H$ into an $L_\infty$ algebra on the multi-string field $\Phi=\sum_n\Psi_n$: $\ell^{(S\mathcal H)}_1=\hat{\mathfrak p}$, $\ell^{(S\mathcal H)}_2(a,b)=\hat{\mathfrak p}(ab)-\hat{\mathfrak p}(a)b\mp a\hat{\mathfrak p}(b)$, …, with the $n$-th brace nonzero for $n\le\max k$. *Mechanism known (BDA/Akman, as cited by MS); the identification with the $p_{k,\ell}$ and the cyclicity are not written anywhere in the caches.*
3. **Cyclicity / action on $\bigoplus_nE_n\mathcal H$.** For an *action* one needs a pairing on $\bigoplus_nE_n\mathcal H$ making the brace-$L_\infty$ cyclic: candidate $\omega_n=\omega_1^{\otimes n}$ restricted to the twisted symmetric power (R7: $\omega_2^{\rm symp}=\omega_1^{\otimes2}\circ(s\otimes s)$). Not in the literature checked; item 2 of the multi-string question explicitly asks for this (write more general actions for SFT). *Missing.*

**Verdict B: implicit (mechanism known, statement missing).** *Known:* IBL$_\infty$ ⇒ $L_\infty$ on $\mathcal H$ by the $(k,1,0)$ slice (trivial); IBL$_\infty$ = BV$_\infty$-type square-zero operator on $\bigoplus_nE_n\mathcal H$ (CFL §1, rem. 2.2), and such operators define $L_\infty$ structures on the algebra by higher antibrackets (BDA 1996, Akman 1995, as cited by MS). *Missing:* (i) the explicit $L_\infty$ products on $\bigoplus_n\mathrm{Sym}^n\mathcal H$ in terms of the $p_{k,\ell}$ (Koszul braces of $\hat p_{k,\ell}$; with the $\Delta$-raised form these are again $\Delta$-contractions of the $V_n$), (ii) a cyclic structure for them, (iii) the reverse: which $L_\infty$ structures on $\bigoplus_n\mathrm{Sym}^n\mathcal H$ come from IBL$_\infty$ on $\mathcal H$ (the "gauge parameter $\Lambda_0$" question). **Deliverable of 7d:** (i) and (ii) explicitly, in the physical grading, with (iii) at least characterised; cite CFL rem. 2.2 and BDA/Akman via MS for the mechanism.

## Working notes (7a)

**W1. The $(2,2)$ instance of (0.5), by hand.** With $B_{mn}(x;y):=V'_m(x)\Delta V'_n(y)$ ($V'_m(x)=\partial V_m(x^{m-1})/(m-1)!$, weights of (1.5) understood) the $\Psi^2\chi^2$ part of the $V_3V_3$ contribution to (0.4) is: from $B(u,u)$, $\tfrac14[V_3(\Psi\Psi)\Delta V_3(\chi\chi)+V_3(\chi\chi)\Delta V_3(\Psi\Psi)+4V_3(\Psi\chi)\Delta V_3(\Psi\chi)]$; from $-B(u,\chi)-B(\Psi,u)+B(\Psi,\chi)$, $-\tfrac14$ pure each, net $-\tfrac14$ pure. So $\{H_0,H_0\}_{33}=\tfrac14\,\mathrm{pure}+\mathrm{mixed}$, while the polarised $L_\infty$ relation ($B(u,u)=0$) gives $\tfrac12\mathrm{pure}+\mathrm{mixed}+(Q\text{-terms})=0$. The $Q$-terms ($V_2V_4$) enter $\{H_0,H_0\}$ with weight $\tfrac12$ and $B(u,u)$ with weight 1. Hence $\{H_0,H_0\}_{(2,2)}=\tfrac12[B(u,u)]_{(2,2)}+\tfrac12\,\mathrm{mixed}$, i.e. (0.5): the IBL $(2,2,0)$ relation for the $\Delta$-raised products is, given cyclic $L_\infty$, equivalent to the vanishing of the mixed configuration $V_3(\Psi,\chi,\cdot)\Delta V_3(\cdot,\Psi,\chi)$ (the $\hat\ell_2\circ_1\hat p_{1,2}$ / $W\!\cdot\!Y$ term of R16). Same counting for general $(k,\ell)$: weight-1 (pure) configurations enter with half the polarised-$L_\infty$ weight, weight-2 (mixed) with the full weight.

**W2. Shear reading of $H_0$.** The map $p\mapsto p+q$ is canonical for the Weyl bracket, so $S(p+q)$ has vanishing self-bracket trivially; $H_0=S(p+q)-S(p)-S(q)$ is the shear of the action minus the augmentation terms, and $\{H_0,H_0\}=2[(S,S)(u)-B(u,p)-B(u,q)+B(p,q)]$ with the full (two-term) bracket — (0.4) rederived, the three subtracted terms being the whole content of Statement A.

**W3. Script findings (all at seed 20260908, toy $(2,4,4,2)$; R16 legs also at $(3,5,5,3)$).**
1. *Generator (iv) works:* $V_3,\dots,V_6$ graded symmetric, saturating at $2n$, $(S,S)=0$ rank by rank to $10^{-12}$ with individual $\ell\circ\ell$ terms $O(10^2$–$10^3)$; perturbing $V_4$ breaks $(3,1,0)$ (residual 1.4).
2. **Uniform $W$ dictionary (new, resolves 010's "avatar of the suspension"):** the products derived from $S$ by the antibracket (1.5) satisfy 010's operator-form relations $[Q',\ell'_2]=0$, $[Q',\ell'_3]+\ell'_2\circ_1\ell'_2=0$ (to $10^{-15}$) with $\ell'_n=W\circ(\Delta\circ V_{n+1})=\tilde\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$, $\tilde\Delta^{ab}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}\Delta^{ab}$ (graded *symmetric* bivector — MS's DeWitt $_ie=(-1)^ie_i$), and $Q'=WQ$, uniformly in $n$; with the plain $\Delta$ they fail (residuals 6.2, 19.7). $Q'$ is nilpotent, cyclic and obeys (1.3). So R16's first-strand weight $W$ is exactly $\tilde\Delta$ vs $\Delta$ on the raised leg, nothing deeper; 7b should raise with $\tilde\Delta$ throughout, which removes $W$ from every lemma.
3. **R16 end to end:** on the generator's $(Q',\ell'_2,\ell'_3)$, Lemma A and B1 hold with 010's dressings $(0,0,-1,1,1)$, $(1,-1)$, $W\!\cdot\!Y$ is inert on the even sector ($20.3\to9\times10^{-16}$), and the $(2,2,0)$ assembly vanishes on the even sector ($1.8\times10^{-15}$, with $|\hat Qm_2-m_2\hat Q|=24$). First confirmation with a genuine cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra; R16's scope ("toy-verified lemma by lemma") is thereby upgraded to "verified end to end on a nontrivial instance". **[Corrected by 7b/7d, §3.3 (3.23)–(3.24), E6: this run is hollow — in the weighted convention $\hat Qm_2-m_2\hat Q$, $p\circ\ell_2$ and $W\!\cdot\!Y$ are each $\bar\varepsilon$-antisymmetric on the even sector and vanish after the $E_2$ projection, so the identity is $0=0$ and the non-hollowness guard (unprojected norm 24) did not guard. It is not a confirmation of R16; R16 is wrong at $(2,2,0)$.]**
4. **Odd-sector fate of the mixed term is convention-dependent:** for $\ell_2=\Delta\circ V_3$ (plain raising, generic $V_3$) $W\!\cdot\!Y$ projected onto $E_2$ vanishes on *all* input sectors, at both toy sizes; for the BV-consistent $\ell'_2=\tilde\Delta\circ V_3$ it does not (projected odd-sector norm 20). Consistent with (1.10): the self-contraction vanishes without the weight and not with it. For 7b: in the consistent convention the odd-sector $(2,2,0)$ relation needs the R1-dressed coderivation term, or fails there; the physical (even) sector is safe either way.
5. $p_{k,\ell}$ outputs are symmetric in $\bar\varepsilon$ only, for all $(k,\ell)$ with $k+\ell\le6$, for both $\Delta$ and $\tilde\Delta$ raising ((1.7) confirmed; the adjacent-swap defect in $\varepsilon$ is $O(10^1$–$10^3)$).

## Verification (7a)

Script `codes/verify-linfty-ibl-repackaging.py`, `.venv` python, numpy only, seed printed, nonzero exit on failure. **48/48 PASS** at `--mult 2,4,4,2` (dim 12, `--maxr 6`); R16 legs also PASS at `--mult 3,5,5,3` (dim 16, `--maxr 4`). Checks: 1a–1e toy space (as 010); 2a–2d generator (symmetry, saturation, non-hollowness, $(S,S)=0$ ranks 2–6, break-on-purpose); 3a–3e the $W$/$\tilde\Delta$ dictionary (independent of the antibracket implementation: 010's commutator/Jacobiator formulas), including the negative control 3c; 4a–4b output symmetry (1.7) and $p_{k,1}=\ell_k$; R16 legs on generic and on $L_\infty$ data (Lemma A, Lemma B1 with unique dressings, 6b, 6b′ diagnostic, even-sector identity, assembly $=0$ on $L_\infty$ data / $\neq0$ on generic data). Falsifiability: 2d, 3c and the generic-data assembly are the legs that fail on wrong input. **Not verified:** (0.1) in CFL's hat form, any $(k,\ell,0)$ with $k+\ell\ge5$, the mixed-configuration identity beyond $(2,2)$, the odd-sector $(2,2,0)$ with dressed coderivation term, cyclicity of the brace-$L_\infty$ of §2.B — all 7b–7d. The $d=\tfrac12$ reconciliation (1.12) is a two-line degree count, checked by hand only.

## §3 Part A — proof of Statement A for general $(k,\ell,0)$ (sub-task 7b)

- Brief: `agent/briefs/active-computator-linfty-ibl-7b-partA-20260908T072100.md`
- Restated literal task: prove, for every $(k,\ell)$ and on the full graded space, that the genus-zero CFL relations (0.1) hold for the $\Delta$-raised products $p_{k,\ell}=p_{k,\ell,0}$ of a cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra, with every sign accounted for once; or exhibit the obstruction. Structure: 3.1 $Q$-move / multi-$\Delta$ generalisation; 3.2 regrouping; 3.3 assembly; 3.4 graded mechanism of 010's inertness; 3.5 R16 recovered; 3.6 genus $\ge1$ exclusion.
- Objective served: the paper's Theorem A (cyclic $L_\infty$ ⇒ genus-zero IBL$_\infty$ of the $\Delta$-raised products), in the physical grading, and the hand re-derivation. Per §0 (0.5) and §2.A, the crux is the **mixed-configuration lemma** for general $(k,\ell)$; everything else is the CFL Prop. 7.2 dictionary (cited) plus (0.4).
- Conventions: §1 verbatim; raising with $\tilde\Delta^{ab}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}\Delta^{ab}$ (W3.2) so that the first-strand weight $W$ never appears; $Q'=WQ$; the generating-function form (0.3)/(1.5) is the object proven.

**Re-targeting (2026-09-08, after 7c).** 7c (§4.2–§4.5, W4–W7) showed Statement A false in general (equivalent to $P_{k,\ell}=0$, seen to fail at $(2,2),(2,3),(3,2)$) and true for $\min(k,\ell)=1$, with residual $R=\tfrac12M$, and the non-augmented relation $B^{\tilde\Delta}_{k,\ell}=0$ true everywhere. §3 therefore proves exactly that; the original headings (Q-move generalisation, regrouping, assembly) are replaced by the list below — the CFL dictionary (Prop. 7.2) is cited, not re-proven, and the object handled throughout is the generating-function form (0.3)/(4.2) with $D=\tilde\Delta$.

### §3.1 The decomposition (0.5): configuration symmetry $C(A|B)=C(B|A)$ and the $\min(k,\ell)=1$ corollary

\subsection*{Setup}
Conventions of §1; the bracket is the one-sided weighted contraction (4.2) with $D=\tilde\Delta$, written $\{F,G\}:=\partial_{\Psi^a}F\,\tilde\Delta^{ab}\,\partial_{\chi^b}G$ (left derivatives, $\Psi,\chi$ Grassmann-even superfields, $u:=\Psi+\chi$). $H_{k,\ell}:=\frac1{k!\ell!}V_{k+\ell}(\Psi^k,\chi^\ell)$ for all $k,\ell\ge0$ with $k+\ell\ge2$; $S(u)=\sum_{k,\ell\ge0}H_{k,\ell}$, $H_0=\sum_{k,\ell\ge1}H_{k,\ell}$ (0.2). $T(i,j|m,n)$ as in (W8.1). Bidegree-$(k,\ell)$ projections are written $|_{(k,\ell)}$.

\subsection*{Derivation}
$$\partial_{\Psi^a}H_{k,\ell}=\frac{1}{(k-1)!\,\ell!}V_{k+\ell}(e_a,\Psi^{k-1},\chi^\ell),\qquad \partial_{\chi^b}H_{k,\ell}=\frac{1}{k!\,(\ell-1)!}V_{k+\ell}(e_b,\Psi^{k},\chi^{\ell-1}).\tag{3.1}$$
$$\{H_{k_1,\ell_1},H_{k_2,\ell_2}\}=T(k_1-1,\ell_1\,|\,k_2,\ell_2-1)\qquad(k_1,\ell_2\ge1;\ =0\ \text{otherwise}).\tag{3.2}$$
$$T(m,n\,|\,i,j)=\frac{1}{i!j!m!n!}\,V(e_a,\Psi^m,\chi^n)\,\tilde\Delta^{ab}\,V(e_b,\Psi^i,\chi^j)=\frac{1}{i!j!m!n!}\,V(e_b,\Psi^i,\chi^j)\,\tilde\Delta^{ba}\,V(e_a,\Psi^m,\chi^n)\cdot(-1)^{\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_b}=T(i,j\,|\,m,n).\tag{3.3}$$
$$B_{k,\ell}:=\{S(u),S(u)\}\big|_{(k,\ell)}=\sum_{\substack{i+m=k,\ j+n=\ell\\ (i,j),(m,n)\ne(0,0)}}T(i,j|m,n),\qquad R_{k,\ell}:=\{H_0,H_0\}\big|_{(k,\ell)}=\sum_{\substack{i+m=k,\ j+n=\ell\\ j\ge1,\ m\ge1}}T(i,j|m,n).\tag{3.4}$$
$$M_{k,\ell}:=\sum_{\substack{i+m=k,\ j+n=\ell\\ i,j,m,n\ge1}}T(i,j|m,n),\qquad P_{k,\ell}:=R_{k,\ell}-M_{k,\ell}.\tag{3.5}$$
$$B_{k,\ell}=2P_{k,\ell}+M_{k,\ell}\qquad(k,\ell\ge1),\tag{3.6}$$
$$R_{k,\ell}=\tfrac12B_{k,\ell}+\tfrac12M_{k,\ell}\qquad(k,\ell\ge1).\tag{3.7}$$
$$\{S(u),S(u)\}=\partial_{\Psi^a}[S(\Psi+\chi)]\,\tilde\Delta^{ab}\,\partial_{\chi^b}[S(\Psi+\chi)]=(\partial_aS)(u)\,\tilde\Delta^{ab}\,(\partial_bS)(u)=B(u,u)=(S,S)\big|_{\Psi\to u}.\tag{3.8}$$
$$\text{cyclic }L_\infty\iff(S,S)=0\iff B(u,u)=0\ \forall u\iff B_{k,\ell}=0\ \forall k,\ell\ge0\ \Longrightarrow\ R_{k,\ell}=\tfrac12M_{k,\ell}\ \forall k,\ell\ge1.\tag{3.9}$$
$$\min(k,\ell)=1\ \Longrightarrow\ M_{k,\ell}=0\ \Longrightarrow\ R_{k,\ell}=\tfrac12B_{k,\ell}=0\quad\text{(given cyclic }L_\infty).\tag{3.10}$$

\subsection*{Commentary}
(3.1): $V$ is multilinear and graded symmetric, $\Psi=\Psi^ae_a$ with $\varepsilon(\Psi^a)=\varepsilon_a$, so $V(\Psi,\ldots)=\Psi^aV(e_a,\ldots)$ with no sign (the coefficient is pulled out of the first slot past nothing), and the left derivative removes one $\Psi^a$; the $k$ equal slots give the factor $k$ that cancels one $k!$. The contracted leg is the *first* slot of each vertex — this is the slot order of (4.2) and of (1.6).
(3.2): definition (W8.1); the bracket differentiates its first argument in $\Psi$ and its second in $\chi$, so it vanishes unless $k_1\ge1$ and $\ell_2\ge1$.
(3.3): relabel $a\leftrightarrow b$; $\tilde\Delta^{ba}=\tilde\Delta^{ab}$ (graded symmetric: $\tilde\Delta^{ba}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_b}\Delta^{ba}=-(-1)^{\varepsilon_b}\Delta^{ab}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}\Delta^{ab}$ on the support $\varepsilon_b=\varepsilon_a+1$, (1.2)); the two scalar factors have parities $\varepsilon_a$ and $\varepsilon_b$ as functions of even superfields, and one of them is even on the support, so commuting them costs $(-1)^{\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_b}=+1$; $\tilde\Delta^{ab}$ is a number. This is 7c's D1 (verified $10^{-15}$ at six signatures). With the plain $\Delta$ the same steps give $T=-T^{\rm mirror}$ (D1's plain-$\Delta$ antisymmetry), which is why the plain convention has $B\equiv0$ and $M\equiv0$.
(3.4): expand $S(u)$ and $H_0$ into $H_{k,\ell}$ pieces and apply (3.2); the constraint $(i,j)\ne(0,0)\ne(m,n)$ is $V_1=0$ (no tadpole in the classical action); in $R$ both vertices automatically have rank $\ge2$.
(3.5): W8, third row; "mixed" = all four free-leg numbers $\ge1$ = §4.1's $k_1\ge2,\ell_1\ge1,k_2\ge1,\ell_2\ge2$.
(3.6): group the ordered tuples of $B_{k,\ell}$ into mirror classes $\{t,\bar t\}$ and use (3.3), $T(\bar t)=T(t)$. By W8: at $k,\ell\ge1$ a class has both members in $R$ iff it is mixed, and otherwise exactly one member in $R$ (the last row of the W8 table shows "neither" is impossible); a self-mirror class ($t=\bar t$) is mixed. Hence each pure class contributes $2T(t)$ to $B$ and $T(t)$ to $P$; each mixed class with $t\ne\bar t$ contributes $2T(t)$ to both $B$ and $M$, and a self-mirror mixed class $T(t)$ to both.
(3.7): $R=P+M$ and (3.6). This is (0.5).
(3.8): chain rule for the shift $u=\Psi+\chi$ — $\partial_{\Psi^a}$ and $\partial_{\chi^a}$ of $F(\Psi+\chi)$ are both $(\partial_aF)(u)$ — and (1.5). No polarisation is needed: as polynomials in the doubled variables, $\{S(u),S(u)\}$ *is* the antibracket $(S,S)$ evaluated at $u$.
(3.9): (1.5) for the first equivalence (standard, Zwiebach 1993 §4); $(S,S)=0$ as a polynomial in $\Psi$ ⇔ $(S,S)(u)=0$ as a polynomial in $(\Psi,\chi)$ (substitute $u=\Psi+\chi$; conversely set $\chi=0$); bidegree pieces vanish separately; then (3.7).
(3.10): W8 — a mixed tuple needs $i,m\ge1$ hence $k\ge2$, and $j,n\ge1$ hence $\ell\ge2$.

\subsection*{Result}
$$\boxed{\;R_{k,\ell}=\tfrac12B_{k,\ell}+\tfrac12M_{k,\ell}\quad(k,\ell\ge1),\qquad B_{k,\ell}=(S,S)\big|_{u=\Psi+\chi,\,(k,\ell)},\qquad M_{k,\ell}=0\ \text{for}\ \min(k,\ell)=1.\;}$$
**Corollary (Statement A at $\min(k,\ell)=1$; proven).** For a cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra, the genus-zero relation (0.3) holds on the full graded space, all input sectors, at every $(k,1)$ (where it is $\tfrac12B_{k,1}$, equivalent to the arity-$k$ relation) and every $(1,\ell)$ (the relations $\{H_{1,1}+\cdots,\cdot\}$ of R16's $(1,2,0)$ type: $\hat Q$-compatibility of $p_{1,\ell}$ plus the $p_{1,\ell'}\circ\ell_{\cdot}$ chains). For $k,\ell\ge2$, (0.3) holds iff $M_{k,\ell}=0$, and $L_\infty$ fixes $M_{k,\ell}=-2P_{k,\ell}$ instead (§3.2).

\subsection*{Verification}
(3.3), (3.7) and $B=0$ on $L_\infty$ data: 7c's checks D1, (0.5) and $B$ at six signatures ($10^{-13}$–$10^{-15}$), and the elementwise $L_\infty$-perturbed identity $R^{\rm pert}-R=\tfrac12\widetilde{(S,S)}^{\rm pert}$ (this leg perturbs $V_{k+\ell}$, which never enters a mixed configuration, so $M^{\rm pert}=M$; it tests the pure-class factor $\tfrac12$ against an independent code path, not the $R\propto M$ proportionality). (3.10): 7c's rows $(1,3)$, $(3,1)$, $(1,4)$ with the sign-flip leg failing at $7.1$, $38.2$. 7b adds check E1 (script): the W8 count — number of ordered tuples in $B$, $R$, $M$ at $(k,\ell)\le(4,4)$ equals $(k+1)(\ell+1)-2$, $k\ell$, $(k-1)(\ell-1)$ — and E2: (3.6)/(3.7) as an identity between sums of *individually computed* configurations at $(2,2)$ (both orientations computed, not inferred), with a break-on-purpose leg (drop the mirror symmetry by using $\Delta$ in one orientation).

### §3.2 The mixed sum $M_{k,\ell}$ for $k,\ell\ge2$: characterisation and generic non-vanishing (the obstruction)

\subsection*{Setup}
As §3.1. Weighted pairing $\tilde\omega(X,Y):=(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}X^c\,\omega_{cd}\,Y^d$ for superfield vectors $X=X^ce_c$, $Y=Y^de_d$ (nondegenerate, since it differs from $\omega_1(X,Y)=X^c\omega_{cd}Y^d$ by a sign per component). "Pure" configurations are those of W8 with $i=0$ (vertex 1 $=V_{j+1}(e_a,\chi^j)$, the raised $p_{1,j}$ with its input contracted) or $n=0$ (vertex 2 $=V_{m+1}(e_b,\Psi^m)$, the product $\ell_m$ with its output contracted).

\subsection*{Derivation}
$$V_{r+1}(e_a,A_1,\dots,A_r)=\omega_1\bigl(e_a,\ell_r(A_1,\dots,A_r)\bigr)=\omega_{ac}\,\ell_r(A_1,\dots,A_r)^c\qquad(\text{no sign on the support}),\tag{3.11}$$
$$V(e_a,\ldots)\,\tilde\Delta^{ab}\,V(e_b,\ldots)=\omega_{ac}X^c\,(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}\Delta^{ab}\,\omega_{bd}Y^d=(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}X^c\omega_{cd}Y^d=\tilde\omega(X,Y),\quad X=\ell(\ldots),\ Y=\ell(\ldots).\tag{3.12}$$
$$P_{k,\ell}=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\frac{1}{j!\,k!\,(\ell-j)!}\,\tilde\omega\bigl(\ell_j(\chi^j),\,\ell_{k+\ell-j}(\Psi^k,\chi^{\ell-j})\bigr)+\sum_{m=1}^{k-1}\frac{1}{(k-m)!\,\ell!\,m!}\,\tilde\omega\bigl(\ell_{k-m+\ell}(\Psi^{k-m},\chi^\ell),\,\ell_m(\Psi^m)\bigr),\tag{3.13}$$
$$M_{k,\ell}=\sum_{\substack{i+m=k,\ j+n=\ell\\ i,j,m,n\ge1}}\frac{1}{i!j!m!n!}\,\tilde\omega\bigl(\ell_{i+j}(\Psi^i,\chi^j),\,\ell_{m+n}(\Psi^m,\chi^n)\bigr),\tag{3.14}$$
$$\text{cyclic }L_\infty\ \Longrightarrow\ M_{k,\ell}=-2P_{k,\ell}\quad(k,\ell\ge1),\tag{3.15}$$
$$M_{2,2}=\tilde\omega\bigl(\ell_2(\Psi,\chi),\ell_2(\Psi,\chi)\bigr)=X_a\tilde\Delta^{ab}X_b\ \ (X_a=V_3(e_a,\Psi,\chi)),\qquad P_{2,2}=\tfrac12\tilde\omega\bigl(Q\chi,\ell_3(\Psi,\Psi,\chi)\bigr)+\tfrac12\tilde\omega\bigl(\ell_3(\Psi,\chi,\chi),Q\Psi\bigr)+\tfrac14\tilde\omega\bigl(\ell_2(\chi,\chi),\ell_2(\Psi,\Psi)\bigr).\tag{3.16}$$
$$\text{cyclic dg Lie }(\ell_{n\ge3}=0):\qquad M_{2,2}=-\tfrac12\,\tilde\omega\bigl(\ell_2(\chi,\chi),\ell_2(\Psi,\Psi)\bigr),\tag{3.17}$$
$$M_{2,2}\equiv0\iff\omega_1\bigl(\ell_2(e_\mu,e_\nu),\ell_2(e_\rho,e_\sigma)\bigr)=0\ \forall\mu,\nu,\rho,\sigma\iff\omega_1\bigl(\ell_2(\ell_2(e_\mu,e_\nu),e_\rho),e_\sigma\bigr)=0\ \forall\iff\ell_2\circ_1\ell_2=0.\tag{3.18}$$
$$\text{Example: }\mathcal H=\mathfrak g_{(1)}\oplus\mathfrak g^*_{(4)},\ \omega_1(c,c^*)=\langle c^*,c\rangle,\ S=\tfrac12\langle c^*,[c,c]\rangle,\ \ell_2=\tilde\Delta\circ V_3,\ Q=0:\quad M_{2,2}\ne0\iff[\mathfrak g,[\mathfrak g,\mathfrak g]]\ne0.\tag{3.19}$$

\subsection*{Commentary}
(3.11): (1.4) and (1.1): $\omega_1(e_a,X^ce_c)=(-1)^{\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_c}X^c\omega_{ac}$ and $\varepsilon_c=\varepsilon_a+1$ on the support makes the sign $+1$.
(3.12): $\omega_{ac}\Delta^{ab}=-\omega_{ca}\Delta^{ab}=-\delta_c{}^b$ (definition of $\Delta$, (1.2)), and $(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}=-(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}$ across the pairing; the scalar factors $X^c$, $Y^d$ are ordered as the two vertices in (W8.1). Every configuration, pure or mixed, is thus a weighted pairing of two products, each with its remaining legs filled by superfields — the bare-variable form of "$\hat p_{k_2,\ell_2}\circ_1\hat p_{k_1,\ell_1}$" with all spectators absorbed into the polynomial.
(3.13): W8 rows 2 and 3 with (3.12): the $i=0$ terms are $T(0,j|k,\ell-j)$, $j=1..\ell$ (these include $n=0$, $j=\ell$: $p_{1,\ell}\circ\ell_k$); the $n=0$, $i\ge1$ terms are $T(k-m,\ell|m,0)$, $m=1..k-1$. Count: $\ell+(k-1)=k+\ell-1=k\ell-(k-1)(\ell-1)$ ✓ (W8).
(3.14): W8 row 2 with (3.12).
(3.15): (3.6) with $B_{k,\ell}=0$ (3.9). **This is the characterisation:** on $L_\infty$ data the mixed sum is not free — it equals minus twice the pure sum, and Statement A at $(k,\ell)$ is therefore equivalent to the *additional* condition $P_{k,\ell}=0$ (equivalently $M_{k,\ell}=0$), a quadratic identity among the $\ell_n$ that is not one of the $L_\infty$ relations: the $L_\infty$ relation at bidegree $(k,\ell)$ is the single equation $2P+M=0$ and cannot separately enforce $P=0$.
(3.16): (3.13)–(3.14) at $(2,2)$; $\ell_1=Q$; the mixed term is (1.10) rewritten with (3.12) (self-mirror tuple $(1,1|1,1)$, weight 1). Its $\tilde\Delta$-contraction does not vanish by antisymmetry because $\tilde\Delta$ is graded *symmetric* — the plain-$\Delta$ version vanishes identically, and that is the version 010 effectively computed on the even sector (W6, §3.3).
(3.17): $\ell_3=0$ kills the two $Q$-terms of (3.16); (3.15).
(3.18): coefficient of $\chi^\mu\chi^\nu\Psi^\rho\Psi^\sigma$ in (3.17) is a nonzero sign times $\omega_1(\ell_2(e_\mu,e_\nu),\ell_2(e_\rho,e_\sigma))$ (graded symmetry of $\ell_2$ matches the symmetry of the monomials, and an odd component squared vanishes on both sides); cyclicity of $V_3$ moves one $\ell_2$ across the pairing; nondegeneracy of $\omega_1$. So for a cyclic dg Lie algebra Statement A at $(2,2)$ holds iff the bracket is 2-step nilpotent, $\ell_2(\ell_2(\cdot,\cdot),\cdot)=0$ — exactly the degeneracy 010 noted in its toy search ("every $Q$-compatible cyclic $\ell_2$ had $\ell_2\circ\ell_2=0$"): on a graded space whose ghost numbers occupy only $\{2,3\}$, $\ell_2\circ_1\ell_2$ vanishes by degree ($\ell_2(\mathcal H^{(3)},\mathcal H^{(2)})$ would sit at ghost 4), Jacobi is automatic for any symmetric $d_{abc}$, and $M_{2,2}=0$ trivially. The obstruction needs at least three ghost-number levels, which the generator (iv) at $(2,4,4,2)$ has.
(3.19): Chevalley–Eilenberg data in BV form: ghost-1 elements $c$ are odd, so graded symmetry of $\ell_2$ on them is antisymmetry and $\ell_2(c,c')=[c,c']$; ghost count $1+1-1=1$ ✓, $1+4-1=4$ ✓; $V_3[c^*_a,c^b,c^c]=f_{bc}{}^a$ and its graded-symmetric reorderings (even–odd swaps cost nothing) saturate at $6$; $\ell_2$ is *defined* from $V_3$ by (1.4) (with $\tilde\Delta$, W3.2), so cyclicity holds by construction, and the mixed component is the coadjoint action *up to the sign fixed by cyclicity* — writing $\ell_2(c,c^*)=+\mathrm{ad}^*_cc^*$ by hand with the physical Koszul signs is *not* cyclic (found in E3's first version: Jacobi selects $+\mathrm{ad}^*$, cyclicity $-\mathrm{ad}^*$ for that pairing sign; the BV construction resolves it). $(S,S)=0$ ⇔ Jacobi (standard); E3 checks the graded Jacobiator of $\ell'_2$ directly. $\omega_1(\ell_2(x,y),\ell_2(z,\xi))=\langle\mathrm{ad}^*_z\xi,[x,y]\rangle=-\langle\xi,[z,[x,y]]\rangle$, nonzero for some $x,y,z,\xi$ unless $[\mathfrak g,[\mathfrak g,\mathfrak g]]=0$. $\mathfrak g=\mathfrak{sl}_2$: obstruction present; $\mathfrak g$ = Heisenberg: absent. (No physical sector here — the example is for the theorem on the full graded space; it is the smallest exactly solvable instance.)

\subsection*{Result}
**Proposition 3.2 (obstruction; proven).** For every cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra and every $k,\ell\ge2$, $M_{k,\ell}=-2P_{k,\ell}$ with $P_{k,\ell}$ the explicit sum (3.13) of $k+\ell-1$ weighted pairings; Statement A at $(k,\ell)$ ⇔ $P_{k,\ell}=0$, a condition independent of the $L_\infty$ relations. At $(2,2)$ on a cyclic dg Lie algebra it is $\ell_2\circ_1\ell_2=0$ (3.18); the Chevalley–Eilenberg algebra of $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ (3.19) violates it. The generator (iv) violates it at $(2,2)$, $(2,3)$, $(3,2)$ (7c). Hence **Statement A as stated in §0 is false in general** — the $\Delta$-raised products with $k,\ell\ge1$ alone do not form a genus-zero IBL$_\infty$ algebra — and it is false already at the first signature with a mixed configuration.

\subsection*{Verification}
7c: $M_{k,\ell}=-2P_{k,\ell}$ is the identity $R=\tfrac12M$ of the table at $(2,2),(2,3),(3,2)$ on all sectors; $M\ne0$ at one generic point (norms 40.6, 256, 271). 7b adds (script, checks E2–E4): E2 — (3.12)/(3.16): the three pure configurations of $(2,2)$ and the mixed one computed by `antibracket` on the doubled space agree elementwise with the weighted pairings of (3.16) computed *directly from the products* $\ell_2,\ell_3,Q$ (independent code path, no doubled space), on all sectors; E3 — (3.18)–(3.19): the tensor $\omega_1(\ell_2(e_\mu,e_\nu),\ell_2(e_\rho,e_\sigma))$ is nonzero for $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ and zero for the 3-dimensional Heisenberg algebra, both built as $\mathfrak g_{(1)}\oplus\mathfrak g^*_{(4)}$ with the Jacobi and cyclicity checks passing (falsifiable: a wrong structure constant fails Jacobi); E4 — break-on-purpose: replacing $\tilde\Delta$ by $\Delta$ in (3.12) makes E2 fail.

### §3.3 The hat-form (0.1) at $(2,2)$ with a derived sign rule, versus 010's four-term relation and versus (0.3): adjudication of R16

\subsection*{Setup}
Three objects are compared at $(2,2)$ on the physical (even-input) sector: (a) the CFL hat-form (0.1), $\hat p_{1,1}\circ p_{2,2}+p_{2,2}\circ\hat p_{1,1}+p_{1,2}\circ p_{2,1}+\hat p_{2,1}\circ_1\hat p_{1,2}=0$ with the composition $\circ_1$ *defined* in bare variables by (1.9) and the sign rule R1 of §1.3; (b) 010's four-term relation $\hat Qm_2-m_2\hat Q-p\circ\ell_2+\hat\ell_2\circ_1\hat p=0$ with its fitted dressings; (c) the generating-function bracket (0.3), $R_{2,2}=A_1+A_2+A_3+A_4$ with $A_1=T(0,1|2,1)$, $A_2=T(1,2|1,0)$, $A_3=T(0,2|2,0)$, $A_4=T(1,1|1,1)$ (W8). Numbers: generator (iv), seed 20260908, toy $(2,4,4,2)$, `--maxr 4`; W9 below.

\subsection*{Derivation}
$$\hat p_{k_2,\ell_2}\circ_1\hat p_{k_1,\ell_1}\ \xleftrightarrow{\ \text{CFL (7.4), }p_\gamma\to\hbar\partial_{q_\gamma}\ }\ \bigl[\overrightarrow{H_{k_2,\ell_2}}\circ\overrightarrow{H_{k_1,\ell_1}}\bigr]_{\text{one contraction}}=\overrightarrow{\{H_{k_2,\ell_2},H_{k_1,\ell_1}\}}\qquad(\text{weight }1,\ \text{no symmetry factor}).\tag{3.20}$$
$$\text{(a)}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \text{(c)}:\qquad\text{CFL }(2,2,0)\ \Longleftrightarrow\ R_{2,2}=A_1+A_2+A_3+A_4=0\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \bigl(\text{given }L_\infty\bigr)\ M_{2,2}=A_4=0.\tag{3.21}$$
$$\text{sign rule for (a) in bare variables, derived: each term is }\tilde\omega(\ell_\bullet(\ldots),\ell_\bullet(\ldots))\text{ of (3.12) with all four legs raised by }\tilde\Delta^{\otimes2}\text{ — uniform, relative signs all }+.\tag{3.22}$$
$$\text{(b) on the generator, }\ell'_n=\tilde\Delta V_{n+1},\ Q'=WQ,\ m_2=\Delta\circ\ell'_3,\ p=\Delta\circ\ell'_2\ (\text{7a's }L_\infty\text{ run}):\quad \Pi_{\bar\varepsilon}\bigl[\hat Qm_2-m_2\hat Q\bigr]_{\rm even}=\Pi_{\bar\varepsilon}[p\circ\ell_2]_{\rm even}=\Pi_{\bar\varepsilon}[W\!\cdot\!Y]_{\rm even}=0\ (10^{-15}),\tag{3.23}$$
$$[\hat Qm_2-m_2\hat Q]_{\rm even},\ [p\circ\ell_2]_{\rm even},\ [W\!\cdot\!Y]_{\rm even}\ \text{are exactly antisymmetric in }\bar\varepsilon\ (\text{anti-swap defect }10^{-15},\ \text{sym. defect }O(40)),\ \text{output ghost sums }\{5,7\}.\tag{3.24}$$
$$\text{uniform raising }\tilde\Delta^{\otimes2}:\quad p^{\tilde\Delta}\circ\ell'_2\big|_{\rm even}=\pm\,\widehat{A_3}\big|_{\rm even}\ (10^{-15};\ \text{odd input sectors differ by the R1 dressings (1.8), }37.2),\qquad\bigl|\Pi_{\bar\varepsilon}[p^{\tilde\Delta}\circ\ell'_2]_{\rm even}\bigr|=18.6,\qquad\bigl|\Pi_{\bar\varepsilon}[\widehat{A_1+A_2+A_3}]_{\rm even}\bigr|=|P_{2,2}|_{\rm even}=20.3,\tag{3.25}$$
$$\text{010's original convention (plain }\Delta\text{ products, generic data): }\ |\Pi_{\bar\varepsilon}[\hat Qm_2-m_2\hat Q]_{\rm even}|=4.5,\ |\Pi_{\bar\varepsilon}[p\circ\ell_2]_{\rm even}|=10.2\ (\text{non-hollow}),\ \text{but }[Q,\ell_3]+\ell_2\circ_1\ell_2\ne0\ \text{for plain products of a cyclic }L_\infty\ (\text{7a check 3c}).\tag{3.26}$$

\subsection*{Commentary}
(3.20): CFL Prop. 7.2, cited: $\hat p_{k,\ell}$ *is* the differential operator $\overrightarrow{H_{k,\ell}}$; the composition of two such operators is the $\star$-product, whose single-contraction part is the one-sided bracket. So the hat-composition $\hat p_{k_2,\ell_2}\circ_1\hat p_{k_1,\ell_1}$ corresponds to the ordered configuration $\{H_{k_2,\ell_2},H_{k_1,\ell_1}\}$ with weight exactly 1 — spectators are the untouched variables of the polynomial. This holds in any grading in which the Weyl variables (anti)commute consistently; in the physical grading that is the odd bracket (1.5) (§1.4, $d=\tfrac12$).
(3.21): (3.20) applied to the four terms; (3.7)/(3.9). The CFL $(2,2,0)$ relation is the statement $R_{2,2}=0$, nothing else; it is false on the generator ($R_{2,2}|_{\rm even}=20.3=\tfrac12|M|$, 7c).
(3.22): this is the "derived sign rule": by (3.12) every configuration is a weighted pairing of two products with superfields in the remaining slots, and the *only* consistent bare-variable realisation of CFL's uniform-sign relation is the uniform $\tilde\Delta$ raising of every output leg (the $\tilde\Delta$ is forced by (1.5): with plain $\Delta$ the bracket of two even functionals vanishes identically, D1). R1's dressings (1.8) are what one gets when the raised legs are moved to non-leftmost slots; they never change the relative weight of a configuration. Hence there is no sign choice in bare variables under which (0.1) becomes a different linear combination of the four configurations than $R_{2,2}$ — in particular none under which the mixed term enters with weight $\tfrac12$ (which would make it $\tfrac12B$, the true relation).
(3.23)–(3.24) (W9): on the physical sector the two outputs of every term of the $(2,2,0)$ relation have opposite parity (their ghost numbers sum to $10-4+1=7$ for the $Q$-terms and $10-3=7$ for the $\ell_2$-chains, or $5$ from the other saturating components — always odd). A raising that is weighted on exactly one of the two legs ($\Delta\otimes\tilde\Delta$: 010's $m_2=\Delta\circ\ell_3$ with $\ell_3=\tilde\Delta V_4$, and likewise $p$) differs from the uniform $\tilde\Delta^{\otimes2}$ raising by $(-1)^{\varepsilon_{a_1}}$, which takes opposite values on the two components related by the output swap; the tensor is therefore *antisymmetric* in the shifted parity precisely where the uniform one is symmetric, and the $E_2$ projector $\Pi_{\bar\varepsilon}$ (010's `Pout`, symmetric in $\bar\varepsilon$) annihilates it. The same happens to $\hat Qm_2$ through the dressing `sh=0` that 7a's fit selected (with the weighted $Q'$ the correct Koszul sign for passing an output is the other one; `sh=1` gives a $\bar\varepsilon$-symmetric tensor, W9). **Consequence: 7a's "R16 end to end on a genuine cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra" (W3.3, §4.4) is hollow: both sides of its even-sector identity are zero after projection, and the non-hollowness guard used the unprojected norm ($|\hat Qm_2-m_2\hat Q|=24.4$).** The 6b "inertness" of $W\!\cdot\!Y$ in that run is the same antisymmetry, not a cancellation.
(3.25): with the uniform raising the objects are $\bar\varepsilon$-symmetric, non-hollow, and equal to the configurations of (c) exactly (the $p\circ\ell_2$ term matches $A_3$ to $10^{-15}$ after raising; the $Q$-terms are the raised $A_1,A_2$ once $\hat Q$ carries the $\bar\varepsilon$ Koszul sign). Their sum on the physical sector is $P_{2,2}=-\tfrac12M_{2,2}\ne0$: **the three-term identity $\hat Qm_2-m_2\hat Q-p\circ\ell_2=0$ that R16 states for the physical sector is false on a genuine cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra** (value 20.3 on the generator, on inputs at ghost $(2,2)$ as well — check E5).
(3.26): 010's original run (generic data, plain products, plain $Q$) is non-hollow, and its Lemma A/B1 are correct raw identities *in that convention*. But its conclusion "$=0$ by Jacobi" assumes that the relation tensor $[Q,\ell_3]+\ell_2\circ_1\ell_2$ with 010's Koszul signs and plain products vanishes for a cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra; 7a's check 3c shows it does not (residual 19.7): the products for which 010's operator relations hold are the weighted ones, and for those (3.23)–(3.25) apply. 010 never evaluated the assembly on an $L_\infty$ instance (its toy search found only hollow $\ell_2$, the degeneracy explained in §3.2 (3.18)).

\subsection*{Result — adjudication of R16}
(a)$\Leftrightarrow$(c) exactly (3.21), with the derived uniform sign rule (3.22); (b) is not a consistent bare-variable form of either: in 010's original convention it is a correct identity between raw tensors whose "$=0$" step is not the cyclic $L_\infty$ relation, and in 7a's weighted convention it is $0=0$ on the physical sector. **R16 is wrong at $(2,2,0)$, not merely narrower:** for a cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra the $(2,2,0)$ relation among the $\Delta$-raised products $\{p_{k,\ell}\}_{k,\ell\ge1}$ fails on the physical sector with residual $\tfrac12M_{2,2}=\tfrac12\tilde\omega(\ell_2(\Psi,\chi),\ell_2(\Psi,\chi))\ne0$, and the three-term reduction it relies on ("the fourth term is inert") is a projection artefact. What survives of R16: the $(1,2,0)$ relation $\Leftrightarrow[Q,\ell_2]=0$ (a $\min(k,\ell)=1$ case, §3.1, proven); the structural byproduct 1 (outputs $\bar\varepsilon$-symmetric, (1.7), proven in §1.3); Lemma A as a raw identity (a form of (1.3)); and the *non-augmented* $(2,2)$ relation $B_{2,2}=2(A_1+A_2+A_3)+A_4=0$, which is the polarised $L_\infty$ relation and holds on all sectors. R6 (the older $(2,2,0)$ structure-equation row) inherits the same correction. **Wording for 7d:** replace "the HS products satisfy the genus-zero IBL relations … (2,2,0) holds on the physical sector" by "the $\Delta$-raised products satisfy the genus-zero relations at $\min(k,\ell)=1$ only; at $(2,2,0)$, false in general (counterexample at $(2,2)$); at every $(k,\ell)\ge(2,2)$ equivalent to the extra condition $P_{k,\ell}=0$, seen to fail at $(2,2),(2,3),(3,2)$ (026 §3.2); the family closes only when extended by $p_{k,0}=V_k$ and $p_{0,\ell}=\tilde\Delta^{\otimes\ell}V_\ell$ (non-augmented, 026 §3.4)".

\subsection*{Verification}
W9 numbers (this sub-task, exploration script, seed 20260908): (3.23)–(3.26) as quoted. Script checks (E5–E7, added by 7b): E5 — $|P_{2,2}|$ restricted to inputs at ghost $(2,2)$ is $O(10)$ on the generator (the physical-sector failure is not a ghost-4 artefact); E6 — 010's $\hat Qm_2-m_2\hat Q$, $p\circ\ell_2$, $W\!\cdot\!Y$ in 7a's weighted convention are $\bar\varepsilon$-antisymmetric on the even sector and vanish after projection (the hollowness diagnosis, falsifiable: with uniform raising the same projections are $O(20)$); E7 — $p^{\tilde\Delta}\circ\ell'_2$ equals the raised $A_3$ elementwise (the map/polynomial dictionary (3.20) at one configuration, independent code paths). Not machine-checked: the exact dressing of $\hat Q$ that maps $A_1+A_2$ onto the raised $Q$-terms (the `sh=1` tensor differs from $-\widehat{A_1}\pm\widehat{A_2}$ by $12.6=|A_2|_{\rm even}$, i.e. the $Q$-on-input term needs its own Koszul sign); this does not affect the verdict, which rests on (c) and on (3.23)–(3.25).

### §3.4 The correct theorem: the non-augmented family $\{p_{k,\ell}\}_{k,\ell\ge0}$ ⇔ cyclic $L_\infty$; relation to CFL's augmentation (7.2) and to Münster–Sachs' $\mathfrak L_c$

\subsection*{Setup}
Non-augmented family: for graded-symmetric forms $V_n$, $n\ge2$, on $\mathcal H$ (no $V_1$), define for all $k,\ell\ge0$ with $k+\ell\ge2$
$$p_{k,\ell}:=\tilde\Delta^{\otimes\ell}\text{-raising of }V_{k+\ell}\text{ on its first }\ell\text{ legs (1.6 with }\tilde\Delta):\quad p_{k,0}=V_k\in\mathrm{Hom}(E_k\mathcal H,\mathbb C),\quad p_{0,\ell}=\tilde\Delta^{\otimes\ell}V_\ell\in E_\ell\mathcal H,\quad p_{k,1}=\ell_k.\tag{3.27}$$
Generating function $\mathbb H_{\rm full}(\Psi,\chi):=S(\Psi+\chi)=\sum_{k,\ell\ge0}H_{k,\ell}$ (all bidegrees, (3.4)); CFL's Weyl variables $p_\gamma\leftrightarrow\Psi$ (inputs), $q_\gamma\leftrightarrow\chi$ (outputs), $\hbar$ odd ($d=\tfrac12$, §1.4).

\subsection*{Derivation}
$$\text{Theorem A}'\ (\text{proven}):\qquad \{\mathbb H_{\rm full},\mathbb H_{\rm full}\}=0\ \Longleftrightarrow\ B_{k,\ell}=0\ \ \forall k,\ell\ge0\ \Longleftrightarrow\ (S,S)=0\ \Longleftrightarrow\ (\mathcal H,\omega_1,\{\ell_n\})\ \text{cyclic }L_\infty.\tag{3.28}$$
$$\text{In configurations: }B_{k,\ell}=\sum_{\substack{i+m=k,\ j+n=\ell\\(i,j),(m,n)\ne(0,0)}}T(i,j|m,n)=\sum_{\substack{k_1+k_2=k+1\\ \ell_1+\ell_2=\ell+1\\ k_1,\ell_2\ge1;\ \ell_1,k_2\ge0}}\hat p_{k_2,\ell_2}\circ_1\hat p_{k_1,\ell_1}\Big|_{E_k}\ \text{(each ordered composition once)}.\tag{3.29}$$
$$\text{Augmentation (CFL (7.2)): }\mathbb H_{\rm full}\big|_{\Psi=0}=S(\chi)=\sum_{\ell\ge2}H_{0,\ell}\ne0,\qquad \mathbb H_{\rm full}\big|_{\chi=0}=S(\Psi)=\sum_{k\ge2}H_{k,0}\ne0.\tag{3.30}$$
$$H_0=\mathbb H_{\rm full}-S(\Psi)-S(\chi)\ \Longrightarrow\ \{H_0,H_0\}=\{S(u),S(u)\}-\{S(u),S(\chi)\}-\{S(\Psi),S(u)\}+\{S(\Psi),S(\chi)\}\ \ (0.4),\qquad \{H_0,H_0\}\big|_{(k,\ell)}=-P_{k,\ell}=\tfrac12M_{k,\ell}\ \ (\text{given }L_\infty).\tag{3.31}$$
$$\text{MS: }\mathfrak L_c=\sum_g\hbar^gL^g+\hbar\,\Omega_c^{-1},\quad L^0=\widehat{\textstyle\sum_n\ell_n}\ \leftrightarrow\ \sum_{n\ge1}H_{1,n}=\partial_aS(\chi)\,\Psi^a\ (\text{linear in }\Psi),\qquad \Omega_c^{-1}=\widehat{\omega_c^{-1}}\ \leftrightarrow\ \tfrac12\tilde\omega^{-1}(\chi,\chi)\ (k=0,\ \ell=2).\tag{3.32}$$
$$\mathbb H_{\rm full}=\underbrace{S(\chi)}_{k=0}+\underbrace{\partial_aS(\chi)\Psi^a}_{\text{MS's }L^0}+\underbrace{\sum_{k\ge2}\frac1{k!}\partial_{a_1}\!\cdots\partial_{a_k}S(\chi)\,\Psi^{a_1}\!\cdots\Psi^{a_k}}_{\text{multi-input }p_{k,\ell},\ k\ge2}\qquad(\text{Taylor expansion in }\Psi).\tag{3.33}$$

\subsection*{Commentary}
(3.28): (3.8)–(3.9). Both directions are one line: the polarised master equation *is* the bracket of $S(\Psi+\chi)$ with itself, and its bidegree pieces are the non-augmented relations. Nothing beyond (1.5) is used; in particular no property of the mixed configurations, which now enter with the same weight as everything else.
(3.29): (3.4) in CFL's labels via (3.20) (Prop. 7.2's dictionary, cited); the sum is CFL (2.4) at $g=0$ *extended to* $\ell_1=0$ (zero-output factor $p_{k_1,0}=V_{k_1}$, which "eats" one output of $p_{k_2,\ell_2}$ and returns a scalar times the rest) and $k_2=0$ (zero-input factor $p_{0,\ell_2}$, an element of $E_{\ell_2}\mathcal H$ one of whose legs is fed into $p_{k_1,\ell_1}$). This is precisely the structure CFL admit in §8 (filtered IBL$_\infty$ algebras on the completed symmetric algebra $\hat EC=\prod_{k\ge0}\hat E_kC$ *with constants*): "in contrast to the unfiltered case, we allow $k=0$ and $\ell=0$" (cache lines 1395–1406, $\hat{\mathfrak p}=\sum_{k,\ell,g\ge0}\hat{\mathfrak p}_{k,\ell,g}\hbar^{k+g-1}$). **So the non-augmented family satisfies the genus-zero IBL relations in generating-function form (polarised master equation), of the non-augmented type CFL admit in §8; the degree bookkeeping requires $d=\tfrac12$, outside CFL's integer-$d$ hypotheses, and the slot symmetries need the per-slot dictionary of §1.2 (iii); it is not an IBL$_\infty$ algebra in the Def. 2.3 sense ($k,\ell\ge1$).** The relation to CFL's degree bookkeeping and the filtration condition (8.1) ($\|p_{k,\ell,g}\|\ge\gamma\chi_{k,\ell,g}$) is not needed at genus zero over a finite-dimensional $\mathcal H$ (CFL Remark 13.2); for the string field the formal $\Delta$-contractions are on the same footing as R1–R15.
(3.30): the two augmentation conditions fail by exactly the pure-input functionals and the pure-output elements; CFL Remark 7.1 ("the second can be arranged if the theory admits an augmentation") — here there is no augmentation because $S(\Psi)$ and $S(\chi)$ are the same nonzero function. Statement A of §0 was the assertion that dropping them is harmless; (3.31) is the exact price: the bracket of the augmented Hamiltonian is minus the pure sum, i.e. half the mixed sum (§3.1–§3.2), and vanishes only where no mixed configuration exists.
(3.32): 1208.5626 eq. (5), cache lines 179–190: $L^g=\hat l^g\in\mathrm{Coder}^{\rm cycl}(SA_c)$ first-order (single output), $\Omega_c^{-1}=\widehat{\omega_c^{-1}}\in\mathrm{Coder}^2(SA_c)$ second-order — a component with **zero inputs and two outputs** (it acts on $1\in A_c^{\wedge0}\subset SA_c$ by inserting $\omega_c^{-1}$); MS therefore *do* include a zero-input term, but only the quadratic bivector at order $\hbar$, and **no zero-output components** and no multi-input $p_{k\ge2,\ell}$. Their $\mathfrak L_c^2=0$ at genus zero is $L^0L^0=0$ ($L_\infty$) and $[L^0,\Omega^{-1}_c]=0$ (invariance of $\omega_c^{-1}$, our (1.3) and its $\ell_n$ analogues, the $\hbar^1$ term together with $[L^0,L^1]$) — a *different* IBL$_\infty$ packaging of the same cyclic $L_\infty$ data, augmented in the $\ell$ direction ($\mathbb H_{\rm MS}|_{q=0}=0$: every term has $\ge1$ output) but not in the $k$ direction ($\mathbb H_{\rm MS}|_{p=0}=\hbar\,\tfrac12\omega^{-1}(q,q)\ne0$).
(3.33): the $\Delta$-raised family contains MS's $L^0$ as its linear-in-$\Psi$ slice, plus the zero-input pieces $S(\chi)$ (which MS replace by the bivector) and the multi-input pieces (which MS do not have). It is the Taylor expansion of the action about a *second* field $\chi$: $p_{k,\ell}$ is the $k$-th derivative of the $\ell$-th-order vertex, "the $k$-point function of the background $\chi$" — the natural object for expanding SFT around a background, which is why the non-augmented relation is just $(S,S)=0$ at the shifted point.

\subsection*{Result}
**Theorem A′ (proven; replaces Statement A).** Let $\{V_n\}_{n\ge2}$ be graded-symmetric forms on $(\mathcal H,\omega_1)$ and $\{p_{k,\ell}\}_{k,\ell\ge0,\,k+\ell\ge2}$ the $\tilde\Delta$-raised family (3.27). Then the genus-zero relations (3.29) hold for all $(k,\ell)$ $\Longleftrightarrow$ $\{\ell_n=p_{n,1}\}$ is a cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra with cyclic forms $V_n$. The family satisfies the genus-zero IBL relations in generating-function form (polarised master equation), of the non-augmented type CFL admit in §8; the degree bookkeeping requires $d=\tfrac12$, outside CFL's integer-$d$ hypotheses, and the slot symmetries need the per-slot dictionary of §1.2 (iii); not in the Def. 2.3 sense. **In which sense "cyclic $L_\infty$ ⇒ IBL$_\infty$ of the $\Delta$-raised products" survives:** (i) *non-augmented / curved*: fully, as Theorem A′, but with no content beyond $(S,S)=0$ polarised; (ii) *augmented (Def. 2.3, $k,\ell\ge1$)*: only for the signatures $\min(k,\ell)=1$ — the $L_\infty$ relations and the $\hat Q$-compatibility chains of the $p_{1,\ell}$ (§3.1); for $k,\ell\ge2$ it fails by $\tfrac12M_{k,\ell}$ (§3.2); (iii) *MC-twisted*: not pursued here — expanding around a solution $\Psi_0$ of the equations of motion changes $S$ but not the structure of (3.30) ($S(\Psi)$ and $S(\chi)$ remain), so no twist restores augmentation; a genuine augmentation would need $S(\chi)=0$ identically (open, likely impossible without $S=0$). The MS loop algebra is the other genus-zero packaging (single-output vertices plus the bivector), augmented in outputs, not in inputs; the two packagings share the $L^0$ slice.

\subsection*{Verification}
(3.28): 7c's $B^{\tilde\Delta}_{k,\ell}=0$ at six signatures on the generator, D0 against `ss_rank` on perturbed data (non-vanishing, $10^{-14}$), and the elementwise $\tfrac12$ identity of the perturbed leg. (3.31): the table's $R=\tfrac12M$ rows. (3.30), (3.32)–(3.33): definitions and cache citations, no machine check applicable beyond E1 (W8 counting, script).
