R6 (the earlier $(2,2,0)$ structure-equation row, "$m_2$ is $Q$-exact") should be re-examined by 7d on the same basis: its four-term equation is (3.21) and does not hold.

### §3.6 Genus $\ge1$ relations are outside the theorem; consistency of the genus-zero truncation

CFL (2.4) at genus $g$ contains $s=1,\dots,g+1$ contractions between the two factors and the genus labels $g_1+g_2=g+1-s$; at $g=0$ only $s=1$, $g_1=g_2=0$ survive, so the genus-zero relations close on $\{p_{k,\ell,0}\}$ alone — the $g$-grading is a filtration by the power of $\hbar$ ($\hbar^{k+g-1}$ in CFL's counting, $\hbar^{\ell+g-1}$ in MS's), and Theorem A′ is its lowest term. In generating-function form: $\mathbb H\star\mathbb H$ expanded in the number of contractions, $\mathbb H\star\mathbb H=\hbar\{\mathbb H,\mathbb H\}+\hbar^2(\text{two contractions between the same two vertices})+\cdots$; the two-contraction term between $V_A$ and $V_B$ is the genus-one gluing $s=2$, and the self-contraction $\tfrac12\tilde\Delta^{ab}\partial_a\partial_b\mathbb H$ (the BV Laplacian, 7c's $H_1$, MS's $[L^0,\Omega_c^{-1}]$) is $p_{k,\ell,1}$. **Not covered by Theorem A′:** (i) the $g\ge1$ relations, which need $p_{k,\ell,g\ge1}$ — for the *classical* action (no $\hbar$ corrections $V_n^{g\ge1}$) they are the statement $\Delta_{\rm BV}S=0$ plus higher, i.e. the quantum master equation, which the classical cyclic $L_\infty$ data do not satisfy in general (MS: $\hbar\Delta_cS\sim\omega_c(l^g(\omega_c^{-1}\wedge e^{c'}))$, their (B8), our review §2.A item 3); (ii) any $\hbar$-homogeneity statement (§1.4's odd $\hbar$, $d=\tfrac12$) beyond the parity bookkeeping. **Why the truncation is consistent:** by the genus count $g=g_1+g_2+s-1$, $g=0$ forces $s=1$, $g_1=g_2=0$ (as above), so the genus-zero relations involve only $\{p_{k,\ell,0}\}$ and close among themselves — Theorem A′ — irrespective of whether the higher-genus relations can be satisfied. 7c's genus leg (adding $H_1$ moves $R_{2,2}$ by $287$) confirms that the genus-one term is not $\hbar$-degenerate with the genus-zero relation; 010's "genus separation" number is superseded by it (§3.5).

## Working notes (7b)

**W8. Enumeration of the two-vertex configurations at general $(k,\ell)$.** Notation (fixes §4.1's $C_D(k_1,\ell_1|k_2,\ell_2)$ in free-leg variables). For $i,j,m,n\ge0$ define the ordered two-vertex contraction with free legs $(i\ \Psi,\ j\ \chi\,|\,m\ \Psi,\ n\ \chi)$,
$$T(i,j\,|\,m,n):=\frac{1}{i!\,j!\,m!\,n!}\;V_{i+j+1}(e_a,\Psi^i,\chi^j)\;\tilde\Delta^{ab}\;V_{m+n+1}(e_b,\Psi^m,\chi^n),\qquad \tilde\Delta^{ab}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}\Delta^{ab},\tag{W8.1}$$
a scalar polynomial of bidegree $(k,\ell)=(i+m,\,j+n)$; vertex 1 is contracted through a $\Psi$-leg (an input of $p_{k_1,\ell_1}$, $k_1=i+1$), vertex 2 through a $\chi$-leg (an output of $p_{k_2,\ell_2}$, $\ell_2=n+1$): $T(i,j|m,n)=C_{\tilde\Delta}(i+1,j\,|\,m,n+1)$ in §4.1's labels, i.e. $\{H_{i+1,j},H_{m,n+1}\}_{\tilde\Delta}$ with $H_{k,\ell}=\frac1{k!\ell!}V_{k+\ell}(\Psi^k,\chi^\ell)$ for all $k,\ell\ge0$, $k+\ell\ge2$. The mirror of $t=(i,j|m,n)$ is $\bar t:=(m,n|i,j)$ (vertex roles exchanged). Constraints at fixed $(k,\ell)$: $i+m=k$, $j+n=\ell$, and both vertices of rank $\ge2$, i.e. $(i,j)\ne(0,0)\ne(m,n)$.

| ordered tuple $t=(i,j\,|\,m,n)$ | $(k_1,\ell_1\,|\,k_2,\ell_2)$ | in $B_{k,\ell}=\{S(u),S(u)\}$ | in $R_{k,\ell}=\{H_0,H_0\}$ | type | mirror $\bar t$ in $R$? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| any, $i+m=k$, $j+n=\ell$ | $(i{+}1,j\,|\,m,n{+}1)$ | weight 1 | weight 1 iff $j\ge1$ and $m\ge1$ | — | iff $n\ge1$ and $i\ge1$ |
| $i,j,m,n\ge1$ | all four $\ge1$, $k_1,\ell_2\ge2$ | 1 | 1 | **mixed** | yes (both orientations in $R$) |
| $j\ge1,\ m\ge1$, ($i=0$ or $n=0$) | $\ell_1,k_2\ge1$; $k_1=1$ or $\ell_2=1$ | 1 | 1 | pure, in $R$ | no |
| $j=0$ or $m=0$ (with $n\ge1$, $i\ge1$) | $\ell_1=0$ ($p_{k_1,0}=V_{k_1}$) or $k_2=0$ ($p_{0,\ell_2}$) | 1 | 0 (augmented away) | pure, not in $R$ | yes |
| ($j=0$ or $m=0$) and ($n=0$ or $i=0$) | — | — | — | impossible at $k,\ell\ge1$ (forces $k=0$, $\ell=0$ or a rank-1 vertex) | — |

Counts at $k,\ell\ge1$: ordered tuples in $B$: $(k+1)(\ell+1)-2$; in $R$: $k\ell$; mixed: $(k-1)(\ell-1)$; check $(k+1)(\ell+1)-2=2\,[k\ell-(k-1)(\ell-1)]+(k-1)(\ell-1)$ ✓ (pure classes counted twice, mixed once). Self-mirror tuples ($t=\bar t$, i.e. $(i,j)=(m,n)$): exist only for $k,\ell$ both even, $(k/2,\ell/2|k/2,\ell/2)$, and are always mixed at $k,\ell\ge2$ (at $(2,2)$: $(1,1|1,1)$, the single mixed configuration). Mixed tuples require $k=i+m\ge2$ and $\ell=j+n\ge2$: none at $\min(k,\ell)=1$.

Instances: $(2,2)$ — pure classes $\{(0,1|2,1),(2,1|0,1)\}$, $\{(1,2|1,0),(1,0|1,2)\}$, $\{(0,2|2,0),(2,0|0,2)\}$ (first member in $R$: $\hat Q m_2$, $m_2\hat Q$, $p_{1,2}\circ\ell_2$), mixed $(1,1|1,1)$ ($\hat\ell_2\circ_1\hat p_{1,2}$). $(2,3)$ — mixed $(1,1|1,2)$, $(1,2|1,1)$ (mirrors). $(3,2)$ — mixed $(1,1|2,1)$, $(2,1|1,1)$ (mirrors). $(3,3)$ — mixed $(1,1|2,2),(2,2|1,1),(1,2|2,1),(2,1|1,2)$: two classes.



**W9. Targeted run for §3.3 (generator (iv), seed 20260908, toy $(2,4,4,2)$, `--maxr 4`; exploration script in the session scratchpad, to be folded into checks E5–E7).** Configurations at $(2,2)$, even-sector norms after $\tilde\Delta^{\otimes2}$ raising: $A_1=23.5$, $A_2=12.6$, $A_3=18.6$, $A_4=40.6$; $A_1+A_2+A_3+\tfrac12A_4=5\times10^{-15}$ ($\tfrac12B$); $|A_1+A_2+A_3|_{\rm even}=20.3$; no sign combination $A_1\pm A_2\pm A_3$ vanishes (norms 20.3, 40.6, 25.1, 31.1). 010's terms in 7a's weighted convention ($\ell'=\tilde\Delta V$, $Q'=WQ$, $m_2=\Delta\circ\ell'_3$, $p=\Delta\circ\ell'_2$, dressing $(0,0,-1,1,1)$): even-sector raw norms $24.4$, $18.6$, $20.3$ for $\hat Qm_2-m_2\hat Q$, $p\circ\ell_2$, $W\!\cdot\!Y$; $\bar\varepsilon$-symmetric swap defects $48.8$, $37.2$, $40.6$; $\bar\varepsilon$-*anti*symmetric defects $3.6\times10^{-15}$, $8.9\times10^{-16}$, $1.8\times10^{-15}$; output ghost sums $\{5,7\}$; projected even norms $1.8\times10^{-15}$, $4.4\times10^{-16}$, $8.9\times10^{-16}$ (all three hollow); $m_2$ itself symmetric (defect $3.6\times10^{-15}$, output ghost sums $\{4,6,8\}$). Uniform $\tilde\Delta^{\otimes2}$ raising: $p\circ\ell'_2$ projected even norm $18.6$, equals $\pm\widehat{A_3}$ to $1.8\times10^{-15}$; $\hat Qm_2-m_2\hat Q$ with `sh=1` symmetric (defect $3.6\times10^{-15}$), projected norm $24.4$, differs from $-\widehat{A_1}\pm\widehat{A_2}$ by $12.6$. Generic data, 010's plain convention: projected even norms $4.5$ ($\hat Qm_2-m_2\hat Q$), $10.2$ ($p\circ\ell_2$), $9.2$ (difference) — non-hollow.

## §4 Toy verification table for Statement A (sub-task 7c — run before 7b as the numerical exploration fixing the target of the proof)

**Restated task.** Evaluate numerically, on the generator (iv) of §1.5 (seed 20260908, toy $(2,4,4,2)$), the genus-zero relation of Statement A at the signatures $(k,\ell)\in\{(1,3),(2,2),(3,1),(1,4),(2,3),(3,2)\}$ — in the generating-function form (0.3) (bidegree $(k,\ell)$ of $\{H_0,H_0\}$, which is the form 7b was told to prove) — on the full graded space, sector by sector (input parity sectors), for both raising conventions (plain $\Delta$, BV-consistent $\tilde\Delta$), with four legs per signature: the residual itself; a sign-flip falsification leg; an $L_\infty$-perturbed leg with proportionality test; a genus-1 (self-contraction) leg. Evaluate the mixed-configuration sum of (0.5) separately. Reproduce 010's numbers. Write the "Target for 7b" paragraph.

**Motivating objective.** Fix, before any proof is attempted, *what is true*: on which sectors and in which convention (0.3) holds, and which mixed configurations survive where it fails. 7b then proves a statement known to be right instead of one guessed.

### §4.1 What is computed (definitions used by the script)

**Doubled space.** $\mathcal H_d:=\mathcal H\oplus\mathcal H$ with basis $(e_a^{\Psi},e_\alpha^{\chi})$, parities $(\varepsilon_a,\varepsilon_\alpha)$ (both superfields $\Psi,\chi$ Grassmann-even, §1.3). A polynomial in $(\Psi,\chi)$ is a graded-symmetric coefficient tensor on $\mathcal H_d$ in the script's convention $F=\frac1{r!}F_{B_1\cdots B_r}u^{B_1}\cdots u^{B_r}$; its bidegree-$(k,\ell)$ piece $F|_{(k,\ell)}$ keeps the components with exactly $k$ $\Psi$-indices. The vertices lift as $\tilde V_n[I_1..I_n]:=V_n[i_1..i_n]$ ($i=I\bmod N$), so $S(\Psi+\chi)=\sum_n\frac1{n!}\tilde V_n$ and
$$H_{k,\ell}:=\tilde V_{k+\ell}\big|_{(k,\ell)}\ (k,\ell\ge1),\qquad H_0=\sum_{k,\ell\ge1}H_{k,\ell}=S(\Psi+\chi)-S(\Psi)-S(\chi)\quad\text{(0.2)}.\tag{4.1}$$

**The bracket (0.3) as a block antibracket.** With the block bivector $\Delta_d^{AB}$ nonzero only for $A=a\in\Psi$, $B=\alpha\in\chi$, $\Delta_d^{a\alpha}=D^{a\alpha}$, the script's `antibracket` (even $F$) computes
$$\{F,G\}_D:=w_a\,\partial_{\Psi^a}F\;D^{a\alpha}\;\partial_{\chi^\alpha}G,\qquad
\text{convention }\tilde\Delta:\ w_a=(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}\ (\text{i.e. }D=\tilde\Delta,\ \text{eq. (1.5)}),\qquad
\text{convention }\Delta:\ w_a=1.\tag{4.2}$$
So the two raising conventions of §1.3/W3.4 are, in generating-function form, $D=\tilde\Delta$ vs $D=\Delta$ in (0.3). No Koszul dressing beyond the superfield bookkeeping enters; this is the binding decision of §1.3.

**Configurations.** For $k_1+k_2=k+1$, $\ell_1+\ell_2=\ell+1$ define the two-vertex contraction
$$C_D(k_1,\ell_1\,|\,k_2,\ell_2):=\bigl\{\tilde V_{k_1+\ell_1}|_{(k_1,\ell_1)},\;\tilde V_{k_2+\ell_2}|_{(k_2,\ell_2)}\bigr\}_D\quad(k_1\ge1,\ \ell_2\ge1),\tag{4.3}$$
(free legs after contraction: $(k_1-1,\ell_1\,|\,k_2,\ell_2-1)$). Then
$$R^D_{k,\ell}:=\{H_0,H_0\}_D\big|_{(k,\ell)}=\sum_{k_1,\ell_1,k_2,\ell_2\ge1}C_D,\qquad
B^D_{k,\ell}:=\{S(u),S(u)\}_D\big|_{(k,\ell)}=\sum_{k_1\ge1,\ \ell_2\ge1}C_D\ (\ell_1,k_2\ge0),\tag{4.4}$$
$$M^D_{k,\ell}:=\sum_{\text{mixed}}C_D,\quad\text{mixed}\iff k_1\ge2\ \text{and}\ \ell_2\ge2\ \ (\text{all four free-leg numbers}\ge1),\qquad P^D_{k,\ell}:=R^D_{k,\ell}-M^D_{k,\ell}.\tag{4.5}$$
$B^{\tilde\Delta}_{k,\ell}$ is the polarised cyclic $L_\infty$ relation $B(u,u)$ of (0.4)–(0.5) at bidegree $(k,\ell)$ (its unpolarised form is `ss_rank`, verified in check 2c; the doubled machinery is cross-checked against it on perturbed data, check D0). Statement (0.5) in this notation: $R^{\tilde\Delta}_{k,\ell}=\tfrac12B^{\tilde\Delta}_{k,\ell}+\tfrac12M^{\tilde\Delta}_{k,\ell}$, which holds iff $C(A|B)=C(B|A)$ configuration by configuration (the vertex-exchange symmetry of the weighted bracket for even $S$; tested as check D1). Hence on $L_\infty$ data: **Statement A at $(k,\ell)$ $\Leftrightarrow M^{\tilde\Delta}_{k,\ell}=0$**, and the table reports $R$, $M$, $P$ separately.

**Sectors.** A component of $R_{k,\ell}$ has $k$ $\Psi$-indices (inputs); its sector is the number $m\in\{0,\dots,k\}$ of odd-parity inputs ($m=0$: even sector, the physical one, $N_{\rm gh}(\Psi)=2$; all $\chi$/output indices are then fixed by saturation). Per-sector norm $=\max|R|$ over the components of that sector.

**Legs per signature and convention.** (i) $|R|$ per sector; (ii) sign-flip: $D\to D_{\rm flip}$ with the $(N_{\rm gh}\,2{\leftrightarrow}3)$ block of $D$ negated relative to the $(1{\leftrightarrow}4)$ block (a relative sign; a global sign of $D$ is invisible since $R$ is linear in $D$); (iii) $L_\infty$-perturbed: $V_{k+\ell}\to V_{k+\ell}+\delta V$ (the highest vertex of the relation), prediction $R^{\rm pert}-R^{\rm unpert}=\tfrac12\,\widetilde{(S,S)}^{\rm pert}\big|_{(k,\ell)}$ exactly, because $V_{k+\ell}$ enters only pure configurations (a mixed configuration has both vertices of rank $\le k+\ell-1$); tested as an elementwise identity, not only as non-vanishing; (iv) genus-1: $H_0\to H_0+H_1$, $H_1:=\sum_n\tfrac12\tilde\Delta^{ab}\tilde V_n[a,b,\cdots]\big|_{k,\ell\ge1}$ (the BV-Laplacian self-contraction; with the antisymmetric plain $\Delta$ the self-contraction of a graded-symmetric vertex vanishes identically, since $\Delta$ pairs opposite parities), residual at $(k,\ell)$ must move.

Columns: $|R|$ per input sector $m=0,\dots,k$ ($m=0$ even/physical); $|M|$ (mixed sum, (4.5)); $|P|$ (pure sum); $|B|$ (polarised $L_\infty$, must vanish); flip = $|R_{\rm flip}|_{\rm even}$ (leg ii); pert = $|R^{\rm pert}-R|$ and the elementwise identity $R^{\rm pert}-R=\tfrac12\widetilde{(S,S)}^{\rm pert}$ (leg iii); genus = $|R(H_0+H_1)-R(H_0)|$ (leg iv). All norms are max-abs of the coefficient tensor.

Additional exact identities per signature (both conventions): D0 — $B^{\tilde\Delta}$ on perturbed data equals the lifted `ss_rank` ($10^{-14}$, both $O(3$–$5)$); D1 — $C(A|B)=+C(B|A)$ for $\tilde\Delta$ and $C(A|B)=-C(B|A)$ for $\Delta$, configuration by configuration ($10^{-15}$); every individual configuration $O(10)$–$O(10^2)$ (non-hollow). Remarks: (a) on the even sector of a $(k,1)$ relation the contracted leg's ghost number is fixed by saturation, so the plain/tilde weight and the block flip are global signs there — hence plain-$\Delta$ $R_{3,1}|_{\rm even}=0$ and $R^{\rm flip}_{3,1}|_{\rm even}=0$, while the odd sectors separate the conventions (plain: 20, 18, 9.3); this is the mechanism by which 010's even-sector checks could not see the weight $W$ on $(k,1)$-type terms. (b) The plain-$\Delta$ "pert" column has no proportionality identity because $B^\Delta\equiv0$ (D1 antisymmetry); the residual nevertheless moves by $O(1)$. (c) The genus leg is tilde-only in the final script (the plain self-contraction vanishes identically); plain values quoted are from the earlier run with the same tensors.

**Script summary line (final run, `--maxr 5 --sigs 2:2,1:3,3:1,1:4,2:3,3:2`):** `summary: 134/134 PASS (seed 20260908)`, wall time 12.6 min (see Verification (7c)).


### §4.3 Mixed-configuration sums of (0.5)

**Which configurations are mixed.** With free legs $(k_1-1,\ell_1\,|\,k_2,\ell_2-1)$, a configuration is mixed iff $k_1\ge2$, $\ell_1\ge1$, $k_2\ge1$, $\ell_2\ge2$. Since $k_1+k_2=k+1$ and $\ell_1+\ell_2=\ell+1$, this requires $k\ge2$ and $\ell\ge2$. Consequently:

- $(1,\ell)$ and $(k,1)$: **no mixed configuration**; $R=\tfrac12B$ and Statement A in form (0.3) *holds* on all sectors in the $\tilde\Delta$ convention (table rows (1,3), (3,1), (1,4)). For $(k,1)$ this is $\tfrac12B_{k,1}$, equivalent to the arity-$k$ relation; for $(1,\ell)$ it is the statement $[\hat Q,p_{1,\ell}]=\ldots$ — the relations of R16's $(1,2,0)$ type — and it holds because the only configurations are the pure ones, which are half the polarised $L_\infty$ relation.
- $(2,2)$: one mixed configuration, $(2,1|1,2)$, i.e. $X_a\tilde\Delta^{ab}X_b$ with $X_a=V_3(e_a,\Psi,\chi)$, eq. (1.10). Norm 40.6 on the even sector, 12.6 on the doubly-odd one; not zero anywhere. Its plain-$\Delta$ counterpart vanishes identically (W4).
- $(2,3)$: two mixed configurations, $(2,2|1,2)$ [$V_4(\cdot,\Psi,\chi,\chi)\,\tilde\Delta\,V_3(\cdot,\Psi,\chi)$] and $(2,1|1,3)$ [$V_3(\cdot,\Psi,\chi)\,\tilde\Delta\,V_4(\cdot,\Psi,\chi,\chi)$]; they are mirrors of each other under vertex exchange (D1: $C(A|B)=C(B|A)$), so $M_{2,3}=2\,C(2,2|1,2)$.
- $(3,2)$: two mixed configurations, $(2,1|2,2)$ and $(3,1|1,2)$, again mirrors; $M_{3,2}=2\,C(3,1|1,2)$.

Norms of $M$ per sector are in the table; in every case with $k,\ell\ge2$ the mixed sum is nonzero on the even sector and $R=\tfrac12M$ exactly. **The object that must vanish for Statement A, and does not, is the mixed sum $M^{\tilde\Delta}_{k,\ell}$, on every input sector, already at $(2,2)$.** No projection or symmetrisation subtlety is involved: the doubled-space tensors are canonically graded symmetric in all slots (inputs in $\varepsilon$; $\chi$-legs in $\varepsilon$, equivalently outputs in $\bar\varepsilon$ after raising, (1.7)).

### §4.4 Reproduction of 010's numbers

The script's R16 block (unchanged by 7c, run in the same invocation) reproduces derivation 010 on the generator's $L_\infty$ data: Lemma A with the unique dressing $(sh_1,sh_2,s_2,s_A,w)=(0,0,-1,1,1)$; Lemma B1 with the unique signs $(s_Y,s_{T_3})=(1,-1)$ — the two machine-measured middle signs of 010's $(+1,-1,-1,+1)$; check 6b: $W\!\cdot\!Y$ raw $20.3\to$ projected $8.9\times10^{-16}$ on the even sector; 6b′: projected odd-sector norm $20.3$; the even-sector $(2,2,0)$ identity to $1.8\times10^{-15}$ with $|\hat Qm_2-m_2\hat Q|=24.4$; on generic data the assembly is $7.16\ne0$. All as in W3.3 — **but not a confirmation: 7b showed (§3.3 (3.23)–(3.24), check E6) that in this weighted convention each of the three even-sector terms is $\bar\varepsilon$-antisymmetric and projects to zero, so the "identity to $1.8\times10^{-15}$" is $0=0$; the reproduction below documents 010's numbers, it does not support R16.** **Reinterpretation (W6):** the even-sector identity of 010 is $B_{2,2}|_{\rm even}=0$ (polarised $L_\infty$) plus the vanishing of a *plain-$\Delta$* self-contraction; 010's "inert" chain term is not the mixed configuration of the $\tilde\Delta$-raised products, which is $40.6$ on the same sector. 010's $(2,2,0)$ relation with its fitted signs is therefore not an instance of (0.1)/(0.3) with a single raising convention; the "uniqueness of the middle signs" is a property of the fit within 010's ansatz and remains true, but the ansatz's third term is not the CFL composition $\hat\ell_2\circ_1\hat p_{1,2}$ with consistently $\tilde\Delta$-raised products.

### §4.5 Target for 7b

**Target for 7b.** On the toy, Statement A in the generating-function form (0.3) — the augmented genus-zero relation among the $\Delta$-raised products with $k,\ell\ge1$ only — holds exactly, on *all* input sectors, in the $\tilde\Delta$ convention, precisely for the signatures without mixed configurations, $\min(k,\ell)=1$ (verified at $(1,3)$, $(3,1)$, $(1,4)$; the $(k,1)$ case is $\tfrac12B_{k,1}$, equivalent to the arity-$k$ relation), and **fails at every $(k,\ell)\ge(2,2)$ tested, equivalent to $P_{k,\ell}=0$** (verified at $(2,2)$, $(2,3)$, $(3,2)$), on every input sector including the physical even one, with residual $R=\tfrac12M$, $M$ the mixed-configuration sum (4.5): at $(2,2)$ the single self-contraction $X_a\tilde\Delta^{ab}X_b$, $X_a=V_3(e_a,\Psi,\chi)$; at $(2,3)$ and $(3,2)$ the two mutually mirrored $V_3$–$V_4$ contractions with one input and one output free on each vertex. In the plain-$\Delta$ convention the mixed sums vanish identically (antisymmetric self-contraction), but so does the polarised bracket $B^\Delta$, so the pure sums are unconstrained by $L_\infty$ and the relation fails at every signature tested, including $\min(k,\ell)=1$. What *does* hold at every signature is the non-augmented relation $B^{\tilde\Delta}_{k,\ell}=0$, i.e. (0.1) extended by the maps $p_{k,0}=V_k$ ($E_k\mathcal H\to\mathbb C$) and the elements $p_{0,\ell}=\tilde\Delta^{\otimes\ell}V_\ell\in E_\ell\mathcal H$ with all configurations (each orientation once). 7b should therefore **not** try to prove Statement A as stated; it should prove (i) the exact identity (0.5) with the configuration symmetry $C(A|B)=C(B|A)$ for the weighted bracket (D1, verified) — one line from $(S,S)$ being symmetric — and (ii) the vanishing of $R$ at $\min(k,\ell)=1$ as a corollary; and it should **characterise the obstruction**: state $M_{k,\ell}\ne0$ as the general fact, identify whether the non-augmented family $\{p_{k,\ell}\}_{k,\ell\ge0}$ is an IBL$_\infty$ structure in a curved/non-augmented sense (CFL's augmentation (7.2) dropped; Münster–Sachs' $\mathfrak D$ without the $D^{0,\cdot}$ and $D^{\cdot,0}$ terms removed) and whether that is what the paper can state. Statement A must be restated before it is proven; the numerics fix the restatement.

### §4.6 Interpretation and scope

**What the table establishes.** On a genuine finite-dimensional cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra with $\ell_1,\dots,\ell_4$ nonzero and every configuration $O(10)$–$O(10^2)$, the generating-function relation (0.3) — which by CFL Prop. 7.2 at leading $\hbar$ order is the genus-zero IBL$_\infty$ relation (0.1) for the family $\{p_{k,\ell}\}_{k,\ell\ge1}$ — holds exactly when no mixed configuration exists ($\min(k,\ell)=1$) and fails otherwise, with the failure equal to half the mixed-configuration sum. The identity (0.5) and the configuration symmetry $C(A|B)=C(B|A)$ are exact ($10^{-15}$); the $L_\infty$-perturbed leg (perturbing $V_{k+\ell}$, which never enters a mixed configuration, so $M^{\rm pert}=M$) tests the pure-class factor $\tfrac12$ against an independent code path, not the $R\propto M$ proportionality ($R^{\rm pert}-R=\tfrac12\widetilde{(S,S)}^{\rm pert}$ elementwise); the doubled machinery is cross-checked against the independent `ss_rank` (D0). The failure is therefore not a sign or convention artefact of the toy but the statement that the mixed self-contractions of the $\tilde\Delta$-raised products do not cancel — Statement A as stated in §0 is false, and 010's even-sector "inertness" is explained (W6) as a plain-$\Delta$ contraction in disguise, not as evidence for the theorem. The plain-$\Delta$ convention is not a way out: its mixed sums vanish but its polarised bracket vanishes identically too, and the relation fails at every signature.

**What it does not establish.** (i) The hat-form (0.1) in bare variables with R1 dressings was not implemented; the equivalence (0.1)$\Leftrightarrow$(0.3) rests on CFL Prop. 7.2 in CFL's grading and on §1.3's translation — a hat-form evaluation with a *derived* (not fitted) sign rule remains to be done, and is the one place where a consistent convention could in principle differ from the superfield bracket by more than a sector-wise sign (7b, §3.4). (ii) Toy size: $\dim\mathcal H=12$, $N_{\rm gh}\in\{1,2,3,4\}$, one seed; the qualitative pattern (which signatures hold) is a combinatorial fact about configurations and does not depend on the seed, but the nonvanishing of $M$ was checked at one generic point only. (iii) Signatures with $k+\ell\ge6$ (e.g. $(3,3)$, $(2,4)$) were not run: rank-6 doubled tensors ($24^6$ entries) exceed the memory budget of the present implementation; a bidegree-block implementation would allow them. (iv) The genus-1 leg only shows that adding a self-contraction term moves the residual by $O(10^2)$; it is not a test of the genus-one relation. (v) Whether the non-augmented family $\{p_{k,\ell}\}_{k,\ell\ge0}$ (with $p_{k,0}=V_k$, $p_{0,\ell}=\tilde\Delta^{\otimes\ell}V_\ell$) is an IBL$_\infty$ algebra in a recognised sense is not decided here — only that its relation, $B^{\tilde\Delta}=0$, is the polarised $(S,S)=0$ and holds.

## Verification (7c)

`.venv/bin/python codes/verify-linfty-ibl-repackaging.py --maxr 5 --sigs 2:2,1:3,3:1,1:4,2:3,3:2` → `summary: 134/134 PASS (seed 20260908)`, exit 0, 12.6 min (class `Doubled`, `part_a_table`; the 48 checks of 7a are unchanged and included). Per signature and convention: non-hollowness of every configuration; the finding check (tilde, $k,\ell\ge2$: $R_{\rm even}\ne0$ and $R=M/2$; tilde, $\min(k,\ell)=1$: $R=0$ on all sectors; plain: $M=0$, $R=P\neq0$, with the $(k,1)$ even-sector exception by saturation); D1 (anti)symmetry of the configurations; (0.5) and $B=0$ (tilde); sign-flip leg (all sectors); $L_\infty$-perturbed leg with the elementwise $\tfrac12$ identity (tilde) or diagnostic (plain); genus-1 leg (tilde); D0 against `ss_rank`. Falsifiability: the finding checks assert nonvanishing *and* an exact identity, so a wrong bracket would fail (0.5), D0 or the proportionality leg; the flip leg breaks the $\min(k,\ell)=1$ relations ($7.1$, $38.2$); the plain convention is the built-in wrong-sign control (D1 flips sign, proportionality fails by $O(1)$). Not machine-checked: the hat-form (0.1) in bare variables; $k+\ell\ge6$; seeds other than 20260908 for §4 (the qualitative pattern is combinatorial, the magnitudes are one-point).

| convention | $\lvert R_{2,2}\rvert$ by sector $m=0,1,2$ | $\lvert M_{2,2}\rvert$ by sector | $\lvert P_{2,2}\rvert$ | $\lvert B_{2,2}\rvert$ | max single config |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| $\tilde\Delta$ | $20.3,\ 20.3,\ 6.3$ | $40.6,\ 40.6,\ 12.6$ | $20.3$ | $8.9\times10^{-15}$ | $40.6$ |
| $\Delta$ | $16.2,\ 16.2,\ 14.3$ | $1.8\times10^{-15}$ (all) | $16.2$ | $1.8\times10^{-15}$ | $23.2$ |

Exact identities found: $R^{\tilde\Delta}=\tfrac12B^{\tilde\Delta}+\tfrac12M^{\tilde\Delta}$ to $10^{-15}$ (so (0.5) and the vertex-exchange symmetry $C(A|B)=C(B|A)$ hold, W1 confirmed at $(2,2)$); $B^{\tilde\Delta}=0$ (the doubled machinery reproduces the polarised $L_\infty$ relation), and on perturbed data $B^{\tilde\Delta}$ equals the lifted `ss_rank` to $10^{-15}$ (D0: independent code path, non-vanishing, $|B_{\rm pert}|=2.5$). Falsification legs: block sign flip $\to|R|_{\rm even}=20.3$ (unchanged in magnitude, so the leg is only weakly informative here — the relation already fails); $L_\infty$-perturbed: $R^{\rm pert}-R=\tfrac12\widetilde{(S,S)}^{\rm pert}|_{(2,2)}$ elementwise to $5\times10^{-15}$ in the $\tilde\Delta$ convention (both sides $O(1)$); genus-1 term moves $R$ by $88$ ($\tilde\Delta$) / $48$ ($\Delta$).

**W5. Diagnosis (check typo → transcription → theorem).** (a) *Check typo:* excluded by D0 and by $B^{\tilde\Delta}=0$ — the same `antibracket` code, on the same lifted tensors, reproduces the $L_\infty$ relation; the only difference between $B$ and $R$ is the set of configurations summed (4.4). (b) *Transcription:* the augmentation (dropping $\ell_1=0$ and $k_2=0$ configurations) is the definition (0.2) of $H_0$ and CFL's condition (7.2); the relative weights of pure vs mixed configurations are those of W1 and are confirmed by the exact identity (0.5). (c) *Theorem:* **Statement A in the generating-function form (0.3) is false on the toy at $(2,2)$, on every input sector, in both conventions.** In the $\tilde\Delta$ convention the obstruction is $M_{2,2}\neq0$: the single mixed configuration $X_a\tilde\Delta^{ab}X_b$ of (1.10) does not vanish after symmetrisation (its even-sector norm is 40.6, twice that of $R$). In the plain-$\Delta$ convention $M\equiv0$ as (1.10) predicts, but then $B^\Delta\equiv0$ identically as well (antisymmetric contraction of two copies of an even functional), so the $L_\infty$ relation gives no constraint on the pure configurations and $R^\Delta=P^\Delta\neq0$.

**W6. Why 010 (R16) saw the even-sector $(2,2,0)$ relation hold.** *(Critic pass A correction: 010's script raises with plain $\Delta$, not $\tilde\Delta$; the mechanism below explains 7a's reimplementation, while for 010's own convention the 6b inertness is the plain-$\Delta$ mixed self-contraction of W5(c)/(1.10), as §3.5 states.)* Its assembly is $[\hat Q,m_2]-p_{1,2}\circ\ell_2=W\!\cdot\!\Delta\circ([Q,\ell_3]+\ell_2\circ_1\ell_2)$ with the chain term $W\!\cdot\!Y$ dropped by "inertness" (check 6b). In configuration language the left side is the pure configurations $(0,1|2,1)$, $(1,2|1,0)$, $(0,2|2,0)$ — each with its two orientations, i.e. weight 2 — which is $B_{2,2}-M_{2,2}$, and the right side is the $\Delta$-raised $L_\infty$ relation; so 010's identity is $B-M=B$ restricted to the even sector, i.e. *010's* mixed term vanishes there while the canonical one (4.3) does not. The reason is a hidden weight: 010's raw tensor is $Y_1{}^{ab}{}_{jk}=\Delta^{ai}\,\ell_2^{\,b}(e_m,x_k)\,\ell_2^{\,m}(e_i,x_j)$ with $\ell_2=\tilde\Delta\circ V_3$, i.e. $\tilde\Delta^{bb'}\tilde\Delta^{mm'}V_3[b',m,k]\,V_3[m',i,j]$: the *output* leg $b'$ sits in the first slot of $V_3$ and the contracted leg $m$ in the second. Bringing the contracted leg first (the slot order of the bracket (4.2), where the derivative index is the first slot) costs $(-1)^{\varepsilon_{b'}\varepsilon_m}$, and saturation ($n_{b'}+n_m+n_k=6$) makes $\varepsilon_m=\varepsilon_{b'}$ whenever the input $x_k$ is even. On the even sector therefore $(-1)^{\varepsilon_{b'}\varepsilon_m}\tilde\Delta^{mm'}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_m}\tilde\Delta^{mm'}=\Delta^{mm'}$: 010's chain term is, on the even sector, the *plain-$\Delta$* self-contraction of (1.10), which vanishes identically after symmetrisation — exactly the "inertness" of 6b, now explained — while on the odd sector it is the weighted one and does not vanish (6b′, $20.3$). So 010's dressings were fitted such that the mixed term is $\Delta$-contracted and the pure terms $\tilde\Delta$-contracted; that is not one bracket, and it is not (0.1) with a single raising convention. The even-sector identity 010 verified is the polarised $L_\infty$ relation $B_{2,2}|_{\rm even}=0$ plus a term that is zero for a reason unrelated to $L_\infty$. **R16's scope must be reread accordingly (7d ledger action).**

**W7. What does hold.** $B^{\tilde\Delta}_{k,\ell}=0$ for all $(k,\ell)$ — the non-augmented relation, in which the family of $\Delta$-raised maps is extended by the pure-input maps $p_{k,0}:=V_k\in\mathrm{Hom}(E_k\mathcal H,\mathbb C)$ ($k\ge2$; $\ell_1=0$ configurations) and the pure-output elements $p_{0,\ell}:=\tilde\Delta^{\otimes\ell}V_\ell\in E_\ell\mathcal H$ ($\ell\ge2$; $k_2=0$ configurations). With those included every configuration appears with weight 1 per orientation and the relation is the polarised $(S,S)=0$ — Münster–Sachs' $\mathfrak D^2=0$ at genus zero without augmentation. This is the candidate replacement for Statement A; see §4.5.

## §5 Part B — the converse: from the genus-zero family on $\mathcal H$ to an $L_\infty$ structure on the multi-string space $W=\bigoplus_{n\ge0}E_n\mathcal H$ (sub-task 7d) (superseded by §5′ where they differ)

**Restated task (7d).** Given the non-augmented $\tilde\Delta$-raised family $\{p_{k,\ell}\}_{k,\ell\ge0}$ of Theorem A′ (§3.4) — the hypothesis "IBL products of the $\Delta$-raised form" of the brief, read against A′ — define multi-string fields $\Psi_n\in E_n\mathcal H$ and read the $p_{k,\ell}$ as single-output products on $W=\bigoplus_nE_n\mathcal H=S\mathcal H$; decide whether the genus-zero relations (3.29) imply $L_\infty$ relations on $W$, in which precise form, with which obstruction, and with which extra structure (cyclicity); recover the $O(g_s^2)$ instance of the pedagogical note §2 presentation (b) with the R7 caveat; state known / implicit / new.

**Motivating objective.** The second item of the multi-string question: given a family of such IBL products, can we write some $L_\infty$ products (in a vector space, since now we have fields $\Psi_n$), and do the IBL relations imply $L_\infty$ on this space? This is the direction needed to write general multi-string SFT actions.

### §5.1 Candidate structures on $W$ (enumeration, ordered by cost)

Notation. $W:=S\mathcal H=\bigoplus_{n\ge0}E_n\mathcal H$ *with* the unit $1\in E_0=\mathbb C$ (CFL §8, the completed symmetric algebra with constants — forced by A′'s $p_{k,0}$ and $p_{0,\ell}$), graded-commutative product $a\cdot b$ (symmetric in $\varepsilon$; §1.2 rule (i)), multi-string field $\Phi=\sum_{n\ge1}\Psi_n$, $\Psi_1=\Psi$, $\Psi_2=\Sigma$. The family assembles into the operator of CFL Prop. 7.2 / (3.29),
$$\hat{\mathfrak p}:=\sum_{k,\ell\ge0,\ k+\ell\ge2}\hbar^{k-1}\,\hat p_{k,\ell}\ :\ W[\hbar^{\pm1}]\to W[\hbar^{\pm1}],\qquad \hat p_{k,\ell}(a_1\cdots a_n)=\sum_{|I|=k}\pm\,p_{k,\ell}(a_I)\cdot a_{I^c}\quad(a_i\in\mathcal H),\tag{5.1}$$
a differential operator of order $k$ on the algebra $W$ for each $(k,\ell)$ (CFL Rem. 2.2; MS 1208.5626 App. A.2, $\mathrm{Coder}^\ell(SA)\cong\mathrm{Hom}(SA,E_\ell A)$). $\hbar$ is odd ($d=\tfrac12$, §1.4); its powers are bookkeeping for the number of inputs and can be set to 1 in finite dimension. In generating-function form $\hat{\mathfrak p}=\mathbb H_{\rm full}(\Psi\to\hbar\tilde\Delta\partial_\chi,\chi)$ with $\chi$-multiplications to the left of the derivatives (CFL §7: coefficients polynomial in $q$, power series in $p$), $\mathbb H_{\rm full}=S(\Psi+\chi)$ (3.27)–(3.33).

Three ways of reading "$L_\infty$ products on $W$ built from the $p_{k,\ell}$" are on the table; they are *different* structures and the record treats all three.

| | structure on $W$ | products | exists iff | cyclic? | where in the project |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (α) | **Koszul-brace / higher-derived-bracket structure** of the single operator $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ w.r.t. the product of $W$ (Bering–Damgaard–Alfaro 1996, Akman 1995 — MS's refs. [26],[27]; Voronov 2005 for the general statement) | $\ell^W_n:=\Phi^n_{\hat{\mathfrak p}}$, curved ($\ell^W_0=\hat{\mathfrak p}(1)=\sum_\ell p_{0,\ell}$) | $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0$ **exactly** (all contraction numbers $s$); genus zero (A′) gives it at leading order in $\hbar$ | not for any degree-diagonal pairing (§5.5) | new here; §5.2–§5.3 |
| (β) | **HS / auxiliary-field structure**: the cyclic $L_\infty$ read off an action on a truncation $W_{\le K}$ with $\Sigma_k$ kinetic operators $p_{k,k}$ (pedagogical note presentation (b); R1, R8, R11, R26) | from $S_{\rm HS}$: $\ell^{(\beta)}_1=Q\oplus(-\tfrac{g^2}{12}m_2)$, $\ell^{(\beta)}_2(\Psi,\Psi)=g\,\ell_2+\tfrac{g^2}{6}m_2(\Psi^2)$, $\ell^{(\beta)}_2(\Sigma,\Psi)=\tfrac{g^2}{6}\ell_3(\Sigma,\Psi)$ (§5.4, (5.23)) | $L_\infty$ to $O(g^3)$ at $K=2$ (R11); exactly at all orders (R26) | w.r.t. the shifted $\omega_2^{\rm symp}$ (R7) | the paper's theory |
| (γ) | **Multiplier / cotangent structure**: Route C, $S_C=\tfrac12\omega_1(\Psi,Q\Psi)+\sum_n\tfrac{g^{n-2}}{n!}p_{n,0}(\sigma_n)+\sum_k\langle\lambda_k,\sigma_k-\sigma_{k-1}\Psi\rangle$ (R13) — fields $\sigma_n\in E_n$ paired off-diagonally with multipliers $\lambda_n\in E_n^\vee$ | uses only the pure-input components $p_{n,0}=V_n$ of A′'s family | exactly (cubic at all orders, R13) | w.r.t. the off-diagonal $\langle\lambda,\sigma\rangle$ (a cotangent pairing) | R13; §5.5 |

**Why (α) is the answer to the literal question and (β), (γ) are its physical relatives.** The multi-string question presupposes that the $p_{k,\ell}$ *themselves* become the products on $W$, with no new data. (α) is the unique such construction: on single-string inputs it returns exactly $\ell^W_n(\Psi_1,\dots,\Psi_n)=\hbar^{n-1}\sum_\ell p_{n,\ell}(\Psi_1,\dots,\Psi_n)$ — "$p_{n,\ell}$ read as an $n$-ary product with values in $E_\ell\subset W$" (Lemma B1, §5.2) — and on multi-string inputs it is determined by the algebra structure of $W$ (the coderivation extension (5.1)), with no freedom. Its $L_\infty$ relations are *equivalent* to $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0$ (Theorem B, §5.3): this is the exact form of "the IBL relations imply $L_\infty$ on $W$", and it also gives the reverse ("which $L_\infty$ structures on $W$ come from IBL data": those whose higher products are the braces of their differential, §5.6). What it does *not* give is an action: (α) is not cyclic for the natural pairings (§5.5), and its Maurer–Cartan equation $\hat{\mathfrak p}(e^\Phi)=0$ is MS's IBL$_\infty$ MC equation (1109.4101 §IV.B, cache lines 447–455), not the classical EOM of $S$. (β) and (γ) are the structures that *do* come with actions; neither is of brace type (§5.4, §5.5), and both use the family in a different way: (β) uses $p_{k,k}$ as kinetic operators of $\Sigma_k$ and the $p_{k,\ell}$ with $\Sigma$'s in the inputs as vertices; (γ) uses the scalar components $p_{n,0}=V_n$ — the components Statement A dropped and A′ restored — as the vertices $\langle V_n|\sigma_n\rangle$, and none of the $\ell\ge1$ products at all.

**Attack angles for Theorem B, ordered by expected cost.** (1) Literature reduction: the brace/derived-bracket theorem for a square-zero operator on a graded-commutative algebra — cited, then specialised. (2) Direct computation: expand $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2$ by contraction number $s$ (the $\star$-product of CFL §7) and identify $s=1$ with (3.29), $s\ge2$ with the genus $\ge1$ relations; the $\hbar$-power $\hbar^{s-1}$ separates them (Working note W10). (3) Numerical: implement $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ on $W_{\le3}$ for the toy generator, compute the braces and the $L_\infty$ residual, and check the $\hbar$-order statement and the two-contraction anomaly (Verification (7d)). All three were run; (2) is the proof, (1) its anchor, (3) its falsifiable check.

### §5.2 Lemma B1 — Koszul braces of one $\hat p_{k,\ell}$; the $p_{k,\ell}$ as single-output products on $W$

\subsection*{Setup}
For a linear map $D:W\to W$ and homogeneous $a_1,\dots,a_n\in W$ define the Koszul braces (Koszul 1985; Bering–Damgaard–Alfaro 1996 eq. (2.3); Akman 1995), with $a_\emptyset:=1$ and $\epsilon(I)$ the Koszul sign of moving the factors $a_I$ to the left of $a_{I^c}$ in $\varepsilon$-parity,
$$\Phi^n_D(a_1,\dots,a_n):=\sum_{I\subseteq\{1,\dots,n\}}(-1)^{n-|I|}\,\epsilon(I)\;D(a_I)\cdot a_{I^c},\qquad a_I:=\prod_{i\in I}a_i.\tag{5.2}$$
Fields are the even superfields $\Phi=\sum_n\Psi_n$ ($N_{\rm gh}(\Psi_n)=2n$, even), and every identity below is stated for polarised even inputs, which covers all parity sectors (the device of §3; $L_\infty$ relations on all inputs $\Leftrightarrow$ identities of even superfields with formal even coefficients).

\subsection*{Derivation}
$$\Phi^0_D=D(1),\qquad \Phi^1_D(a)=D(a)-D(1)a,\qquad \Phi^2_D(a,b)=D(ab)-D(a)b-(-1)^{\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_D}aD(b)+D(1)ab.\tag{5.3}$$
$$\sum_{n\ge0}\frac1{n!}\Phi^n_D(a^n)=e^{-a}D(e^{a})\qquad(a\ \text{even}).\tag{5.4}$$
$$D\ \text{is a differential operator of order}\le k\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \Phi^{k+1}_D=0\ (\text{Koszul's definition; then }\Phi^n_D=0\ \forall n>k).\tag{5.5}$$
$$\text{Lemma B1 (i): for }D=\hat p_{k,\ell}\text{ of (5.1) and inputs }a_i\in E_{m_i}\mathcal H:\quad \Phi^n_{\hat p_{k,\ell}}(a_1,\dots,a_n)=\sum_{\substack{J\subseteq\text{strands}(a_1\cdots a_n)\\|J|=k,\ J\cap\text{strands}(a_i)\ne\emptyset\ \forall i}}\pm\,p_{k,\ell}(J)\cdot(\text{strands}\setminus J).\tag{5.6}$$
$$\text{(ii) single-string inputs }a_i=\Psi_i\in\mathcal H:\quad \Phi^n_{\hat p_{k,\ell}}(\Psi_1,\dots,\Psi_n)=\delta_{n,k}\,p_{k,\ell}(\Psi_1,\dots,\Psi_k);\qquad \ell^W_n(\Psi_1,\dots,\Psi_n):=\Phi^n_{\hat{\mathfrak p}}(\Psi_1,\dots,\Psi_n)=\hbar^{n-1}\sum_{\ell\ge0}p_{n,\ell}(\Psi_1,\dots,\Psi_n).\tag{5.7}$$
$$\text{(iii) curvature and differential: }\ \ell^W_0=\hat{\mathfrak p}(1)=\hbar^{-1}\sum_{\ell\ge2}p_{0,\ell}=\hbar^{-1}\sum_{\ell\ge2}\tilde\Delta^{\otimes\ell}V_\ell,\qquad \ell^W_1(a)=\hat{\mathfrak p}(a)-\hat{\mathfrak p}(1)\,a.\tag{5.8}$$
$$\text{(iv) MC element of the brace structure }\Leftrightarrow\ e^{-\Phi}\hat{\mathfrak p}(e^\Phi)=0\ \Leftrightarrow\ \hat{\mathfrak p}(e^\Phi)=0\quad(\text{MS 1109.4101 §IV.B: }\mathfrak L(e^{\mathfrak c})=0).\tag{5.9}$$
$$\text{(v) on a single string, }\hbar\,e^{-\Psi}\hat{\mathfrak p}(e^{\Psi})=\sum_{k,\ell}\frac{\hbar^{k}}{k!\,\ell!}\,\tilde\Delta^{\otimes\ell}V_{k+\ell}(\Psi^k,\cdot^\ell)\ \longleftrightarrow\ S(\hbar\Psi+\chi)\ (\chi\text{-legs raised}):\quad \hat{\mathfrak p}(e^\Psi)=0\iff S\equiv0.\tag{5.10}$$

\subsection*{Commentary}
(5.3)–(5.5): standard (BDA 1996 §2; Akman 1995 §1; Voronov 2005 "Higher derived brackets" §2 — the braces are the "higher derived brackets" of $D$ with respect to the abelian subalgebra $W\subset\mathrm{End}(W)$ acting by multiplication). (5.4) is the generating identity behind the $L_\infty$ MC equation; for $D$ odd and $a$ even both sides are homogeneous.
(5.6): inclusion–exclusion. $\hat p_{k,\ell}(a_I)\cdot a_{I^c}$ is, by (5.1), the sum over $k$-subsets $J$ of the strands of $a_I$ of $p_{k,\ell}(J)\cdot$(rest); a given $J$ touching exactly the set $T\subseteq\{1,\dots,n\}$ of inputs occurs in the term $I$ iff $I\supseteq T$, with the sign $(-1)^{n-|I|}$ and a common Koszul factor; $\sum_{I\supseteq T}(-1)^{n-|I|}=(1-1)^{n-|T|}=\delta_{T,\{1..n\}}$. Only the $J$ that draw at least one strand from every input survive. This is also the statement that $\hat p_{k,\ell}$ has order exactly $k$: a $k$-subset cannot touch more than $k$ inputs, so $\Phi^{n>k}=0$, consistent with (5.5) and CFL Rem. 2.2.
(5.7): with $m_i=1$ each input has one strand; "$J$ touches every input" forces $|J|=n$, so $n=k$ and $J=$ all strands, no spectators. Summing (5.1) over $(k,\ell)$ with $k=n$ gives the second equality. **This is the precise sense in which "the $p_{n,\ell}$ are read as single-output $n$-ary products on $W$":** the $E_\ell$-component of $\ell^W_n$ on single-string inputs is $p_{n,\ell}$, for every $\ell\ge0$ at once, including $\ell=0$ (the scalar $V_n$) and $\ell=1$ ($\ell_n$ itself). On multi-string inputs (5.6) fixes $\ell^W_n$ with no further choice: e.g. $\ell^W_2(\Psi,\Sigma)$ contains, from $k=2$, the terms with one strand from $\Psi$ and one from $\Sigma$ (never $p_{2,\ell}(\Sigma)\cdot\Psi$, which is subtracted), and from $k=3$ the term $p_{3,\ell}(\Psi,\sigma_1,\sigma_2)$ with all strands used — §5.4 lists them.
(5.8): the non-augmented family is *curved* on $W$: $\ell^W_0\ne0$ is the sum of pure-output elements $p_{0,\ell}\in E_\ell$ (A′'s $S(\chi)$ terms, (3.30)). The differential $\ell^W_1$ is $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ minus the multiplication by the curvature; on $\Psi\in E_1$: $\ell^W_1(\Psi)=\sum_\ell p_{1,\ell}(\Psi)=Q\Psi+p_{1,2}(\Psi)+\cdots$ (the $k=1$ derivation slice, MS's $L^0$, (3.32)), on $\Sigma\in E_2$: $\ell^W_1(\Sigma)=\hat Q\Sigma+\hat p_{1,\ell\ge2}(\Sigma)+\hbar\sum_\ell p_{2,\ell}(\Sigma)$, which contains $\hbar\,m_2(\Sigma)$ (the HS kinetic operator) **and** $\hat Q\Sigma=(Q\otimes1+1\otimes Q)\Sigma$ — the brace structure gives $\Sigma$ a $Q$-kinetic term, the HS action does not (§5.4).
(5.9): (5.4) with $\Phi$ even, and $e^{-\Phi}$ invertible. The MC elements of the brace $L_\infty$ algebra on $W$ are exactly MS's IBL$_\infty$ Maurer–Cartan elements ("constant morphisms", cache lines 447–455; CFL Rem. 7.3's $A=\mathbb F|_{p^+=0}$). This identifies the brace structure as the $L_\infty$ algebra *implicit* in the IBL$_\infty$ literature's MC theory — the closest existing statement to the multi-string question (§5.6).
(5.10): $\hat p_{k,\ell}(e^\Psi)=\frac1{k!}p_{k,\ell}(\Psi^k)e^\Psi$ for an order-$k$ operator on the exponential of a single-string element (all $k$-subsets of the strands of $\Psi^m$, $\binom mk$ of them, times $\Psi^{m-k}$; resummed). The right-hand side is the full generating function (3.27) at the shifted point, and vanishing in $\chi$ for all $\chi$ means $S\equiv0$. **Consequence:** classical solutions $\partial S(\Psi)=0$ are *not* MC elements of the brace structure on single strings; the components that make Theorem A′ true ($p_{k,0}$, $p_{0,\ell}$) are exactly those that block the reading "MC on $W$ = EOM on $\mathcal H$". With the augmented family ($k,\ell\ge1$) the same computation gives $S(\hbar\Psi+\chi)-S(\hbar\Psi)-S(\chi)$, whose $\ell=1$ part *is* the EOM — but that family does not satisfy the relations (Statement A false, §3.2). The two readings cannot both hold; the record takes A′ as the theorem and (5.10) as the price.

\subsection*{Result}
**Lemma B1.** The braces of $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ give single-output products $\ell^W_n:W^{\otimes n}\to W$ with $\ell^W_n(\Psi_1,\dots,\Psi_n)=\hbar^{n-1}\sum_{\ell\ge0}p_{n,\ell}(\Psi_1,\dots,\Psi_n)$ on single strings, (5.6) on multi-strings, curvature $\ell^W_0=\sum_\ell p_{0,\ell}/\hbar$; their MC equation is the IBL$_\infty$ MC equation $\hat{\mathfrak p}(e^\Phi)=0$. Proven (combinatorial, all sectors by polarisation); machine check B1 in Verification (7d).

### §5.3 Theorem B — the brace $L_\infty$ structure on $W$ and its relation to the IBL relations (genus zero and beyond)

\subsection*{Setup}
$D:=\hat{\mathfrak p}$ of (5.1), odd; $\ell^W_n:=\Phi^n_D$ (5.2); $F(a):=e^{-a}D(e^a)=\sum_n\frac1{n!}\ell^W_n(a^n)$ for even $a\in W$ (5.4). The (curved) $L_\infty$ relations on $W$ in generating form are $\sum_{n\ge0}\frac1{n!}\ell^W_{n+1}\bigl(a^n,F(a)\bigr)=0$ for all even $a$ (the standard $\ell(e^a\wedge\ell(e^a))=0$; polarisation gives every arity and every parity sector). Contraction-number expansion of the operator product (CFL §7, normal ordering "$q$ left, $p$ right", $p\to\hbar\tilde\Delta\partial_q$): for symbols $A,B$,
$$A\star B=\sum_{s\ge0}\frac{\hbar^s}{s!}\,(\partial_{\Psi}^{s}A)\cdot_{\tilde\Delta^{\otimes s}}(\partial_{\chi}^{s}B)\qquad(\text{signs per (1.5); }s=1\text{ is the bracket }\{A,B\}\text{ of (0.3)}).\tag{5.11}$$

\subsection*{Derivation}
$$e^{-a}D(e^a\,b)=\sum_{n\ge0}\frac1{n!}\Bigl[\Phi^{n+1}_D(a^n,b)+\Phi^n_D(a^n)\,b\Bigr]\qquad(a\ \text{even},\ b\ \text{homogeneous}).\tag{5.12}$$
$$e^{-a}D^2(e^a)=e^{-a}D\bigl(e^a\,F(a)\bigr)=\sum_n\frac1{n!}\Phi^{n+1}_D\bigl(a^n,F(a)\bigr)+F(a)^2=\sum_n\frac1{n!}\ell^W_{n+1}\bigl(a^n,F(a)\bigr)\qquad(F\ \text{odd}\Rightarrow F^2=0).\tag{5.13}$$
$$\text{Hence: }\{\ell^W_n\}\text{ is a (curved) }L_\infty\text{ algebra on }W\iff e^{-a}D^2e^a=0\ \forall a\iff D^2=\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0.\tag{5.14}$$
$$\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=\hbar^{-2}\,\overrightarrow{\mathbb H\star\mathbb H}=\hbar^{-2}\Bigl[\underbrace{\mathbb H\cdot\mathbb H}_{=0\ (\mathbb H\ \text{odd})}+\hbar\,\underbrace{\{\mathbb H,\mathbb H\}}_{=0\ \text{by A}'}+\frac{\hbar^2}{2}\,(\partial_\Psi^2\mathbb H)\cdot_{\tilde\Delta^{\otimes2}}(\partial_\chi^2\mathbb H)+O(\hbar^3)\Bigr],\qquad \mathbb H=S(\Psi+\chi).\tag{5.15}$$
**Correction (B′b, 2026-09-14).** The underbrace "$\mathbb H\cdot\mathbb H=0$ ($\mathbb H$ odd)" is false: $\mathbb H$ is not parity-homogeneous (§5′.1) and its even components do not anticommute; $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2\ne0$ already in the free theory ((5′.4)). The $s=0$ term is nonzero but is not by itself an obstruction to curved $L_\infty$: the criterion is (B′.4), not $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0$; (5.13)–(5.14) are superseded by §5′.3.
$$\text{At brace arity }n\ (\text{= number of inputs consumed}):\quad \Phi^n_{\hat{\mathfrak p}^2}=\sum_{s\ge1}\hbar^{\,n+s-2}\sum_{\ell}\ \sum_{\substack{k_1+k_2=n+s\\ \ell_1+\ell_2=\ell+s}}\Phi^n\bigl(\hat p_{k_2,\ell_2}\circ_s\hat p_{k_1,\ell_1}\bigr),\tag{5.16}$$
**Correction (B′b, 2026-09-14).** (5.16) drops the $s=0$ term on the false ground of (5.15); the correct arity-$n$ residual is $\mathcal R_n=\sum_{s_{\rm W}\ge1}\hbar^{n+s_{\rm W}-2}(\cdots)$ with Lemma W's $s_{\rm W}$ (derivatives of the left factor on the full output of the right factor, including its $\partial a$ factors), see Theorem B′; $s_{\rm W}=1$ contains A′ but is not equal to it on multi-string inputs.
$$\text{two-contraction term of the }\Delta\text{-raised family, bidegree }(k,\ell):\quad A^{(2)}_{k,\ell}=\tfrac12\Bigl[S_{ab}(u)\,\tilde\Delta^{aa'}\tilde\Delta^{bb'}\,S_{a'b'}(u)\Bigr]_{\Psi^k\chi^\ell},\qquad S_{ab}:=\partial_a\partial_bS,\ u=\Psi+\chi\ (\text{signs per (1.5)}).\tag{5.17}$$

\subsection*{Commentary}
(5.12): expand $D(e^a b)$ by (5.2) with $b$ as an extra input: the subsets $I$ of the inputs either contain $b$ (giving $\Phi^{n+1}(a^n,b)$ after resummation) or not (giving $\Phi^n(a^n)\,b$); this is BDA's (2.6)/Voronov's Lemma 1, re-derived in two lines.
(5.13): $D(e^a)=e^aF(a)$ by definition of $F$; apply (5.12) with $b=F(a)$; $F(a)$ is an odd element of the graded-commutative $W$ (it is $D$ of an even element), so $F^2=0$. The right-hand side is exactly the generating function of the curved $L_\infty$ relations (arity by arity after polarising $a$; the $n=0$ term $\ell^W_1(\ell^W_0)=0$ included).
(5.14): "$\Leftarrow$" is (5.13). "$\Rightarrow$": if $e^{-a}D^2e^a=0$ for all even $a$ (with formal even coefficients), then $D^2(a^n)=0$ for all $n$ by expanding in $a$, and polarisation gives $D^2$ on every monomial of $W$; $D^2=0$. This is the *converse* needed for the multi-string question, in the brace reading: $L_\infty$ on $W$ $\Rightarrow$ the full IBL$_\infty$ relations $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0$.
(5.15): CFL Prop. 7.2 ($\hat{\mathfrak p}\circ\hat{\mathfrak p}\leftrightarrow\mathbb H\star\mathbb H$, with the $\hbar^{-1}$ of (7.3)); (5.11) expanded. The $s=1$ term is the polarised classical master equation, zero by Theorem A′ (3.28). The $s=2$ term is (5.17): two genus-zero vertices joined by two propagators, a one-loop diagram; §3.6 already placed it in the $g=1$ relation of CFL (2.4) ($g_1+g_2+s-1=1$, $s=2$), where in a *full* IBL$_\infty$ algebra it is balanced by the $s=1$ terms with one genus-one factor $\hat p_{\cdot,\cdot,1}$ (including the BV-Laplacian pieces, MS (B8)). The classical family has no such factors, so $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=\hbar^0A^{(2)}+O(\hbar)\ne0$ in general.
(5.16): the composition $\hat p_{k_2,\ell_2}\hat p_{k_1,\ell_1}$ carries $\hbar^{k_1+k_2-2}$; its part with $s$ contractions is a differential operator of order $k_1+k_2-s$ (that many inputs consumed), whose brace of arity $n$ is nonzero only for $n\le k_1+k_2-s$, and equals the CFL composition $\hat p_{k_2,\ell_2}\circ_s\hat p_{k_1,\ell_1}$ restricted to $E_n$ at $n=k_1+k_2-s$ (Lemma B1 (i) applied to the composite; lower braces $n<k_1+k_2-s$ vanish on single-string inputs and are the multi-string extension otherwise). So at fixed brace arity $n$ the number of contractions and the power of $\hbar$ are tied, $\hbar^{n+s-2}$: **the $s$-expansion of the IBL relations is the $\hbar$-expansion of the $L_\infty$ relations on $W$ at fixed arity.** The leading term $\hbar^{n-1}$ is the genus-zero relation at $k=n$ (all $\ell$).
(5.17): $\partial_{\Psi^a}\partial_{\Psi^b}S(\Psi+\chi)=\partial_{\chi^a}\partial_{\chi^b}S(\Psi+\chi)=S_{ab}(u)$; the two-contraction term of (5.11) at $A=B=\mathbb H$. On the free theory ($V_{\ge3}=0$) it is the constant $\tfrac12(\omega Q)_{ab}\tilde\Delta^{aa'}\tilde\Delta^{bb'}(\omega Q)_{a'b'}\propto\mathrm{str}(Q^2)=0$; with $V_3\ne0$ its $(1,1)$ bidegree $V_3(e_a,e_b,\Psi)\tilde\Delta\tilde\Delta V_3(e_{a'},e_{b'},\chi)$ is the one-loop bubble and is generically nonzero (machine check B3).

\subsection*{Result — superseded by §5′.3 (Theorem B′); kept for the record}
**Theorem B (UNPROVEN — critic pass B, 2026-09-13: $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ is not parity-homogeneous, so (5.13)'s "$F$ odd ⇒ $F^2=0$" fails; $W=S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ does not host the $\bar\varepsilon$-symmetric outputs; re-derivation pending).** Let $\{p_{k,\ell}\}_{k,\ell\ge0}$ be the non-augmented $\tilde\Delta$-raised family of a cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra (Theorem A′) and $\ell^W_n:=\Phi^n_{\hat{\mathfrak p}}$ its Koszul braces on $W=S\mathcal H$. Then: (i) $\{\ell^W_n\}_{n\ge0}$ is a curved $L_\infty$ algebra on $W$ **if and only if** $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0$, i.e. iff the family satisfies the IBL$_\infty$ relations at *every* contraction number $s$; (ii) the genus-zero relations of Theorem A′ are exactly the leading term of the $L_\infty$ relations on $W$ in the $\hbar$-filtration by number of inputs, (5.16): at every arity $n$ the relation holds at order $\hbar^{n-1}$; (iii) the first obstruction to an exact $L_\infty$ structure on $W$ is the two-contraction term (5.17) at order $\hbar^{n}$, the one-loop two-vertex diagram, which vanishes for the free theory and not in general — and which a *quantum* action (genus-one vertices $p_{k,\ell,1}$, MS's $\mathfrak L_c$) is designed to cancel. In words: **the $\Delta$-raised family gives an $L_\infty$ structure on the multi-string space $W$ to leading order in $\hbar$ (the classical statement), and an exact one precisely when the family is a full IBL$_\infty$ algebra — the converse holds too: an $L_\infty$ structure on $W$ of brace type is the same thing as an IBL$_\infty$ algebra on $\mathcal H$.** The passage to $\mathcal H$: $\ell_n=p_{n,1}$ is the $E_1$-component of $\ell^W_n$ on single strings (5.7), and cyclic $L_\infty$ on $\mathcal H$ is A′'s other side.

### §5.4 The $O(g_s^2)$ instance on $\mathcal H\oplus\mathrm{Sym}^2\mathcal H$: comparison with presentation (b)

\subsection*{Setup}
$W_{\le2}=\mathbb C\oplus\mathcal H\oplus E_2\mathcal H$, fields $(\Psi,\Sigma)$, $\Sigma=\sigma_1\sigma_2$ (symbolically, a sum of such). Brace products from Lemma B1, coupling constants absorbed in the $V_n$ ($V_n\propto g^{n-2}$). HS action (R1/R8, pedagogical note (SHS)): $S_{\rm HS}=\tfrac12\omega_1(\Psi,Q\Psi)+\tfrac1{3!}V_3(\Psi^3)+\tfrac1{4!}\bigl[2V_4(\Sigma,\Psi,\Psi)-V_4(\Sigma,\Sigma)\bigr]$, cyclic products $\ell^{(b)}_n$ defined through $\omega_1\oplus\omega_2^{\rm symp}$ (R7).

\subsection*{Derivation}
$$\ell^W_0=\hbar^{-1}\bigl[p_{0,2}+p_{0,3}+\cdots\bigr],\qquad p_{0,2}=\tilde\Delta^{\otimes2}V_2\ (\text{the raised kinetic form}),\ p_{0,3}=\tilde\Delta^{\otimes3}V_3.\tag{5.18}$$
$$\ell^W_1(\Psi)=Q\Psi+p_{1,2}(\Psi)+p_{1,3}(\Psi)+\cdots;\qquad \ell^W_1(\Sigma)=\underbrace{\hat Q\Sigma+\hat p_{1,2}(\Sigma)+\cdots}_{k=1\ \text{(derivations)}}+\hbar\underbrace{\bigl[\omega_1(\sigma_1,Q\sigma_2)+\ell_2(\sigma_1,\sigma_2)+m_2(\Sigma)+p_{2,3}(\Sigma)+\cdots\bigr]}_{k=2:\ p_{2,\ell}(\sigma_1,\sigma_2)}.\tag{5.19}$$
$$\ell^W_2(\Psi,\Psi)=\hbar\bigl[V_2(\Psi,\Psi)+\ell_2(\Psi,\Psi)+m_2(\Psi,\Psi)+p_{2,3}(\Psi,\Psi)+\cdots\bigr].\tag{5.20}$$
$$\ell^W_2(\Psi,\Sigma)=\hbar\sum_{\ell}\bigl[p_{2,\ell}(\Psi,\sigma_1)\,\sigma_2\pm p_{2,\ell}(\Psi,\sigma_2)\,\sigma_1\bigr]+\hbar^2\bigl[V_3(\Psi,\sigma_1,\sigma_2)+\ell_3(\Psi,\sigma_1,\sigma_2)+p_{3,2}(\Psi,\Sigma)+\cdots\bigr].\tag{5.21}$$
$$\ell^W_3(\Psi,\Psi,\Psi)=\hbar^2\bigl[V_3(\Psi^3)+\ell_3(\Psi^3)+p_{3,2}(\Psi^3)+\cdots\bigr],\qquad \ell^W_2(\Sigma,\Sigma')\ni\hbar^3\bigl[V_4(\Sigma,\Sigma')+\ell_4(\Sigma,\Sigma')+\cdots\bigr].\tag{5.22}$$
$$\text{HS: }\ \ell^{(b)}_1=Q\oplus\bigl(-\tfrac1{12}m_2\bigr),\quad \ell^{(b)}_2(\Psi,\Psi)=\ell_2(\Psi,\Psi)+\tfrac16m_2(\Psi,\Psi),\quad \ell^{(b)}_2(\Sigma,\Psi)=\tfrac16\ell_3(\sigma_1,\sigma_2,\Psi),\quad \ell^{(b)}_{n\ge3}=0.\tag{5.23}$$
$$\text{EOM: }\ Q\Psi+\tfrac12\ell_2(\Psi^2)+\tfrac16\ell_3(\Sigma,\Psi)=0,\qquad \tfrac1{12}\bigl[m_2(\Psi,\Psi)-m_2(\Sigma)\bigr]=0\ \Rightarrow\ \Sigma=\Psi^2\ (\text{R8; R9/R13 for uniqueness}).\tag{5.24}$$
$$\ell^{(b)}_1\big|_{E_2}=-\tfrac1{12}\,\Phi^1_{\hat p_{2,2}},\qquad \ell^{(b)}_2(\Psi,\Psi)=\Phi^2_{\hat p_{2,1}}+\tfrac16\Phi^2_{\hat p_{2,2}},\qquad \ell^{(b)}_2(\Sigma,\Psi)=\tfrac16\Phi^2_{\hat p_{3,1}}(\Sigma,\Psi)\qquad(\hbar=1).\tag{5.25}$$

\subsection*{Commentary}
(5.18)–(5.22): Lemma B1 (5.6)–(5.8) written out; the $\pm$ in (5.21) is the R1 dressing of the strand $\sigma_2$ passing $p_{2,\ell}(\Psi,\sigma_1)$ (1.8). Every HS object appears: $Q$, $\ell_2$, $m_2(\Sigma)$ (the $\Sigma$ kinetic operator, in $\ell^W_1$), $m_2(\Psi,\Psi)$ (the source, in $\ell^W_2$), $\ell_3(\Sigma,\Psi)$ (the $\Psi$-EOM coupling, in $\ell^W_2$). But the brace structure contains much more: (i) $\hat Q\Sigma$ — $\Sigma$ propagates with the free kinetic operator (HS: no $Q$-term for $\Sigma$, R11); (ii) $\ell_2(\sigma_1,\sigma_2)$ — $\Sigma$ sources $\Psi$ through the cubic vertex; (iii) the one-strand couplings $p_{2,\ell}(\Psi,\sigma_i)\sigma_j$; (iv) the scalar components $V_n(\ldots)\in E_0$ and the curvature (5.18); (v) all $p_{k,\ell\ge3}$, valued in $E_{\ge3}$, so **the truncation to $W_{\le2}$ is not consistent for the brace structure** — it lives on all of $W$ — whereas the HS truncation at $\Sigma$ is consistent because it is defined by an action; (vi) the relative weight of $m_2(\Sigma)$ in $\ell_1$ and $m_2(\Psi,\Psi)$ in $\tfrac12\ell_2$ is $+1:+\tfrac12$ for the braces and $-1:+1$ for HS — the HS sign is what makes $\Sigma=\Psi^2$ the solution (5.24); the brace structure's $\Sigma$-equation is not "$\Sigma=\Psi^2$" (cf. (5.10)).
(5.23)–(5.24): $\partial_\Psi$ and $\partial_\Sigma$ of $S_{\rm HS}$, raised with $\tilde\Delta$ and $\tilde\Delta^{\otimes2}$; the $\Sigma\Psi$ term of $\sum_n\frac1{n!}\ell_n(\Phi^n)$ is $\ell_2(\Sigma,\Psi)$ (two orderings over $2!$). R1's $2\!:\!1$ ratio gives the relative sign in (5.24).
(5.25): each HS product is a *single* brace $\Phi^k_{\hat p_{k,\ell}}$ with $k+\ell\in\{3,4\}$ (plus $Q$), re-weighted: **presentation (b) is the $V_4$-slice of the brace structure with HS coefficients, not the brace structure of any operator** — no $D$ has $\Phi^1_D|_{E_2}\propto m_2$ without also $\hat Q$ and $\ell_2(\sigma_1,\sigma_2)$ (a $D$ containing $\hat p_{2,2}$ contains, by (5.1), its action on every $E_{n\ge2}$, and the $k=1$ pieces are forced by $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0$ at $(1,\ell)$). Consistency: (b) satisfies the $L_\infty$ relations only to $O(g^3)$ (R11): e.g. $(\ell^{(b)}_1)^2|_{E_2}=\tfrac1{144}m_2^2\ne0$ at $O(g^4)$ since $m_2$ is not nilpotent (it is the kinetic operator); exactness needs the whole tower (R26). The brace structure by contrast is exact iff $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0$ (Theorem B) and never truncates. **Cyclicity caveat (R7):** (b) is cyclic w.r.t. $\omega_1\oplus\omega_2^{\rm symp}$, $\omega_2^{\rm symp}=\omega_1^{\otimes2}\circ(s\otimes s)$ — the parity shift on $E_2$ is the same one-unit shift that CFL's odd $\hbar$ supplies per extra output (§1.4); the brace structure is *not* cyclic for any degree-diagonal pairing (§5.5), so (b) is not a cyclic sub-structure of (α) either. **Critic pass B, to be checked in the re-derivation:** the negative "(b) is not a brace structure" above is argued only for $D$ drawn from the $\Delta$-raised family; the general statement, for *any* $D$, is one line via §5.6's own test with $D=\ell^{(b)}_1$: "$\Phi^2_{\ell^{(b)}_1}(\Psi,\Psi)=-\tfrac1{12}m_2(\Psi\Psi)-2\Psi\,Q\Psi\ne\ell_2(\Psi,\Psi)+\tfrac16m_2(\Psi\Psi)$" (critic pass B).

\subsection*{Result}
The $O(g_s^2)$ HS theory (presentation (b)) is recovered inside the brace structure as the re-weighted $V_4$-slice (5.25); it is not itself of brace type, and the two structures differ by the terms (i)–(vi). Both project to the same cyclic $L_\infty$ data on $\mathcal H$. What A′ guarantees for (b) at this order is exactly the $\min(k,\ell)=1$ relations ($[Q,\ell_2]=0$, $[Q,\ell_3]+\ell_2\circ\ell_2=0$, the $\hat Q$-chains of $p_{1,\ell}$) and hence the composite gauge law (R11); the $(2,2,0)$ relation plays no role in (b), consistent with §3.3's verdict on R16.

### §5.5 Cyclicity on $W$: the obstruction, and what restores it

\subsection*{Setup}
A cyclic structure for $\{\ell^W_n\}$ needs an odd non-degenerate $\omega_W$ on $W$ with $\omega_W(a_0,\ell^W_n(a_1,\dots,a_n))$ graded symmetric in all $n+1$ entries. Natural candidates: degree-diagonal pairings $\omega_W=\bigoplus_nc_n\,\omega_n$, $\omega_n$ on $E_n$ built from $\omega_1^{\otimes n}$ (R7: parity $n$, so odd only after the shift $s^{\otimes n}$ on even $n$ — the same one-unit shift that CFL's odd $\hbar$ supplies per extra output, §1.4; we grant it).

\subsection*{Derivation}
$$\text{(a) }\hbar\text{-weights: }\ \omega_W\bigl(a_0,\ell^W_n(a_1,\dots,a_n)\bigr)\ \text{carries }\hbar^{k-1}\text{ with }k=\#\text{inputs consumed}=\textstyle\sum_{i\ge1}\deg a_i-(\text{spectators});\ \text{rotating }a_0\text{ into the inputs changes }k\text{ unless }\deg a_0=\deg(\text{output}).\tag{5.26}$$
$$\text{(b) at }\hbar=1,\ a,b\in E_1,\ c\in E_2:\quad \omega_W\bigl(c,\ell^W_2(a,b)\bigr)=c_2\,\omega_2\bigl(c,m_2(a,b)\bigr)=\pm c_2\,V_4(c,a,b),\tag{5.27}$$
$$\omega_W\bigl(a,\ell^W_2(c,b)\bigr)=c_1\Bigl[\underbrace{V_4(a,c,b)}_{\text{from }\Phi^2_{\hat p_{3,1}}}+\underbrace{\omega_1(\sigma_1,Q b)\,\omega_1(a,\sigma_2)\pm(\sigma_1\leftrightarrow\sigma_2)}_{\text{from }\Phi^2_{\hat p_{2,0}}:\ V_2(\sigma_i,b)\,\sigma_j}\Bigr],\qquad c=\sigma_1\sigma_2.\tag{5.28}$$
$$\text{(c) Frobenius condition: cyclicity of a brace structure w.r.t. }\omega_W\text{ requires }\omega_W(c,a\cdot b)\text{ to be invariant, }\omega_W(c,ab)=\pm\,\omega_W(c\cdot a,b),\ \text{impossible for degree-diagonal }\omega_W\ (\deg c=\deg a+\deg b\ \text{vs}\ \deg c+\deg a=\deg b).\tag{5.29}$$
$$\text{(d) cotangent lift: on }T^*W:=W\oplus W^\vee\ \text{with the canonical odd pairing }\langle\Phi^\vee,\Phi\rangle,\quad \mathcal S(\Phi,\Phi^\vee):=\bigl\langle\Phi^\vee,\ e^{-\Phi}\hat{\mathfrak p}(e^{\Phi})\bigr\rangle=\sum_n\frac1{n!}\langle\Phi^\vee,\ell^W_n(\Phi^n)\rangle,\qquad(\mathcal S,\mathcal S)=0\iff\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0.\tag{5.30}$$

\subsection*{Commentary}
(5.26): the filtration $\hbar^{k-1}$ by number of inputs is not rotation-invariant: in (5.27) the output is in $E_2$ and $k=2$; in (5.28) the output is in $E_1$ and $k=3$ (or $k=2$ with a spectator). With $\hbar$ a formal parameter no $\omega_W$ can make (5.27) and (5.28) equal. At $\hbar=1$ (allowed in finite dimension) the $V_4$ terms match — this is the cyclicity of $m_2$ w.r.t. $\omega_2^{\rm symp}$ found in R7 — but the $\hat p_{2,0}$ term of (5.28) survives: it comes from the *scalar* components $p_{k,0}=V_k$ of A′'s family, which have no partner in (5.27). The brace structure is therefore not cyclic w.r.t. any $\bigoplus_nc_n\omega_n$: **obstruction proven at arity 2** (the term $\omega_1(\sigma_1,Qb)\omega_1(a,\sigma_2)$ is nonzero for generic $a,b,\sigma_i$ since $\omega_1$ and $Q$ are non-degenerate on their supports).
(5.29): the structural reason. For $D=\ell^W_1$ self-adjoint w.r.t. $\omega_W$ (which the $\Delta$-raised family is, strand by strand: $\omega_\ell(a,p_{k,\ell}b)=\pm V_{k+\ell}(a,b)=\pm\omega_k(b,p_{\ell,k}a)$), cyclicity of $\Phi^2_D(a,b)=D(ab)-D(a)b\mp aD(b)+D(1)ab$ reduces to invariance of the trilinear form $\omega_W(c,ab)$, i.e. to $(W,\cdot,\omega_W)$ being a (graded) Frobenius algebra. The symmetric algebra $S\mathcal H$ with a degree-diagonal form is not Frobenius (it is not even finite-dimensional; the degree count (5.29) is the elementary form of the obstruction). This is the precise "which symmetry of $W$'s products is violated" of the brief: *the cyclic symmetry of $\omega_W(a_0,\ell_n^W(a_1..a_n))$ under rotations that move a multi-string entry between the output and an input slot.*
(5.30): standard (any $L_\infty$ algebra is the "body" of a cyclic one on its cotangent bundle; the BV action of a theory with zero classical action and the given $L_\infty$ gauge structure). Fields: the tower $\Psi_n$ and dual multipliers $\Psi^\vee_n\in E_n^\vee$ paired off-diagonally — the structure of R13's Route C, $\langle\lambda_k,\sigma_k-\sigma_{k-1}\Psi\rangle$, whose kinetic form is exactly a cotangent pairing (R13: "non-degenerate iff $\omega_1$ is"). R13's action is *not* (5.30) (its constraint is the Veronese $\sigma_k=\sigma_{k-1}\Psi$, not $e^{-\Phi}\hat{\mathfrak p}e^\Phi=0$), but the two share the mechanism by which a non-cyclic multi-string structure becomes cyclic: double the fields with multipliers, pair off-diagonally. **What restores cyclicity, answered:** not R7's shift alone (it fixes the parity of $\omega_2$, needed but not sufficient), not a truncation (the brace structure does not truncate, §5.4 (v)); the cotangent lift (5.30) does, at the price of the multiplier fields, and the HS/all-orders structures (β) do by being *different* structures built from an action.

\subsection*{Result}
The brace $L_\infty$ structure on $W$ is not cyclic for any degree-diagonal pairing (obstruction (5.27)–(5.29), proven at arity 2 by the $p_{2,0}$ term and the $\hbar$-weights); it becomes cyclic on the cotangent lift $T^*W$ (5.30), where its action is the multiplier-type action of the IBL MC equation. An action *on $W$ itself* with the $p_{k,\ell}$ as products therefore requires leaving the brace family — which is what HS (β) and Route C (γ) do.

### §5.6 The reverse characterisation and the literature status of part B

**Which $L_\infty$ structures on $W$ come from IBL$_\infty$ data on $\mathcal H$ (item (iii) of §2.B).** By Theorem B: exactly those of brace type, i.e. $\{\ell^W_n\}$ such that $\ell^W_n=\Phi^n_{\ell^W_1+\ell^W_0\cdot}$ — the higher products are determined by the differential (and curvature) through the algebra structure of $W$. Test: an $L_\infty$ structure on $W$ is of brace type iff $D:=\ell^W_1+\ell^W_0\cdot$ satisfies $\ell^W_n(a_1..a_n)=\Phi^n_D(a_1..a_n)$ for all $n$; then $D=\hat{\mathfrak p}$ decomposes by order and output degree into $p_{k,\ell}=\Phi^k_{\hat p_{k,\ell}}|_{\text{single strings}}$ (5.7), and $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0$ gives the full IBL$_\infty$ relations. Conversely a non-brace structure (HS (β), Route C (γ)) does not define IBL$_\infty$ data by this route; its single-string slice $\ell_n=\ell^W_n|_{\mathcal H^{\otimes n}\to\mathcal H}$ may still be $L_\infty$ (it is, for (β) and (γ), by construction from $S$). The "gauge parameter $\Lambda_0$" question (item 8, $\Lambda=\sum_{n\ge0}\Lambda_n$): in the brace structure gauge parameters are elements of $W$ of the appropriate degree, including $\Lambda_0\in E_0=\mathbb C$, which acts through $\ell^W_1(\Lambda_0)=\hat{\mathfrak p}(\Lambda_0)-\hat{\mathfrak p}(1)\Lambda_0=0$: a constant parameter is inert in the brace structure; the non-trivial $\Lambda_0$ of the IBL literature acts through the *quantum* ($\hbar$-corrected, genus $\ge1$) terms, outside this record.

**Literature status.** *Known:* the brace/higher-derived-bracket theorem (5.13)–(5.14) for a square-zero operator on a graded-commutative algebra (Bering–Damgaard–Alfaro hep-th/9604027, Akman q-alg/9506027 — MS's refs. [26],[27]; Voronov math/0304038), and CFL's remark that $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ is a BV$_\infty$-type operator on $EC$ (Rem. 2.2, §1). *Implicit:* the identification of the IBL$_\infty$ Maurer–Cartan equation $\mathfrak L(e^{\mathfrak c})=0$ (MS 1109.4101 §IV.B; CFL Rem. 7.3) as the MC equation of the brace $L_\infty$ algebra (5.9) — MS state the MC equation but not the $L_\infty$ algebra it belongs to. *New here:* (i) Lemma B1's explicit dictionary $\ell^W_n|_{\text{single strings}}=\hbar^{n-1}\sum_\ell p_{n,\ell}$ (5.7) and the multi-string rule (5.6); (ii) Theorem B's $\hbar$-filtration statement (5.16) tying the contraction number to the arity — the precise sense in which the *genus-zero* relations (A′) give $L_\infty$ on $W$; (iii) the non-cyclicity obstruction (5.27)–(5.29) and its cotangent resolution (5.30) in this context; (iv) the placement of the HS theory as the re-weighted $V_4$-slice (5.25) and of Route C as a cotangent-type structure. *Not established:* a cyclic $L_\infty$ structure **on $W$ itself** built from the $p_{k,\ell}$ with a physical (non-multiplier) action beyond the HS/all-orders tower — the object needed for more general actions; §5.5 shows that it cannot be the brace structure and must, like (β)/(γ), come from an action on $W$ whose products are components of the braces. Whether every such action is of HS/Route-C type (an auxiliary-field rewriting of $S$) is open (§6).

New checks: **B1a** (5.5) $\Phi^{k+1}=0$ for an order-$k$ operator on multi-string inputs; **B1b** (5.7) $\Phi^k$ on single strings $=p_{k,\ell}$, $\Phi^{n<k}=0$ there, $\Phi^0=D(1)$; **B1c** (5.6) $\Phi^2(x_0x_1,x_2)=p(x_0,x_2)x_1+p(x_1,x_2)x_0$ (one strand from each input); **B1d** (5.4) generating identity through $t^4$ for a *curved* operator; **B1e** (5.12); **B1f** (5.13) first equality, with the $F^2$ term kept (commutative test algebra: $F^2\ne0$ there, so the identity is tested in its stronger form; the graded case adds only $F^2=0$); **B1g** break-on-purpose: dropping the curvature term $D(1)ab$ from $\Phi^2$ violates (5.4) (defect 5.6); **B3** (5.17) two-contraction term at bidegree $(1,1)$ on the generator: $|A^{(2)}|=13.6$ (one-loop bubble, nonzero), free-theory constant $=0$. Method for B1: a commutative polynomial algebra in 3 variables (degree $\le7$) with a random order-2 operator $\hat p_{2,2}$ of the form (5.1) plus a random order-1 part and a multiplication (curvature); braces by (5.2); exponentials as truncated series in a formal $t$. This tests the *combinatorial* content of Lemma B1 and of (5.12)–(5.14), which is parity-independent; Koszul signs are not exercised.

**Not machine-checked (and why):** (i) the operator form of $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ on bare physical polynomials and hence the $s=1$ relation as an operator identity — it needs the odd-$\hbar$ per-output sign dressing that §1.3 deliberately avoided (§4.6 (i)); the $s=1$ statement is verified in generating-function form by 7c (E-checks, D0) and Theorem B(ii) rests on that plus (5.16), a counting argument; (ii) the non-cyclicity obstruction (5.27)–(5.28): a two-line component computation, checked by hand only; (iii) $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2\ne0$ as a whole on the toy (would need (i)); B3 checks the leading obstruction term (5.17) instead, in the doubled-tensor convention of 7c with both contractions weighted by $\tilde\Delta$ — the *nonvanishing* is insensitive to the relative sign convention of the two contractions (the contraction is symmetric under relabelling for graded-symmetric $V_3$ and $\tilde\Delta$), the free-theory zero is $\mathrm{str}(Q^2)=0$ for any weight. Falsifiability of the block: B1g; B3's free-theory leg; the inherited legs of §4.

## Working notes (7d)

**W10. Contraction number vs $\hbar$ power at fixed arity (used in (5.16)).** $\hat p_{k,\ell}$ carries $\hbar^{k-1}$; $\hat p_{k_2,\ell_2}\circ\hat p_{k_1,\ell_1}$ carries $\hbar^{k_1+k_2-2}$ and decomposes by the number $s$ of outputs of the first factor consumed by the second ($0\le s\le\min(\ell_1,k_2)$); the $s$-part consumes $k_1+k_2-s$ strands of its input, so it is a differential operator of order $k_1+k_2-s$ and equals CFL's $\hat p_{k_2,\ell_2}\circ_s\hat p_{k_1,\ell_1}$ (CFL (2.3)) restricted to $E_{k_1+k_2-s}$ on its top brace. At brace arity $n$: $s=k_1+k_2-n$, $\hbar^{n+s-2}$. CFL's own weight $\hbar^{k+g-1}$ with $k=k_1+k_2-s$, $g=s-1$ gives $\hbar^{k_1+k_2-2}$: consistent. The $s=0$ part (no contraction, order $k_1+k_2$) is the normal-ordered product $\overrightarrow{H_{k_2,\ell_2}H_{k_1,\ell_1}}$; summed over all pairs it is $\overrightarrow{\mathbb H\cdot\mathbb H}=0$ because $\mathbb H$ is odd in the graded-commutative symbol algebra (this uses $d=\tfrac12$: for CFL's even $\hbar$, $|\mathbb H|=-1$ odd as well). **Correction (B′b, 2026-09-14):** the last sentence is false — $\mathbb H$ is inhomogeneous in every grading (§5′.1) and $\overrightarrow{\mathbb H\cdot\mathbb H}\ne0$ ((5′.4)); moreover the $s=0$ operator applied to $e^a$ is not $F^2e^a$ (extra $\partial\partial a$ terms, (W.2)). See §5′.3.

**W11. Cost of the operator implementation (decision).** An implementation of $\hat p_{k,\ell}$ on polynomials $f(\chi)$ over the 12-dim toy via `left_deriv`/`poly_product` was designed (contract $k$ derivative indices with $\tilde\Delta^{\otimes k}V_{k+\ell}$, multiply by $\chi^\ell$); the sign of each term requires the parity flip $\Psi^a\to\hbar\tilde\Delta^{ab}\partial_{\chi^b}$ ($\varepsilon_b=\varepsilon_a+1$) to be compensated by an odd $\hbar$, i.e. a per-output Koszul dressing — the hat-form problem of §1.3. Estimated at more than the remaining time box; deferred (Verification (7d) (i)). The braces on single strings would then give an independent operator-form second method for A′ at $(2,\ell)$; recommended for the critic or a follow-up.

**W12. Sub-family count (orchestrator's question, 2026-09-08).** Augmented relations with at least one nonzero term for $F=\{Q,\ell_2,\ell_3,p_{1,2},m_2\}$: $(1,1)$, $(2,1)$, $(3,1)$, $(1,2)$, $(2,2)$ — and $(1,3)$: $\hat p_{1,2}\circ_1\hat p_{1,2}$ (bidegree $(1,3)$ of $\{H_{1,2},H_{1,2}\}$, $T(0,2|1,1)$ of W8), same order $g^2$ as $(2,2)$, which in the full augmented family is balanced by $[\hat Q,\hat p_{1,3}]$ (§3.1: $\min(k,\ell)=1$ holds) and is therefore nonzero when $p_{1,3}$ is dropped. At $O(g^3)$: $(4,1)$ ($\ell_2\ell_3+\ell_3\ell_2$, needs $\ell_4$), $(3,2)$ ($[\hat p_{1,2},\hat\ell_3]+[\hat m_2,\hat\ell_2]$, needs $p_{3,2}$), $(2,3)$ ($[\hat p_{1,2},\hat m_2]$, needs $p_{2,3}$); at $O(g^4)$: $(3,3)$ ($\hat m_2\circ_1\hat m_2$, needs $p_{3,3}$ and the $V_5,V_6$ terms). See §6.

## §5′ Part B re-derived with parities (2026-09-13)

**Reading note (2026-09-19, fix pass after critic 026D).** §5′.1–§5′.4 were written before §5′.5–§5′.7 and are kept as the record of that state. Wherever they say "up to the décalage signs of WB2 (c)", "the décalage signs are not fixed", "a faithful host … is open", or read the doubled symbol algebra as an *operator* on $S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$, read instead: the signs are **fixed** for $\ell=1$ by (5′.12) (no décalage sign, verified to arity 3, W6/W6a/W7); for $\ell\ge2$ there is **no** realisation as a Koszul-brace structure of differential operators on a single graded-commutative polynomial host in the strand variables with even $\hbar$ (§5′.7 Verdict (C)), so the doubled symbol algebra is bookkeeping only, not an operator host; untested, not excluded: hosts with separate input/output generators, the $\star$-calculus (W10), an odd parameter in a non-graded-commutative host.

**Restated task (B′a).** Critic pass B (`critic-026B-rest-20260908T101600.md`, §5 findings) showed that (5.13) uses "$F$ odd ⇒ $F^2=0$" while $\hat{\mathfrak p}=\sum\hbar^{k-1}\hat p_{k,\ell}$ is not parity-homogeneous on $W=S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$, and that $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ cannot host the $\bar\varepsilon$-symmetric outputs (1.7). Sub-task B′a does three bounded things: (§5′.1) an exhaustive parity table of the components in every grading in use, with the one-line answer "is there any grading making $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ homogeneous?", plus the sign of (1.4) under $\tilde\Delta$; (§5′.2) the free-theory falsifier $\{Q,p_{0,2}\}$: $F=e^{-\Phi}\hat{\mathfrak p}e^{\Phi}$, $F^2$, and the curved $L_\infty$ relations, by hand and on the toy with odd inputs present. §5′.3–§5′.4 are B′b's.

**Motivating objective.** Item 2 of the multi-string question: can the $\Delta$-raised IBL products be read as $L_\infty$ products on a multi-string space, with IBL ⇒ $L_\infty$? No "Proven" may reach the ledger until the parity question is settled.

**Conventions.** §1 throughout; physical parity $\varepsilon=N_{\rm gh}\bmod2$; $\tilde\Delta^{ba}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_b}\Delta^{ba}$; $\ell_n=\tilde\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$ (1.4); R23: $N_{\rm gh}(p_{k,\ell,g})=3\ell-2k+g$; cohomological sign ($Q$ degree $+1$). Odd inputs are kept explicitly in §5′.1–§5′.2; in §5′.3 the generating-function statements are made for even $a\in A\otimes\Lambda$, $\Lambda$ an auxiliary Grassmann algebra, which is how odd input sectors are reached (B′d, critic 026C).

### §5′.1 Parity table

\subsection*{Setup}
$\hat p_{k,\ell}:E_n\mathcal H\to E_{n-k+\ell}\mathcal H$ is the coderivation extension (5.1) of $p_{k,\ell}$ ($k$ inputs, $\ell$ outputs, $N_{\rm gh}=3\ell-2k$, R23 with $g=0$). Let $\sigma$ be a uniform per-strand shift, $\deg A=N_{\rm gh}(A)-\sigma$ on every strand of $W_\sigma:=S(\mathcal H[\sigma])$. By (1.11), $\deg_\sigma\hat p_{k,\ell}=(3-\sigma)\ell+(\sigma-2)k$, so its parity is
$$\pi_\sigma(\hat p_{k,\ell})=\ell+\sigma(k+\ell)\bmod 2=\begin{cases}\ell\bmod2&\sigma\ \text{even (a: }\sigma=0,\ \text{MS }\sigma=2)\\ k\bmod2&\sigma\ \text{odd (b: any odd shift, CFL }\sigma=3)\end{cases}\tag{5′.1}$$
Symmetry type of the slots (1.7), independent of $\sigma$: inputs graded symmetric in $\varepsilon$, outputs in $\bar\varepsilon=\varepsilon+1$. A tensor that is $\bar\varepsilon$-symmetric in a pair of strands of **equal** parity is $\varepsilon$-*anti*symmetric in that pair ($(-1)^{\bar\varepsilon_a\bar\varepsilon_b}=-(-1)^{\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_b}$ iff $\varepsilon_a=\varepsilon_b$), hence projects to zero in $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$; for strands of opposite parity the two symmetries agree.

\subsection*{Table}
| component | $(k,\ell)$ | $N_{\rm gh}$ (R23) | (a) parity on $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ / MS $A_c$ ($\sigma$ even): $\ell$ | (b) uniform odd shift / CFL $C[1]$ ($\sigma=3$): $k$ | outputs symmetric in | survives projection to $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ on physical inputs? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| $\hat Q=\hat p_{1,1}$ | (1,1) | $+1$ | odd | odd | — (one output) | yes |
| $\hat\ell_2$ | (2,1) | $-1$ | odd | **even** | — | yes |
| $\hat\ell_3$ | (3,1) | $-3$ | odd | odd | — | yes |
| $\hat\ell_n=\hat p_{n,1}$ | (n,1) | $3-2n$ | odd | $n\bmod2$ | — | yes |
| $m_2=\hat p_{2,2}$ | (2,2) | $+2$ | **even** | even | $\bar\varepsilon$; on $\Psi\otimes\Psi$ ($N_{\rm gh}=2,2$) outputs at $(3,3)$, both odd, plain-symmetric | **no**: $\varepsilon$-antisymmetric pair of odd strands $\mapsto0$ |
| $\hat p_{1,2}$ | (1,2) | $+4$ | even | odd | $\bar\varepsilon$; on $\Psi$ outputs at $(3,3)$ | **no** |
| $\hat p_{0,2}=\Theta$ | (0,2) | $+6$ | even | even | $\bar\varepsilon$; support $(3,3),(2,4),(4,2)$: equal parities only | **no** ($\Theta\mapsto0$ in $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$, §5′.2, check P3) |
| $V_k=\hat p_{k,0}$ | (k,0) | $-2k$ | even | $k\bmod2$ | — (scalar) | yes |
| $\hat p_{k,\ell}$ | (k,ℓ) | $3\ell-2k$ | $\ell\bmod2$ | $k\bmod2$ | $\bar\varepsilon$ | only the components with opposite-parity output pairs |

Ghost-number column cross-checked against R23 ($3\ell-2k+g$, $g=0$) and against the toy support (check P2 below: every raised tensor is supported on $\sum_{\rm out}n-\sum_{\rm in}n=3\ell-2k$).

\subsection*{Result}
**Conclusion (one line).** *No grading of $W$ makes $\hat{\mathfrak p}=\sum_{k,\ell}\hbar^{k-1}\hat p_{k,\ell}$ parity-homogeneous:* $\hat Q$ ($k=\ell=1$) is odd and $m_2$ ($k=\ell=2$) is even under every per-strand shift, uniform or not (for $k=\ell$ the shift cancels), and under any grading depending only on the strand number $n$ of $E_n$ (both map $E_n\to E_n$). Column (a) splits $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ into $D_{\rm odd}=\sum_{\ell\ \rm odd}$, $D_{\rm even}=\sum_{\ell\ \rm even}$; column (b) into $\sum_{k\ \rm odd}$, $\sum_{k\ \rm even}$. The only device that formally restores homogeneity is the odd $\hbar$ of §1.4 ($|\hbar|=-1$, $d=\tfrac12$): it fixes the *degree* of every $\hbar^{k-1}\hat p_{k,\ell}$ to $+1$ (column (b), (1.12)) but not the *Koszul signs* — an odd $\hbar$ in a graded-commutative $W[\hbar]$ has $\hbar^2=0$, which kills all $k\ge3$ components; so the brace theorem for an odd operator (Bering–Damgaard–Alfaro 1996, Akman 1995, Voronov 2005) does not apply to $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ in any grading, and (5.13)'s "$F$ odd ⇒ $F^2=0$" has no valid reading. Independently, $W=S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ is not a faithful host: the $\bar\varepsilon$-symmetric outputs of $m_2$, $\hat p_{1,2}$, $\hat p_{0,2}$ on physical inputs are equal-parity pairs and project to zero (last column). The faithful host for the *outputs* is $S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$ (CFL's $C[1]$ parity), where the *inputs* are then mis-symmetrised — the per-slot obstruction of §1.2 (iii), now at the level of $W$.

**Sign of (1.4) under $\tilde\Delta$ (settled).** With $\omega_1(e_c,e_b)=\omega_{cb}$, $\Delta=\omega^{-1}$ ($\omega_{cb}\Delta^{ba}=\delta_c^a$) and $\ell_n=\tilde\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$, i.e. $\ell_n(\cdots)=(-1)^{\varepsilon_b}\Delta^{ba}V_{n+1}(e_a,\cdots)e_b$:
$$\omega_1(e_c,\ell_n(A_1,\dots,A_n))=(-1)^{\varepsilon_b}\omega_{cb}\Delta^{ba}V_{n+1}(e_a,\cdots)=(-1)^{\varepsilon_c+1}V_{n+1}(e_c,A_1,\dots,A_n)=-(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}\,V_{n+1}(e_c,A_1,\dots,A_n),\tag{5′.2}$$
using $\varepsilon_b=\varepsilon_c+1$ on the support of $\omega_{cb}$ (1.2). This is pass A's $\omega(x_0,\tilde\ell_3(\ldots))=-(-1)^{\varepsilon_0}V_4(x_0,\ldots)$, now for every $n$; on physical (even) $x_0$ the dressing is the constant $-1$. In particular $p_{1,1}=\tilde\Delta\circ V_2=-(-1)^{\varepsilon}Q=:\tilde Q$ (odd, $\tilde Q^2=0$, a derivation of $W_\sigma$ for every $\sigma$). Machine check P1 (toy, $n=1,2,3$). (1.4)'s qualifier is replaced accordingly (the one edit to §1).

### §5′.2 Free-theory test

\subsection*{Setup}
Family $\{p_{1,1},p_{0,2}\}$ of the free theory $S=\tfrac12V_2(\Psi,\Psi)$, $V_2=\omega_1(\cdot,Q\cdot)$: $p_{1,1}=\tilde\Delta\circ V_2=\tilde Q=-(-1)^{\varepsilon}Q$ (5′.2) — the outcome below is identical for $Q$ itself, both being odd derivations squaring to zero — and $\Theta:=p_{0,2}=\tilde\Delta^{\otimes2}V_2\in\mathcal H^{\otimes2}$, $\Theta^{ab}=\tilde\Delta^{aa'}\tilde\Delta^{bb'}\omega_1(e_{a'},Qe_{b'})$, supported on $n_a+n_b=6$ (equal parities), $\bar\varepsilon$-symmetric (1.7), $N_{\rm gh}=6$. Host: by §5′.1, $\Theta\mapsto0$ in $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$, so the test is run on $W_3:=S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$ (every strand with the shifted parity $\bar\varepsilon$; CFL's $C[1]$ parity), where $\Theta=\tfrac12\Theta^{ab}x_ax_b$ is a nonzero **even** element and $\hat Q=\sum_a(Qx_a)\partial^L_a$ (likewise $\hat{\tilde Q}$) is an odd derivation. Then $\hat{\mathfrak p}=D:=\hat{\tilde Q}+\hbar^{-1}\Theta\cdot$, with $\hat{\tilde Q}$ odd and $\Theta\cdot$ even: inhomogeneous, as the table says ($\ell=1$ vs $\ell=2$, or $k=1$ vs $k=0$).
