Inputs $a\in W_3$ of either parity are allowed; $\hbar$ is an even bookkeeping parameter here (set to 1 in the checks).

\subsection*{Derivation}
$$D^2=\hat{\tilde Q}^2+\hat{\tilde Q}\circ\Theta+\Theta\circ\hat{\tilde Q}+\Theta^2=\hbar^{-1}(\hat{\tilde Q}\Theta)\cdot+2\hbar^{-1}\,\Theta\,\hat{\tilde Q}+\hbar^{-2}\Theta^2\cdot\qquad(\Theta\ \text{even}\Rightarrow\hat{\tilde Q}(\Theta f)=(\hat{\tilde Q}\Theta)f+\Theta\hat{\tilde Q}f).\tag{5′.3}$$
$$\hat{\tilde Q}\Theta=0\quad(\text{the }(0,2)\text{ genus-zero relation }\hat p_{1,1}\circ\hat p_{0,2}=0\text{ of A′; machine check P4a}),\qquad\text{so }D^2=2\hbar^{-1}\Theta\,\hat{\tilde Q}+\hbar^{-2}\Theta^2\ne0.\tag{5′.4}$$
$$\text{Braces (5.2) with Koszul signs, any input parities: }\ \ell^W_0=\hbar^{-1}\Theta,\quad \ell^W_1(a)=D(a)-D(1)a=\hat{\tilde Q}a,\quad \ell^W_{n\ge2}=0\ (\hat{\tilde Q}\text{ order }1,\ \Theta\cdot\text{ order }0).\tag{5′.5}$$
$$\text{Curved }L_\infty\text{ relations: }\ \ell^W_1(\ell^W_0)=\hbar^{-1}\hat{\tilde Q}\Theta=0,\qquad (\ell^W_1)^2=\hat{\tilde Q}^2=0,\qquad\text{all others empty: SATISFIED.}\tag{5′.6}$$
$$F(a):=e^{-a}De^{a}=\hat{\tilde Q}a+\hbar^{-1}\Theta\ (a\ \text{even}),\qquad F^2=\hbar^{-2}\Theta^2+2\hbar^{-1}\Theta\,\hat{\tilde Q}a\ne0\quad((\hat{\tilde Q}a)^2=0,\ \text{odd}).\tag{5′.7}$$
$$e^{-a}D^2e^{a}=2\hbar^{-1}\Theta\hat{\tilde Q}a+\hbar^{-2}\Theta^2=F(a)^2,\qquad\text{whereas }\sum_{n\ge0}\tfrac1{n!}\ell^W_{n+1}(a^n,F(a))=\hat{\tilde Q}F(a)=0.\tag{5′.8}$$
$$\text{General identity (any linear }D\text{, even }a\text{; (5.12) with }b=F(a)\text{):}\quad \sum_{n\ge0}\frac1{n!}\Phi^{n+1}_D\bigl(a^n,F(a)\bigr)=e^{-a}D^2e^{a}-F(a)^2.\tag{5′.9}$$

\subsection*{Commentary}
(5′.3)–(5′.4): the cross terms do not cancel because $\Theta\cdot$ is even: for a homogeneous odd $D$ they would ($D^2=\tfrac12[D,D]$). Both surviving terms are the $s=0$ (no-contraction, normal-ordered) part of $\mathbb H\star\mathbb H$ in (5.11): $\Theta^2\leftrightarrow S_2(\chi)^2$ and $2\Theta\hat{\tilde Q}\leftrightarrow 2S_2(\chi)\,\partial_\chi S_2\cdot\hbar\tilde\Delta\partial_\chi$ — exactly the "$\mathbb H\cdot\mathbb H=0$ because $\mathbb H$ is odd" of (5.15)/W10, which is therefore **false**: the even components of $\mathbb H$ do not anticommute. The $s=1$ part ($\tilde Q^2$, $\tilde Q\Theta$) and $s=2$ part ($\propto\mathrm{str}\,Q^2$, B3) vanish.
(5′.5)–(5′.6): the braces of the free family form a curved differential module ($Q$-differential with $Q$-closed curvature) — a curved $L_\infty$ algebra — **although** $D^2\ne0$. Hence Theorem B (i) "curved $L_\infty$ **iff** $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0$" is false in the direction $L_\infty\Rightarrow\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0$ already for the free theory.
(5′.7)–(5′.9): (5.13) replaced $F^2$ by 0; (5′.9) is what (5.12) actually gives, no parity assumption used (the derivation of (5.12) is combinatorics in the even element $a$, linear in $b$). For the free theory the entire $e^{-a}D^2e^a$ is accounted for by $F^2$, so the $L_\infty$ relations hold. The correct criterion is therefore not $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0$ but $e^{-a}\hat{\mathfrak p}^2e^a=F(a)^2$ for all even $a$ — B′b's starting point (Working notes (B′)).

\subsection*{Result}
**Free theory.** On $W_3=S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$ the braces of $\hat{\mathfrak p}=\hat{\tilde Q}+\hbar^{-1}\Theta$ are $\ell^W_0=\Theta/\hbar$, $\ell^W_1=\hat{\tilde Q}$, $\ell^W_{n\ge2}=0$ and satisfy the curved $L_\infty$ relations; $F^2=\Theta^2/\hbar^2+2\Theta\hat{\tilde Q}a/\hbar\ne0$; $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=2\Theta\hat{\tilde Q}/\hbar+\Theta^2/\hbar^2\ne0$. So: the braces DO satisfy curved $L_\infty$ in the free theory, $F^2$ does NOT vanish, and $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2$ does NOT vanish — Theorem B (i) cannot hold as stated (neither its proof nor its "iff"); what holds is (5′.9) with $F^2$ kept. On $W=S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ the same family collapses to $\hat{\tilde Q}$ alone ($\Theta\mapsto0$), a trivially true but lossy statement.

\subsection*{Verification}
Script block `part_b_parity_checks` in `codes/verify-linfty-ibl-repackaging.py` (graded-commutative polynomial algebra on the 12 toy strands with parity $\bar\varepsilon$ or $\varepsilon$, Koszul-signed braces, odd inputs present): P1 (5′.2) for $n=1,2,3$; P2 ghost-number support $3\ell-2k$ for $(1,1),(2,1),(3,1),(2,2),(1,2),(0,2),(3,0),(4,0)$; P3 $\Theta\mapsto0$ in $S_\varepsilon$, $\ne0$ in $S_{\bar\varepsilon}$; P4a–e (5′.4)–(5′.9); P5 critic's counterexample $D=\partial_{\theta_1}\partial_{\theta_2}$, $a=t\theta_1\theta_2$ ($F=-t+ta$, $F^2\ne0$, $L_\infty$ residual $=-F^2$, $D^2=0$) as the break-on-purpose leg: with the parity dressing removed the leg FAILS. Run 2026-09-13, `.venv/bin/python codes/verify-linfty-ibl-repackaging.py`: `summary: 92/92 PASS (seed 20260908)`; the new legs P0, P1, P2, P3, P4a–e, P5, P5′ all PASS (P3: $|\Theta|_{S_\varepsilon}=0$, $|\Theta|_{S_{\bar\varepsilon}}=1.41$; P4e: $|e^{-a}D^2e^a|=|F^2|=7.72$, $L_\infty$ residual $0$; P4c: parity-blind $|\Phi^2_{\hat Q}(\text{odd},\text{odd})|=29.7$ vs $0$ with Koszul signs; P5: $|F^2|=0.843$, residual $=-F^2$, and P5′ confirms the leg FAILS with the parity dressing removed). What the block does not test: any interacting component ($V_{\ge3}$ in the polynomial algebra, W11 still open) and the mis-symmetrisation of inputs on $S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$ (no input symmetrisation is exercised in the free theory).

### §5′.3 Corrected statement (B′b)

**Restated task (B′b).** Prove or refute WB1: the Koszul braces of $\hat{\mathfrak p}=\sum_{k,\ell}\hbar^{k-1}\hat p_{k,\ell}$ form a curved $L_\infty$ algebra iff the contracted ($s\ge1$) part of $\hat{\mathfrak p}\star\hat{\mathfrak p}$ vanishes. Ingredients: (5′.9) (any linear $D$, even $a$) and a Wick-type Lemma W separating $e^{-a}D_1D_2e^a$ from $F_1F_2$. Parities tracked at every step; the only standing assumption is "$a$ even". Objective: the exact core of the answer to the IBL ⇒ $L_\infty$ question, to be ledgered.

**Conventions.** As §5′ header. Graded-commutative algebra $A$ (polynomials in even/odd generators $x_a$, parity $|x_a|$); differential operators of finite order with left derivatives $\partial_a=\partial^L_a$, $\partial_a(x_bf)=\delta_{ab}f+(-1)^{|a||b|}x_b\partial_af$; Koszul sign rule for every transposition; $D$ may be parity-inhomogeneous ($D=D_0+D_1$). Contraction number $s$ as in (5.11)/W10.

#### Lemma W (Wick-type)

\subsection*{Setup}
$D_1,D_2$ differential operators of finite order on $A$, written normal-ordered, $D_i=\sum_{I}c^{(i)}_I(x)\,\partial^I$ (coefficients left, $\partial^I=\partial_{i_1}\cdots\partial_{i_r}$ right), each $D_i$ possibly parity-inhomogeneous ($c^{(i)}_I$ of arbitrary parities). $a\in A$ even; $F_i:=F_{D_i}(a)=e^{-a}D_ie^{a}$ (so $D_ie^a=e^aF_i$ — $e^a$ is even and central). Every equality below holds term by term in the normal-ordered expansion, so it suffices to prove it for monomial $D_i=c_i\partial^{I_i}$ with $c_i$ homogeneous.

\subsection*{Derivation}
*Order $1\times1$.* $D_i=c_i\partial_{\alpha_i}$, $|D_i|=|c_i|+|\alpha_i|$. Then $F_2=e^{-a}c_2\partial_{\alpha_2}e^a=c_2\,\partial_{\alpha_2}a$ (chain rule for a single derivative on the even $e^a$; no sign since $a$ even), and
$$D_1D_2e^a=c_1\partial_{\alpha_1}\bigl(e^a\,c_2\,\partial_{\alpha_2}a\bigr)=c_1(\partial_{\alpha_1}a)\,e^a c_2\partial_{\alpha_2}a+c_1e^a\,\partial_{\alpha_1}\bigl(c_2\,\partial_{\alpha_2}a\bigr)\tag{W.1}$$
(Leibniz on the product $e^a\cdot(c_2\partial_{\alpha_2}a)$; $e^a$ even, so $\partial_{\alpha_1}$ passes it without sign). The first term is $e^a\,(c_1\partial_{\alpha_1}a)(c_2\partial_{\alpha_2}a)=e^aF_1F_2$ — no reordering was needed, hence no Koszul sign. The second is $e^a\,c_1\partial_{\alpha_1}F_2$: the derivative of $D_1$ acts on the output $F_2$ of $D_2$. Expanding it once more,
$$c_1\partial_{\alpha_1}F_2=c_1(\partial_{\alpha_1}c_2)\,\partial_{\alpha_2}a+(-1)^{|\alpha_1||c_2|}c_1c_2\,\partial_{\alpha_1}\partial_{\alpha_2}a,\tag{W.2}$$
i.e. it splits into the symbol contraction (derivative on the coefficient $c_2$; W10's $s=1$) and a term in which the derivative of $D_1$ hits the derivative of $a$ produced by $D_2$ (a second derivative of $a$; absent from W10's symbol calculus, where $\Psi\to\hbar\tilde\Delta\partial_\chi$ acts on $\chi$ only). Hence, for order $1\times1$,
$$e^{-a}D_1D_2e^a-F_1F_2=c_1(\partial_{\alpha_1}c_2)\,\partial_{\alpha_2}a+(-1)^{|\alpha_1||c_2|}c_1c_2\,\partial_{\alpha_1}\partial_{\alpha_2}a=c_1\,\partial_{\alpha_1}\!\bigl(F_2\bigr)=\Phi^1_{D_1}(F_2).\tag{W.3}$$
*General order.* $D_1=c_1\partial^{I}$, $|I|=r$. Leibniz for $\partial^I$ on the product $e^a\cdot F_2$ (with $e^a$ even the only signs are those of the derivatives passing each other and passing $F_2$'s odd content, exactly the Koszul signs of the graded Leibniz rule):
$$\partial^I\bigl(e^aF_2\bigr)=\sum_{J\subseteq I}\pm\,(\partial^{I\setminus J}e^a)\,(\partial^{J}F_2)=e^a\sum_{J\subseteq I}\pm\,B_{I\setminus J}(a)\,\partial^JF_2,\qquad e^{-a}\partial^Ke^a=:B_K(a)\ (\text{complete Bell polynomial in derivatives of }a).\tag{W.4}$$
The $J=\emptyset$ term is $e^aB_I(a)F_2$ and $c_1B_I(a)=F_1$; so
$$e^{-a}D_1D_2e^a-F_1F_2=\sum_{s\ge1}T_s,\qquad T_s:=c_1\sum_{J\subseteq I,\ |J|=s}\pm\,B_{I\setminus J}(a)\,\partial^JF_2(a),\tag{W.5}$$
$T_s$ = the terms in which exactly $s$ derivatives of $D_1$ act on the output $F_2(a)$ of $D_2$ (on its coefficients *or* on the derivatives of $a$ it contains). Comparing with (5.12) at $b=F_2$ (whose derivation is the same Leibniz expansion organised by the number $n$ of $a$-factors hit):
$$\sum_{s\ge1}T_s=\sum_{n\ge0}\frac1{n!}\Phi^{n+1}_{D_1}\bigl(a^n,F_2(a)\bigr).\tag{W.6}$$

\subsection*{Commentary}
(W.1)–(W.3): no parity assumption on $c_i,\alpha_i$; the only sign, $(-1)^{|\alpha_1||c_2|}$, is the Koszul sign of $\partial_{\alpha_1}$ passing $c_2$; "$a$ even" is used to write $\partial e^a=e^a\partial a$ and to put $e^a$ anywhere. (W.4)–(W.5): the same, for any $r$; $B_K(a)=e^{-a}\partial^Ke^a$ has the parity of $\partial^K$, $\sum_{k\in K}|k|$ (even only for an even multi-index; corrected B′d, critic 026C). The Leibniz/Bell-polynomial expansion (W.4)–(W.5) is an *interpretation* of the terms of (W.7), not its proof: the proof is (5.12) evaluated at $b=F_{D_2}(a)$, and (5.12) is linear in $D$ and combinatorics in the even element $a$, so the parity of $D$ never enters it. (W.6): the brace $\Phi^{n+1}_{D_1}(a^n,b)$ is by (5.2)/(5.12) the connected part of $D_1(a^nb)$ in which $b$ and each $a$ are hit by at least one derivative — this is the $s\ge1$ part of (W.5) re-summed by $n$.
**What Lemma W's $s$ is not.** (W.2) shows that "derivatives of $D_1$ on the output of $D_2$" is strictly larger than W10's symbol contraction number: it also counts derivatives of $D_1$ landing on the $\partial a$-factors of $F_2$. For an odd homogeneous $D_1=D_2=D$ both extra classes cancel inside $D^2=\tfrac12[D,D]$ (the normal-ordered symbol square vanishes by antisymmetry), which is why the classical brace theorem never needs the distinction; for the inhomogeneous $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ they do not (Theorem B′ below).

\subsection*{Result}
$$\boxed{\;e^{-a}D_1D_2e^{a}-F_{D_1}(a)F_{D_2}(a)=\sum_{n\ge0}\frac1{n!}\Phi^{n+1}_{D_1}\bigl(a^n,F_{D_2}(a)\bigr)=\text{[all terms with $\ge1$ derivative of $D_1$ on $F_{D_2}(a)$]}\;}\qquad(a\ \text{even; any parities of }D_1,D_2).\tag{W.7}$$
Checks: B′a's counterexample $D=\partial_{\theta_1}\partial_{\theta_2}$, $a=t\theta_1\theta_2$: $F=-t+ta$, $e^{-a}D^2e^a=0$, $F^2=t^2(1-a)^2=t^2(1-2a)\ne0$, and the right side of (W.7) is $\Phi^1_D(F)+\Phi^2_D(a,F)=-F^2$ (P5): both derivatives of the left $D$ act on the $a$ inside $F$ — a pure "$\partial\partial a$"-type term, $s_{\rm W10}=0$. Free theory: $D=\hat{\tilde Q}+\Theta/\hbar$; the only derivative is $\hat{\tilde Q}$'s, and it acts on $F_2=\hat{\tilde Q}a+\Theta/\hbar$ giving $\hat{\tilde Q}^2a+\hat{\tilde Q}\Theta/\hbar=0$: (W.7) right side $0$, consistent with (5′.8). Machine check W1–W2 below.

#### Theorem B′

\subsection*{Setup}
$D:=\hat{\mathfrak p}=\sum_{k,\ell}\hbar^{k-1}\hat p_{k,\ell}$ acting on a graded-commutative host $A$ (host fixed below; the theorem holds on any graded-commutative algebra on which $D$ is a finite-order differential operator, e.g. $S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$, $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$, or the doubled symbol algebra), $\ell^W_n:=\Phi^n_D$ the Koszul braces (5.2), $F(a)=e^{-a}De^a=\sum_n\frac1{n!}\ell^W_n(a^n)$, $a$ even. $D=D_0+D_1$ its even/odd parts (in the host's grading); $F=F_0+F_1$ accordingly, $F_1^2=0$, $F^2=F_0^2+2F_0F_1$. Curved $L_\infty$ in generating form: $\mathcal R(a):=\sum_{n\ge0}\frac1{n!}\ell^W_{n+1}(a^n,F(a))=0$ for all even $a\in A\otimes\Lambda$, $\Lambda$ an arbitrary auxiliary Grassmann algebra ($D$ and the braces extended $\Lambda$-linearly). Polarisation in an even scalar $t$ of $\mathcal R(ta)$ with $a\in A_{\rm even}$ recovers the multilinear relations on *even* inputs only; odd inputs $x$ are reached through $\eta x$ with $\eta\in\Lambda$ odd, so quantifying over $A\otimes\Lambda$ is equivalent to the multilinear relations $J_n=0$ in every input-parity sector (B′d, critic 026C; counterexample without $\Lambda$: W12).

\subsection*{Derivation}
$$\text{(5′.9)}=\text{(W.7) with }D_1=D_2=D:\qquad \mathcal R(a)=e^{-a}D^2e^a-F(a)^2=F_{D^2}(a)-F_D(a)^2.\tag{B′.1}$$
$$\text{Hence }\ \{\ell^W_n\}\ \text{curved }L_\infty\iff F_{D^2}(a)=F_D(a)^2\ \ \forall a\ \text{even in }A\otimes\Lambda\iff \Phi^n_{D^2}(a^n)=\sum_{m=0}^n\binom nm\,\Phi^m_D(a^m)\,\Phi^{n-m}_D(a^{n-m})\ \ \forall n,\ \forall a\ \text{even in }A\otimes\Lambda\tag{B′.2}$$
(compare coefficients of the formal even parameter in $a\to ta$; polarising over $A\otimes\Lambda$, the braces of $D^2$ are the shuffle square of the braces of $D$ on inputs of both parities). In terms of the parity components, with $D^2=D_0^2+\{D_0,D_1\}+D_1^2$ ($\{\cdot,\cdot\}$ the plain anticommutator, $D_0$ even so $D_0D_1+D_1D_0$ is what appears):
$$\text{(B′.2)}\iff F_{D_1^2}(a)+\bigl[F_{D_0D_1+D_1D_0}(a)-2F_0F_1\bigr]+\bigl[F_{D_0^2}(a)-F_0^2\bigr]=0,\tag{B′.3}$$
where by (W.7) each bracket is the "$\ge1$ derivative of the left factor on the output of the right factor" part of the corresponding product, and $F_{D_1^2}=e^{-a}D_1^2e^a$ is entirely of that type ($F_1^2=0$).
*The symbol form of WB1 is false.* WB1 conjectured $\mathcal R=0\iff(\hat{\mathfrak p}\star\hat{\mathfrak p})_{s\ge1}=0$ with W10's contraction number $s$ (derivatives on coefficients only). Counterexample inside the non-augmented genus-zero family: $D=\hat p_{2,0}=\tfrac12V_2^{ab}\partial_a\partial_b$ (even in every grading, §5′.1 table, constant coefficients). Its symbol square has no contraction: $(D\star D)_{s\ge1}=0$, and the sub-family $\{p_{2,0}\}$ satisfies A′ trivially ($\{V_2(\Psi),V_2(\Psi)\}=0$, no $\chi$). Its braces are $\ell^W_0=0$, $\ell^W_1(a)=\tfrac12V_2^{ab}\partial_a\partial_ba$, $\ell^W_2(a,b)=\pm V_2^{ab}\partial_aa\,\partial_bb$, $\ell^W_{\ge3}=0$; the arity-one relation $\ell^W_1\ell^W_1+\ell^W_2(\ell^W_0,\cdot)=(\ell^W_1)^2=\tfrac14V_2^{ab}V_2^{cd}\partial_a\partial_b\partial_c\partial_d\ne0$ on quartic $a$ (all four derivatives on one factor: the $\partial\partial a$-type term of (W.2), $s_{\rm W10}=0$). So $(\hat{\mathfrak p}\star\hat{\mathfrak p})_{s\ge1}=0$ does **not** imply curved $L_\infty$; the converse direction is *not established* either (no example with $\mathcal R=0$ and $(D\star D)_{s\ge1}\ne0$ is on record — the free theory has both zero), since $\mathcal R$ mixes the $s\ge1$ contractions with the $\partial\partial a$ terms and only the sum is constrained (softened B′d, critic 026C). The correct $s$-statement is (B′.4) below, with Lemma W's $s$ (derivatives on the full output $F(a)$).

\subsection*{Result}
**Theorem B′.** *Let $D=\hat{\mathfrak p}$ be a finite-order differential operator on a graded-commutative algebra $A$ (any parities of its components). Its Koszul braces form a curved $L_\infty$ algebra on $A$ (multilinear relations in every input-parity sector) iff, for every auxiliary Grassmann algebra $\Lambda$ (equivalently, for $\Lambda$ with enough odd generators),*
$$\boxed{\;F_{D^2}(a)=F_D(a)^2\quad\forall a\in (A\otimes\Lambda)_{\rm even}\;}\iff\boxed{\;\bigl[D\cdot D\bigr]_{s_{\rm W}\ge1}(a):=\sum_{n\ge0}\tfrac1{n!}\Phi^{n+1}_D\bigl(a^n,F_D(a)\bigr)=0\quad\forall a\in (A\otimes\Lambda)_{\rm even},\;}\tag{B′.4}$$
*where $s_{\rm W}$ counts derivatives of the left factor acting on the output $F_D(a)$ of the right factor (Lemma W); equivalently the braces of $D^2$ factorise as in (B′.2). Restricting the quantifier to $a\in A_{\rm even}$ gives the relations on even inputs only (counterexample W12). Special cases: (i) $D$ odd homogeneous: $F^2=0$ and (B′.4) $\iff D^2=0$ on $A\otimes\Lambda$, i.e. $D^2=0$ on all of $A$ — the classical brace theorem (5.13)–(5.14), now with its hypothesis stated. (ii) $D$ even homogeneous: (B′.4) $\iff F_{D^2}=F_D^2$, which is not $D^2=0$ (free theory §5′.2, counterexample P5, $\hat p_{2,0}$ above). (iii) $D=\hat{\mathfrak p}$ inhomogeneous: (B′.3).* Parity assumptions used: only "$a$ even" ((5.12), (W.4)), with $a$ ranging over $A\otimes\Lambda$; no homogeneity of $D$. Lemma W holds verbatim on $A\otimes\Lambda$ (it is (5.12), linear in $D$, on a graded-commutative algebra), so the extension costs nothing.
**Proven** (Lemma W + (5′.9)); machine check W3 (toy) below. The $\hbar$-graded corollary and the role of A′: at brace arity $n$ the terms of $\mathcal R$ coming from $\hat p_{k_2,\ell_2}\hat p_{k_1,\ell_1}$ carry $\hbar^{k_1+k_2-2}$ and consume $n=k_1+k_2-s_{\rm W}$ inputs, so $\mathcal R_n=\sum_{s_{\rm W}\ge1}\hbar^{n+s_{\rm W}-2}(\cdots)$ — (5.16) with $s\to s_{\rm W}$ — and the leading term, $\hbar^{n-1}$, is $s_{\rm W}=1$. This $s_{\rm W}=1$ term contains the $s_{\rm W10}=1$ contraction (A′, (3.29)) **plus** the one-derivative-on-$\partial a$ terms of the even–even and even–odd products (for odd–odd products they cancel). Hence the corollary "A′ ⇒ curved $L_\infty$ to leading order in $\hbar$ at every arity" is **not** established by A′ alone; it holds iff the $s_{\rm W}=1$, $s_{\rm W10}=0$ terms vanish, which on single-string inputs is automatic ($\partial\partial x=0$: Lemma B1's $\ell^W_n(\Psi_1..\Psi_n)=\hbar^{n-1}\sum_\ell p_{n,\ell}$ survives) but on multi-string inputs is an additional condition, tested on the toy below (W3/W4). Correspondingly the first obstruction is not (5.17) alone: (5.17) is the $s_{\rm W10}=2$ part of the $\hbar^{n}$ term, to which the $s_{\rm W}=2$, $s_{\rm W10}\le1$ terms add.

#### Free theory and toy check

\subsection*{Verification}
Script block `part_b_parity_checks` (B′b legs), `.venv/bin/python codes/verify-linfty-ibl-repackaging.py`, run 2026-09-14 (B′d): `summary: 99/99 PASS (seed 20260908)`. Scope (B′d, critic 026C): what is machine-checked is the *identity* (B′.1) for inhomogeneous $D$ (W1, W1′, W3), the free-theory instance (P4a–e), the counterexamples (P5, W2, W5) and (5′.10) (W4); the multilinear iff of Theorem B′ itself is proven, not machine-checked. W1: Lemma W (W.7) on random normal-ordered operators of order $\le2$ with coefficients of mixed parity on $\mathbb C[x_1,x_2|\theta_1,\theta_2]$, even $a$ of degree 2 with odd–odd monomials, 3 trials (max $|e^{-a}D_1D_2e^a-F_1F_2|=60.7$, equal to the brace sum to $10^{-9}$). W1′ break-on-purpose: replacing $F_1F_2$ by $F_2F_1$ (the Koszul sign of the odd parts) makes the leg FAIL — the sign dressing is load-bearing. W2: $D=\hat p_{2,0}=\tfrac12V_2^{ab}\partial_a\partial_b$ on $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ of the 8-strand toy, quartic even $a$: $D(1)=0$, $|\ell^W_1\ell^W_1a|=0.23\ne0$ while $(D\star D)_{s\ge1}=0$ identically (constant coefficients) — WB1's symbol form refuted on the machine as well as by hand. The leg is carried by $|\ell^W_1\ell^W_1a|$, which is truncation-free; the script's $|\mathcal R(a)|$ clause is computed with `exp_conj(..., deg=8)` on a quartic $a$, where $a^{\ge3}$ exceeds the degree cap while $D^2$ lowers the degree by 4, so its value (6762) is a possible truncation artefact and is not relied on (critic 026C). W3: the identity (B′.1), $\mathcal R(a)=F_{D^2}(a)-F_D(a)^2$, for random inhomogeneous $D$ of order $\le2$ with odd generators present, 3 trials, residual nonzero (max 2.57) and equal to both sides — same code path as W1 with $D_1=D_2$; it checks the identity, not the implication in either direction. W5 (B′d): the critic's quantifier counterexample, $A=\Lambda(\theta)$, $D=\theta\partial_\theta$: the old criterion over $A_{\rm even}$ (constants) holds vacuously ($|{\rm residual}|=0$) while $|(\ell^W_1)^2\theta|=1\ne0$; the corrected criterion over $(A\otimes\Lambda(\eta))_{\rm even}$, $a=\eta\theta$, has residual $\mathcal R(a)=a\ne0$, as it must. W5′ break-on-purpose: for $D'=\partial_\theta$ (odd, $D'^2=0$, genuine $L_\infty$ braces) the corrected residual is zero, so asserting it nonzero FAILS. Free theory: P4e (B′a) is the case $\mathcal R=0$, $F_{D^2}=F^2\ne0$.
**Not done (time box):** the brief's leg "Theorem B′ on the toy's genus-zero family truncated to $W_{\le3}$, residual vs (5.17) computed independently". Obstruction found while setting it up: the extension $\hat p_{k,\ell}$ of the *unsuspended* tensors to $S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$ is not the coderivation with the $L_\infty$ signs — on $S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$ the odd components ($k$ odd) need the décalage signs of $\mathcal H\to\mathcal H[1]$ to satisfy $[\hat Q,\hat\ell_2]=0$ with the graded commutator, and the record's $\hat p_{k,\ell}$ (5.1) does not specify them. Until that is fixed (WB2 (c)), an interacting toy on $S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$ would test the sign convention, not Theorem B′. Consequently the corollary's status (Theorem B′, last paragraph) is: *conditional* — A′ gives the $s_{\rm W10}=1$ part of the $\hbar^{n-1}$ term; whether the $s_{\rm W}=1,s_{\rm W10}=0$ terms of the even–even/even–odd products vanish on multi-string inputs is open (WB2 (b)). On single-string inputs they are absent and Lemma B1 stands.

#### Host space

**Decision (one paragraph).** Theorem B′ itself is host-independent: it needs only a graded-commutative algebra on which $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ is a finite-order differential operator and an even $a$. What the host decides is *which* operator $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ is (its Koszul signs) and whether the components survive. (i) $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ hosts inputs correctly but kills the $\bar\varepsilon$-symmetric output pairs of $m_2,\hat p_{1,2},\hat p_{0,2}$ on physical inputs (P3): lossy, so a theorem there is about the projected structure. (ii) $S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$ hosts outputs faithfully, mis-symmetrises inputs, and needs the décalage signs (above) for the odd components. (iii) The doubled symbol algebra $\mathbb C[\chi]_{\bar\varepsilon}\otimes\mathbb C[\Psi]_{\varepsilon}$ with $\Psi\to\hbar\tilde\Delta\partial_\chi$ is MS's bookkeeping and CFL's $\star$-product setting; it hosts both slot symmetries by construction (1.7)/(1.5) and it is where A′ was verified (7c). **Recommendation (bookkeeping only; the host question is open pending WB2 (c)).** The doubled symbol algebra (iii) is the convenient bookkeeping: $\hat{\mathfrak p}=\hbar^{-1}\overrightarrow{\mathbb H}$ is unambiguous as a *symbol* in two sets of variables, and the free-theory test (§5′.2, $W_3$) is its $\chi$-part. But once $\Psi\to\hbar\tilde\Delta\partial_\chi$ is performed the *operator* whose braces Theorem B′ is about acts on $\mathbb C[\chi]_{\bar\varepsilon}=S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$ — that is host (ii): inputs are $\bar\varepsilon$-symmetrised and the odd components need the same décalage signs as in (ii) (WB2 (c)). So (iii) does not remove the obstruction attributed to (ii); it is (ii) with cleaner symbol bookkeeping (critic 026C, B′d). Theorem B′ itself is host-independent; every host-specific statement about the braces of $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ (Lemma B1's dictionary, (5.25), (5.27)–(5.28)) holds up to the décalage signs of WB2 (c). On single-string inputs $\chi_a$ the braces reduce to $\ell^W_n(\chi_{a_1}..\chi_{a_n})=\hbar^{n-1}\sum_\ell p_{n,\ell}$ (Lemma B1, up to those signs). Options (i)–(ii) and their obstructions are recorded in WB2.

### §5′.4 Consequences for §5.4–§5.6 (B′c)

**Restated task (B′c).** Wording and consequences only, no new derivation: one paragraph per old sub-section, verdict first (survives / survives with restriction / withdrawn / open), reason from §5′.1–§5′.3.

#### §5.4 HS instance

**Verdict: survives with restriction (the slice computation (5.25) and the R11 statement stand; the comparison as written on $W=S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ is empty on the physical sector and is restated on the doubled symbol algebra); the negative "(b) is not a brace structure" is now proven in general (W4).** (i) On $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$, $m_2(\Psi,\Psi)$ for physical $\Psi$ has ghost-$(3,3)$, equal-parity, $\bar\varepsilon$-symmetric outputs and projects to zero (§5′.1 table, P3; W4 confirms $|m_2(\Psi\Psi)|_{S_\varepsilon}=10^{-14}$ on the toy), so (5.20)'s $m_2(\Psi,\Psi)$ and the "source" term of the comparison vanish there. The comparison (5.18)–(5.25) is therefore to be read on the recommended host (§5′.3, option (iii)): the doubled symbol algebra, where $\hat{\mathfrak p}=\hbar^{-1}\overrightarrow{\mathbb H}$ and the braces on single-string inputs $\chi_a$ are $\hbar^{n-1}\sum_\ell p_{n,\ell}$ (Lemma B1, up to the décalage signs of WB2 (c)); on that host every term of (5.18)–(5.22) is nonzero and the term-by-term dictionary (5.25) — each HS product is a single re-weighted brace $\Phi^k_{\hat p_{k,\ell}}$, $k+\ell\in\{3,4\}$ — stands as a computation, with "brace of $\hat{\mathfrak p}$" now meaning the Koszul brace of an inhomogeneous operator (Theorem B′), no longer of an odd one. (ii) *"(b) is not a brace structure", now general.* The old argument covered only $D$ from the $\tilde\Delta$-raised family. The critic's one-line test settles it for any $D$: if (b) were the brace structure of some $D$, then $D=\ell^{(b)}_1$ (the arity-one brace is $D-D(1)$, and $D(1)=\ell^{(b)}_0=0$) and $\ell^{(b)}_2=\Phi^2_{\ell^{(b)}_1}$. With $\ell^{(b)}_1=Q$ on $E_1$, $-\tfrac1{12}m_2$ on $E_2$, nothing else (5.23), and $\Psi$ even,
$$\Phi^2_{\ell^{(b)}_1}(\Psi,\Psi)=\ell^{(b)}_1(\Psi\Psi)-\ell^{(b)}_1(\Psi)\Psi-\Psi\,\ell^{(b)}_1(\Psi)=-\tfrac1{12}m_2(\Psi\Psi)-2\,\Psi\,Q\Psi\ \ne\ \ell_2(\Psi,\Psi)+\tfrac16m_2(\Psi\Psi)=\ell^{(b)}_2(\Psi,\Psi),\tag{5′.10}$$
the two sides differing in the $E_1$ component ($\ell_2(\Psi,\Psi)$ vs $0$), in the $E_2$ component ($-2\Psi Q\Psi$ vs $0$) and in the $m_2$ coefficient ($-\tfrac1{12}$ vs $+\tfrac16$). Arithmetic verified on the toy: **W4** ($|\Phi^2-{\rm rhs}|=0$, $|\Phi^2-\ell^{(b)}_2|=21.4$ on $S_\varepsilon$, where the $m_2$ terms are absent and the $E_1/E_2$ mismatch alone is seen; the coefficient mismatch is by hand, (5.23)). Verdict: **proven** — presentation (b) is not the Koszul brace structure of any operator on $W$, for any host and any parity of $D$ (the test uses only $D(1)=0$ and $\Psi$ even). (iii) The remaining content of §5.4 — items (i)–(vi) of its commentary, the non-consistency of the truncation to $W_{\le2}$ for the braces, $(\ell^{(b)}_1)^2|_{E_2}=\tfrac1{144}m_2^2\ne0$ — uses only the brace *products* (Lemma B1) and $S_{\rm HS}$, not the $L_\infty$ property, and survives; the phrase "the brace structure by contrast is exact iff $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0$ (Theorem B)" in the (5.25) commentary is **withdrawn** and replaced by "iff $F_{\hat{\mathfrak p}^2}(a)=F_{\hat{\mathfrak p}}(a)^2$ (Theorem B′, (B′.4))". (iv) Independent of §5 and standing (critic, R11): the HS gauge algebra uses only the $\min(k,\ell)=1$ relations; $(2,2,0)$ never enters; cyclicity of (b) via $\omega_1\oplus\omega_2^{\rm symp}$ (R7) is a property of $S_{\rm HS}$, not of the braces.

#### §5.5 cyclicity

**Verdict: (5.26)–(5.29) survive (they use only the brace *products*, not the $L_\infty$ property); (5.30) survives with its criterion corrected.** (i) *Products only.* The $\hbar$-weight argument (5.26) uses the filtration $\hbar^{k-1}$ of $\hat p_{k,\ell}$; the components (5.27)–(5.28) are the arity-two braces $\Phi^2$ of $\hat p_{2,2}$, $\hat p_{3,1}$, $\hat p_{2,0}$ on the stated inputs (Lemma B1 (5.6), which §5′.3 confirms on single-string inputs and whose multi-string rule is combinatorial, B1c); the Frobenius argument (5.29) uses $\Phi^2_D(a,b)=D(ab)-D(a)b\mp aD(b)+D(1)ab$ and the strand-wise self-adjointness of the $\tilde\Delta$-raised family. None of these steps invokes $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0$, the parity homogeneity of $\hat{\mathfrak p}$, or the curved $L_\infty$ relations; they are statements about the multilinear maps $\{\ell^W_n\}$, whatever relations those maps satisfy. Hence the obstruction — no degree-diagonal $\omega_W=\bigoplus c_n\omega_n$ makes $\omega_W(a_0,\ell^W_n(a_1..a_n))$ cyclic, seen at arity 2 through the $\hat p_{2,0}$ term $\omega_1(\sigma_1,Qb)\,\omega_1(a,\sigma_2)$ of (5.28), which has no partner in (5.27) — **stands**, with the same status as before: hand computation, not machine-checked (Verification (7d) (ii)). Host caveat: on $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ the $m_2$ output of (5.27) projects to zero (§5′.1), which only strengthens the mismatch; on the faithful hosts the Koszul dressing of (5.27)–(5.28) awaits the décalage signs (WB2 (c)) — the *nonvanishing* of the unpaired $\hat p_{2,0}$ term is insensitive to those signs (it is a single product of two non-degenerate pairings), the precise sign of each term is not. (ii) *(5.30) corrected.* $\mathcal S(\Phi,\Phi^\vee)=\langle\Phi^\vee,e^{-\Phi}\hat{\mathfrak p}e^\Phi\rangle=\sum_n\frac1{n!}\langle\Phi^\vee,\ell^W_n(\Phi^n)\rangle$ is the multiplier action of the braces, and $(\mathcal S,\mathcal S)=0$ iff the $\{\ell^W_n\}$ satisfy the curved $L_\infty$ relations (standard cotangent-lift statement, true for any family of graded-symmetric maps). The old right-hand side "$\iff\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0$" is **withdrawn** and replaced by "$\iff F_{\hat{\mathfrak p}^2}(a)=F_{\hat{\mathfrak p}}(a)^2$ for all even $a$ (Theorem B′, (B′.4))". With this correction the conclusion of §5.5 — the brace family is not cyclic on $W$ for any degree-diagonal pairing and becomes cyclic on $T^*W$, whose action is the multiplier form of the IBL MC equation — is unchanged in form; what changed is the condition under which $\mathcal S$ is a solution of the classical master equation. (iii) Also unchanged: R7's shift is the parity part of a pairing on $E_2$, needed and not sufficient for cyclicity of the braces; R13's Route C shares the cotangent mechanism but is not (5.30). These are statements about pairings and products, independent of Theorem B/B′.

#### §5.6 reverse characterisation and literature

**Verdict: the brace-type test survives with the criterion corrected; the link "brace-type $L_\infty$ on $W$ $\Rightarrow$ IBL$_\infty$ data on $\mathcal H$" is withdrawn as stated and is open; the MC identification survives as a statement about *form*; (5.10) survives; the literature paragraph is amended.** (i) *Reverse characterisation, restated.* An $L_\infty$ structure $\{\ell^W_n\}$ on a graded-commutative host is *of brace type* iff, with $D:=\ell^W_1+\ell^W_0\cdot$, one has $\ell^W_n=\Phi^n_D$ for all $n$ — unchanged, a definition. By Theorem B′ such a $D$ satisfies $F_{D^2}(a)=F_D(a)^2$ for all even $a$ ((B′.4)); the old continuation "then $D=\hat{\mathfrak p}$ … and $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0$ gives the full IBL$_\infty$ relations" is **withdrawn**: (B′.4) does not imply $D^2=0$ (free theory, §5′.2: curved $L_\infty$ braces with $D^2\ne0$), and neither $D^2=0$ nor the symbol-level $(D\star D)_{s\ge1}=0$ is equivalent to (B′.4) (P5, W2). What (B′.4) says about the components $p_{k,\ell}=\Phi^k_{\hat p_{k,\ell}}|_{\rm single\ strings}$ is: at order $\hbar^{n-1}$ the $s_{\rm W10}=1$ contractions — the genus-zero relations of A′ — **plus** the $s_{\rm W}=1,s_{\rm W10}=0$ terms vanish (Theorem B′, last paragraph); on single-string inputs the latter are absent, so A′ is the single-string content of (B′.4) at leading order, and beyond that the correspondence with CFL's IBL$_\infty$ relations (contracted terms with CFL's sign bookkeeping) is **open** (WB2 (b); "Open question" below). The negative half stands: a non-brace structure (HS (β), Route C (γ)) defines no such $D$ (W4 for (β)), while its single-string slice may still be $L_\infty$. (ii) *Gauge parameter $\Lambda_0$.* $\ell^W_1(\Lambda_0)=D(\Lambda_0)-D(1)\Lambda_0=0$ for a constant $\Lambda_0$ is a brace identity, independent of any relation: survives. (iii) *MC equation (5.9)–(5.10).* $F(a)=e^{-a}\hat{\mathfrak p}e^a=0\iff\hat{\mathfrak p}(e^a)=0$ is an identity of the braces (Lemma B1/(5.4), proven for any $D$ and even $a$); its identification with MS's $\mathfrak L(e^{\mathfrak c})=0$ is an identification of **form** (same equation on the same generating object), not a theorem that the braces are MS's $L_\infty$ algebra — MS never state one, and the braces are $L_\infty$ only under (B′.4). (5.10) — on single strings the A′ family's MC equation forces $S\equiv0$, so it is not the EOM — survives (critic: correct). (iv) *Literature, amended.* Known: the brace theorem for an **odd** square-zero operator (BDA, Akman, Voronov) — now the special case (i) of Theorem B′; the inhomogeneous case (Theorem B′, Lemma W) is not in the caches and we have not located it in the literature (not claimed new beyond "not found"). Implicit (MS §IV.B, CFL Rem. 7.3): the MC equation in the form (5.9). New here, with corrected status: Lemma B1's dictionary (5.7) (single strings, up to décalage signs) — stands; the $\hbar$-filtration (5.16) — **corrected**: $\mathcal R_n=\sum_{s_{\rm W}\ge1}\hbar^{n+s_{\rm W}-2}(\cdots)$ with Lemma W's $s_{\rm W}$, so "genus zero $=$ leading $\hbar$ order" holds on single-string inputs only; the non-cyclicity obstruction and its cotangent resolution — stand (§5.5 above); the placement of HS as the re-weighted $V_4$-slice and of Route C as cotangent-type — stand (products-level). Not established, unchanged: a cyclic $L_\infty$ on $W$ itself from the $p_{k,\ell}$ with a physical action beyond HS/Route C (§6 4(d)).

#### Open question (multi-string leading order)

**Status: open (WB2 (b)); not for the ledger.** *Statement.* Let $\hat{\mathfrak p}=\sum_{k,\ell}\hbar^{k-1}\hat p_{k,\ell}$ be the coderivation extension of the non-augmented $\tilde\Delta$-raised family of a cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra (Theorem A′ holds), realised on the doubled symbol algebra with its Koszul signs fixed (WB2 (c)). Write the $L_\infty$ residual (B′.1) at brace arity $n$ as $\mathcal R_n=\hbar^{n-1}\mathcal R_n^{(1)}+\hbar^{n}\mathcal R_n^{(2)}+\cdots$ (Theorem B′, last paragraph). A′ is the statement that the $s_{\rm W10}=1$ part of $\mathcal R_n^{(1)}$ vanishes at every $n$. **Question:** does the remainder of $\mathcal R^{(1)}_n$ — the terms of Lemma W with exactly one derivative of the left factor landing on a $\partial a$-factor produced by the right factor (the $\partial\partial a$-type terms of (W.2), $s_{\rm W}=1$, $s_{\rm W10}=0$), which arise from the even–even and even–odd component products and are absent on single-string inputs — vanish on multi-string inputs $a\in E_{\ge2}$ for this family? If yes: "A′ $\Rightarrow$ curved $L_\infty$ on $W$ to leading order in $\hbar$ at every arity" holds and the corrected Theorem B for the paper is (B′.4) with this $\hbar$-corollary. If no: the corollary holds on single-string inputs only (Lemma B1, exact) and fails on $W$ beyond them; $\hat p_{2,0}$ (W2) shows that nothing in the *form* of the family forbids the failure — there $\mathcal R\ne0$ with $(D\star D)_{s\ge1}=0$ — but $\hat p_{2,0}$ alone is not the full family, whose cross terms may cancel. *What would settle it.* The interacting-family toy leg of B′b's brief, redesigned: build $D=\hat{\tilde Q}+\hbar\hat\ell_2+\hbar^{-1}\Theta+\hbar\hat p_{1,2}+\hbar\,\hat m_2$ (all $k+\ell\le4$ components, $V_2,V_3,V_4$ of the 12-strand generator) as a differential operator on the doubled symbol algebra `GPoly`, with the décalage signs of $\hat p_{k,\ell}$ fixed first by requiring $[\hat{\tilde Q},\hat\ell_2]=0$ and $[\hat{\tilde Q},\hat\ell_3]+\tfrac12[\hat\ell_2,\hat\ell_2]=0$ as graded commutators (WB2 (c), a sign-fixing step, not a theorem); take even $a$ of strand degree 2 and 3 with both parities of strands present; extract the $\hbar^{n-1}$ coefficient of $\mathcal R_n(a)$ for $n=1,2,3$ and compare with the $s_{\rm W10}=1$ contraction alone (which A′ makes vanish, 7c). A nonzero difference settles "no" (one counterexample suffices); zero on the toy is evidence for "yes" and calls for the hand argument (does the graded symmetry of $V_n$ make the even–even $\partial\partial a$ terms cancel in pairs, as antisymmetry does for odd–odd?). *Size estimate.* Sign-fixing (c): one computator sub-task of the B′b kind, ≈30–40 min including the two commutator legs; the leg itself: ≈20 min once (c) is in place; a hand proof of "yes", if the toy points there: a further Lemma-W-type sub-task, ≈45 min. Not needed for the paper's IBL section if the paper states Theorem B′ and the single-string corollary only (recommended wording in §7).

### §5′.5 Décalage signs of $\hat p_{k,\ell}$ (WB2 (c), 2026-09-14)

**Restated task.** Fix the sign rule under which the extensions $\hat p_{k,\ell}$ of the toy's cyclic $L_\infty$ data ($V_2,V_3,V_4$ of `build_generator`, $(S,S)=0$) to a polynomial algebra `GPoly` satisfy the $L_\infty$ relations as graded commutators of differential operators, i.e. $[\hat Q,\hat\ell_2]=0$ and $[\hat Q,\hat\ell_3]+\tfrac12[\hat\ell_2,\hat\ell_2]=0$ with $\hat\ell_n:=\widehat{\tilde\Delta\circ V_{n+1}}$, $\hat Q=\widehat{\tilde\Delta\circ V_2}=\hat{\tilde Q}$; deliver a signed constructor `phat(k, l, tensor)` for WB2 (b). **Objective:** make the multi-string leading-order test (WB2 (b), decision D-a) a test of Theorem B′ rather than of a convention. Hosts considered: (i) $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ (`GPoly(PAR)`, W4's `gh`) and (ii) $S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$ (`GPoly(1-PAR)`, the $\chi$-part of the doubled symbol algebra of §5′.3). Conventions: physical parity per slot as in §5′.1; `GPoly.deriv` is the left derivative; "naive extension" = the coderivation (5.1) reading a monomial's variables as inputs in the order written.

**Free constants.** (1) Relative normalisation $\hat Q:\hat\ell_2:\hat\ell_3$ — fixed by the generator ($S=\sum V_n/n!$, $\ell_n=\tilde\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$, checks 3a–3e), not fitted. (2) Normalisation of the extension: $1/(k!\,\ell!)$ per component (coderivation of a symmetric tensor), fixed by requiring $\hat\ell_k(\chi_{a_1}\cdots\chi_{a_k})=\ell_k(e_{a_1},\dots,e_{a_k})$ (check W6a below). (3) No anchor fitted from the relations under test.

#### Candidate sign rules

**Operator form.** For a tensor $P^{o_1\dots o_\ell}{}_{a_1\dots a_k}$ ($\ell$ raised outputs, $k$ input slots, $P=\tilde\Delta^{\otimes\ell}\circ V_{k+\ell}$ as in `raise_map(..., weighted=True)`), the extension to `GPoly` is the differential operator
$$\hat p_{k,\ell}=\frac1{k!\,\ell!}\sum_{o,a}\ s_{\rm rule}(o,a)\ P^{o_1\dots o_\ell}{}_{a_1\dots a_k}\ \chi_{o_1}\cdots\chi_{o_\ell}\ \partial_{a_k}\cdots\partial_{a_1},\tag{5′.11}$$
with left derivatives, the first input slot innermost (so that on a monomial it evaluates the tensor on the variables in the order written — the coderivation (5.1) — check W6a), and $s_{\rm rule}=\pm1$ a per-component dressing depending on the slot parities only. Three candidates, on each of the two hosts:

- **(a) naive**: $s=1$. All Koszul signs come from the left derivatives and the graded product of the host.
- **(b) left décalage on inputs**: $s=(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^k(i-1)\varepsilon_{a_i}}$ — the sign of $s^{\otimes k}$ applied to the input strand $(x_1,\dots,x_k)$ (Lada–Markl convention, $\mathcal H\to\mathcal H[1]$ slot by slot); outputs undressed ($\ell=1$ here).
- **(c) right décalage on inputs**: $s=(-1)^{\sum_{i=1}^k(k-i)\varepsilon_{a_i}+k(k-1)/2}$ — the other standard décalage (suspensions pulled out to the right, $\varepsilon(\sigma)$-type sign $(-1)^{k(k-1)/2}$). (b) and (c) differ by $(-1)^{(k-1)\sum_i\varepsilon_{a_i}+k(k-1)/2}$; since $\sum_i\varepsilon_{a_i}=\varepsilon_o+\pi(\ell_k)$ with $\pi(\ell_k)=1$, this is a dressing depending on the output parity, so (b) and (c) are genuinely different operators for $k\ge2$.

**Hosts.** (i) $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$: $\chi_a$ of parity $\varepsilon_a$; $\hat\ell_k$ has parity $\varepsilon_o+\sum\varepsilon_a=\pi(\ell_k)=1$ for every $k$ (table §5′.1, column (a)): all three operators odd, the graded commutator is the anticommutator and the relations are $D^2=0$, $D=\hat Q+\hat\ell_2+\hat\ell_3$, degree by degree. (ii) $S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$: $\chi_a$ of parity $\bar\varepsilon_a$; $\hat\ell_k$ has parity $k\bmod2$ (column (b)): $\hat Q,\hat\ell_3$ odd, $\hat\ell_2$ even, so $\tfrac12[\hat\ell_2,\hat\ell_2]\equiv0$ identically and "$[\hat Q,\hat\ell_3]+\tfrac12[\hat\ell_2,\hat\ell_2]$" cannot contain the $\ell_2\circ\ell_2$ term; on (ii) the test is run both as graded commutators (the brief's literal form) and as $D^2=0$ (the form that has the right content).

**Expected failure modes (written before running).** On (ii) a symmetry obstruction is visible by hand: $\partial_a\partial_b=(-1)^{\bar\varepsilon_a\bar\varepsilon_b}\partial_b\partial_a$, while the input pair of $P$ is $\varepsilon$-symmetric; for $\varepsilon_a=\varepsilon_b$ the two symmetries are opposite, so the contraction of an equal-parity input pair vanishes for *any* per-component dressing $s(o,a)$ that is symmetric under $a\leftrightarrow b$, and a position-dependent dressing such as (b)/(c) makes the pair $\bar\varepsilon$-*anti*symmetric (§1.2 (iii): the décalage turns $\varepsilon$-symmetric into $\bar\varepsilon$-antisymmetric, i.e. $S_\varepsilon\to\Lambda_{\bar\varepsilon}$, not $S_{\bar\varepsilon}$), still killed. Prediction: on (ii) $\hat\ell_2(\Psi\Psi)=0$ for physical $\Psi$ under all three rules (degenerate host for inputs), and the relations fail or hold vacuously on the surviving opposite-parity sectors. On (i) the naive rule (a) is the standard coderivation extension of an $L_\infty$ algebra to its symmetric coalgebra, expected to pass exactly (each $\hat\ell_k$ has one $\chi$, so every product $\hat\ell_k\hat\ell_m$ has at most one contraction: no $\hbar^2$ terms); (b)/(c) on (i) insert a sign that is not a coboundary of the relations and are expected to fail at arity 2 already ($[\hat Q,\hat\ell_2]$ with $\hat\ell_2$ dressed by $(-1)^{\varepsilon_{a_2}}$ on the $Q$-on-second-slot term). "Failure" for the record = a nonzero component of $D^2$ or of the graded commutator on some random element of degree $\le3$ with odd generators present.

#### Fixing by $[\hat Q,\hat\ell_2]=0$

Script `part_b_decalage_checks` (`.venv/bin/python codes/verify-linfty-ibl-repackaging.py`, run 2026-09-14): `summary: 103/103 PASS (seed 20260908)` (99 previous unchanged + W6a, W6, W6′, W6″). Family: $\hat Q,\hat\ell_2,\hat\ell_3$ from $\tilde\Delta\circ V_{2,3,4}$ (`raise_map(weighted=True)`, the 3a–3e family), extended by (5′.11); test elements $f$: random sums of degrees 1, 2, 3 in both parities (odd generators present), 3 seeds; $\Psi$ random physical ($N_{\rm gh}=2$). Operator parities in the host read off the tensors: (i) all odd; (ii) $\hat Q,\hat\ell_3$ odd, $\hat\ell_2$ even (as §5′.1 predicts). Max norms over seeds:

| host | family | rule | $\lvert[\hat Q,\hat\ell_2]\rvert$ | $\lvert\hat Q\hat\ell_2+\hat\ell_2\hat Q\rvert$ | $\lvert\hat\ell_2 f\rvert$ | $\lvert\hat\ell_2(\Psi\Psi)\rvert$ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (i) $S_\varepsilon$ | $\tilde\Delta$ | (a) | $2.8\cdot10^{-14}$ | same | 111 | 14 |
| (i) | $\tilde\Delta$ | (b) | 50 | same | 56 | 4.2 |
| (i) | $\tilde\Delta$ | (c) | 52 | same | 44 | 31 |
| (i) | plain $\Delta$ | (a) | 59 | same | 87 | 15 |
| (ii) $S_{\bar\varepsilon}$ | $\tilde\Delta$ | (a) | 60 | 75 | 45 | $0$ |
| (ii) | $\tilde\Delta$ | (b) | $0$ (vacuous) | $0$ | $\mathbf 0$ | $0$ |
| (ii) | $\tilde\Delta$ | (c) | $0$ (vacuous) | $0$ | $\mathbf 0$ | $0$ |

Arity 2 already selects: on $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ only the naive rule (a) closes; (b), (c) and the plain-$\Delta$ family fail at $O(50)$ (W6′). On $S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$ the predicted degeneracy is exact and stronger than predicted: rule (a) kills the equal-parity input pairs ($\hat\ell_2(\Psi\Psi)=0$, W6″) and its surviving opposite-parity part does not close ($[\hat Q,\hat\ell_2]=60$); rules (b)/(c) make $\hat\ell_2\equiv0$ *entirely* — the décalage turns the $\varepsilon$-symmetric input pair into a $\bar\varepsilon$-antisymmetric one for every parity combination (§1.2 (iii): $S_\varepsilon\to\Lambda_{\bar\varepsilon}$), and the $\bar\varepsilon$-symmetric $\partial_a\partial_b$ then annihilates it — so the relations hold vacuously there. No sign dressing rescues host (ii): the obstruction is the symmetry type of the derivatives, not a sign.

#### Arity-3 check

Same runs, second relation. Host (i), rule (a): $\lvert[\hat Q,\hat\ell_3]+\tfrac12[\hat\ell_2,\hat\ell_2]\rvert=1.6\cdot10^{-13}$ with $\lvert\hat\ell_2\hat\ell_2f\rvert=159$ (non-vacuous; the $\ell_2\circ\ell_2$ term is $O(100)$ and is cancelled by $\hat Q\hat\ell_3+\hat\ell_3\hat Q$), W6. Rejected: (b) 59, (c) 64, plain $\Delta$ 89 (W6′). Host (ii), rule (a): the graded commutator $[\hat Q,\hat\ell_3]$ vanishes ($6\cdot10^{-15}$) but $\tfrac12[\hat\ell_2,\hat\ell_2]\equiv0$ for even $\hat\ell_2$ while $\hat\ell_2\hat\ell_2f=34\ne0$: the graded-commutator form of the arity-3 relation is parity-ill-posed on (ii) (it cannot contain the $\ell_2\circ\ell_2$ term), and the $D^2$ form $\hat Q\hat\ell_3+\hat\ell_3\hat Q+\hat\ell_2\hat\ell_2$ is $34\ne0$. Rules (b), (c) on (ii): vacuous ($\hat\ell_2=\hat\ell_3=0$). Sectors covered: input elements of degrees 1–3, both parities, 12 strands; the $\varepsilon$-symmetric extension of $\hat\ell_3$ on monomials with repeated and equal-parity variables is exercised by W6a and by the random elements.

#### Sign rule (result)

\subsection*{Result}
$$\boxed{\ \hat p_{k,\ell}=\frac1{k!\,\ell!}\sum_{o,a}P^{o_1\dots o_\ell}{}_{a_1\dots a_k}\ \chi_{o_1}\cdots\chi_{o_\ell}\ \partial_{a_k}\cdots\partial_{a_1}\quad\text{on }S_\varepsilon\mathcal H=\mathbb C[\chi]_\varepsilon,\qquad s_{\rm rule}\equiv1\ }\tag{5′.12}$$
i.e. **no extra décalage sign**: the operators carry only the Koszul signs of the host's graded product and left derivatives, with the first input slot innermost. Relative to the naive extension of W4 (`Qhat = gh.derivation(toy.Q)`, `ell2`, `m2hat`) the only changes are (1) the family is the $\tilde\Delta$-raised one ($\hat Q=\hat{\tilde Q}$, $\tilde Q=\mathrm{diag}(-1)^\varepsilon Q$, not $Q$ — W4's `Qhat` used the bare $Q$, which is the plain-$\Delta$ family and fails, row 4 of the table) and (2) $k$ inputs are read by $k$ left derivatives in slot order, not by exponent look-up as `m2hat` does (the look-up drops the derivative's Koszul sign on odd variables). **Status: Verified at arity $\le3$ on the 12-strand toy (W6, W6′, W6″; 3 seeds; both parity sectors), for $\ell=1$.** Interpretation: on $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ this is the textbook coderivation extension of an $L_\infty$ algebra whose products are graded symmetric in the *same* parity as the host's variables (physical parity = the $L[1]$ parity, §1.2 rule (i)); no suspension is needed because $\mathcal H_{\rm phys}$ is already the suspended space. It is *not* the décalage of §1.1–§1.2 ($\mathcal H\to\mathcal H[1]$): both décalage dressings (b), (c) fail on (i), and on (ii) — the host where a décalage would be the natural device — they annihilate the operators. The décalage signs anticipated in WB2 (c) do not exist as a repair of host (ii); what B′b observed there ("$[\hat Q,\hat\ell_2]\ne0$ on $S_{\bar\varepsilon}$") is the input-symmetry obstruction of §1.2 (iii), not a missing sign.

\subsection*{Verification}
W6a: (5′.11)/(5′.12) on monomials equals the raised tensor in the order written, including an equal-parity pair (3,3) and a triple — fixes the normalisation $1/k!$ independently of the relations. W6: both relations to $10^{-13}$, non-vacuous ($\ell_2\circ\ell_2$ term 159). W6′ (break-on-purpose): (b), (c), plain $\Delta$ each fail at $O(50)$. W6″: host (ii) degeneracy. Not checked: $\ell\ge2$ components (no relation at arity $\le3$ with $\ell=1$ involves them), $k=0$, the $\ell!$ and output-ordering conventions of (5′.11) for $\ell\ge2$, and the doubled symbol algebra as an *operator* algebra (below).

#### Hand-off to WB2 (b)

**Constructor.** `phat_op(alg, P, k, l, rule="a", eps=toy.PAR)` in `codes/verify-linfty-ibl-repackaging.py` (docstring = (5′.11)), with `alg = GPoly(list(toy.PAR), dmax)` (host (i)) and `P = raise_map(toy, V[k+l], l, weighted=True)` (outputs first, `P[o_1..o_l, a_1..a_k]`). The brief's signature `phat(k, l, tensor) -> operator` is `lambda k, l, T: phat_op(alg, T, k, l, "a", toy.PAR)`. Derivatives are memoised over input prefixes; $\hat\ell_3$ on a degree-3 element of 12 strands costs $\approx1$ s.

**What is fixed and what is not.** Fixed (verified): all $\hat p_{k,1}=\hat\ell_k$, $k=1,2,3$, on host (i), with (5′.12). Not constrained by this test, WB2 (b) must decide or check independently: (1) $\hat p_{k,\ell}$ with $\ell\ge2$ ($\hat m_2=\hat p_{2,2}$, $\hat p_{1,2}$, $\hat p_{0,2}=\Theta$): the normalisation $1/\ell!$ and the ordering of $\chi_{o_1}\cdots\chi_{o_\ell}$ in (5′.11) (outputs are $\bar\varepsilon$-symmetric, §1.2 (iii), and on host (i) an equal-parity output pair is $\varepsilon$-antisymmetric, hence *projected to zero* by the $\varepsilon$-commuting $\chi$'s — P3; the surviving opposite-parity output pairs are $\varepsilon$-symmetric and their ordering sign is trivial, so (5′.11) is unambiguous on what survives, but host (i) sees only the projected structure for $\ell\ge2$); (2) $\hat p_{k,0}=V_k$ ($k\ge2$; $\hat p_{2,0}=\tfrac12V_2^{ab}\partial_b\partial_a$ with the inputs raised by $\tilde\Delta$ as in W2): inputs only, so (5′.12) applies with no output ambiguity, but untested; (3) $k=0$ ($\Theta$): pure multiplication, $\varepsilon$-projected on (i). Consequently the multi-string test of WB2 (b) on host (i) with (5′.12) is a test of Theorem B′ for the *$\varepsilon$-projected* family — the even–even/even–odd products it asks about come from the $\ell\ge2$ components, of which host (i) keeps only the opposite-parity output pairs. That is a faithful statement about the projected structure, not about the full CFL family. The faithful host for $\ell\ge2$ outputs, $S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$, is dead for the inputs (W6″: no sign rule; the obstruction is the symmetry type of the derivatives). **Remark (expected, unchecked, outside the three candidates):** the doubled symbol algebra of §5′.3 read as an operator algebra, $\Psi^a\to\hbar\tilde\Delta^{ac}\partial_{\chi_c}$ on $\mathbb C[\chi]_{\bar\varepsilon}$ with even $\hbar$, is parity-consistent ($\tilde\Delta^{ac}\partial_{\chi_c}$ has parity $\varepsilon_a$) but makes every $\hat p_{k,\ell}$ *even*; the leading term of the commutator of two normal-ordered symbols is then the even bracket induced by $[\tilde\Delta\partial_\chi,\chi\tilde\Delta]\propto\tilde\Delta^2=\mathbb 1$, not the BV antibracket (1.5), so $(S,S)=0$ does not imply $[\hat{\mathfrak p},\hat{\mathfrak p}]=0$ there at any order. This is the operator-level form of §5′.1's "no grading makes $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ homogeneous; the antibracket needs an odd $\hbar$" (MS's odd $x$), and it is the input to the MV-algebra question (Markl–Voronov 1511.01591), which this sub-task does not answer. Recommendation for WB2 (b): run on host (i) with (5′.12) and state the projection caveat; if the $\ell\ge2$ components are needed faithfully, the operator must live on the doubled algebra with an odd parameter, which is a different host than any tested here (re-brief).


### §5′.6 Multi-string leading order (WB2 (b), 2026-09-14)

**Restated task.** Settle the host question for the operator $D=\sum_{k,\ell}\hbar^{k-1}\hat p_{k,\ell}$ ($k+\ell\le4$, $\tilde\Delta$-raised family of the toy's $(S,S)=0$ generator) and run the first falsifier of "A′ ⇒ curved $L_\infty$ to leading order in $\hbar$ on multi-string inputs": on the host where the $\ell=1$ family is verified (host (i), (5′.12)) compute the Theorem B′ residual $\mathcal R(a)=F_{D^2}(a)-F_D(a)^2$ on even multi-strand $a$, bin it by $\hbar$-order, and compare its $\hbar^{n-1}$ coefficient at brace arity $n$ with the single-contraction part A′ makes vanish. **Objective (D-a):** decide whether the extra terms of WB2 (b) are a counterexample, evidence of vanishing, or invisible for lack of a faithful even-parameter host; record what the host analysis implies for the MV/BV∞ reading (not answered here). Conventions: §5′.1 parities, (5′.11)–(5′.12), `phat_op`, `GPoly(list(toy.PAR), dmax)`; $\hat Q=\hat{\tilde Q}$ (never W4's bare `Qhat`).

**Free constants.** (1) Weights $\hbar^{k-1}$ per component: CFL's input count (§1.4), not fitted. (2) Normalisation $1/(k!\,\ell!)$ of (5′.11): fixed on monomials (W6a for $\ell=1$; W7a below for $\ell\ge2$, $k=0$). (3) No anchor fitted from $\mathcal R(a)$.

#### Host analysis

Components of $D$ with $k+\ell\le4$ (weights $\hbar^{k-1}$): $\hat p_{1,1}=\hat{\tilde Q}$, $\hat p_{2,1}=\hat\ell_2$, $\hat p_{3,1}=\hat\ell_3$, $\hat p_{0,2}=\Theta$, $\hat p_{1,2}$, $\hat p_{2,2}=\hat m_2$, $\hat p_{0,3}$, $\hat p_{1,3}$, $\hat p_{0,4}$, $\hat p_{2,0}$, $\hat p_{3,0}$, $\hat p_{4,0}$ (twelve; $V_1=0$). Parities from (5′.1): host with variables of parity $\varepsilon$ gives $\pi=\ell\bmod2$, host with $\bar\varepsilon$ gives $\pi=k\bmod2$. Theorem B′'s hypotheses (graded-commutative host, finite-order $D$, even $a\in A\otimes\Lambda$) hold on every candidate: the host question is *only* which operator $D$ is and which of its components survive. Faithfulness test: $\hat m_2(\Psi\Psi)\ne0$ and $\hat p_{1,2}(\Psi)\ne0$ for physical even $\Psi$ (outputs at $N_{\rm gh}=(3,3)$, equal parity, $\bar\varepsilon$-symmetric).

**(i) $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ with (5′.12).** Inputs faithful (W6a, W6). Outputs: an equal-parity output pair is $\varepsilon$-antisymmetric and is annihilated by the $\varepsilon$-commuting $\chi$'s (P3); so $\hat m_2(\Psi\Psi)=\hat p_{1,2}(\Psi)=\Theta=0$ on the physical sector, and for $\ell\ge2$ only the opposite-parity output pairs survive (present only on inputs with odd strands). $D$ is inhomogeneous ($\hat{\tilde Q},\hat\ell_n,\hat p_{0,3},\hat p_{1,3}$ odd; $\Theta,\hat p_{1,2},\hat m_2,\hat p_{0,4},\hat p_{k,0}$ even). Not faithful on the physical sector; faithful for the projected family.

**(ii) $S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$.** Dead for inputs (W6″): equal-parity $\varepsilon$-symmetric input pairs are annihilated by the $\bar\varepsilon$-commuting derivatives under every per-component sign rule; $\hat\ell_2(\Psi\Psi)=0$. Not faithful.

**(iii) Doubled symbol algebra, $\Psi^a\to\hbar\tilde\Delta^{ac}\partial_{\chi_c}$, $\hbar$ even.** After the substitution the operator acts on $\mathbb C[\chi]_{\bar\varepsilon}$ — the *variables* of host (ii) — but with the tensor raised on **all** slots: $\hat p_{k,\ell}\to\hbar^{k-1}\frac1{k!\ell!}\chi_{o_1}\cdots\chi_{o_\ell}\,(\tilde\Delta^{\otimes(k+\ell)}V_{k+\ell})^{o_1\dots o_\ell c_1\dots c_k}\,\partial_{c_k}\cdots\partial_{c_1}$. By (1.7) the fully raised tensor is $\bar\varepsilon$-symmetric in every slot, and on $\mathbb C[\chi]_{\bar\varepsilon}$ both the $\chi$'s and the $\partial_\chi$'s are $\bar\varepsilon$-commuting: **no slot is projected** — the input obstruction of (ii) is absent because the substitution raises the inputs too (this is the point missed in §5′.3's "(iii) is (ii) with cleaner bookkeeping"). Physical $\Psi$ ($N_{\rm gh}=2$) maps to $\chi_c$ with $N_{\rm gh}(c)=3$ ($\bar\varepsilon$-even); $\hat m_2(\chi_c\chi_d)$ has outputs at $(3,3)$, $\bar\varepsilon$-even, symmetric: survives. Faithful for inputs and outputs. Price: every component has parity $\sum\bar\varepsilon_o+\sum\bar\varepsilon_c=(k+\ell)+\ell\equiv k+2\ell$… computed on the raised tensor: $\sum_{\rm slots}\varepsilon=\ell\bmod2$ for the $\ell$-raised tensor, $+k$ more flips for the inputs, host parity $\bar\varepsilon$ on every slot: $\pi=(k+\ell)+(k+\ell)=0$ — **all $\hat p_{k,\ell}$ even, $D$ even, $\hbar$ even** (§5′.5 remark, confirmed). Theorem B′ applies (case (ii) of B′.4: the criterion is $F_{D^2}=F_D^2$, not $D^2=0$). Sanity of the free part: the single-contraction square of $\hat{\tilde Q}$ is $\propto\tilde\Delta V_2\tilde\Delta V_2\tilde\Delta$ with $V_2\tilde\Delta V_2=\omega Q\,{\rm sgn}\,Q=-\omega\,{\rm sgn}\,Q^2=0$ ($Q$ odd anticommutes with ${\rm sgn}=(-1)^\varepsilon$): vanishes, as it must. **[Superseded by §5′.7, W8b′: this five-factor form drops one $\tilde\Delta$. The product of two fully raised tensors has six factors, $\tilde\Delta V_2\tilde\Delta\,\tilde\Delta V_2\tilde\Delta$, and $\tilde\Delta\tilde\Delta=+\mathbb 1$ (5′.15) collapses the middle pair to $\tilde\Delta V_2V_2\tilde\Delta\ne0$: the free part of host (iii) does *not* vanish.]** What is *not* established for (iii): that its $s_{\rm W10}=1$ part of $D\cdot D$ (the symmetric single contraction of an even operator) is A′'s bracket with 7c's sign convention — the §5′.5 remark shows the *commutator* is not the antibracket, but Theorem B′ uses the product, whose contraction is symmetric like $(S,S)$ for even $S$. Untested; it is the one convention check a run on (iii) needs first.

**(iv) Münster–Sachs packaging** ($A_c=s^{-2}\tilde A_c$, $\sigma=2$, $N_{\rm gh}(\Delta)=5$, all $l_n^g$ odd, parameter $x$ *odd* counting outputs, §1.4). With $\sigma$ even the component parity is $\ell\bmod2$ (5′.1), and MS restore homogeneity by the odd $x^{\ell+g-1}$: $x^{\ell-1}\hat p_{k,\ell}$ has parity $\ell+(\ell-1)=1$ for every $(k,\ell)$ — all odd, as required for their $\mathfrak D^2=0$. But an odd formal parameter has $x^2=0$ in a graded-commutative $W[x]$: all $\ell\ge3$ outputs are killed, and $\ell=2$ survives only at $x^1$. The $\tilde\Delta$-raised family with $\ell\ge2$ outputs embeds in MS's $W[x]$ only through its $\ell\le2$ slice, with $\ell=2$ at the top form degree; as a host for the $\ell\ge2$ *operator* family with an even parameter it does not exist (MS's construction is the $\ell\le1$ slice plus a nilpotent bookkeeping of the next one; their $\mathfrak D$ is not a differential operator on a polynomial algebra in $x$).

| host | variables / parity | $\pi(\hat p_{k,\ell})$ | $D$ homogeneous? | B′ hypotheses | $\hat m_2(\Psi\Psi)\ne0$, $\hat p_{1,2}\Psi\ne0$? | faithful sector | parameter |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (i) $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ | $\chi_a$, $\varepsilon_a$ | $\ell\bmod2$ | no | yes | **no** (P3) | inputs; opposite-parity output pairs only | $\hbar$ even |
| (ii) $S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$ | $\chi_a$, $\bar\varepsilon_a$ | $k\bmod2$ | no | yes | no (inputs dead, W6″) | none | $\hbar$ even |
| (iii) doubled, $\Psi\to\hbar\tilde\Delta\partial_\chi$ | $\chi_c$, $\bar\varepsilon_c$, all slots raised | $0$ | yes (even) | yes | **yes for slot symmetries** (confirmed W8a) — but contraction is *not* A′'s (§5′.7, (5′.15)) | slot symmetries only; free-level control fails (W8b) | $\hbar$ even |
| (iv) MS $W[x]$ | $\sigma=2$, $x$ odd | $\ell\bmod2$; $x^{\ell-1}\hat p$ odd | yes (odd) | not a polynomial host ($x^2=0$) | $\ell=2$ at $x^1$ only | $\ell\le2$ slice | $x$ odd |

**Conclusion of the table.** Of the four, (iii) is the only candidate that is faithful for both slot symmetries with an even parameter; it is the host the open question of §5′.3 asks for, at the price of an even $D$ (so "curved $L_\infty$" there is Theorem B′ case (ii), never $D^2=0$). (i) is faithful for the projected family and is where the verified $\ell=1$ constructor lives; the test below runs there. "None faithful with an even parameter" is therefore **not** the outcome — the property a faithful host needs is that inputs and outputs be symmetrised in the *same* parity as the host's variables, which (iii) achieves by raising the inputs. **[Superseded by §5′.7, W8b/W8b′: faithful slot symmetries are not enough. Host (iii) raises *both* slot types, so its single contraction is $\tilde\Delta\tilde\Delta=+\mathbb 1$, a bare index sum, not A′'s single-$\tilde\Delta$ contraction, and its single-string control fails at the free level. The table lacks a column "contraction = A′'s?", which reads "no" for (iii) and "yes, $\ell\le1$ only" for (i); with it, "none faithful with an even parameter" *is* the outcome for the class of §5′.7.]**

#### Projected test on host (i)

**Set-up (script `part_b_multistring_checks`, functions `phat_terms`, `apply_terms`).** Host `GPoly(list(PAR)+[0], 24)`: the 12 strands with physical parity plus one auxiliary even variable $t$. Family: all twelve $k+\ell\le4$ components from `raise_map(V[k+l], l, weighted=True)` (outputs $\tilde\Delta$-raised, inputs bare — the uniform convention of (5′.11), fixed for $\ell=1$ by W6a and re-checked here for $\ell=2$ and $\ell=0$), summed per input number: $D_k:=\sum_\ell\hat p_{k,\ell}$, $k=0..4$, so that the weight $\hbar^{k-1}$ is common to each $D_k$. The $\hbar$-order is tracked by the pair index $k_1+k_2-2$ and the brace arity by $t$: with $a\to ta$,
$$R_{k_1k_2}(ta):=e^{-ta}D_{k_1}D_{k_2}e^{ta}-F_{k_1}(ta)F_{k_2}(ta),\qquad \mathcal R(ta)=\sum_{k_1,k_2}\hbar^{k_1+k_2-2}R_{k_1k_2}(ta),\qquad C_n:=\Bigl|\sum_{k_1+k_2=n+1}[t^n]R_{k_1k_2}(ta)\Bigr|,\tag{5′.13}$$
exact through $t^3$ ($e^{\pm ta}$ expanded to $a^3$; no total-degree truncation bites, bound $\le20<24$). $C_n$ is the $\hbar^{n-1}$ coefficient of $\mathcal R$ at arity $n$: pairs with $k_1+k_2<n+1$ cannot produce $n$ factors of $a$, and at $k_1+k_2=n+1$ the $t^n$ term is the $s_{\rm W}=1$ part (Lemma W). Its $s_{\rm W10}=1$ piece is what A′ makes vanish (7c); $C_n$ is therefore the remainder the open question of §5′.3 asks about — *if the host is a consistent realisation of the family*. Test elements: even $a$ of strand degree 2 and 3 in the first four strands ($N_{\rm gh}=1,1,2,2$: odd strands present), three seeds; outputs range over all twelve strands.

**W7a (components on monomials).** $\hat p_{k,2}$, $k=0,1,2$: the coefficient of $\chi_{o_1}\chi_{o_2}$ ($o_1<o_2$) in $\hat p_{k,2}(\chi_{a_1}\cdots\chi_{a_k})$ equals $P^{o_1o_2}{}_{a}$ for opposite-parity output pairs (12 instances) and $0$ for equal-parity pairs with $P\ne0$ (4 instances, P3); $\hat p_{2,0}(\chi_a\chi_b)=V_{2,ab}$ (3 instances, odd pairs included). **New:** $\hat p_{0,3},\hat p_{1,3},\hat p_{0,4}$ vanish identically on host (i) — every output triple contains an equal-parity pair, and the sum over $S_3$ of $(-1)^{\#\text{equal-parity inversions}}$ is zero — so the projected family on (i) is $\{\hat{\tilde Q},\hat\ell_2,\hat\ell_3,\hat p_{1,2}|_{\rm opp},\hat m_2|_{\rm opp},\hat p_{2,0},\hat p_{3,0},\hat p_{4,0}\}$ with $\Theta=0$ and $D_0=0$. PASS.

**W7c (Lemma W on the family).** $[t^{k_1+k_2}]R_{k_1k_2}=0$ for every pair, max $1.1\cdot10^{-12}$ (the $s_{\rm W}=0$ part $F_1F_2$ cancels exactly). PASS — a falsifiable control of the residual machinery on the actual operators.

**W7 (the $\hbar^{n-1}$ coefficients).** Full projected family, three seeds, $\max C_n$: $C_1=1.0\cdot10^{2}$, $C_2=1.4\cdot10^{3}$, $C_3=1.3\cdot10^{4}$ — all nonzero, at every arity, with the $(1,1)$ pair alone contributing $O(10^2)$ at $n=1$. Control on the same kind of element: the $\ell=1$ sub-family $\hat{\tilde Q}+\hbar\hat\ell_2+\hbar^2\hat\ell_3$ (verified $D^2=0$, W6) gives $C_n=(0,\ 4\cdot10^{-15},\ 3\cdot10^{-13})$. PASS as a *finding* (nonzero full, zero control).

**W7′ (break-on-purpose on the control).** $V_4\to V_4+0.1\,\text{(random symmetric)}$: the $\ell=1$ sub-family has $C_3=24\ne0$ with $C_1=C_2=0$ (the $(3,1,0)$ relation fails at $t^3$, $\hbar^2$; the arity-2 relation does not involve $V_4$). PASS.

**W7″ (physical single string — the decisive leg *for the host finding*; no leg of §5′.6 would fail if Theorem A′ were false on single strings, A′ entering only through the control 7c).** $a=\Psi$ random physical ($N_{\rm gh}=2$, even). On host (i): $\hat m_2(\Psi\Psi)=2\cdot10^{-14}$, $\hat p_{1,2}\Psi=0$, $\Theta=0$ (P3), $\hat\ell_2(\Psi\Psi)=24\ne0$, $\ell=1$ family $C_n(\Psi)=0$ — **but the full projected family has $C_1(\Psi)=6.6$, $C_2=24$, $C_3=96$**, with $|\hat p_{1,2}\hat{\tilde Q}\Psi|=6.56$ and $|\hat{\tilde Q}\hat p_{1,2}\Psi|=0$. Lemma B1 with A′ gives $C_n(\Psi)=0$ for the *unprojected* family on single strings (§5′.3: $\partial\partial\Psi=0$, the braces reduce to $\hbar^{n-1}\sum_\ell p_{n,\ell}$ and A′ is the relation). So the nonzero is an artefact of the host: $\hat{\tilde Q}\Psi$ is a single odd string, $\hat p_{1,2}$ of it has outputs at $N_{\rm gh}=(3,4)$ — opposite parity, kept — whereas its partners in the $(1,2)$-type relation, $\hat{\tilde Q}(\hat p_{1,2}\Psi)$ (outputs $(3,3)$ before $\hat{\tilde Q}$ acts) and $\hat m_2(\Theta\text{-contracted},\Psi)$ ($\Theta$ supported on equal-parity pairs), are projected to zero *before* $\hat{\tilde Q}$ can turn an equal-parity pair into an opposite-parity one. **The $\varepsilon$-projection $S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H\to S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ does not commute with $\hat{\tilde Q}$** (a strand's parity flips under $Q$), hence is not a morphism of the structure, and the projected family is not a consistent truncation of the full one. PASS as a finding.

\subsection*{Verification}
Script run 2026-09-14, new legs W7a, W7c, W7, W7′, W7″ (20 s); full run `summary` pasted in the Status/report (103 previous PASS unchanged + 5 new). Falsifiability: W7′ breaks the control; the same code path produced $10^{-13}$ zeros on the closed $\ell=1$ family and $O(10^2$–$10^4)$ on the projected family; W7c checks Lemma W on the actual operators. A first version of `C_n` summed *norms* over pairs instead of polynomials and made the control fail ($C_2=46$) — diagnosed as a check error (the $\hat{\tilde Q}\hat\ell_2$ and $\hat\ell_2\hat{\tilde Q}$ bins cancel only in the sum) and fixed before any interpretation. Not done: an independent operator-level computation of the $s_{\rm W10}=1$ part (the comparison with A′ is through 7c); any run on host (iii).

#### Verdict and what remains

\subsection*{Result}
**Verdict (C), sharpened.** The question as posed — "do the $s_{\rm W}=1$, $s_{\rm W10}=0$ terms vanish on multi-string inputs for the raised family" — has **no faithful test on any host with a verified constructor**: host (i) is not merely lossy for $\ell\ge2$ but *inconsistent* (W7″: the projection does not commute with $\hat{\tilde Q}$, so the projected family fails the $\hbar^0$, arity-1 relation on a single physical string where the full family provably satisfies it). Consequently the nonzero $C_n$ of W7 on multi-string inputs is **not** a counterexample for the full family — it is not even evidence: the projected residual contains projection artefacts of the same order as the terms under test, and the relation between the projected and unprojected residuals is not a truncation (it is unknown beyond "they differ already on single strings"). Verdict (A) is therefore *not* claimed; ledger rows R31/R32 are unaffected. On the record, WB2 (b) reduces to: Theorem B′ (B′.4) exact and host-independent; the single-string corollary (Lemma B1, exact, unprojected); the multi-string leading-order corollary **open**, untestable on (i), (ii), (iv). The paper's Theorem B is (B′.4) with the single-string restriction of its $\hbar$-corollary — as §5′.3 already recommended.

**What would settle it.** Host (iii) of the table — $\mathbb C[\chi]_{\bar\varepsilon}$ with *all* slots $\tilde\Delta$-raised, every $\hat p_{k,\ell}$ even, $\hbar$ even — is faithful for inputs and outputs by symmetry type (no P3 projection anywhere, no input mis-symmetrisation), so the projection artefact of W7″ cannot occur there. A run there needs, in order: (1) the constructor `phat_op(GPoly(list(1-PAR)), raise_map(V[k+l], k+l, weighted=True), k, l)` with the first $\ell$ raised slots as outputs; (2) the single-string control $C_n(\chi_c)=0$ for $\chi_c$ at $N_{\rm gh}=3$ (Lemma B1 + A′ — this is the leg that *must* pass on a faithful host, and that failed on (i)); (3) a convention check that the $s_{\rm W10}=1$ part of $D\cdot D$ there is A′'s bracket in 7c's `tilde` convention (an even operator's single contraction is symmetric, as $(S,S)$ is for even $S$; the §5′.5 remark about the *commutator* is not an obstruction to the *product* Theorem B′ uses); (4) then W7 on multi-string $a$. Estimated one computator sub-task of this size (the machinery `residual_bins`, `C_n`, W7c is host-agnostic). If (2) fails on (iii) too, the fault is in the convention, not in the family, and the doubled host is not a realisation either — then the question is genuinely without an even-parameter operator host, which this run does *not* claim.

**Scope of what is established here.** (a) $\ell\ge3$ components vanish on $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ (W7a, hand argument). (b) Lemma W holds on the actual raised family (W7c). (c) The $\varepsilon$-projected family is inconsistent (W7″) — this retroactively qualifies §5′.5's hand-off ("a faithful statement about the projected structure"): there is no projected *structure* to make statements about beyond $\ell\le1$. (d) Host (iii) is faithful by symmetry type (hand, table); untested.

#### Implication for the MV/BV∞ reading (remark)

Can $\hat{\mathfrak p}=\sum_{k,\ell}\hbar^{k-1}\hat p_{k,\ell}$ be an odd square-zero operator on a graded-commutative $\mathbb k[[\hbar]]$-algebra with $\hbar$ even (Markl–Voronov 1511.01591, Def. 1)? **No**, on every host of the table, for the one-line reason of §5′.1 made operator-level: with $\hbar$ even, multiplication by $\hbar^{k-1}$ does not change parity, and the component parity is $\ell\bmod2$ (hosts with $\varepsilon$-variables: (i)), $k\bmod2$ ((ii)), or $0$ for all components ((iii), all slots raised) — so $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ is inhomogeneous on (i)/(ii) ($\hat{\tilde Q}$ odd, $\hat m_2$ even, under any strand-shift grading) and *even* on the only faithful host (iii). Odd homogeneity is obtained only with the odd parameter of MS ((iv), $x^{\ell-1}$), which is not a $\mathbb k[[\hbar]]$-algebra ($x^2=0$). On (iii) the structure is Theorem B′ case (ii): an even $D$ with $F_{D^2}=F_D^2$, i.e. a "commutative BV$_\infty$"-type object with an even generator — not MV's odd-$\Delta$ setting. Whether an MV-type algebra exists after a different suspension of the *parameter* (an odd $\hbar$ with a non-graded-commutative host, or MS's $x$ read as a formal odd variable in a *tensor* rather than symmetric algebra) is the MV-algebra question and is not addressed.

### §5′.7 Host (iii): all slots raised (WB2 (b2), 2026-09-14)

**Restated task.** Run the four legs of §5′.6 "What would settle it" on host (iii): $\mathbb C[\chi]_{\bar\varepsilon}$ (variables of parity $1-\varepsilon_a$, plus the auxiliary even $t$), every $\hat p_{k,\ell}$, $k+\ell\le4$, built from the *fully* $\tilde\Delta$-raised tensor `raise_map(V[k+l], k+l, weighted=True)` with the first $\ell$ raised slots as outputs. Legs in order, stop at the first failure: W8a constructor/parity, W8b single-string control, W8c convention against 7c, W8 multi-string. **Objective (D-a):** does A′ give curved $L_\infty$ to leading order in $\hbar$ on multi-string inputs — answered only on a host whose single-string control passes. Conventions: §5′.6 (weights $\hbar^{k-1}$, (5′.13) for $C_n$, `phat_terms`/`apply_terms`/`residual_bins` unchanged). No free constant fitted: the $1/(k!\ell!)$ normalisation is fixed on monomials (W8a), the weights by CFL's input count.

#### Constructor and parity check

**Set-up (script `part_b_host3_checks`, driver-tested then run in `main`).** Host `GPoly([1-e for e in PAR]+[0], 24)`: twelve $\chi_c$ of parity $\bar\varepsilon_c=1-\varepsilon_c$ plus the even $t$. Components $\hat p_{k,\ell}$, $k+\ell\le4$ (the twelve of §5′.6), each from the *fully* raised tensor $P=\tilde\Delta^{\otimes(k+\ell)}\circ V_{k+\ell}$ (`raise_map(V[k+l], k+l, weighted=True)`), first $\ell$ slots read as outputs, extension (5′.11) with rule (a) (`phat_terms`, unchanged); $D_k=\sum_\ell\hat p_{k,\ell}$; residual bins and $C_n$ exactly as (5′.13).

**Parity by hand.** A raised slot has $N_{\rm gh}=5-N_{\rm gh}(b)$, so the fully raised tensor from $V_n$ (saturating at $N_{\rm gh}=2n$) has $\sum_{\rm slots}N_{\rm gh}=5n-2n=3n$, i.e. $\sum_{\rm slots}\varepsilon\equiv n$ and $\sum_{\rm slots}\bar\varepsilon\equiv n+n\equiv0$: every term of every $\hat p_{k,\ell}$ is even on $\mathbb C[\chi]_{\bar\varepsilon}$ (as §5′.6 stated).

**W8a (machine).** Every term of the twelve components has parity $0$ (parity of the $\chi$-monomial plus that of the input variables). On monomials, the coefficient of $\chi_{o_1}\cdots\chi_{o_\ell}$ ($o$ increasing, distinct; $a$ distinct) in $\hat p_{k,\ell}(\chi_{a_1}\cdots\chi_{a_k})$ equals $P[o,a]$ in the order written, 65 instances (8 per component with $\ell\ge1$), among them **27 equal-parity output pairs and 12 equal-parity input pairs, all nonzero** — the two symmetry types that host (i) (P3) and host (ii) (W6″) killed. Max deviation $<10^{-9}$. **PASS.** Host (iii) is faithful *by slot symmetry*, as the §5′.6 table predicted.

#### Single-string control

**Correspondence.** $\tilde\Delta$ shifts $N_{\rm gh}$ by $5-2N$: physical $\Psi$ ($N_{\rm gh}=2$, $\varepsilon=0$) maps to $\chi_c$ with $N_{\rm gh}(c)=3$, $\bar\varepsilon_c=0$ — an even variable, as Theorem B′ requires. Test element $a=\sum_{N_{\rm gh}(c)=3}r_c\chi_c$, three seeds; $C_n$ from (5′.13) with the full family and, as in W7, with the $\ell=1$ sub-family $\{\hat p_{1,1},\hat p_{2,1},\hat p_{3,1}\}$.

**W8b (machine): the control FAILS.** Max over three seeds:
$$C_1(\chi_c)=6.8\cdot10^{3},\qquad C_2=1.6\cdot10^{4},\qquad C_3=2.2\cdot10^{4};\qquad \ell=1\text{ sub-family: }(2.4,\ 27,\ 3.8\cdot10^{2}),\tag{5′.14}$$
with **every** bin nonzero, including the $a$-independent $(1,0,0)$ bin $|\hat{\tilde Q}\Theta|=1.65\cdot10^{3}$ and the $(1,1,1)$ bin $|\hat{\tilde Q}\hat{\tilde Q}\chi_c|=O(10^3)$ — the free-theory relations $Q\Theta=0$, $Q^2=0$ already fail. Lemma B1 + A′ require $C_n(\chi_c)=0$ on a faithful host; the brief's rule: stop, diagnose (check-typo → transcription → convention).

**Diagnosis, in the record's order.** (1) Check typo: no — the same `residual_bins`/`C_n` gave $10^{-13}$ zeros on the closed $\ell=1$ family of host (i) (W7, W7″) and $O(10^2)$ on a perturbed one (W7′); the only host-(iii)-specific code is the constructor, which W8a verifies on monomials. (2) Transcription: no — the constructor is the literal prescription of §5′.6 (1) and of the brief. (3) **Convention — found, and it is an obstruction, not a sign.** On $\mathbb C[\chi]$ the single contraction of two operators is $\partial_c\chi_o=\delta_{co}$, a $\delta$ between an *input* index of the left factor and an *output* index of the right one. On host (iii) both are raised, so the contraction carries $\sum_o\tilde\Delta^{ao}\tilde\Delta^{ob}$, and with $\tilde\Delta^{ab}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}\Delta^{ab}$, $\Delta^{a\bar a}\Delta^{\bar aa}=-(\Delta^{a\bar a})^2$, $\varepsilon_a+\varepsilon_{\bar a}=1$:
$$(\tilde\Delta\tilde\Delta)^{ab}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_a+\varepsilon_{\bar a}}\Delta^{a\bar a}\Delta^{\bar aa}\,\delta^{ab}=+\delta^{ab}.\tag{5′.15}$$
Hence the $s_{\rm W10}=1$ part of $\hat p^{(1)}\hat p^{(2)}$ on host (iii) is $\tilde\Delta^{\otimes}\bigl(V^{(1)}{}_{\cdots a}\,V^{(2)}{}_{a\cdots}\bigr)\tilde\Delta^{\otimes}$ — a **bare index sum**, pairing $N_{\rm gh}=x$ with $x$ — and not $V^{(1)}\tilde\Delta V^{(2)}$, which pairs $x$ with $5-x$. It is not A′'s bracket, up to no constant: the two objects do not even sit at the same ghost number ($3(n_1+n_2)-2N_{\rm gh}(o)$ versus $5(n_1+n_2-2)-(2n_1+2n_2-5)$, of opposite parity). Free part explicitly: $\hat{\tilde Q}$ on (iii) is the derivation with matrix $M=\tilde\Delta V_2\tilde\Delta$, so $\hat{\tilde Q}^2$ has matrix $\tilde\Delta(V_2V_2)\tilde\Delta$ with $V_2V_2=WQWQ\ne0$ (in the toy $(V_2V_2)[a,a]=V_2[a,g]V_2[g,a]=-1$), whereas $V_2\tilde\Delta V_2=0$. §5′.6's "sanity of the free part ($\propto\tilde\Delta V_2\tilde\Delta V_2\tilde\Delta$)" dropped one $\tilde\Delta$: the product of two fully raised tensors has six factors, $\tilde\Delta V_2\tilde\Delta\,\tilde\Delta V_2\tilde\Delta$, and (5′.15) collapses the middle pair.

**W8b′ (machine, falsifiable diagnosis).** $|\tilde\Delta\tilde\Delta-\mathbb 1|=0$; $\hat{\tilde Q}\hat{\tilde Q}\chi_c$ computed as a differential operator equals $\tilde\Delta(V_2V_2)\tilde\Delta\,\chi_c$ to $10^{-16}$ (independent path: operator on `GPoly` versus $12\times12$ matrices); $|V_2V_2|_{\max}=1.00$; $|V_2\tilde\Delta V_2|=0$ (the true free relation). **PASS.** The check fails if $\tilde\Delta$ is replaced by $\Delta$ ($\Delta\Delta=-\mathbb 1$, the sign then propagates into the operator comparison) or if the matrix prediction is replaced by $\tilde\Delta V_2\tilde\Delta V_2\tilde\Delta$ (which is $0$ while the operator value is not).

#### Convention check against 7c

Not run as a numerical leg (leg 2 failed); answered by (5′.15) instead, and the answer is stronger than "which normalisation differs": the $s_{\rm W10}=1$ part of $D\cdot D$ on host (iii) is $\tilde\Delta^{\otimes}(V^{(1)}\!\cdot V^{(2)})\tilde\Delta^{\otimes}$ with a bare index sum, and A′'s bracket is $V^{(1)}\tilde\Delta V^{(2)}$; they differ by no constant (different ghost number, (5′.15) and below), and the 7c `tilde` sign convention is not reached.

**General obstruction (hand, exact).** Let the host be any graded-commutative polynomial algebra on generators $\chi_c$ indexed by the strands, with parities $\pi_c$, and let $\hat p_{k,\ell}=\frac1{k!\ell!}\chi_{o_1}\cdots\chi_{o_\ell}\,T^{o\,a}\,\partial_{a_k}\cdots\partial_{a_1}$ with $T=(A^{\otimes\ell}\otimes B^{\otimes k})\circ V_{k+\ell}$ for linear raising maps $A$ (outputs) and $B$ (inputs) — the class containing (5′.12) ($A=\tilde\Delta$, $B=\mathbb 1$), host (ii) and host (iii) ($A=B=\tilde\Delta$). The single contraction of $\hat p^{(1)}\hat p^{(2)}$ is $\partial_c\chi_o=\delta_{co}$, so it inserts $B^{\rm T}\!A$ between $V^{(1)}$ and $V^{(2)}$. A′ needs $B^{\rm T}\!A\propto\tilde\Delta$, which flips the ghost-number parity ($x\mapsto5-x$); hence exactly one of $A,B$ flips parity, and the outputs of $T$ are symmetric in one of $\varepsilon,\bar\varepsilon$ while its inputs are symmetric in the other. But on a graded-commutative host $\partial_c$ has the parity of $\chi_c$, so outputs and inputs are both symmetrised in $\pi$: one of the two slot types is projected (host (i): outputs, P3; host (ii): inputs, W6″). Conversely, matching both symmetries ($A,B$ both flipping or both preserving) gives $B^{\rm T}\!A$ parity-preserving — $0$ or $2$ factors of $\tilde\Delta$, (5′.15) — never A′'s contraction. **No differential-operator realisation of the $\ell\ge2$ family on a single graded-commutative polynomial host with an even parameter has both faithful slot symmetries and A′'s single-$\tilde\Delta$ contraction.** The escape is the one §1.4 found for CFL's homogeneity: $\Psi^a\to\hbar\tilde\Delta^{ac}\partial_c$ with $\hbar$ *odd* gives $\Psi^a$ the parity $\varepsilon_a$ on an $\varepsilon$-host with bare outputs ($B^{\rm T}\!A=\tilde\Delta^{\rm T}$, one $\tilde\Delta$) — and an odd graded-commutative parameter has $\hbar^2=0$ (host (iv), $x^2=0$), so the $k\ge2$ inputs die.
