Add: "sub-family reading: the augmented components of $V_{\le4}$ need $p_{1,3}$ as well and still fail $(2,2,0)$ (026 §6, W12)."
- **R6 — re-scoped (critic pass A, 2026-09-08, endorsed; critic-026A-r16-reconciliation).** Keep R6's structural content: C6a–d verify only the *form* of CFL's $(2,2,0)$ equation (which products enter, $\ell_3$ absent, signature ordering, equal degree), not that the $\Delta$-raised products satisfy it. Re-scope the consequence: "for the $\Delta$-raised products of a cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra this equation does not hold (026 §3.3; residual $\tfrac12M_{2,2}$), so the $Q$-exactness of $m_2$ inferred from it is withdrawn; the equation that does hold is the non-augmented $B_{2,2}=0$ (026 §3.4)."
- **R7 — annotate, HOLD released (B′c).** "The shift $\omega_2^{\rm symp}$ is the parity part of a cyclic pairing on $E_2$; for the brace *products* of $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ on $W$ it is necessary but not sufficient — no degree-diagonal pairing makes them cyclic (026 §5.5, products-level, unaffected by the §5 re-derivation: 026 §5′.4)."
- **R13 — annotate (no status change).** "Route C's multiplier pairing is a cotangent-type pairing; its vertices $\langle V_n|\sigma_n\rangle$ are the pure-input components $p_{n,0}$ of the non-augmented family of A′ (026 §5.1, §5.5)."
- **R11, R26 — annotate, HOLD released with amended wording (B′c).** "Presentation (b)'s products are the re-weighted $V_4$-slice of the brace *products* of $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ (026 (5.25), read on the doubled symbol algebra as bookkeeping: 026 §5′.5–§5′.7, no single graded-commutative polynomial operator host in the strand variables with even $\hbar$ and slot-uniform raising for the $\ell\ge2$ components; untested, not excluded: separate input/output generators, non-polynomial product, $\star$-calculus, odd parameter) and are not the Koszul brace structure of any operator (026 (5′.10), W4, proven); what A′ guarantees for (b) is the $\min(k,\ell)=1$ relations, hence the composite gauge law; the $(2,2,0)$ relation plays no role in the HS theory and in the all-orders tower (026 §5.4, §5′.4)." Justification: every clause is a products-level or $S_{\rm HS}$-level statement, none uses Theorem B (026 §5′.4 §5.4 (iii)–(iv)).


## §8 Harold's convention: sign dictionary, the $(2,2)$ and $(1,\ell)$ relations with fields inserted (2026-09-14)

**Restated task.** (i) Give the exact componentwise dictionary between the products $\ell^{\rm H}_n$, $p^{\rm H}_{k,\ell}$ defined by Harold's *sign-free* pairing identities and the $\tilde\Delta$-raised $p_{k,\ell}$ of (1.6); (ii) show where the sign goes when it is not in the raising (into the bracket) and that §3's statements are convention-independent; (iii) write the non-augmented $(2,2)$ relation as an identity of maps on $(\Psi,\Psi)$ in Harold's convention, with its physical reading; (iv) same for the $(1,\ell)$ relations, $\ell=2,3$; (v) machine check `part_h_checks`; (vi) fix the self-inconsistent sentence at (1.4).

**Motivating objective.** The question addressed: why raising an index carries a sign, given that $\mc V_n(A_1,\dots,A_n)=\braket{A_1,\ell_{n-1}(A_2,\dots,A_n)}$ has none; and the $p_{2,2}$ relations with fields inserted, read the way one reads "$Q$ is a derivation of $\ell_2$". Both must be on record before the companion note is revised in the sign-free convention.

**Regime.** Finite-dimensional $\mathcal H$ with physical ghost grading, genus zero, classical (no $\hbar$); $\Psi$ even at $N_{\rm gh}=2$; everything below is a statement about the same tensors as §1–§3, only re-coordinatised. Nothing of §3–§5′ is re-derived; equations are cited.

### §8.1 The dictionary: Harold's sign-free products vs the $\tilde\Delta$-raised ones

\subsection*{Setup}
Conventions of §1.1 ($\omega_{ab}$, $\Delta^{ab}$, $\tilde\Delta^{ab}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}\Delta^{ab}$, $\bar\varepsilon_a=\varepsilon_a+1$, $\omega_{ab}\Delta^{bc}=\delta_a{}^c$, and on the support of either tensor the two indices have opposite parity). Two contraction identities are used constantly and are recorded once:
$$\Delta^{cb}\,\omega_{ba}=\delta_a{}^c,\qquad \omega_{cb}\,\Delta^{ba}=\delta_c{}^a.\tag{8.1}$$
Both follow from $\omega_{ab}\Delta^{bc}=\delta_a{}^c$ and the antisymmetry of $\omega$ and $\Delta$.

**Harold's definitions (the sign-free pairing identities).** For $n\ge1$ and $k,\ell\ge0$,
$$\mathcal V_{n+1}(A_0,A_1,\dots,A_n)=:\braket{A_0,\ell^{\rm H}_n(A_1,\dots,A_n)},\tag{8.2}$$
$$\mathcal V_{k+\ell}(A_1,\dots,A_\ell,B_1,\dots,B_k)=:\braket{A_1\otimes\cdots\otimes A_\ell,\ p^{\rm H}_{k,\ell}(B_1,\dots,B_k)}_\ell.\tag{8.3}$$

**Ordering convention for the $\ell$-fold pairing.** We take the strandwise Koszul form,
$$\braket{A_1\otimes\cdots\otimes A_\ell,\ C_1\otimes\cdots\otimes C_\ell}_\ell:=(-1)^{\sum_{i<j}\varepsilon_{C_i}\varepsilon_{A_j}}\ \prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\braket{A_i,C_i}.\tag{8.4}$$
Reason for this choice and not another: (8.4) *is* $\omega_1^{\otimes\ell}$ as a bilinear form on $\mathcal H^{\otimes\ell}$ in the standard Koszul convention — strand $i$ of the left argument pairs with strand $i$ of the right, and the prefactor is the sign of the shuffle $A_1\cdots A_\ell C_1\cdots C_\ell\mapsto A_1C_1A_2C_2\cdots$ (move $C_1$ past $A_2\dots A_\ell$, then $C_2$ past $A_3\dots A_\ell$, …). It is the pairing of R7's $\omega_2^{\rm symp}$ and the one under which "$\Sigma$ is tested strand by strand". Any other choice (reversed strand order, or dropping the shuffle sign) changes (8.3) by a sign that depends only on the output parities and can be read off from (8.6) below; the physical-sector statements of §8.1–§8.4 are unaffected, and the choice is flagged wherever it matters.

\subsection*{Derivation}
**One leg.** Write $\ell^{\rm H}_n(A)=\ell^{\rm H}_n(A)^c e_c$. Then (8.2) with $A_0=e_a$ reads $V_{n+1}(e_a,A)=\omega_{ac}\,\ell^{\rm H}_n(A)^c$ (no sign: pulling the number-valued component $\ell^{\rm H\,c}$ out of the second slot of $\omega_1$ costs $(-1)^{\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_c}$, which is $+1$ on the support $\varepsilon_c=\varepsilon_a+1$). Contracting with $\Delta^{ba}$ and using (8.1),
$$\ell^{\rm H}_n(A)^b=\Delta^{ba}\,V_{n+1}(e_a,A_1,\dots,A_n):\qquad \ell^{\rm H}_n=\Delta\circ V_{n+1}\ \text{(plain raising, Zwiebach's product).}\tag{8.5}$$
The record's product is $\ell_n=\tilde\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$ (1.4), so componentwise
$$\ell_n(A)^b=(-1)^{\varepsilon_b}\,\ell^{\rm H}_n(A)^b=-(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}\,\ell^{\rm H}_n(A)^b\quad(a\ \text{the paired index},\ \varepsilon_a=\bar\varepsilon_b),\tag{8.6}$$
and, dually, $\omega_1(e_c,\ell_n(\cdots))=-(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}V_{n+1}(e_c,\cdots)$, which is (1.4)/(5′.2) and (2.10) of the companion. **So the "sign when raising an index" is exactly the statement (8.6): one factor $-(-1)^{\varepsilon}$ per raised leg, $\varepsilon$ the parity of the *paired* basis state, equivalently $(-1)^{\varepsilon}$ with $\varepsilon$ the parity of the *output* index.** In particular $\ell_1=\tilde\Delta\circ V_2=(-1)^{\varepsilon_{\rm out}}Q$ — the record's $Q'=WQ$ of (3.22) — whereas $\ell^{\rm H}_1=Q$ on the nose.

**$\ell$ legs.** Let $C:=p^{\rm H}_{k,\ell}(B)$ with components $C^{c_1\dots c_\ell}$ (numbers, for $B$ basis states). Put $A_i=e_{b_i}$ in (8.3) and use (8.4):
$$V_{k+\ell}(e_{b_1},\dots,e_{b_\ell},B)=(-1)^{\sum_{i<j}\varepsilon_{c_i}\varepsilon_{b_j}}\ \omega_{b_1c_1}\cdots\omega_{b_\ell c_\ell}\,C^{c_1\dots c_\ell}.\tag{8.7}$$
Contract with $\Delta^{d_1b_1}\cdots\Delta^{d_\ell b_\ell}$ and use (8.1) on each factor; on the support $\varepsilon_{b_j}=\bar\varepsilon_{c_j}$, so the shuffle sign becomes a function of the $c$'s alone and survives the contraction:
$$\Delta^{c_1b_1}\cdots\Delta^{c_\ell b_\ell}\,V_{k+\ell}(e_{b_1},\dots,e_{b_\ell},B)=(-1)^{\sum_{i<j}\varepsilon_{c_i}\bar\varepsilon_{c_j}}\ p^{\rm H}_{k,\ell}(B)^{c_1\dots c_\ell}.\tag{8.8}$$
Comparing with the definition (1.6), $p_{k,\ell}^{c_1\dots c_\ell}=(-1)^{\sum_i\varepsilon_{c_i}}\,\Delta^{c_1b_1}\cdots\Delta^{c_\ell b_\ell}V_{k+\ell}(e_{b},B)$:
$$\boxed{\;p_{k,\ell}(B)^{c_1\dots c_\ell}=\underbrace{(-1)^{\sum_{i}\varepsilon_{c_i}}}_{\text{one }-(-1)^{\varepsilon_{\rm paired}}\text{ per raised leg}}\ \underbrace{(-1)^{\sum_{i<j}\varepsilon_{c_i}\bar\varepsilon_{c_j}}}_{\text{interleaving sign of }(8.4)}\ p^{\rm H}_{k,\ell}(B)^{c_1\dots c_\ell}.\;}\tag{8.9}$$

**Two legs, explicitly.** $\ell=2$:
$$p_{k,2}(B)^{c_1c_2}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_{c_1}+\varepsilon_{c_2}}\,(-1)^{\varepsilon_{c_1}\bar\varepsilon_{c_2}}\;p^{\rm H}_{k,2}(B)^{c_1c_2}.\tag{8.10}$$

**On physical inputs.** If all $k$ inputs sit at $N_{\rm gh}=2$ then, since $N_{\rm gh}(p_{k,\ell})=3\ell-2k$ (1.6), every nonvanishing component has $\sum_i n_{c_i}=2k+(3\ell-2k)=3\ell$, hence
$$\sum_i\varepsilon_{c_i}\equiv 3\ell\equiv\ell\ (\mathrm{mod}\ 2)\ \Longrightarrow\ (-1)^{\sum_i\varepsilon_{c_i}}=(-1)^{\ell}\quad\text{on every component.}\tag{8.11}$$
So the first factor of (8.9) is the announced $(-1)^\ell$ *on all components at once*, not only on the all-odd-output one. The interleaving factor is $+1$ for $\ell\le2$ on physical inputs (at $\ell=2$ the two output parities are equal by (8.11), and $\varepsilon\bar\varepsilon=0$ for either value), but **not** for $\ell\ge3$: at $\ell=3$ the component $(n_{c_1},n_{c_2},n_{c_3})=(4,3,2)$ has $\varepsilon_{c_2}\bar\varepsilon_{c_3}=1$ and the factor is $-1$, while $(3,3,3)$ gives $+1$. Hence
$$p_{k,1}=-\,\ell^{\rm H}_k,\qquad p_{k,2}=+\,p^{\rm H}_{k,2}\quad\text{on physical inputs},\tag{8.12}$$
and for $\ell\ge3$ the two conventions differ component by component, by (8.9).

**Output symmetry, the structural price.** By (1.7) the outputs of $p_{k,\ell}$ are graded symmetric in $\bar\varepsilon$. Transporting this through (8.10): for $\ell=2$, the ratio $\sigma(c_1,c_2)/\sigma(c_2,c_1)$ of the two dictionary signs is $(-1)^{\varepsilon_{c_1}+\varepsilon_{c_2}}$, so
$$p^{\rm H}_{k,2}(B)^{c_1c_2}=(-1)^{\bar\varepsilon_{c_1}\bar\varepsilon_{c_2}}(-1)^{\varepsilon_{c_1}+\varepsilon_{c_2}}\,p^{\rm H}_{k,2}(B)^{c_2c_1}=-(-1)^{\varepsilon_{c_1}\varepsilon_{c_2}}\,p^{\rm H}_{k,2}(B)^{c_2c_1}:\tag{8.13}$$
in Harold's convention the two outputs are graded **anti**symmetric in the physical parity $\varepsilon$ — the symmetry type of $\omega_1$ itself. On the components with $\varepsilon_{c_1}+\varepsilon_{c_2}$ even, which by (8.11) is *every* component on physical inputs, $\varepsilon$-antisymmetry and $\bar\varepsilon$-symmetry coincide, so $p^{\rm H}_{2,2}(\Psi,\Psi)\in\mathrm{Sym}^2\mathcal H$ exactly as the companion's Example states. Off the physical sector they are different subspaces.

\subsection*{Result}
(8.9) is the dictionary; (8.10) is the two-leg case; (8.12) is its physical-sector reading. **Answer to the sign question.** The sign-free identity (8.2) is not in conflict with (1.4): they define *different products*, $\ell^{\rm H}_n=\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$ and $\ell_n=\tilde\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$, differing by $(-1)^{\varepsilon_{\rm out}}$ per raised leg. Neither is forced by the pairing; what is forced is that the weight $(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}$ of the BV antibracket (1.5) appear *somewhere*. The $\tilde\Delta$ convention puts it in the raising, and buys two things: (i) every product contraction that closes an antibracket is then a bare index sum (3.12), and (ii) the outputs of $p_{k,\ell}$ are uniformly $\mathrm{Sym}_{\bar\varepsilon}$ for all $k,\ell$ (1.7). Harold's convention puts it in the bracket instead (§8.2), and buys $\ell^{\rm H}_1=Q$ and $\ell^{\rm H}_2=$ Zwiebach's string product with no dressing, at the price that the output symmetry type is $\Lambda_\varepsilon$ rather than $\mathrm{Sym}_{\bar\varepsilon}$ (8.13) and that the interleaving sign of (8.4) is visible from $\ell=3$ on. Both are consistent; the companion note is written in the second and the record in the first, and (8.9) is the translation.

\subsection*{Verification}
Check H1 (§8.5): (8.6) at $\ell=1$ and (8.10) at $\ell=2$, elementwise on the toy generator, together with a break-on-purpose leg (drop the interleaving factor → nonzero defect off the physical sector). The $\ell=2$ physical-sector statement $p_{2,2}=p^{\rm H}_{2,2}$ (8.12) is checked separately on the $N_{\rm gh}=2$ input block, and (8.13) is checked as a symmetry defect.

### §8.2 Where the sign lives: the bracket carries the weight

\subsection*{Setup}
Harold's products $\ell^{\rm H}_n=\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$ (8.5) throughout this sub-section; $\Psi,\chi$ even superfields; left derivatives.

\subsection*{Derivation}
**The weighted pairing.** Define, as in §3.2,
$$\tilde\omega(X,Y):=(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}\,X^c\,\omega_{cd}\,Y^d\qquad(X=X^ce_c,\ Y=Y^de_d).\tag{8.14}$$
It is non-degenerate (it differs from $\omega_1$ by a sign per component) and, unlike $\omega_1$, graded **symmetric** on its support, which is why it does not annihilate the diagonal: $\tilde\omega(X,X)\ne0$ in general while $\omega_1(X,X)=0$.

**The one-sided bracket and the antibracket.** The one-sided bracket of §3.1 is $\{F,G\}=\partial_{\Psi^a}F\,\tilde\Delta^{ab}\,\partial_{\chi^b}G$. Since $\partial_{\Psi^a}S(\Psi)=\omega_{ac}\mathcal E^c$ with
$$\mathcal E(\Psi):=\sum_{n\ge2}\frac{1}{(n-1)!}\,\ell^{\rm H}_{n-1}(\Psi^{n-1})=Q\Psi+\tfrac12\ell^{\rm H}_2(\Psi,\Psi)+\tfrac16\ell^{\rm H}_3(\Psi^3)+\cdots\tag{8.15}$$
the equation-of-motion vector in Harold's convention, (3.12) gives at once
$$(S,S)=\tilde\omega\bigl(\mathcal E(\Psi),\mathcal E(\Psi)\bigr),\qquad \{S(u),S(u)\}=\tilde\omega\bigl(\mathcal E(u),\mathcal E(u)\bigr),\ u=\Psi+\chi.\tag{8.16}$$
More generally every configuration of §3 is a weighted pairing of two of Harold's products,
$$T(i,j|m,n)=\frac{1}{i!j!m!n!}\;\tilde\omega\bigl(\ell^{\rm H}_{i+j}(\Psi^i,\chi^j),\ \ell^{\rm H}_{m+n}(\Psi^m,\chi^n)\bigr).\tag{8.17}$$

**Rule.** *Every $\Delta$-contraction that closes a bracket carries $(-1)^{\varepsilon}$ of the strand it closes.* In Harold's variables the weight is never in a product; it is the single factor $(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}$ of (8.14), carried once per closed contraction.

**Convention-independence of §3.** Replacing Harold's products by the record's, $X^c\mapsto(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}X^c$ on both arguments of (8.14), gives
$$\tilde\omega\bigl((-1)^{\varepsilon}X,(-1)^{\varepsilon}Y\bigr)=(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}(-1)^{\varepsilon_d}X^c\omega_{cd}Y^d=(-1)^{\varepsilon_d}X^c\omega_{cd}Y^d=-\,\tilde\omega(X,Y),\tag{8.18}$$
using $\varepsilon_d=\varepsilon_c+1$ on the support of $\omega_{cd}$. The factor is $-1$ *uniformly*: independent of $(i,j,m,n)$, of the input sector, and of the component. Every statement of §3 is a homogeneous linear identity among the $T$'s — mirror symmetry $T(i,j|m,n)=T(m,n|i,j)$ (3.3), $B_{k,\ell}=2P_{k,\ell}+M_{k,\ell}$ (3.6), $R_{k,\ell}=\tfrac12 B_{k,\ell}+\tfrac12M_{k,\ell}$ (3.7), $B_{k,\ell}=0\Leftrightarrow$ cyclic $L_\infty$ (3.9), $M_{k,\ell}=-2P_{k,\ell}$ (3.15), $M_{2,2}\equiv0\Leftrightarrow\ell_2\circ_1\ell_2=0$ (3.18) — and is therefore unchanged.

\subsection*{Commentary}
(8.16): apply (3.12) with $X=Y=\mathcal E$. The factor $1/(n-1)!$ in (8.15) is the one left after the left derivative removes one $\Psi$ from $\frac1{n!}V_n(\Psi^n)$, as in (3.1).
(8.17): this is (3.12)–(3.14) read with the products of (8.5); note that §3.2 already *is* written in Harold's convention — its (3.11), $V_{r+1}(e_a,A)=\omega_1(e_a,\ell_r(A))$ with no sign, is (8.2). The only places in §3 where the $\tilde\Delta$-raised products appear are the map-form comparisons of §3.3 ((3.22)–(3.26)), which are the objects of §8.3.
(8.18): the same computation as the one which shows that $(S,S)$ would vanish identically with plain $\omega_1$ ((1.5), companion (2.3)); here it is used only as a bookkeeping statement about a uniform rescaling.

\subsection*{Result}
$$\boxed{\;(S,S)=\tilde\omega(\mathcal E,\mathcal E),\qquad T(i,j|m,n)=\tfrac{1}{i!j!m!n!}\tilde\omega\bigl(\ell^{\rm H}_{i+j}(\Psi^i\chi^j),\ell^{\rm H}_{m+n}(\Psi^m\chi^n)\bigr),\qquad T^{\tilde\Delta}=-T^{\rm H}.\;}$$
All statements of §3.1–§3.2 hold verbatim in Harold's convention. In particular Theorem A′ (R31) and Proposition 3.2 are convention-independent: only the *name* of the family changes, $p_{k,\ell}\leftrightarrow p^{\rm H}_{k,\ell}$ by (8.9). **No wording of R31 or R32 needs changing**; the annotation to make (below, §8.6) is that the record's $p_{k,\ell}$ are the $\tilde\Delta$-raised representatives and that (8.9) is the translation to the sign-free ones.

\subsection*{Verification}
(8.16) and (8.18) are one-line component identities; (8.17) is (3.12)–(3.14), already machine-checked by E2 (elementwise, all sectors, with the plain-$\Delta$ break leg E4). No new check is needed for §8.2 beyond H2's use of Harold's products throughout.

### §8.3 The non-augmented $(2,2)$ relation as a map identity on $(\Psi,\Psi)$

\subsection*{Setup}
Everything from §3; nothing re-derived. Bidegree $(2,2)$ of $\{S(u),S(u)\}=0$, $u=\Psi+\chi$ (3.8)–(3.9). Ordered configurations are labelled $T(i,j|m,n)$ as in (3.3): vertex 1 carries $i$ free $\Psi$-legs and $j$ free $\chi$-legs, vertex 2 carries $m$ and $n$, and the remaining leg of each is contracted. Harold's products (8.5), weighted pairing (8.14), so that every configuration is (8.17). The dictionary to the $\tilde\Delta$-raised map form is (8.9).

**Convention inside this sub-section — the two products are mixed and each occurrence is labelled.** $\ell^{\rm H}_n=\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$ denotes Harold's sign-free product and $\ell_n=p_{n,1}=\tilde\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$ the raised one; on physical inputs the two are related by $p_{n,1}=-\ell^{\rm H}_n$ (8.12). In the $\chi$-test column of the table below, and in (8.19), **all products are Harold's** (an unadorned $\ell$ there means $\ell^{\rm H}$); in the map-form column and in (8.21)–(8.32) an unadorned $\ell_n$ is the **raised** $p_{n,1}$ — which is what the script computes — *except* where $\ell^{\rm H}$ is written explicitly, which happens in exactly the two rows ($A_4$, $A_1'$) where the output of a one-output product stays a free strand of the result (§8.7.2). No coefficient or sign of (8.21)/(8.30)/(8.31) depends on this labelling; what depends on it is only which symbol carries the $-$ of (8.12).

\subsection*{Derivation}
**The seven ordered configurations.** With $i=k_1-1$, $j=\ell_1$, $m=k_2$, $n=\ell_2-1$ (3.2), the $(2,2)$ bidegree of $\{S(u),S(u)\}$ has $(k+1)(\ell+1)-2=7$ ordered tuples (E1). Listing them by the pair of vertices $(k_1,\ell_1;k_2,\ell_2)$ and giving each its map reading:

| # | $(i,j|m,n)$ | $(k_1,\ell_1;k_2,\ell_2)$ | weight | $\chi$-test form | map form |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| $A_1$ | $(0,1|2,1)$ | $(1,1;2,2)$ | $\tfrac12$ | $\tilde\omega(Q\chi,\ell_3(\Psi,\Psi,\chi))$ | $\hat Q$ on the outputs of $p_{2,2}(\Psi,\Psi)$ |
| $A_2$ | $(1,2|1,0)$ | $(2,2;1,1)$ | $\tfrac12$ | $\tilde\omega(\ell_3(\Psi,\chi,\chi),Q\Psi)$ | $p_{2,2}(Q\Psi,\Psi)$ |
| $A_3$ | $(0,2|2,0)$ | $(1,2;2,1)$ | $\tfrac14$ | $\tilde\omega(\ell_2(\chi,\chi),\ell_2(\Psi,\Psi))$ | $p_{1,2}\bigl(\ell_2(\Psi,\Psi)\bigr)$ |
| $A_4$ | $(1,1|1,1)$ | $(2,1;1,2)$ | $1$ | $\tilde\omega(\ell_2(\Psi,\chi),\ell_2(\Psi,\chi))$ | $(\ell^{\rm H}_2\otimes1)\bigl(p_{1,2}(\Psi)\otimes\Psi\bigr)$, symmetrised |
| $A_1'$ | $(2,1|0,1)$ | $(3,1;0,2)$ | $\tfrac12$ | $=A_1$ by (3.3) | $(\ell^{\rm H}_3(\Psi,\Psi,\cdot)\otimes1)\,p_{0,2}$ |
| $A_2'$ | $(1,0|1,2)$ | $(2,0;1,3)$ | $\tfrac12$ | $=A_2$ by (3.3) | $(V_2(\Psi,\cdot)\otimes1\otimes1)\,p_{1,3}(\Psi)$ |
| $A_3'$ | $(2,0|0,2)$ | $(3,0;0,3)$ | $\tfrac14$ | $=A_3$ by (3.3) | $(V_3(\Psi,\Psi,\cdot)\otimes1\otimes1)\,p_{0,3}$ |

(The "weight" column is the $1/(i!j!m!n!)$ of (8.17); the $\chi$-test column is that weight times the displayed pairing.) The three primed rows are the mirrors of the three unprimed pure ones and carry the *same value* (3.3); they are the terms containing the non-augmented components $p_{0,2}$, $p_{2,0}=V_2$, $p_{0,3}$. $A_4$ is self-mirror.

**The relation.** $B_{2,2}=0$ (3.9), i.e. with the weights,
$$\boxed{\;\underbrace{\tfrac12\tilde\omega\bigl(Q\chi,\ell_3(\Psi,\Psi,\chi)\bigr)}_{A_1}+\underbrace{\tfrac12\tilde\omega\bigl(\ell_3(\Psi,\chi,\chi),Q\Psi\bigr)}_{A_2}+\underbrace{\tfrac14\tilde\omega\bigl(\ell_2(\chi,\chi),\ell_2(\Psi,\Psi)\bigr)}_{A_3}\;+\;A_1'+A_2'+A_3'\;+\;\underbrace{\tilde\omega\bigl(\ell_2(\Psi,\chi),\ell_2(\Psi,\chi)\bigr)}_{A_4}=0\;}\tag{8.19}$$
which by mirror symmetry collapses to
$$2\,(A_1+A_2+A_3)+A_4=0,\qquad\text{i.e.}\qquad P_{2,2}=-\tfrac12M_{2,2}\tag{8.20}$$
— (3.6), (3.15), (3.16). In map form, on physical inputs ($N_{\rm gh}(\Psi)=2$), with the $\tilde\Delta$-raised products of (1.6), the plain $Q$, and $\ell^{\rm H}_n=\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$ where indicated,
$$-\tfrac12\hat Q\,p_{2,2}(\Psi,\Psi)+p_{2,2}(Q\Psi,\Psi)-\tfrac12p_{1,2}\bigl(\ell_2(\Psi,\Psi)\bigr)-2\bigl[(\ell^{\rm H}_2\otimes1)\bigl(p_{1,2}(\Psi)\otimes\Psi\bigr)\bigr]_{\rm sym}-\bigl[(\ell^{\rm H}_3(\Psi,\Psi,\cdot)\otimes1)p_{0,2}\bigr]_{\rm sym}$$
$$\qquad+\ \bigl(V_2(\Psi,\cdot)\otimes1^{\otimes2}\bigr)p_{1,3}(\Psi)\ +\ \tfrac12\bigl(V_3(\Psi,\Psi,\cdot)\otimes1^{\otimes2}\bigr)p_{0,3}\;=\;0,\tag{8.21}$$
**Which product is which in (8.21).** The $\ell_2$ inside $p_{1,2}\bigl(\ell_2(\Psi,\Psi)\bigr)$ (third term) is the $\tilde\Delta$-**raised** $p_{2,1}$, not $\ell^{\rm H}_2$ — it is `L2 = p(2,1)` in the script's `F_phys` — and by (8.12) $p_{2,1}=-\ell^{\rm H}_2$ on physical inputs, so reading that symbol as Harold's product would flip the sign of the term. The fourth and fifth terms carry Harold's sign-free $\ell^{\rm H}_2,\ell^{\rm H}_3$, written explicitly, because there the output of the one-output product is a free strand of the result (§8.7.2, dressing $\sigma_4$ from (8.6)). Here $\hat Q=Q\otimes1+1\otimes Q$ is the coderivation on two output strands with the Koszul sign of the strand passed taken in the shifted parity $\bar\varepsilon$ (8.26), $[\cdot]_{\rm sym}$ is the normalised graded symmetrisation over the two free output strands in $\bar\varepsilon$, "$\otimes1$" feeds strand 1 of a symmetric tensor into the map to its left, and every term is evaluated at $(\Psi,\Psi)$ as a map $\mathrm{Sym}^2_\varepsilon\mathcal H\to\mathrm{Sym}^2_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$. The coefficients are $\tfrac12\binom2i\binom2j$ per single-strand term, measured row by row in **§8.7.2** (the coefficient table is there and is not duplicated here): the $\tfrac12$ on $\hat Qp_{2,2}$ is the $1/2!$ of the two background legs on $V_4$, which the two-strand coderivation does not see; the $2$ on the mixed term is the sum over the two strands of $p_{1,2}(\Psi)$ that $\ell_2$ can absorb. Off the physical sector the same identity holds with the R1 sign dressings of §8.7.2, (8.30). The first two terms are $-\tfrac12[\hat Q,p_{2,2}](\Psi,\Psi)$ (graded commutator; $p_{2,2}\hat Q_{\rm in}(\Psi,\Psi)=2p_{2,2}(Q\Psi,\Psi)$ on even $\Psi$). *(This display supersedes the sign-free, all-weights-$1$ form written here before 2026-09-19; the earlier version had the $\hat Q$-term a factor $2$ too large and the mixed term a factor $2$ too small — §8.7.2.)*

\subsection*{Commentary}
Row $A_1$: vertex 1 is $V_2(e_a,\chi)$, i.e. $Q$ acting on a strand that leaves the diagram as an output; vertex 2 is $V_4(e_b,\Psi,\Psi,\chi)$, i.e. $p_{2,2}(\Psi,\Psi)$ with one of its two outputs contracted into the $Q$. So $A_1$ is $Q$ on *one* strand of $p_{2,2}(\Psi,\Psi)$, i.e. $-\tfrac12\hat Q\,p_{2,2}(\Psi,\Psi)$ with $\hat Q$ the coderivation (both strands, Koszul sign of (1.8) in $\bar\varepsilon$ for the strand it passes): the $\tfrac12$ is the $1/2!$ of the two background legs (§8.7.2, ratio $-\tfrac12$ exact on all sectors).
Row $A_2$: vertex 2 is $V_2(e_b,\Psi)=Q\Psi$ contracted into an *input* slot of the $V_4$; the other input stays $\Psi$, the two $\chi$'s are the outputs.
Row $A_3$: vertex 2 is $\ell_2(\Psi,\Psi)$, fed into the single input of $p_{1,2}$.
Row $A_4$: both vertices are $V_3(e,\Psi,\chi)$ — each is $p_{1,2}(\Psi)$ with one strand contracted and one free. Reading vertex 2 as $p_{1,2}(\Psi)$ and vertex 1 as $\ell_2$ absorbing one of its strands together with the remaining $\Psi$ gives the stated map form. **$A_4$ *is* the mixed term**: $(i,j,m,n)=(1,1,1,1)$ are all $\ge1$, so $M_{2,2}=A_4$ (3.16). There are seven terms, not eight: the "$(\ell_2\otimes1)$ on $p_{1,2}(\Psi)\otimes\Psi$" term and "the mixed term" of the brief are the same object.
Rows $A_i'$: the mirror of a configuration is the same number (3.3) read with the roles of the two vertices exchanged; since the bracket differentiates its left argument in $\Psi$ and its right in $\chi$, exchanging the roles turns a vertex with $j$ free $\chi$-legs and one contracted leg into one with $j+1$ output legs, i.e. it turns $p_{k,\ell}$ into $p_{k-1,\ell+1}$. This is exactly how the non-augmented components $p_{0,2},p_{2,0},p_{0,3}$ enter: they are not extra physical input, they are the mirror readings of $A_1,A_2,A_3$.
(8.21): the relative signs and the coefficients are those of §8.7.2, measured row by row; the R1 dressings hidden in "$\hat{\ }$" and in "$_{\rm sym}$" are made explicit there ((8.30) for all sectors), and reduce on physical inputs to plain $Q$, $\ell_n\to-\ell^{\rm H}_n$ in the two rows ($A_4$, $A_1'$) where an $\ell_n$ output is a free strand — which is why (8.21) mixes the raised $\ell_2=p_{2,1}$ of its third term with the explicitly sign-free $\ell^{\rm H}_2,\ell^{\rm H}_3$ of its fourth and fifth (Setup convention paragraph; $p_{2,1}=-\ell^{\rm H}_2$ by (8.12)) — and a $-$ on the two rows with two free strands from one raised vertex. (At the level of the *configurations* — as opposed to the named maps — the uniform $\tilde\Delta$ raising still makes every one of them $\tilde\omega(\ell_\bullet,\ell_\bullet)$ with weight $+1$, which is (3.22); the relative signs of (8.21) come from the dressings, not from the configurations.)

\subsection*{Result — what it says physically}
At $(2,1)$ the relation is $[Q,\ell_2]=0$: *$Q$ is a derivation of the string product.* At $(3,1)$ it is $[Q,\ell_3]+\ell_2\circ_1\ell_2=0$: *the failure of $Q$ to be a derivation of $\ell_3$ is the Jacobiator of $\ell_2$.* The $(2,2)$ analogue is read off (8.20)–(8.21) and is **not** of that shape:

*The combination that measures the failure of $Q$ to be a derivation of the $\Sigma$ kinetic operator $p_{2,2}$, corrected by the chain in which the quartic vertex is replaced by two cubic ones, is*
$$\underbrace{[\hat Q,p_{2,2}](\Psi,\Psi)}_{\text{failure of }Q\text{ to be a derivation of }p_{2,2}}\;+\;\underbrace{p_{1,2}\bigl(\ell_2(\Psi,\Psi)\bigr)}_{\text{the }\ell_3\text{-analogue of the Jacobiator}}\;=\;-4\bigl[(\ell^{\rm H}_2\otimes1)\bigl(p_{1,2}(\Psi)\otimes\Psi\bigr)\bigr]_{\rm sym}-2\bigl[(\ell^{\rm H}_3\otimes1)p_{0,2}\bigr]_{\rm sym}+2\bigl(V_2\otimes1^{\otimes2}\bigr)p_{1,3}(\Psi)+\bigl(V_3\otimes1^{\otimes2}\bigr)p_{0,3}\tag{8.32}$$
(this is $-2\times$(8.21); §8.7.3), *i.e. the failure of $Q$ to be a derivation of $p_{2,2}$, corrected by the Jacobiator analogue, equals the two-string state glued from two cubic vertices along a propagator with one leg of each on $\Psi$ (the mixed term), plus the three no-input/no-output terms; by mirror symmetry the latter reproduce the left-hand side up to sign ($+2P_{2,2}$ against the left-hand side's $-2P_{2,2}$), so the relation is $2P_{2,2}=-M_{2,2}$.* In $\chi$-test form this is the sharpest statement: $2P_{2,2}=-M_{2,2}=-\tilde\omega(\ell_2(\Psi,\chi),\ell_2(\Psi,\chi))$ (3.16), the *square* of the cubic vertex with one leg on the background and one on the test state. So at $(2,2)$ the pattern "$Q$-derivation up to a Jacobiator" breaks: what would be the analogue of the Jacobi relation does not close on its own. It closes only when the three mirror terms are added — the terms carrying $p_{0,2}$, $p_{2,0}=V_2$ and $p_{0,3}$, i.e. the components of the family that have *no input* or *no output* — and since each mirror equals one of the first three terms (3.3), that is the same as saying the true relation weighs the mixed term once against *twice* the augmented terms. They bring no new tensor. That is the content of Theorem A′ (R31): the non-augmented family closes, the augmented one does not.

**Keep separate.** *Augmented (CFL Def. 2.3, the relation $R_{2,2}=0$, i.e. $A_1+A_2+A_3+A_4=0$): **FALSE** in general*, by $\tfrac12M_{2,2}$ (3.21), (3.25); numerically $20.3$ on the generator, on physical inputs too (E5). *Non-augmented ($B_{2,2}=0$, (8.19)/(8.21)): **TRUE** on all sectors*, it is the bidegree-$(2,2)$ part of the polarised classical master equation. The two differ by the *weight of the mixed term*: the true relation counts $A_4$ once against $2(A_1+A_2+A_3)$; the augmented one counts it once against $(A_1+A_2+A_3)$. There is no bare-variable sign convention under which the augmented combination becomes the true one (3.22).

\subsection*{Verification}
Check H2 (§8.5): the seven configurations of the table, built directly as tensors from $V_2,V_3,V_4$ with graded symmetrisation over the two input and two output slots (no doubled-space code path), sum to zero elementwise on all input-parity sectors; break-on-purpose leg: dropping $A_4$ leaves an $O(10)$ residual, and giving $A_4$ the augmented weight $\tfrac12$ likewise. Contraction with even ($N_{\rm gh}=2$) and odd ($N_{\rm gh}=3$) test inputs is reported separately. The identification of individual rows with their *named* map expressions — and with it every coefficient of (8.21) — is verified elementwise in §8.7 (`part_h2_named_maps`, 16 checks H2N).

### §8.4 The $(1,\ell)$ relations, map form and meaning

\subsection*{Setup}
As §8.3. At $k=1$ the bracket needs $k_1\ge1$ and $k_1+k_2=k+1=2$, so the *augmented* family ($k_i\ge1$) forces $k_1=k_2=1$: every term is a composition of two $p_{1,\cdot}$'s, with $p_{1,1}=Q$ (Harold's convention; $\tilde\Delta$-raised: $p_{1,1}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_{\rm out}}Q$, (8.6)).

\subsection*{Derivation}
**Augmented form.** For $\ell\ge1$,
$$\sum_{\substack{\ell_1+\ell_2=\ell+1\\ \ell_1,\ell_2\ge1}}\hat p_{1,\ell_2}\circ_1\hat p_{1,\ell_1}=0,\tag{8.22}$$
$\ell$ terms, matching $|R_{1,\ell}|=k\ell=\ell$ (E1). **Non-augmented form.** Allowing $k_1=2$, $k_2=0$ adds exactly the $\ell$ mirror tuples $(k_1,\ell_1;k_2,\ell_2)=(2,\ell_2-1;0,\ell_1+1)$, whose terms pair $p_{2,\cdot}$ with $p_{0,\cdot}$; by (3.3) each equals its unprimed partner, so
$$B_{1,\ell}=2R_{1,\ell},\qquad\text{and }M_{1,\ell}=0\ \text{(3.10)}:\quad\text{augmented and non-augmented say the same thing at }\min(k,\ell)=1.\tag{8.23}$$
This is why $\min(k,\ell)=1$ is the sector where Statement A survives (§3.1 Corollary) — and it is the only structural difference from $(2,2)$, where the mixed self-mirror term $A_4$ breaks the proportionality.

**$\ell=2$.** $(\ell_1,\ell_2)\in\{(1,2),(2,1)\}$:
$$\hat p_{1,2}\circ_1 \hat Q+\hat Q\circ_1\hat p_{1,2}=0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad p_{1,2}(Q\Psi)+\hat Q\,p_{1,2}(\Psi)=0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad [\hat Q,p_{1,2}]=0,\tag{8.24}$$
with $\hat Q$ the coderivation extension acting on both output strands with the Koszul sign of (1.8). Non-augmented: add the two mirror terms $\bigl(\ell_2(\Psi,\cdot)\otimes1\bigr)p_{0,2}$ and $\bigl(V_2(\Psi,\cdot)\otimes1\otimes1\bigr)p_{1,2}$-type; they double (8.24) and add nothing.

**$\ell=3$.** $(\ell_1,\ell_2)\in\{(1,3),(2,2),(3,1)\}$:
$$p_{1,3}(Q\Psi)\;+\;\bigl(\hat p_{1,2}\otimes1\bigr)\bigl(p_{1,2}(\Psi)\bigr)_{\rm sym}\;+\;\hat Q\,p_{1,3}(\Psi)\;=\;0,\tag{8.25}$$
where the middle term is $\hat p_{1,2}$ acting on one of the two strands of $p_{1,2}(\Psi)$ (the other strand spectating), graded-symmetrised over the three resulting output strands in $\bar\varepsilon$. Non-augmented: add the three mirrors ($p_{2,2}$ with $p_{0,2}$; $p_{2,1}=\ell_2$ with $p_{0,3}$; $p_{2,0}=V_2$ with $p_{0,4}$), which double (8.25).

\subsection*{Result — what they mean}
$p_{1,\ell}(\Psi)$ is the $\ell$-string state emitted by the vertex $\mathcal V_{\ell+1}$ when one of its legs absorbs the background field $\Psi$ and the other $\ell$ legs are cut open with propagators: a map $\mathcal H\to\mathcal H^{\otimes\ell}$ of ghost number $3\ell-2$. The relations (8.22) say that this tower is **BRST-compatible**: at $\ell=2$, $p_{1,2}$ commutes with $\hat Q$ (8.24); at $\ell=3$, $p_{1,3}$ fails to commute with $\hat Q$ by exactly the two-step emission $p_{1,2}\circ p_{1,2}$ (8.25). These are the single-input components of the coderivation-form genus-zero relations (0.1): (8.22) is the $k=1$ part of $\sum\hat p_{k_2,\ell_2}\circ_1\hat p_{k_1,\ell_1}=0$, the $\circ_1$-composition of CFL, *not* the square of an operator on a host space — §5′.2 and §5′.7 show that the family has no realisation as a square-zero differential operator on a single graded-commutative polynomial host in the strand variables with even $\hbar$ for $\ell\ge2$, in the class with slot-uniform parity-homogeneous raising maps and $\delta$-contraction (other hosts untested, not excluded). Unlike the $(k,1)$ relations, which are the $L_\infty$ relations of the string products, and unlike $(2,2)$, which fails in the augmented form, the $(1,\ell)$ tower closes on its own (§3.1 Corollary, proven, all sectors).

**Which of these the cubic action uses at $O(g_s^2)$ (026 §5′.4, R11).** The HS gauge algebra uses only the $\min(k,\ell)=1$ relations; $(2,2,0)$ never enters, at $O(g_s^2)$ or at any order (§5.4 (iv), §5′.4). At $O(g_s^2)$ the quartic vertex enters $S_{\rm HS}$ through $\mathcal V_4(\Psi,\Psi,\cdot,\cdot)=p_{2,2}$ contracted with the auxiliary $\Sigma$, and the gauge variation with the composite law $\delta\Sigma=\delta\Psi\otimes\Psi+\Psi\otimes\delta\Psi$ closes because of the two relations $(2,1)$ and $(1,2)$: $(2,1)$ is $[Q,\ell_2]=0$, and $(1,2)$ — relation (8.24) — is *equivalent* to $[Q,\ell_2]=0$ (§3.3 Result: the surviving half of R16), since $p_{1,2}=\tilde\Delta\circ\ell_2$ is the raised $\ell_2$. So the only structural input at $O(g_s^2)$ is that $Q$ is a derivation of the cubic product, once on the one-output side and once on the two-output side; $p_{1,3}$, and with it (8.25), first matters at $O(g_s^3)$.

\subsection*{Verification}
Check H3 (§8.5): (8.24) and (8.25) as elementwise tensor identities on the toy generator, all input-parity sectors, with a break-on-purpose leg (sign flip on one term → $O(10)$ residual).

### §8.5 Machine check (`part_h_checks`, H1–H3)

Added to `codes/verify-linfty-ibl-repackaging.py` as `part_h_checks(toy, V)` (helpers `h_config_tensor`, `h_tuples`, `_par_tensor`), called from `main()` after `part_a_table`. No existing check was altered. Toy: generator (iv), multiplicities $(2,4,4,2)$, seed 20260908 (printed by the script); the extra random test vectors of H2d use seed 20260914 (printed in the check name). 15 named checks, all PASS.

**(i) H1 — the dictionary.** *H1a* (two rows, $n=2,3$): the *defining* pairing identities, computed as $\omega_{cd}X^d$ from the two raisings independently — $\braket{e_c,\ell^{\rm H}_n(A)}=V_{n+1}(e_c,A)$ (defect $0$) and $\braket{e_c,\ell_n(A)}=-(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}V_{n+1}(e_c,A)$ (defect $0$), with the break leg "$\ell_n$ sign-free" failing at $12.3$ and $49.7$. *H1b*: $p^{\rm H}_{2,2}$ obtained from $p_{2,2}$ by the dictionary (8.10) is fed back into the *defining* two-fold identity (8.3) with the pairing (8.4), and reproduces $V_4$ exactly (defect $0$); two break legs — dropping the interleaving sign of (8.4), and using the $\tilde\Delta$-raised $p_{2,2}$ in place of $p^{\rm H}_{2,2}$ — both fail at $17.3$. This is the falsifiable form: the check tests the dictionary against the sign-free pairing identity, not against the algebra that produced it. *H1c*: (8.12) on the $N_{\rm gh}=2$ input block, $p_{k,1}=-\ell^{\rm H}_k$ and $p_{k,2}=+p^{\rm H}_{k,2}$ (defects $0$), while the two differ by $17.3$ off that block. *H1d*: (8.13), $p^{\rm H}_{2,2}$ is $\varepsilon$-antisymmetric in its outputs (defect $4\cdot10^{-15}$) and *not* $\varepsilon$-symmetric (defect $49.7$).

**(ii) H2 — the $(2,2)$ relation.** The seven configurations of the §8.3 table are built by `h_config_tensor` directly from $V_2,V_3,V_4$ — one $\tilde\Delta$-contraction of two vertices, the Koszul reordering sign of §8.3, then shuffle-symmetrisation of the $\chi$- and $\Psi$-blocks — with **no doubled-space code path** (independent of `Doubled`, of `part_b7_checks` and of `part_a_table`). *H2a*: exactly 7 ordered tuples. *H2b*: their sum vanishes elementwise on all input-parity sectors, $1.1\cdot10^{-14}$, against a largest single term of $40.6$. *H2c* (break-on-purpose): dropping the mixed term $A_4$ leaves $40.6$; giving $A_4$ the **augmented** weight $\tfrac12$ — i.e. asking for $R_{2,2}=0$ instead of $B_{2,2}=0$ — leaves $20.3=|P_{2,2}|$, reproducing the number of (3.25) from an independent code path. *H2d*: contraction with two independent random even and two independent random odd test vectors, sum $2\cdot10^{-15}$ and $8\cdot10^{-15}$ with $|A_4|=11.1$ and $|A_4|\ne0$ respectively. (Two *independent* vectors are needed: the inputs of $A_4$ are graded symmetric in $\varepsilon$, hence antisymmetric on two odd slots, so $u\otimes u$ annihilates it — a structural fact, not a defect. A numeric vector supported on odd $N_{\rm gh}$ is not a Grassmann-odd superfield; it is an elementwise probe of the tensor identity restricted to those slots.) *H2e*: the mirror rows are equal (defect $0$), which is (3.3) and licenses the collapse $B_{2,2}=2(A_1+A_2+A_3)+A_4$.

**(iii) H3 — the $(1,\ell)$ relations.** $\ell=2$: augmented sum ($2$ terms) $4\cdot10^{-16}$, non-augmented ($4$ terms) $9\cdot10^{-16}$, largest term $6.15$; break leg (sign flip on one term) $12.3$. $\ell=3$: augmented ($3$ terms) $8\cdot10^{-15}$, non-augmented ($6$ terms) $1.5\cdot10^{-14}$, largest term $24.4$; break leg $48.8$. Term counts $\ell$ and $2\ell$ are asserted, which is (8.23).

**What is *not* machine-checked.** The identification of each individual row of the §8.3 table with its *named* map expression ($A_1$ with $\hat Q\,p_{2,2}$, $A_4$ with $(\ell_2\otimes1)(p_{1,2}(\Psi)\otimes\Psi)$, …) is verified elementwise in §8.7 (`part_h2_named_maps`, H2N); the R1 dressings it needs are (8.30) and the physical-sector form is (8.31). (8.19) is unchanged; the map form (8.21) is the corrected one of §8.7.3, the pre-2026-09-19 version having had two wrong coefficients. Still unchecked: the $\ell\ge3$ interleaving factor of (8.9) is derived but checked only at $\ell\le2$ (H1b), and the dressings are established at $(2,2)$ only — at $(1,2)$ the finding is that *no* dressing is needed (the control of §8.7.5), so there is nothing established there to carry over — and not at $(1,3)$ or beyond. The graded-commutator step $p_{2,2}\hat Q_{\rm in}(\Psi,\Psi)=2p_{2,2}(Q\Psi,\Psi)$ used to pass from (8.31) to (8.32) is now checked as well (§8.7.3, §8.7.5). Still to keep in view: the fact that the individual signs of (8.30) are $\sigma$-representative-dependent (§8.7.1, "Free constants").

### §8.6 Tidy: the sentence at (1.4), and the ledger

**The fix (2026-09-14).** The sentence displayed at (1.4) read
> "i.e. $\omega_1(e_c,\ell_n(\cdots))=V_{n+1}(e_c,\cdots)$ — exactly: $\omega_1(e_c,\ell_n(\cdots))=-(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}V_{n+1}(e_c,\cdots)$, (5′.2); the dressing of §1.5 is this sign."

which asserts two incompatible things in one breath. The second is the correct one for the $\tilde\Delta$-raised $\ell_n$ of (1.4): from $\omega_{cb}\tilde\Delta^{ba}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_b}\delta_c{}^a=-(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}\delta_c{}^a$ on the support (8.1). The first is correct for the *plain*-$\Delta$ product $\ell^{\rm H}_n=\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$ (8.5) — Harold's convention and the one §3.2 (3.11) actually uses. The sentence has been replaced by a statement that names both products. Reason for the correction: the ambiguous form is what prompted the sign question of this record (2026-09-14) and the open `\draftcomment` in `knowledge/companion-ibl-bookkeeping.tex` §2.2; §8.1 resolves it.

**Ledger.** No new row. R31 and R32 need **no rewording *on account of the convention change***: by (8.18) every relation of §3 is invariant under it, and Theorem A′ / Theorem B′ are statements about the family, not about the representative. (Independently of this, R32's *open note* was amended on 2026-09-19 with §5′.7's Verdict (C) in its calibrated form, and R31/R32's script-count citations were reconciled to 141/141, then to 142/142 on 2026-09-19 when the graded-commutator check was added — that is a separate matter from §8's convention question.) One annotation is worth adding to R31 when it is next touched: *"the family is written with the $\tilde\Delta$-raised representatives $p_{k,\ell}$ (026 (1.6)); the sign-free representatives $p^{\rm H}_{k,\ell}$ of Harold's convention are related by 026 (8.9), one factor $-(-1)^{\varepsilon}$ per raised leg plus the interleaving sign of the $\ell$-fold pairing; on physical inputs $p_{k,1}=-\ell^{\rm H}_k$, $p_{k,2}=p^{\rm H}_{k,2}$. The relations are unchanged (026 §8.2)."*

### §8.7 The seven configurations as named maps: coefficient table and the $\hat Q$-term of (8.21) (2026-09-19)

**Task (brief `computator-026-83-named-maps-20260919T123500`).** Build each row $A_1,\dots,A_3'$ of the §8.3 table as a tensor from *named* map compositions ($\hat Q$, $p_{2,2}$, $p_{1,2}$, $\ell_2$, $\ell_3$, $p_{0,2}$, $p_{1,3}$, $p_{0,3}$, $V_2$, $V_3$) on the toy, compare elementwise with the configuration tensor `h_config_tensor`, and fix the coefficient of every term of (8.21). **Objective.** Companion §3.4 copies (8.21) as the statement of how $Q$ fails to be a derivation of $p_{2,2}$; the reviewer (critic-026D) counts the $\hat Q$-term as a factor $2$ too large when $\hat Q$ is the coderivation. The record's §8.5 declared this identification unchecked.

#### §8.7.1 Definitions (fixed once, before any tensor is built)

All maps are in the record's $\tilde\Delta$-raised convention (1.6): $p_{k,\ell}=$ `raise_map(V_{k+\ell}, ℓ, weighted=True)`, so $\ell_n:=p_{n,1}=\tilde\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$, $p_{1,1}=\ell_1=(-1)^{\varepsilon_{\rm out}}Q=:Q'$ (8.6); $Q$ itself is the plain toy matrix; $V_2=\omega_1Q$, $V_3$, $V_4$ enter as $p_{k,0}$ (no raised leg). Components: a map $F:\mathcal H^{\otimes k}\to\mathcal H^{\otimes\ell}$ is the array $F^{c_1\cdots c_\ell}{}_{x_1\cdots x_k}$, $F(e_{x_1},\dots,e_{x_k})=F^{c\cdots}{}_{x\cdots}\,e_{c_1}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{c_\ell}$; the raised legs of $p_{k,\ell}$ are output slots $1..\ell$ in leg order.

**(D1) $\hat Q$ on two strands (coderivation).** For $F$ with two output strands,
$$(\hat Q_{\rm out}F)^{cd}{}_{xy}:=Q^c{}_{c'}F^{c'd}{}_{xy}+(-1)^{\bar\varepsilon_c}\,Q^d{}_{d'}F^{cd'}{}_{xy},\tag{8.26}$$
i.e. $Q\otimes1+1\otimes Q$ with the Koszul sign of the strand passed taken in the **shifted** parity $\bar\varepsilon$ (rule R1: output strands carry $\bar\varepsilon$), so that $\hat Q_{\rm out}$ preserves $\mathrm{Sym}^2_{\bar\varepsilon}$. On two input strands the coderivation uses the **physical** parity,
$$(F\hat Q_{\rm in})^{cd}{}_{xy}:=F^{cd}{}_{x'y}Q^{x'}{}_x+(-1)^{\varepsilon_x}\,F^{cd}{}_{xy'}Q^{y'}{}_y,\tag{8.27}$$
so that $F\hat Q_{\rm in}$ is defined on $\mathrm{Sym}^2_\varepsilon$. Both variants with the *other* parity in the Koszul sign are also built and reported (they are the natural candidates; a definition is chosen once for all rows, and the choice that reproduces the rows is stated as the result, not adjusted per row). $Q$ vs $Q'$: the named maps use the plain $Q$ (the record's (8.21) and the companion write $Q$, $\hat Q$); the ratio to the row then shows whether $Q$ or $Q'$ is the object actually appearing.

**(D2) Symmetrisation.** $\mathrm{Sym}_{\rm out}$ is the *normalised* graded symmetriser over the two output strands in $\bar\varepsilon$, $\mathrm{Sym}_{\rm out}(F)^{cd}=\tfrac12\bigl(F^{cd}+(-1)^{\bar\varepsilon_c\bar\varepsilon_d}F^{dc}\bigr)$; $\mathrm{Sym}_{\rm in}$ the normalised graded symmetriser over the two input slots in $\varepsilon$. "Normalised" so that a map already in $\mathrm{Sym}^2$ is unchanged: then "$F$ evaluated at $(\Psi,\Psi)$" is independent of whether one writes $F$ or $\mathrm{Sym}_{\rm in}F$. The suffix $_{\rm sym}$ of (8.21) is read as $\mathrm{Sym}_{\rm out}\mathrm{Sym}_{\rm in}$ in this sense; where a map is built by acting on *one* strand of a symmetric tensor ("$\ell_2\otimes1$", "$V_3(\Psi,\Psi,\cdot)\otimes1^{\otimes2}$"), strand 1 is used and the result is $\mathrm{Sym}_{\rm out}$-projected; no extra factor.

**(D3) Dictionary map ↔ $\chi$-test ↔ configuration tensor.** The $\chi$-test scalar of a map $F$ evaluated at $(\Psi,\Psi)$ is defined as the supernumber polynomial
$$\langle F\rangle:=\frac{1}{2!}\,\tilde\omega_{cc'}\tilde\omega_{dd'}\,F^{c'd'}{}_{xy}\;\chi^c\chi^d\Psi^x\Psi^y,\qquad \tilde\omega_{cc'}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}\omega_{cc'}\ (8.14),\tag{8.28}$$
the $1/2!$ being the $1/j!$ of the two $\chi$-legs, and no $1/2!$ on the $\Psi$ side because a map is *evaluated* at $(\Psi,\Psi)$, not expanded as $\frac1{2!}(\cdot)\Psi\Psi$. `h_config_tensor` returns the coefficient tensor $C_{cdxy}$ of a row in the polynomial convention of `poly_product`, $T=\frac{1}{2!\,2!}C_{cdxy}\chi^c\chi^d\Psi^x\Psi^y$ with $C$ graded symmetric (in $\varepsilon$) in $(c,d)$ and in $(x,y)$. Comparing coefficients of (8.28) with that convention,
$$C[F]_{cdxy}=2\,\mathrm{Sym}_{\rm out}\mathrm{Sym}_{\rm in}\bigl(\tilde\omega\otimes\tilde\omega\cdot F\bigr)_{cdxy},\tag{8.29}$$
where the symmetrisers act on the lowered tensor (in $\varepsilon$ on both blocks, which is the symmetry of a polynomial coefficient; on the lowered outputs $\varepsilon_c=\bar\varepsilon_{c'}$, so this is $\mathrm{Sym}_{\bar\varepsilon}$ of the map's outputs). Lowering with $\tilde\omega$ on each strand: $\tilde\omega_{cc'}\tilde\Delta^{c'c''}=-\delta_c{}^{c''}$, so two raised legs lowered give $+V$, one gives $-V$. **Free constants** (scope of the claim, calibrated 2026-09-19 after critic 026E). (8.29) carries no adjustable sign and nothing in it is fitted; the "chosen once for all rows, not adjusted per row" promise of (D1) covers **the $\hat Q$ Koszul convention D1 only**, whose alternatives ($\varepsilon$-Koszul, $Q'$ in place of $Q$) are rejected globally (§8.7.5). It does **not** cover the per-row sign dressings $\sigma$ of the second pass (§8.7.2): those were selected row by row from the sign group and matched to their R1 reading afterwards. Of the five, $\sigma_4$ on rows $A_4$ and $A_1'$ is an *independent* prediction — it is (8.6), anchored by check H1a, not by the $(2,2)$ identity — and $\sigma_2,\sigma_2'$ are plain Koszul signs; $\sigma_3$ is the first factor of the dictionary (8.9). The fit is therefore not vacuous: the falsifiable content per row is that a *constant* ratio exists over the full $\sim10^3$-component support, which a wrong $\sigma$ generically destroys (it shows up as `spread`). What is nevertheless *not* measured is the split between a coefficient and its dressing: since $\varepsilon_c+\varepsilon_d+\varepsilon_x+\varepsilon_y\equiv1$ on the support, $\sigma\to-\sigma$ is an allowed change of representative, so only the products coefficient $\times\,\sigma$ in (8.30) are determined, not the individual signs. The physical-sector identity (8.31) is free of this ambiguity: it is verified directly on the $N_{\rm gh}=2$ block as a dressing-free statement, not merely inherited from (8.30). If a row's ratio comes out $-1$ or $\pm2$, $\pm\tfrac12$, that *is* the finding.

**The seven rows as named compositions (decomposition before coding).**

| row | named map $F$ (before $\mathrm{Sym}$) | coefficient claimed in (8.21) |
|---|---|---|
| $A_1$ | $\hat Q_{\rm out}\,p_{2,2}$ | $1$ |
| $A_2$ | $p_{2,2}\circ(Q\otimes1)$, i.e. $p_{2,2}(Q\Psi,\Psi)$ | $1$ |
| $A_3$ | $p_{1,2}\circ\ell_2$ | $\tfrac12$ |
| $A_4$ | $(\ell_2\otimes1)\circ(p_{1,2}\otimes1)$: strand 1 of $p_{1,2}(e_x)$ into slot 1 of $\ell_2$, $e_y$ into slot 2 | $1$ |
| $A_1'$ | $(\ell_3\otimes1)\circ p_{0,2}$: $\ell_3(e_x,e_y,\cdot)$ on strand 1 of $p_{0,2}$ | $1$ |
| $A_2'$ | $(V_2(e_x,\cdot)\otimes1^{\otimes2})\circ p_{1,3}(e_y)$: strand 1 of $p_{1,3}$ closed on $V_2$ | $1$ |
| $A_3'$ | $(V_3(e_x,e_y,\cdot)\otimes1^{\otimes2})\circ p_{0,3}$: strand 1 of $p_{0,3}$ closed on $V_3$ | $\tfrac12$ |

Each composition feeds a raised strand into the **first** slot of the receiving vertex map, so no R1 dressing (1.8) enters except through the symmetrisers and $\hat Q$; the measured ratio then tests exactly the claimed coefficient. The measured quantity per row is the elementwise ratio $C^{\rm row}/C[F]$ (over the support of both), reported as a single number if constant, else as a sign pattern.

#### §8.7.2 Coefficient table

**First pass (definitions (D1)–(D3) applied literally, no R1 dressing; seed 20260908, toy $(2,4,4,2)$).** Elementwise ratio $C^{\rm row}/C[F]$ over the common support:

| row | named map $F$ | ratio $C^{\rm row}/C[F]$ | reading |
|---|---|---|---|
| $A_1$ | $\hat Q_{\rm out}p_{2,2}$, plain $Q$, $\bar\varepsilon$-Koszul (8.26) | $-\tfrac12$ **uniform** (support 1096 = full) | $A_1=-\tfrac12\hat Qp_{2,2}(\Psi,\Psi)$ on all sectors; with $Q'$ or with $\varepsilon$-Koszul: no proportionality / empty support |
| $A_2$ | $p_{2,2}(Q\cdot,\cdot)$ | not proportional with $Q$; with $Q'$: $\pm1$, sign pattern | magnitude 1 ✓, sign is an R1 dressing (below) |
| $A_3$ | $p_{1,2}\circ\ell_2$ | $\pm\tfrac12$, sign pattern | magnitude $\tfrac12$ ✓ (the claimed coefficient) |
| $A_4$ | $(\ell_2\otimes1)(p_{1,2}\otimes1)$, strand 1 | $\pm2$ on half the support, map vanishes on the other half | the plain symmetriser cancels an R1-dressed tensor; magnitude 2 where nonzero |
| $A_1'$ | $(\ell_3\otimes1)p_{0,2}$ | not proportional | R1 dressing needed |
| $A_2'$ | $(V_2\otimes1^{\otimes2})p_{1,3}$ | not proportional | R1 dressing needed |
| $A_3'$ | $(V_3\otimes1^{\otimes2})p_{0,3}$ | $\pm\tfrac12$, sign pattern | magnitude $\tfrac12$ ✓ |

The one row that is clean at first pass is the one under dispute: $A_1$ is exactly $-\tfrac12\hat Q_{\rm out}p_{2,2}$ with $\hat Q$ the coderivation (8.26) — the reviewer's factor $2$ is confirmed, on every component and every input sector. The remaining rows need the R1 dressings (1.8) before the plain symmetrisers of (D2) reproduce them.

**Second pass: the R1 dressings.** Write $\sigma(c,d,x,y)$ for a sign applied to the *lowered* tensor $\tilde\omega\otimes\tilde\omega\cdot F$ before the symmetrisers of (8.29), $C[F,\sigma]:=2\,\mathrm{Sym}_{\rm out}\mathrm{Sym}_{\rm in}(\sigma\cdot\tilde\omega\tilde\omega F)$; $\varepsilon_c,\varepsilon_d$ are the parities of the lowered ($\chi$-) slots, $\varepsilon_c=\bar\varepsilon_{c'}$ for the raised output $c'$. On the support of every row $\varepsilon_c+\varepsilon_d+\varepsilon_x+\varepsilon_y\equiv1$ (total ghost number $7$: $4+8-5$ for $A_1$, etc.), so each $\sigma$ is defined modulo the four factors $(-1)^{\varepsilon_i\sum_j\varepsilon_j}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_i}$; the representative below is the sparsest, and its R1 reading is given. **How the $\sigma$'s were obtained:** row by row, by selecting from that sign group the element that makes the ratio constant, and matching the result afterwards to its R1 reading; the two that matter most, $\sigma_4$ on $A_4$ and $A_1'$, are *predicted independently* by (8.6) (anchored by H1a) rather than fitted from the $(2,2)$ identity. **What is measured:** in (8.30) only the product coefficient $\times\,\sigma$ per row, since $\sigma\to-\sigma$ is an allowed change of representative on this support — a reader re-deriving (8.30) with a different representative will get different individual signs. The physical-sector form (8.31) carries no such ambiguity (§8.7.3). All ratios are exact ($<10^{-13}$) on the full support, all input-parity sectors, seeds 20260908 and 20260919 (script `part_h2_named_maps`, §8.7.5):

| row | named map $F$ (strand 1, before $\mathrm{Sym}$) | dressing $\sigma$ | ratio $C^{\rm row}/C[F,\sigma]$ | R1 reading of $\sigma$; value on physical inputs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| $A_1$ | $\hat Q_{\rm out}p_{2,2}$ (8.26) | $1$ | $-\tfrac12$ | none; $-\tfrac12$ |
| $A_2$ | $p_{2,2}(Qe_x,e_y)$ | $(-1)^{\varepsilon_y}$ | $+1$ | $Q$ enters from the right: $\mathrm{Sym}_{\rm in}$ of $\sigma F$ is $\tfrac12[p_{2,2}(e_x,Qe_y)+(-1)^{\varepsilon_y}p_{2,2}(Qe_x,e_y)]=\tfrac12p_{2,2}\circ\overleftarrow{\hat Q}_{\rm in}$; $+1$ |
| $A_3$ | $p_{1,2}\circ\ell_2$ | $(-1)^{\varepsilon_c+\varepsilon_d}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_{c'}+\varepsilon_{d'}}$ | $+\tfrac12$ | first factor of the dictionary (8.9) on the two raised strands of $p_{1,2}$; $-\tfrac12$ (outputs of $p_{1,2}$ on a ghost-number-3 input have $\varepsilon_{c'}+\varepsilon_{d'}\equiv1$) |
| $A_4$ | $(\ell_2\otimes1)(p_{1,2}(e_x)\otimes e_y)$ | $(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}$ | $+2$ | $(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}\tilde\omega_{cc'}=\omega_{cc'}$ and $\omega\circ\ell_2=-\tilde\omega\circ\ell^{\rm H}_2$: **$\ell_2\to-\ell^{\rm H}_2$, all sectors**; coefficient $-2$ with $\ell^{\rm H}_2$ |
| $A_1'$ | $(\ell_3(e_x,e_y,\cdot)\otimes1)\,p_{0,2}$ | $(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}$ | $+1$ | same: **$\ell_3\to-\ell^{\rm H}_3$, all sectors**; coefficient $-1$ with $\ell^{\rm H}_3$ |
| $A_2'$ | $(V_2(e_x,\cdot)\otimes1^{\otimes2})\,p_{1,3}(e_y)$ | $(-1)^{\varepsilon_x\bar\varepsilon_y}$ | $+1$ | $e_x$ (argument of $V_2$) passes $p_{1,3}(e_y)$, which is odd ($N_{\rm gh}(p_{1,3})=7$), parity $\bar\varepsilon_y$; $+1$ |
| $A_3'$ | $(V_3(e_x,e_y,\cdot)\otimes1^{\otimes2})\,p_{0,3}$ | $(-1)^{\varepsilon_c+\varepsilon_d}$ | $-\tfrac12$ | (8.9) first factor on the two free strands of $p_{0,3}$; $+\tfrac12$ |

**Counting rule behind the magnitudes.** Each row's coefficient tensor has $\binom{2}{j}\binom{2}{i}$ shuffle terms (assignments of the two $\chi$'s and the two $\Psi$'s to the two vertices), the map side has $2$ (the diagonal evaluation of (8.29)) times the number of terms in $F$. Hence, for a single-strand $F$ with normalised symmetrisers, $|{\rm coefficient}|=\tfrac12\binom{2}{i}\binom{2}{j}$: $A_1$: $\tfrac12\cdot1\cdot2=1$ per strand, and $\hat Q$ has two strands, so $\tfrac12\hat Q$; $A_2$: $1$; $A_3$: $\tfrac12$; $A_4$: $2$; $A_1'$: $1$; $A_2'$: $1$; $A_3'$: $\tfrac12$. The factor $2$ of $A_4$ is the sum over the two strands of $p_{1,2}(\Psi)$ that $\ell_2$ can absorb (an un-normalised, coderivation-type sum); the factor $\tfrac12$ of $A_1$ is the $1/2!$ of the two identical background legs on $V_4$, which the coderivation $\hat Q$ does not see. **The claimed coefficients of (8.21) are wrong in two places: the $\hat Q$-term is a factor $2$ too large (the reviewer's finding), and the mixed term is a factor $2$ too small when $_{\rm sym}$ is normalised.** The relative signs of (8.21) (all $+$) are also not what the tensors give; see (8.30)–(8.31).

#### §8.7.3 Corrected map identity and reading

\subsection*{Result}
**All sectors** (definitions (D1)–(D3), dressings $\sigma$ of the table, $\tilde\Delta$-raised products, plain $Q$):
$$\boxed{\;-\tfrac12\,\hat Q\,p_{2,2}(\Psi,\Psi)\;+\;\bigl[p_{2,2}(Q\Psi,\Psi)\bigr]_{\sigma_2}\;+\;\tfrac12\bigl[p_{1,2}(\ell_2(\Psi,\Psi))\bigr]_{\sigma_3}\;+\;2\bigl[(\ell_2\otimes1)(p_{1,2}(\Psi)\otimes\Psi)\bigr]_{\sigma_4,{\rm sym}}\;+\;\bigl[(\ell_3(\Psi,\Psi,\cdot)\otimes1)p_{0,2}\bigr]_{\sigma_4,{\rm sym}}\;+\;\bigl[(V_2(\Psi,\cdot)\otimes1^{\otimes2})p_{1,3}(\Psi)\bigr]_{\sigma_2'}\;-\;\tfrac12\bigl[(V_3(\Psi,\Psi,\cdot)\otimes1^{\otimes2})p_{0,3}\bigr]_{\sigma_3}\;=\;0,\;}\tag{8.30}$$
$[\cdot]_\sigma$ meaning the named single-strand map, R1-dressed by $\sigma$ on its lowered slots and then $\mathrm{Sym}_{\rm out}\mathrm{Sym}_{\rm in}$-projected (normalised), evaluated at $(\Psi,\Psi)$; $\sigma_2=(-1)^{\varepsilon_y}$, $\sigma_3=(-1)^{\varepsilon_c+\varepsilon_d}$, $\sigma_4=(-1)^{\varepsilon_c}$, $\sigma_2'=(-1)^{\varepsilon_x\bar\varepsilon_y}$. Verified as the sum of the seven named-map tensors (no configuration code path), $1.2\cdot10^{-14}$ against terms of size $23$–$41$.

**Physical sector** ($N_{\rm gh}(\Psi)=2$, every component): the dressings are uniform, $\sigma_2=\sigma_2'=1$, $\sigma_3=-1$, and $\sigma_4$ is the replacement $\ell_n\to-\ell^{\rm H}_n$ ($\ell^{\rm H}_n=\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$, Harold's product (8.5)), so
$$\boxed{\;-\tfrac12\,\hat Q\,p_{2,2}(\Psi,\Psi)+p_{2,2}(Q\Psi,\Psi)-\tfrac12\,p_{1,2}\bigl(\ell_2(\Psi,\Psi)\bigr)-2\bigl[(\ell^{\rm H}_2\otimes1)(p_{1,2}(\Psi)\otimes\Psi)\bigr]_{\rm sym}-\bigl[(\ell^{\rm H}_3(\Psi,\Psi,\cdot)\otimes1)p_{0,2}\bigr]_{\rm sym}+\bigl(V_2(\Psi,\cdot)\otimes1^{\otimes2}\bigr)p_{1,3}(\Psi)+\tfrac12\bigl(V_3(\Psi,\Psi,\cdot)\otimes1^{\otimes2}\bigr)p_{0,3}=0.\;}\tag{8.31}$$
Verified on the $N_{\rm gh}=2$ input block ($1.1\cdot10^{-14}$; the same expression is $55$ off that block, so the block restriction is load-bearing). **(8.31) is not merely (8.30) restricted**: it is assembled independently, with $\ell^{\rm H}$ and no sign tensors, and checked as a stand-alone identity on that block, so it does not inherit the dressing-representative ambiguity of §8.7.1 ("Free constants"); only (8.30)'s individual signs carry it. Here $\hat Q$ is the coderivation (8.26) with the $\bar\varepsilon$-Koszul sign, $Q$ is plain, $p_{k,\ell}$ are $\tilde\Delta$-raised, $_{\rm sym}$ is the normalised $\mathrm{Sym}_{\bar\varepsilon}$ over the two free output strands, "$\otimes1$" feeds strand 1 of the symmetric tensor. (The two $\ell^{\rm H}$'s are the only place Harold's products enter; everything else is in the record's convention. The mixed term can equivalently be written without the $2$ as $\hat\ell^{\rm H}_2$-type sum over *both* strands of $p_{1,2}(\Psi)$.)

**The reading.** Since $p_{2,2}$ is even and $\Psi$ is even, $p_{2,2}\hat Q_{\rm in}(\Psi,\Psi)=2\,p_{2,2}(Q\Psi,\Psi)$ (machine-checked 2026-09-19, defect $3.6\cdot10^{-15}$; break legs: coefficient $1$ fails at $6.6$, and the identity fails at $17.6$ for two *independent* even vectors, so it is a property of the diagonal evaluation at $(\Psi,\Psi)$, not of the tensor), so the first two terms of (8.31) are $-\tfrac12[\hat Q,p_{2,2}](\Psi,\Psi)$ with $[\hat Q,p_{2,2}]:=\hat Q_{\rm out}p_{2,2}-p_{2,2}\hat Q_{\rm in}$ the graded commutator — a genuine commutator, not the sum written in (8.21). Multiplying (8.31) by $-2$:
$$\underbrace{[\hat Q,p_{2,2}](\Psi,\Psi)}_{\text{failure of }Q\text{ to be a derivation of }p_{2,2}}+\underbrace{p_{1,2}\bigl(\ell_2(\Psi,\Psi)\bigr)}_{\text{Jacobiator analogue}}=-4\bigl[(\ell^{\rm H}_2\otimes1)(p_{1,2}(\Psi)\otimes\Psi)\bigr]_{\rm sym}\;-\;2\bigl[(\ell^{\rm H}_3\otimes1)p_{0,2}\bigr]_{\rm sym}+2\bigl(V_2\otimes1^{\otimes2}\bigr)p_{1,3}(\Psi)+\bigl(V_3\otimes1^{\otimes2}\bigr)p_{0,3}.\tag{8.32}$$
In $\chi$-test form (8.32) is $B_{2,2}=0$ written as $A_1+A_2+A_3=-(A_4+A_1'+A_2'+A_3')$, which becomes (8.20), $2(A_1+A_2+A_3)+A_4=0$, only *after* using the mirror symmetry $A_i'=A_i$. The two statements the reviewer asked to reconcile are the same fact seen before and after that substitution: (i) *the augmented combination $A_1+A_2+A_3+A_4$ is not zero (it equals $\tfrac12A_4$, the mixed term with half weight), and becomes zero only when the three mirror terms $A_1'+A_2'+A_3'=A_1+A_2+A_3$ are added* — this is "closes only when the mirrors are added"; (ii) *after the substitution, the true relation counts the mixed term once against $2(A_1+A_2+A_3)$, whereas the augmented one counts it once against $(A_1+A_2+A_3)$* — this is "differ only in the weight of the mixed term". Both are correct; the wording the tensors support is: **"$Q$ is a derivation of $p_{2,2}$ up to the Jacobiator analogue" fails at $(2,2)$ by the mixed term $A_4=(\ell_2\otimes1)(p_{1,2}\otimes\Psi)$-type gluing of two cubic vertices; the relation that does hold, (8.31)/(8.32), is the one in which the three no-input/no-output terms $p_{0,2},p_{2,0},p_{0,3}$ are kept — and since each of those equals one of the first three terms (mirror symmetry (3.3)), keeping them is the same as doubling $A_1+A_2+A_3$ relative to $A_4$.** So "$Q$-derivation up to Jacobiator" still fails at $(2,2)$; what changes with respect to §8.3 is only the coefficient and sign bookkeeping of the map form, not the conclusion.

**Meaning of the dressings.** Rows $A_4$ and $A_1'$ are the two in which the *output* of a one-output product $\ell_n$ is a free strand of the result; there the $\tilde\Delta$-raised $\ell_n$ is the wrong object by exactly the sign $-(-1)^{\varepsilon_{\rm out}}$ of (8.6), i.e. those two rows want Harold's $\ell^{\rm H}_n$ (with a $-$). Rows $A_3$, $A_3'$ are the two in which *two* free strands come from the same raised vertex and the row carries the first factor $(-1)^{\sum\varepsilon_{\rm out}}$ of (8.9) — the same "$(-1)^\ell$ on physical inputs" of (8.11)–(8.12). Rows $A_2$, $A_2'$ carry an input-side Koszul sign that is $+1$ on even inputs. $A_1$ carries none. This is the concrete content of the "R1 dressings needed to write each row as a named composition" flagged open at (3.26) and §8.5: they are all instances of (8.6)/(8.9) plus one input-side Koszul sign.

\subsection*{Verification}
§8.7.5. What is *not* checked: the $(1,3)$ rows and any $(k,\ell)$ beyond $(2,2)$ and $(1,2)$; the dressings are established at $(2,2)$ only (at $(1,2)$ the finding is that none is needed — same statement as §8.5's "at $(2,2)$ and $(1,2)$"); and the individual signs of (8.30) are representative-dependent (§8.7.1).

#### §8.7.4 Replacement text and routing (for the fix pass; §8.3, §8.5 and the companion are not edited here)

**(a) Replace (8.21) and the sentence after it** ("In map form, with the $\tilde\Delta$-raised products of (1.6), where each configuration … carries no relative sign: (8.21) … holds unchanged in Harold's convention.") by:

> In map form, on physical inputs ($N_{\rm gh}(\Psi)=2$), with the $\tilde\Delta$-raised products of (1.6), the plain $Q$, and $\ell^{\rm H}_n=\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$ where indicated,
> $$-\tfrac12\hat Q\,p_{2,2}(\Psi,\Psi)+p_{2,2}(Q\Psi,\Psi)-\tfrac12p_{1,2}(\ell_2(\Psi,\Psi))-2\bigl[(\ell^{\rm H}_2\otimes1)(p_{1,2}(\Psi)\otimes\Psi)\bigr]_{\rm sym}-\bigl[(\ell^{\rm H}_3(\Psi,\Psi,\cdot)\otimes1)p_{0,2}\bigr]_{\rm sym}+\bigl(V_2(\Psi,\cdot)\otimes1^{\otimes2}\bigr)p_{1,3}(\Psi)+\tfrac12\bigl(V_3(\Psi,\Psi,\cdot)\otimes1^{\otimes2}\bigr)p_{0,3}=0,\tag{8.21}$$
> where $\hat Q=Q\otimes1+1\otimes Q$ is the coderivation on two output strands with the Koszul sign of the strand passed taken in the shifted parity $\bar\varepsilon$, $[\cdot]_{\rm sym}$ is the normalised graded symmetrisation over the two free output strands in $\bar\varepsilon$, "$\otimes1$" feeds strand 1 of a symmetric tensor into the map to its left, and every term is evaluated at $(\Psi,\Psi)$. The coefficients are $\tfrac12\binom2i\binom2j$ per single-strand term (§8.7.2): the $\tfrac12$ on $\hat Qp_{2,2}$ is the $1/2!$ of the two background legs on $V_4$, which the two-strand coderivation does not see; the $2$ on the mixed term is the sum over the two strands of $p_{1,2}(\Psi)$ that $\ell_2$ can absorb. Off the physical sector the same identity holds with the R1 sign dressings of §8.7.2, (8.30). The first two terms are $-\tfrac12[\hat Q,p_{2,2}](\Psi,\Psi)$ (graded commutator; $p_{2,2}\hat Q_{\rm in}(\Psi,\Psi)=2p_{2,2}(Q\Psi,\Psi)$ on even $\Psi$).

**(b) Commentary, row $A_1$** — replace "So $A_1$ is $\hat Q$ (the coderivation extension …) applied to $p_{2,2}(\Psi,\Psi)$." by: "So $A_1$ is $Q$ on *one* strand of $p_{2,2}(\Psi,\Psi)$, i.e. $-\tfrac12\hat Q\,p_{2,2}(\Psi,\Psi)$ with $\hat Q$ the coderivation (both strands): the $\tfrac12$ is the $1/2!$ of the two background legs (§8.7.2, ratio $-\tfrac12$ exact on all sectors)."

**(d) "Result — what it says physically", displayed equation** — replace by (8.32) and the sentence: "*i.e. the failure of $Q$ to be a derivation of $p_{2,2}$, corrected by the Jacobiator analogue, equals the two-string state glued from two cubic vertices along a propagator with one leg of each on $\Psi$ (the mixed term), plus the three no-input/no-output terms; by mirror symmetry the latter reproduce the left-hand side up to sign ($+2P_{2,2}$ against the left-hand side's $-2P_{2,2}$), so the relation is $2P_{2,2}=-M_{2,2}$.*" Keep the "Keep separate" paragraph; replace "It closes only when the three mirror terms are added" by "It closes only when the three mirror terms are added — and since each mirror equals one of the first three terms, that is the same as saying the true relation weighs the mixed term once against twice the augmented terms."

**(e) §8.5 last paragraph** — replace "is stated from the leg count and (3.12) and is not verified elementwise … it does not affect (8.19)/(8.21)" by: "is verified elementwise in §8.7 (`part_h2_named_maps`, H2N); the R1 dressings are (8.30) and the physical-sector form is (8.31). (8.19) is unchanged; (8.21) is corrected in §8.7.4 (a)."

**(f) Companion `knowledge/companion-ibl-bookkeeping.tex`** (science-writer, Harold's notation): `eq:comp:22-map` → the physical-sector form (8.31) with the list items 1 and 4 reworded ("$\tfrac12\hat Q$ …: $Q$ on one of the two open strands, the $\tfrac12$ being the symmetry factor of the two background legs" / "twice the …: either of the two strands may be glued"); the "One caveat on the naming" paragraph → deleted or replaced by one sentence saying the placement of the Koszul signs is now fixed and, on physical inputs, amounts to using the sign-free products $\ell^{\rm H}$ in the two terms where a cubic or quartic product's output is left open; `eq:comp:22-reading` → (8.32) with the commutator $[\hat Q,p_{2,2}]$ on the left; the sentence "It closes only once the last three terms … are added" → the wording of (d). Note for the writer: in Harold's convention $p_{k,1}=-\ell^{\rm H}_k$ on physical inputs (8.12) but the two $\ell^{\rm H}$'s in (8.31) act on a non-physical strand, so they must stay $\ell^{\rm H}$ (not be traded for $-\ell_n$).

#### §8.7.5 Verification (`part_h2_named_maps`, 16 checks H2N; script total 142/142 PASS, seeds 20260908 and 20260919)

Independent construction: each row is assembled from `raise_map`, `Q`, `V_2`, `V_3` only, lowered with $\tilde\omega\otimes\tilde\omega$, dressed, and symmetrised by (8.29); `h_config_tensor` is used only as the reference side. Per row: the elementwise ratio over the full support is constant to $<4\cdot10^{-13}$ and equals the table's coefficient; break-on-purpose legs per row: factor $2$ on the named map leaves $18$–$41$, sign flip leaves $37$–$81$. Assembly: the seven named-map terms with the corrected coefficients sum to $1.2\cdot10^{-14}$; with coefficient $1$ on $\hat Qp_{2,2}$ (as in the old (8.21)) the residual is $23.6$. D1 alternatives: the $\varepsilon$-Koszul coderivation gives a tensor with support disjoint from $A_1$, and $Q'=(-1)^\varepsilon Q$ inside $\hat Q$ is not proportional (spread $8.6$). Physical-sector form (8.31): $1.1\cdot10^{-14}$ on the $N_{\rm gh}=2$ input block, $55$ off it. H2d's even/odd random input pairs (seed 20260914): named-map assembly sums to $2\cdot10^{-15}$ with the $\hat Q$-term at $8.0$ / $10.5$. Graded-commutator step (added 2026-09-19): $p_{2,2}\hat Q_{\rm in}(\Psi,\Psi)=2p_{2,2}(Q\Psi,\Psi)$ on even $\Psi$, defect $3.6\cdot10^{-15}$, with two break legs — coefficient $1$ instead of $2$ fails at $6.6$, and two *independent* even vectors in place of the diagonal $(\Psi,\Psi)$ fail at $17.6$ (so the check tests the diagonal evaluation, not a tensor identity).
