## §9 Addendum (2026-09-26): scope of R16 — $P_{k,\ell}\ne0$ at every $(k,\ell)$ with $k,\ell\ge2$

- Brief: `agent/briefs/active-computator-r16-scope-20260926T102500.md` (Task 9f). Append-only; nothing above this heading is edited.
- Restated literal task: R16/R31 say the augmented relation at $(k,\ell)$, $k,\ell\ge2$, is equivalent (on cyclic $L_\infty$ data) to the extra condition $P_{k,\ell}=0$, "seen to fail at $(2,2),(2,3),(3,2)$". Either prove that $P_{k,\ell}=0$ is violated for some cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra at *every* $k,\ell\ge2$, or narrow the rows to the signatures machine-checked.
- Objective served: R16 is the negative companion of Theorem A′ (R31); the paper cites it as the reason the augmented relations play no role, so its quantifier must be exactly what is shown.
- Conventions: §1 and §3.1 verbatim (bracket $\{F,G\}=\partial_{\Psi^a}F\,\tilde\Delta^{ab}\,\partial_{\chi^b}G$, $u=\Psi+\chi$, $H_{k,\ell}$, $T(i,j|m,n)$, $B,R,M,P$ of (3.4)–(3.5)). No new convention.

**Definition quoted (§3.1 (3.4)–(3.5), §3.2 (3.13)).** $R_{k,\ell}=\sum_{i+m=k,\,j+n=\ell,\,j\ge1,\,m\ge1}T(i,j|m,n)$, $M_{k,\ell}$ the same sum restricted to $i,j,m,n\ge1$, $P_{k,\ell}:=R_{k,\ell}-M_{k,\ell}$ (the $k+\ell-1$ pure configurations, explicitly (3.13)); on cyclic $L_\infty$ data $B_{k,\ell}=2P_{k,\ell}+M_{k,\ell}=0$ (3.6), (3.15), so the augmented relation $R_{k,\ell}=0$ is equivalent to $P_{k,\ell}=0$ and to $M_{k,\ell}=0$.

### §9.1 Generating-function form of $P$ and $M$

\subsection*{Setup}
§3.1 conventions. Gradient $\mathcal F_a(u):=(\partial_aS)(u)=\sum_{n\ge1}\frac1{n!}V_{n+1}(e_a,u^n)$ (covector-valued; by (3.11) $\mathcal F_a=\omega_{ac}\sum_n\frac1{n!}\ell^{\rm H}_n(u^n)^c$, with $\ell^{\rm H}_n$ the sign-free products meant in (3.11); with the $\tilde\Delta$-raised $\ell_n$ of (1.4) a factor $-(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}$ appears, which does not affect (9.4)). Pairing $\langle X,Y\rangle:=X_a\,\tilde\Delta^{ab}\,Y_b$ ($=\tilde\omega$ of the raised vectors, (3.12)). Split $\mathcal F(\Psi+\chi)=\mathcal F(\Psi)+\mathcal F(\chi)+\delta\mathcal F(\Psi,\chi)$, $\delta\mathcal F$ = the components of bidegree $(i,j)$ with $i,j\ge1$ ($\mathcal F(0)=0$ since $V_1=0$).

\subsection*{Derivation}
$$\partial_{\Psi^a}H_{i+1,j}=\mathcal F_a(u)\big|_{(i,j)},\qquad \partial_{\chi^b}H_{m,n+1}=\mathcal F_b(u)\big|_{(m,n)},\qquad T(i,j|m,n)=\big\langle\mathcal F|_{(i,j)},\mathcal F|_{(m,n)}\big\rangle,\tag{9.1}$$
$$\sum_{k,\ell}B_{k,\ell}=\langle\mathcal F(u),\mathcal F(u)\rangle,\qquad \sum_{k,\ell}R_{k,\ell}=\langle\mathcal F(\chi)+\delta\mathcal F,\ \mathcal F(\Psi)+\delta\mathcal F\rangle,\qquad \sum_{k,\ell}M_{k,\ell}=\langle\delta\mathcal F,\delta\mathcal F\rangle,\tag{9.2}$$
$$\sum_{k,\ell\ge1}P_{k,\ell}=\langle\mathcal F(\chi),\mathcal F(\Psi)\rangle+\langle\mathcal F(\chi),\delta\mathcal F\rangle+\langle\delta\mathcal F,\mathcal F(\Psi)\rangle,\tag{9.3}$$
$$\text{cyclic }L_\infty:\quad P_{k,\ell}=-\tfrac12M_{k,\ell}=-\tfrac12\langle\delta\mathcal F,\delta\mathcal F\rangle\big|_{(k,\ell)}=\Big[\langle\mathcal F(\Psi+\chi),\mathcal F(\Psi)+\mathcal F(\chi)\rangle-\langle\mathcal F(\Psi),\mathcal F(\chi)\rangle\Big]_{(k,\ell)}\quad(k,\ell\ge1).\tag{9.4}$$

\subsection*{Commentary}
(9.1): (3.1); the bidegree-$(i,j)$ part of $\sum_n\frac1{n!}V_{n+1}(e_a,u^n)$ is $\binom{i+j}{i}\frac1{(i+j)!}V(e_a,\Psi^i,\chi^j)$ by graded symmetry ($\Psi,\chi$ even), which is $\frac1{i!j!}V(e_a,\Psi^i,\chi^j)$. (9.2): sum (3.4)–(3.5) over bidegrees; the constraint $j\ge1$ on vertex 1 selects $\mathcal F(\chi)+\delta\mathcal F$, $m\ge1$ on vertex 2 selects $\mathcal F(\Psi)+\delta\mathcal F$, and $i,n\ge1$ in addition selects $\delta\mathcal F$ in both. (9.3): $R-M$; componentwise it is (3.13) (the $j=\ell$ term of (3.13) is $\langle\mathcal F(\chi),\mathcal F(\Psi)\rangle$, the $j<\ell$ terms are $\langle\mathcal F(\chi),\delta\mathcal F\rangle$, the $m\le k-1$ terms are $\langle\delta\mathcal F,\mathcal F(\Psi)\rangle$). (9.4): first equality (3.15); last equality: expand $\langle a-b-c,a-b-c\rangle$ with $a=\mathcal F(u)$, $b=\mathcal F(\Psi)$, $c=\mathcal F(\chi)$, use $\langle a,a\rangle=\langle b,b\rangle=\langle c,c\rangle=0$ ((3.9) at $u$, $\Psi$, $\chi$) and the symmetry $\langle X,Y\rangle=\langle Y,X\rangle$ (3.3).

\subsection*{Result}
(3.13) is the closed form of $P_{k,\ell}$ in the products; (9.3)–(9.4) are its generating function. On cyclic $L_\infty$ data $P=-\tfrac12\langle\delta\mathcal F,\delta\mathcal F\rangle$: the augmented relations at all $k,\ell\ge2$ together say that the mixed part of the equation-of-motion vector is $\tilde\omega$-isotropic, a quadratic condition that the master equation $\langle\mathcal F(u),\mathcal F(u)\rangle=0$ does not contain.

### §9.2 Spectator family: $M_{k,\ell}=h_{k-2,\ell-2}\,x^{k-2}y^{\ell-2}\,M^{\mathfrak h}_{2,2}$

\subsection*{Setup}
Base: a cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra $(\mathfrak h,\omega^{\mathfrak h})$ with purely cubic action $S_{\mathfrak h}=\frac1{3!}V_3(\psi^3)$, $(S_{\mathfrak h},S_{\mathfrak h})=0$ ($Q=0$, only $\ell_2$), e.g. the CE algebra $\mathfrak g_{(1)}\oplus\mathfrak g^*_{(4)}$ of (3.19). Write $\sigma:=\mathcal F^{\mathfrak h}$ (quadratic) and $\sigma(\psi+\xi)=\sigma(\psi)+\sigma(\xi)+\sigma^\times(\psi,\xi)$, $\sigma^\times$ bilinear; $M^{\mathfrak h}_{2,2}=\langle\sigma^\times,\sigma^\times\rangle$ (3.16). $g(x)=\sum_{r\ge0}g_rx^r$ a formal power series (or a polynomial).

\subsection*{Derivation}
$$\mathcal H'=\mathfrak h\oplus\langle e,e^*\rangle,\quad N_{\rm gh}(e)=2,\ N_{\rm gh}(e^*)=3,\quad \omega_1(e,e^*)=1,\ \ \omega_1(\mathfrak h,\langle e,e^*\rangle)=0;\qquad \Psi=\psi+x\,e+x^*e^*,\ \ \chi=\xi+y\,e+y^*e^*,\tag{9.5}$$
$$S'(\Psi)=g(x)\,S_{\mathfrak h}(\psi),\qquad V'_{3+r}(e^{\otimes r},a,b,c)=r!\,g_r\,V_3(a,b,c)\ \ (e\text{ in any }r\text{ slots}),\ \text{all other components }0,\tag{9.6}$$
$$(S',S')=g(x)^2\,(S_{\mathfrak h},S_{\mathfrak h})=0,\tag{9.7}$$
$$\mathcal F'_a(\Psi)=g(x)\,\sigma_a(\psi)\ (a\in\mathfrak h),\qquad \mathcal F'_{e}(\Psi)=g'(x)\,S_{\mathfrak h}(\psi),\qquad \mathcal F'_{e^*}=0,\tag{9.8}$$
$$\delta\mathcal F'_a=A\,\sigma_a(\psi)+B\,\sigma_a(\xi)+C\,\sigma^\times_a,\qquad A=g(x+y)-g(x),\ \ B=g(x+y)-g(y),\ \ C=g(x+y),\tag{9.9}$$
$$\langle\sigma(\psi),\sigma(\psi)\rangle=0,\qquad \langle\sigma(\psi),\sigma^\times\rangle+\langle\sigma^\times,\sigma(\psi)\rangle=0,\qquad \langle\sigma(\psi),\sigma(\xi)\rangle+\langle\sigma(\xi),\sigma(\psi)\rangle=-\langle\sigma^\times,\sigma^\times\rangle=-M^{\mathfrak h}_{2,2},\tag{9.10}$$
$$M'(\Psi,\chi)=\langle\delta\mathcal F',\delta\mathcal F'\rangle=(C^2-AB)\,M^{\mathfrak h}_{2,2}(\psi,\xi)=h_g(x,y)\,M^{\mathfrak h}_{2,2}(\psi,\xi),\qquad h_g(x,y)=g(x+y)\,[g(x)+g(y)]-g(x)\,g(y),\tag{9.11}$$
$$P'_{k,\ell}=-\tfrac12\,[h_g]_{k-2,\ell-2}\;x^{k-2}\,y^{\ell-2}\;M^{\mathfrak h}_{2,2}(\psi,\xi)\qquad(k,\ell\ge2),\tag{9.12}$$
$$g=e^{x}:\quad h=e^{2x+y}+e^{x+2y}-e^{x+y},\qquad [h]_{a,b}=\frac{2^a+2^b-1}{a!\,b!}\ \ge\ \frac1{a!\,b!}>0,\tag{9.13}$$
$$g=1+x+c\,x^2:\quad [h]_{0,0}=1,\ \ [h]_{1,0}=[h]_{0,1}=2,\ \ [h]_{1,1}=4c+1,\ \ [h]_{2,0}=[h]_{0,2}=2c+1.\tag{9.14}$$

\subsection*{Commentary}
(9.5): $e$ is even (ghost 2), $e^*$ odd (ghost 3), so $x$ is an even and $x^*$ an odd coordinate; the pairing saturates at ghost 5 as the toy's $F\leftrightarrow A$ block (§1.5), and each $e$ adds 2 to a vertex's ghost count, so $V'_n$ saturates at $2n$ like every vertex of generator (iv). (9.6): $\frac1{(3+r)!}V'_{3+r}(\Psi^{3+r})=\binom{3+r}{r}\frac{r!\,g_r}{(3+r)!}\,x^r\,V_3(\psi^3)=g_r\,x^r\,S_{\mathfrak h}(\psi)$; $e$ is even, so inserting it costs no Koszul sign and graded symmetry is inherited from $V_3$; $V'_1=V'_2=0$ ($Q'=0$). (9.7): $\tilde\Delta$ is block-diagonal; the $\langle e,e^*\rangle$ block is proportional to $\partial_xS'\,\partial_{x^*}S'$ and $S'$ does not depend on $x^*$; on the $\mathfrak h$ block $\partial_a(gS_{\mathfrak h})=g\,\partial_aS_{\mathfrak h}$ with $g$ an even scalar. So $(\mathcal H',\omega_1,\ell'_n=\tilde\Delta\circ V'_{n+1})$ is cyclic $L_\infty$, with $\ell'_n\ne0$ for every $n\ge2$ with $g_{n-2}\ne0$. (9.8): the $e$-component of $\mathcal F'$ pairs only with the $e^*$-component, which vanishes, so the $\langle e,e^*\rangle$ block drops from every pairing below. (9.9): $\delta$ of $g(x)\sigma(\psi)$ is $g(x+y)\sigma(\psi+\xi)-g(x)\sigma(\psi)-g(y)\sigma(\xi)$. (9.10): the master equation of the base, $\langle\sigma(u),\sigma(u)\rangle=0$ at $u=\psi+\xi$ ((3.8)–(3.9)), split into bidegrees $(4,0)$, $(3,1)$, $(2,2)$; the split is clean because $\sigma$ is homogeneous of degree 2 — this is where $Q=0$ and pure cubicity of $S_{\mathfrak h}$ are used; with $Q\ne0$ the bidegree pieces mix and (9.11) does not factorise. The combinations appear in (9.11) exactly as in (9.10), so no symmetry of $\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle$ is needed. (9.10) lists only the $(4,0)$, $(3,1)$, $(2,2)$ pieces; the $B^2$ and $BC$ terms of (9.11) use the $(0,4)$ and $(1,3)$ pieces, which are the $\psi\leftrightarrow\xi$ images. (9.11): expand with $A,B,C$ even scalars; the $A^2$, $B^2$, $AC$, $BC$ terms vanish by (9.10), the $AB$ term gives $-AB\,M^{\mathfrak h}_{2,2}$, the $C^2$ term $C^2M^{\mathfrak h}_{2,2}$; $C^2-AB=g(x+y)[g(x)+g(y)]-g(x)g(y)$. (9.12): (9.4); $M^{\mathfrak h}_{2,2}$ has bidegree $(2,2)$ in $(\psi,\xi)$, $x$ counts in the $\Psi$-degree and $y$ in the $\chi$-degree. (9.13): Taylor coefficients of the three exponentials. (9.14): $[h]_{1,0}=2g_1$, $[h]_{1,1}=4g_2+g_1^2$, $[h]_{0,2}=2g_2+g_1^2$ at $g_0=1$.

\subsection*{Result}
**Proposition 9.1 (proven; verified, §9.3).** For every cubic cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra $\mathfrak h$ with $Q=0$ and every $g$, the spectator extension (9.5)–(9.6) is cyclic $L_\infty$ and
$$\boxed{\;P'_{k,\ell}=-\tfrac12\,[h_g]_{k-2,\ell-2}\,x^{k-2}y^{\ell-2}\,M^{\mathfrak h}_{2,2}(\psi,\xi),\qquad h_g=g(x+y)[g(x)+g(y)]-g(x)g(y)\qquad(k,\ell\ge2).\;}$$
**Corollary 9.2.** Take $\mathfrak h=\mathrm{CE}(\mathfrak{sl}_2)$, for which $M^{\mathfrak h}_{2,2}\ne0$ ((3.19), E3), and $g=e^x$. Then $P'_{k,\ell}=-\tfrac12\frac{2^{k-2}+2^{\ell-2}-1}{(k-2)!\,(\ell-2)!}\,x^{k-2}y^{\ell-2}M^{\rm CE}_{2,2}\ne0$ for **every** $k,\ell\ge2$: the condition $P_{k,\ell}=0$ — equivalently the augmented relation $R_{k,\ell}=0$ — is not implied by the cyclic $L_\infty$ relations at any signature with $k,\ell\ge2$, and one algebra violates all of them at once. For a fixed $(k,\ell)$, truncating $g$ at $x^{k+\ell-4}$ gives a finite example with vertices $V_3,\dots,V_{k+\ell-1}$ (the coefficient $[h]_{k-2,\ell-2}$ involves $g_0,\dots,g_{k+\ell-4}$ only).

**Calibration of "generic".** $P_{k,\ell}$ is a polynomial in the structure constants; Corollary 9.2 shows it is not identically zero on the cyclic $L_\infty$ structures, at any $k,\ell\ge2$. It does vanish on sub-loci: on every cyclic dg Lie algebra at $(k,\ell)\ne(2,2)$, trivially (no product of arity $\ge3$ enters $M_{k,\ell}$), and inside the spectator family on $[h_g]_{k-2,\ell-2}=0$ — e.g. (9.14) with $c=-\tfrac14$ gives $P'_{3,3}=0$ through a cancellation among seven nonzero configurations of $R_{3,3}$ (three pure, four mixed) while $P'_{2,2}$, $P'_{2,4}$, $P'_{4,2}\ne0$. So $P_{3,3}=0$ does not imply $P_{k,\ell}=0$ at some other signatures (e.g. $(2,2)$, $(2,4)$, $(4,2)$). The cancellation family is quadratic in $g$, so $[h]_{a,b}=0$ for $a+b>4$ and $P'=0$ at every $k+\ell>8$.

**Interpretation.** $S'=e^{x}S_{\rm CE}$ is the CE action with its coupling promoted to an exponential of a ghost-2 field (a dilaton-like modulus). The augmented relations at $(k,\ell)\ne(2,2)$ fail through the coupling dependence of the vertices, and the failure at $(k,\ell)$ is the $(2,2)$ obstruction dressed by the Taylor coefficient $[h]_{k-2,\ell-2}$. Nothing is claimed here for the closed-SFT algebra. The witness has $P'_{k,\ell}\equiv0$ on ghost-2 inputs at every $(k,\ell)$ (its only ghost-2 direction is the spectator $e$; the CE base sits at ghost 1 and 4, so $M^{\rm CE}_{2,2}(\psi,\xi)$ vanishes once $\psi=0$ or $\xi=0$), so Cor. 9.2 is silent on the physical sector, where the failure is seen on the generator (iv) at $(2,2)$ only (check E5).

### §9.3 Verification (`codes/verify-r16-scope.py`)

Run: `.venv/bin/python codes/verify-r16-scope.py` (default `--kmax 5`), **`summary: 183/183 PASS (seed 20260926)`**, exit 0, 1.6 s; log `agent/scratch/r16-scope-run1.log`. The existing script `codes/verify-linfty-ibl-repackaging.py` is not modified.

Method: exact sparse Grassmann polynomials over $\mathbb Q$ in the doubled coordinates, parity $=N_{\rm gh}$ mod 2. Every quantity is computed from the definitions (3.1)–(3.5): $H_{p,q}$ = bidegree parts of $S'(\Psi+\chi)$ built by polynomial multiplication, left derivatives, $\{F,G\}=\partial_{\Psi^a}F\,\tilde\Delta^{ab}\,\partial_{\chi^b}G$ with $\tilde\Delta^{ab}=(-1)^{\varepsilon_a}\Delta^{ab}$, $\Delta=\omega^{-1}$, then $T(i,j|m,n)$ and the sums $B,R,M,P$. The factorisation (9.9)–(9.11) is not used; $h_g$ is computed by sympy; $M^{\mathfrak h}_{2,2}$ is computed by the same definition-level code on the base action alone, so what is tested is the relation between two definition-level quantities. Independent of the dense-tensor code of 026 (not imported).

|---|---|---|
| `9.2-master` (25) | $B'_{k,\ell}=0$: the extension is cyclic $L_\infty$ (9.7) | PASS at every signature; break leg: a Jacobi-violating random $f$ gives $\lvert B'_{2,3}\rvert=56$ (FAILS as it must) |
| `9.2-nonhollow` (25) | configurations $T(i,j\mid m,n)$ individually nonzero | PASS (e.g. 10/14 nonzero at $(3,3)$ in the cancellation family) |
| `3.15` (25) | $M'=-2P'$ | PASS; break leg: Jacobi-violating $f$ gives $\lvert M'+2P'\rvert=56$ |
| `9.2-closed` (25) | (9.12), exact equality of polynomials | PASS at $\mathfrak{sl}_2$/exp: all $(k,\ell)\in\{2,..,5\}^2$ and $(2,6)$, $(6,2)$ (e.g. $[h]=\tfrac52$ at $(3,4)$, $\tfrac5{12}$ at $(5,5)$); $\mathfrak{aff}(1)$/exp at $(2,2),(2,3),(3,3),(2,4)$; cancellation family at $(3,3),(2,4),(4,2)$; break leg at every one of the 25 signatures: flipping the sign of the $g(x)g(y)$ term is rejected |

(Counts: 25 signature-family pairs × 7 checks + 3 base + 1 + 2 + 2 = 183.)

Node-check remark (2026-09-30): the `9.2-nonaug` checks restate check `3.15` (`R + P` is the same polynomial as `M + 2P`, since the code sets $P:=R-M$; "$\lvert R\rvert>0$ iff $[h]\ne0$" is `9.2-nonzero`, "$B=0$" is `9.2-master`). So 25 of the 183 are restatements (158 distinct checks), and the claim "non-augmented $B=0$" has no separate test. No script change.

Run history (diagnosis before fixing, per protocol). First trial run (`--kmax 2`, not logged): the break-leg variant $h\to g(x+y)^2$ was not rejected at $a=0$ or $b=0$ — for $g=e^x$, $2^{a+b}=2^a+2^b-1$ iff $(2^a-1)(2^b-1)=0$, so that variant coincides with $h$ there: a design error of the break leg, not of the result; replaced by the sign flip of the $g(x)g(y)$ term, which differs from $h$ by $2g(x)g(y)$, all of whose Taylor coefficients are positive for $g=e^x$. Second trial run: the `9.2-nonaug` check asserted $R\ne0$ also at the cancellation point, where $R=-P=0$ is the prediction — check typo, corrected to "nonzero iff $[h]\ne0$". No result or prediction changed; the logged run is the third.

Limiting case: $g=1$ gives $h=1$, i.e. $P'_{2,2}=-\tfrac12M^{\mathfrak h}_{2,2}$ ((3.16)–(3.17)) and $P'_{k,\ell}=0$ at every other signature (dg Lie). This is the $(0,0)$ coefficient of every family above; the other signatures of $g=1$ are hollow (no configuration survives) and were not run as evidence.

Not verified: the physical closed-SFT algebra (not in scope: the statement is about the class); generator (iv) beyond $(2,2),(2,3),(3,2)$ (not needed once the general statement is proven; its rank-6 and rank-7 tensors on the 24-dimensional doubled space are outside the dense machinery's memory budget); the sign conventions of the sparse code against 026's dense code — the bracket is implemented from (3.1)–(3.2), and `9.2-master` with its break leg shows it is a consistent BV bracket in which the CE action solves the master equation, which is all Proposition 9.1 uses.

### §9.4 Scope of the exclusion, proposed R16/R31 wording, self-review

#### Scope of the exclusion

- **Class and reason.** Cyclic $L_\infty$ algebras on a finite-dimensional graded space with odd symplectic $\omega_1$ (§1), i.e. the class Statement A quantifies over. The result is of "not implied" type: for each $k,\ell\ge2$ there is a member of the class — one member serves all $(k,\ell)$ — in which $P_{k,\ell}\ne0$.
- **Relevant cases outside.** (i) The closed-SFT $L_\infty$ algebra and its level truncations: $P_{k,\ell}$ is not evaluated there at any signature, so whether the augmented relations fail for closed SFT itself is open. The paper does not need it: the HS action uses only the $\min(k,\ell)=1$ relations and the non-augmented family (R31). Whether the augmented relations fail for the closed-SFT vertices is open; the mechanism of Cor. 9.2 is a field-redefinition effect (see below) and does not decide it. (ii) Cyclic dg Lie algebras satisfy the augmented relation at every $(k,\ell)\ne(2,2)$ trivially. (iii) The physical sector (inputs at ghost 2): the witness has $P'_{k,\ell}\equiv0$ on ghost-2 inputs at every $(k,\ell)$ (its only ghost-2 direction is the spectator $e$; the CE base sits at ghost 1 and 4), so Cor. 9.2 is silent on the physical sector, where the failure is seen on the generator (iv) at $(2,2)$ only (E5, $\lvert P_{2,2}\rvert=20.3$).
- **Weakest relaxation that evades it.** Drop the augmentation: with $p_{k,0}=V_k$ and $p_{0,\ell}=\tilde\Delta^{\otimes\ell}V_\ell$ the relations close at every $(k,\ell)$ (Theorem A′, §3.4; `9.2-nonaug` only restates check `3.15`, see §9.3, and $B=0$ on the spectator family is `9.2-master`). Alternatively restrict to algebras with $\langle\delta\mathcal F,\delta\mathcal F\rangle\equiv0$ (9.4), e.g. 2-step-nilpotent cubic ones. A third relaxation: ask the question up to cyclic $L_\infty$ isomorphism. The witness is mapped by $(c,c^*,x,x^*)\mapsto(e^{x}c,\,e^{-x}c^*,\,x,\,x^*-c^*_ic^i)$ (it preserves $\theta=c^*_i\,dc^i+x^*dx$ and sends $S'=e^xS_{\rm CE}$ to $S_{\rm CE}$) to $\mathrm{CE}(\mathfrak{sl}_2)\oplus\langle e,e^*\rangle$ with no $e$-couplings, a cyclic dg Lie algebra where $P_{k,\ell}=0$ at every $(k,\ell)\ne(2,2)$. The non-implication of Cor. 9.2 is therefore about the given products, as R16 poses it; the $(2,2)$ failure is invariant (CE is minimal, an isomorphism keeps $\ell_1=0$ and conjugates $\ell_2$ by the symplectic linear part, so $M_{2,2}\ne0$ survives). The same weight argument, $\psi^a\mapsto g(x)^{w_a}\psi^a$ with $w_a=(5-2n_a)/3$ ($n_a$ the ghost number), removes $g$ for any cubic base with $g_0\ne0$. Whether some cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra has $P_{k,\ell}\ne0$ at $(k,\ell)\ne(2,2)$ in every isomorphic representative is open.

#### Ledger wording (applied by the orchestrator, following the node check)

The wording originally proposed here for R16 dropped the "on ghost-2 inputs" qualifier and omitted the isomorphism limit; it is superseded by the critic's text (`agent/reports/critic-026-s9-node-20260930T233600.md`, *Proposed R16 / R31 wording*), which the orchestrator has already applied to the ledger. R16, A4 paragraph, replacing "equivalently by the extra condition $P_{k,\ell}=0$, seen to fail at $(2,2)$, $(2,3)$ and $(3,2)$ — the three signatures checked (026 §3.2; value 20.3 on the generator, also on ghost-$(2,2)$ inputs, check E5)":

> equivalently by the extra condition $P_{k,\ell}=0$ ($P=-\tfrac12M$), which at every $(k,\ell)$ with $k,\ell\ge2$ the cyclic $L_\infty$ relations do not imply on the full graded space: the cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra $\mathrm{CE}(\mathfrak{sl}_2)\oplus\langle e_{(2)},e^*_{(3)}\rangle$ with $S=e^{x}S_{\rm CE}$ has $P_{k,\ell}=-\tfrac12\frac{2^{k-2}+2^{\ell-2}-1}{(k-2)!(\ell-2)!}x^{k-2}y^{\ell-2}M^{\rm CE}_{2,2}\ne0$ at all of them (026 §9, Prop. 9.1/Cor. 9.2, proven; `codes/verify-r16-scope.py` 183/183 at $k,\ell\le5$ and $(2,6)$, $(6,2)$). This witness vanishes identically on ghost-2 inputs, and at $(k,\ell)\ne(2,2)$ it is removed by the cyclic $L_\infty$ isomorphism $(c,c^*,x,x^*)\mapsto(e^{x}c,e^{-x}c^*,x,x^*-c^*_ic^i)$, which maps it to a dg Lie algebra: the non-implication concerns the given products, not their isomorphism class (the $(2,2)$ failure of CE($\mathfrak{sl}_2$) is invariant). On the generator (iv) the failure is also seen at $(2,2)$ on ghost-2 inputs (value 20.3, check E5) and at $(2,3)$, $(3,2)$ (026 §3.2, 7c table). Excl. within cyclic $L_\infty$ algebras (a not-implied statement): $P_{k,\ell}$ is not evaluated on the closed-SFT algebra; cyclic dg Lie algebras satisfy the augmented relation at every $(k,\ell)\ne(2,2)$.

#### Self-review

1. *Objective or literal task?* The objective (R16's quantifier equals what is shown) is met in the wide form: the general statement is proven, so the row can say "every $k,\ell\ge2$", with "generic" calibrated to "not implied / not identically zero", not "nonzero on every algebra".
2. *Weakest step.* (9.10): the bidegree split of the base master equation needs a purely cubic base action. It is stated as a hypothesis of Proposition 9.1, and the closed form is machine-checked at 25 signature-family pairs on two base algebras, with a cancellation control that tests an exact coefficient.
3. *Not checked.* The closed-SFT algebra; generator (iv) at larger signatures. Neither is needed for a not-implied statement, which one explicit counterexample settles.
4. *What a stronger physicist would raise.* "Is $e^{x}S_{\rm CE}$ equivalent to an algebra with $P=0$ by a field redefinition?" Yes, explicitly: the map $(c,c^*,x,x^*)\mapsto(e^{x}c,\,e^{-x}c^*,\,x,\,x^*-c^*_ic^i)$ of the weakest-relaxation paragraph removes the witness at every $(k,\ell)\ne(2,2)$. But $P_{k,\ell}$, like Statement A itself, is a statement about the given products and is not invariant under nonlinear field redefinitions (§6 4(b): the non-augmented family is the Taylor expansion about a background). The counterexample is valid for the statement as posed. Whether the augmented relations can be restored after a suitable redefinition is a different question and is not asked by R16.
